# GLM review 2026-10-31 (glm-5.3): 535 findings, 233 high severity, 17 shard(s)

Revision 3475934fd866. Shards: agentbox, agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dreamlab-ai-website, estate, knowledgegraph, nostr-rust-forum, ontomark, sidestr-rs, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visionflow, visiongraph, visiontouch-rs, vowl-wasm. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox / critical

### 01. Same-UID trust boundary: any in-container process can forge `X-Agentbox-Pubkey`
- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-11.14
- Evidence: `peerIsProxy` accepts any loopback TCP peer (`agentbox/management-api/lib/agent-event-auth.js:98-105`). Every process in the container runs as the same uid (devuser), so any MCP server, hook, agent process, or script can connect to 127.0.0.1:9090, forge `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` with any 64-hex value, and be issued a `did:nostr` identity. The authors note "a process INSIDE the container reaches management-api over loopback and so passes peerIsProxy" (`agent-event-auth.js:37-39`) and call it "the same-UID boundary (TODO-unified X-1)."
- Failure: a compromised MCP server or a malicious agent script impersonates the operator by setting `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` to the operator's key and issues mandates, approves governance actions, or emits events attributed to another agent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — but severity understated; presented alongside code-cleanup items when it invalidates the identity boundary for all in-container threats)

### 02. Host Docker socket is world-writable by default: every devuser process has host root
- Topics: AB-06.13
- Evidence: `docker-compose.override.yml:117` binds the host Docker socket rw into the container. With `role_isolation = 0` (the default, `agentbox.toml:2506`), the entrypoint runs `chmod o+rw` on the socket every boot (`entrypoint-unified.sh:598-601`). Driving the Docker daemon is root on the host. The compose comment names this as risk R2 (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`).
- Failure: any process running as devuser — including any agent, MCP server, or npm postinstall script — mounts the host filesystem or spawns a privileged container, achieving full host compromise.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open R2 in AB-06.13 — but the default is `role_isolation = 0`, so the risk is active in every default deployment, not a future concern)

### 03. Host OAuth credential bind is writable both directions
- Topics: AB-06.11
- Evidence: the host `~/.claude` is bound read-write at `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude` (`docker-compose.override.yml:84-85`) for the sole purpose of `claude-cred-sync` converging `.credentials.json`. The authors state: "a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's `.claude`; only the configuration-read path was closed."
- Failure: a compromised in-container tool writes a poisoned `.credentials.json` to the host bind; `claude-cred-sync` propagates it to the host's `~/.claude`; the host's Claude Code session is hijacked or its refresh token is replaced.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in AB-06.11)

### 04. Audit chain cannot detect tail deletion: the most recent events can be removed without trace
- Topics: AB-03.12
- Evidence: "INVARIANT deletion at the tail is the one tamper mode a bare hash chain cannot see (`lib/audit-chain.js:14-16`)."
- Failure: an attacker with write access to the JSONL files deletes the last N audit events (e.g., the ones recording an unauthorised action); `verifyFiles` reports `ok: true` because the chain from genesis to the new tail is intact. There is no external anchor or periodic checkpoint.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as an invariant property, not flagged as a defect)

### 05. `payment_settlement` declared `zero-tolerance` but no route passes it to the authority gate
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: "`payment_settlement` is declared `zero-tolerance` with its own task properties (`agentbox.toml:1405`, `:1429`) but no route passes that action class to the authority gate; `mandate_revoke` is the only class any route names (`routes/llm-marketplace.js:473`)."
- Failure: a payment settlement operation is executed without the zero-tolerance governance check that the manifest promises — no signed 31402/31403 cycle, no authority gate, no receipt.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### 06. Privacy filter fail-opens on traversal failure when policy is not strict
- Topics: AB-04.5
- Evidence: `fail()` branches on policy: "strict throws AdapterWriteRejected (`privacy-filter.js:669-675`), otherwise fail-open counter and caller continues (`privacy-filter.js:677-682`)."
- Failure: an agent submits a payload with a reference cycle or nesting beyond the depth limit; the privacy layer cannot traverse it; under a non-strict policy the payload is written to the adapter unredacted, leaking any identifiers or PII it carries.
- Confidence: medium (the default policy is not stated in the material — inferred that non-strict exists as a selectable mode and may be the default)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### 07. Mandate registry can disagree with the relay: entries with no signed event behind them
- Topics: AB-11.7, AB-11.9
- Evidence: "routes/mandate.js:152 — the registry write is never blocked purely by a missing relay signer." The state diagram shows `Minted → UnsignedRegistered` with the note "an entry can sit in registry.json with no signed replaceable event behind it, so the local registry and the relay can disagree about what is granted."
- Failure: the local registry grants access that no relay-verified event supports; a consumer that checks only the relay (the correct sovereign behaviour) denies what the registry allows, or vice versa — a mandate check that depends on which source is consulted.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence in AB-11.9)

### 08. Backup proceeds without the memory DB when PostgreSQL is down, producing a silently incomplete archive
- Topics: AB-07.8
- Evidence: "alt PG not running → B-->>OP: WARNING ruvector-postgres not running — memory DB NOT included in this backup (`agentbox.sh:326`)." The backup continues and produces a tarball. The memory DB is "2M+ rows" and was previously absent from backups entirely.
- Failure: an automated nightly backup runs while PG is restarting; the operator restores from what looks like a valid backup and discovers the entire memory database is gone. The WARNING goes to stdout but the exit code is presumably 0.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the warning exists in the script but is not called out as a defect; the exit code is not stated to be non-zero)

### 09. Break-glass bearer defaults to no expiry, no scope, and in-process-only audit
- Topics: AB-10.3
- Evidence: `BREAK_GLASS = roleSecret('NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER')` (`proxy.mjs:53`). ADR-2027 "adds optional expiry + scope bounds and a per-use fingerprint audit trail (`breakGlassUse` counters), still an in-process receipt, not a durable audit store" — "both bounds default-OFF and opt-in" (`proxy.mjs:529-568`).
- Failure: an operator sets the break-glass bearer for a one-time emergency fix and forgets to unset it; the token never expires, has unlimited scope, and its use is invisible after a restart. Anyone with the shared secret has permanent unauthenticated access to the entire management surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (ADR-2027 and SECURITY-profiles note the custody gap, but the default-off state of all bounds is not surfaced as an active risk)

### 10. `loadOrMint` persist failure yields an unstable DID that changes on every restart
- Topics: AB-11.2
- Evidence: "write throws → catch — persisted = false, NON-FATAL" (`agent-identity.js:164-166`). The divergence note says "key persistence failure yields a valid but unstable identity — the DID changes on next boot."
- Failure: the profile key directory becomes unwritable (disk full, permission change); the agent mints a fresh key on every boot; all URNs, mandates, and pod ACLs bound to the old DID are orphaned; the agent is locked out of its own data.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence in SECURITY-profiles)

### 11. Voicelink hooks are registered on every boot while ADR-2151 is proposed, unimplemented and inactive
- Topics: AB-01.1, AB-05.17, AB-08.16
- Evidence: ADR-2151 is `decision_status: proposed`, `implementation_status: none`, `activation_status: inactive` (`docs/adr/ADR-2151-voicelink-edge-voice.md:5-7`), yet `[voicelink] enabled = true` and `record_events = true` (`agentbox.toml:2226`, `:2233`) cause the entrypoint to register Stop and Notification hooks (`entrypoint-unified.sh:2168-2196`) and the flake to bake `voice-paned` (`flake.nix:1847-1849`).
- Failure: voice recording is active in production with no accepted design record; if the recording behaviour is wrong (wrong consent model, wrong retention), the system is capturing voice events that were never approved for production use.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in AB-01.1, AB-05.17, AB-08.16)

### 12. Adapter connect timeout permanently quarantines a slot even if the service recovers
- Topics: AB-04.6, AB-04.7
- Evidence: "Timeout is a CONNECT FAILURE identical in consequence to an explicit rejection" (`lifecycle.js:32-33`). The late settle is "recorded never re-armed" (`lifecycle.js:264-270`); a slot that connects at 11s with a 10s deadline is quarantined and stays quarantined — `dispatch throws AdapterQuarantined 503`.
- Failure: a transient network blip or slow PG start causes the memory adapter to take 11 seconds to connect; it is permanently quarantined for the lifetime of the process; every memory operation returns 503 until a manual container restart.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the behaviour is described but not flagged as a recovery gap)

### 13. EXP-B8 tick signs with the JunkieJarvis key without consulting the manifest gate
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: "the experiment tick itself signs its one forum summary with the JunkieJarvis key (`scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:528-530`) … and publishes it (`:550-563`) without consulting `junkiejarvisEnabled`."
- Failure: a manifest with `junkiejarvis = false` still sees a signed JunkieJarvis post if the withdrawn tick fires again (e.g., a stale cron entry or manual invocation), bypassing the governance gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in AB-05.13)

### 14. MCP hub is a single point of failure for eight MCP servers
- Topics: AB-02.17, AB-09.8
- Evidence: the hub parks FATAL after `startretries=2` if its projection is not written within 120s (`hub/mod.rs:259`, `flake.nix:3302`). "all refuse connections while this program is down" — eight servers are hub-routed (`agentbox.toml:1600-1603`).
- Failure: the `mcp-hub.json` projection is late or fails during a slow boot; the hub goes FATAL; eight MCP servers (including governance and skill routing tools) become simultaneously unavailable with no automatic recovery until the operator intervenes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt in AB-02.17)

### 15. `skill-route.cjs` fail-opens silently: on error, timeout, 429/529 or a none pick, no routing context is injected and no indication is given
- Topics: AB-08.5
- Evidence: "INVARIANT fail-open - any error, timeout, 429/529 or a none pick returns without injection (`hooks/skill-route.cjs:20` and `:45`)."
- Failure: the System One proxy is degraded or the judge model is unavailable; every user turn receives no skill routing context; agents systematically fail to find the right skills; no warning reaches the operator or the user because the hook silently returns nothing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the fail-open is stated as an invariant, not flagged as an operability risk)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Default privacy-filter policy** — whether the fail-open path in `privacy-filter.js` is reachable under the shipped configuration or only through explicit opt-out (AB-04.5 describes both modes but not the default).
2. **Actual runtime identity of processes** — whether every MCP server, hook and agent truly runs as the same uid, or whether some drop to a different user (the material states devuser is the default but does not enumerate every program's `user=` line).
3. **Backup exit code when PG is down** — whether `./agentbox.sh backup` exits zero or non-zero when the WARNING is emitted (AB-07.8 shows the warning but does not state the exit code).
4. **Audit-chain external anchoring** — whether any external checkpoint, remote replication, or write-once storage exists alongside the JSONL hash chain (the material states the invariant but does not say whether mitigation exists elsewhere).
5. **Multi-instance behaviour of `loadOrMint`** — what happens when two processes call `loadOrMint` for the same profile concurrently (the file write is `writeFileSync` but the read-then-mint-then-write sequence has a TOCTOU window the material does not address).

## agentbox / premortem

# Post-mortem: agentbox production incident (2027-11-02)

## Context

A client engagement was terminated after an attacker, having gained code execution inside the agentbox container via a prompt-injected tool call, was able to (a) read every secret in the container, (b) forge governance events attributed to a legitimate agent identity, and (c) exfiltrate the client's Claude OAuth refresh token to the host filesystem. The audit chain recorded the attacker's actions under the compromised agent's DID for six hours before detection, and the operator could not prove to the client which actions were genuine.

---

### R-1 — Same-UID boundary: any in-container process forges agent identity via loopback X-Agentbox-Pubkey

- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-11.14, AB-11.16, AB-02.24, AB-05.16
- Chain of events:
  1. A Claude Code session loads a compromised skill or tool (supply-chain or prompt injection).
  2. The injected code runs a Bash command as devuser inside the container.
  3. It connects to management-api on 127.0.0.1:9090 — a loopback TCP peer.
  4. `peerIsProxy` returns true (the peer is 127.0.0.1).
  5. The process sets `X-Agentbox-Pubkey: <victim-agent-64-hex>` on its request.
  6. `proxyVerifiedPubkey` accepts the header, returns the forged pubkey.
  7. The process emits `POST /v1/agent-events/emit` with `source_urn` matching the victim agent's DID.
  8. `reconcileSourceUrn` passes (claimed source_urn equals the forged verified DID).
  9. The event enters the hash-chained audit log attributed to the victim agent.
  10. Downstream consumers (forum governance, trajectory analysis, approvals) act on false attribution.
- Evidence:
  - "a process INSIDE the container can still connect over loopback and forge the stamp, the same-UID boundary (TODO-unified X-1)" — AB-10.14.
  - "`proxyVerifiedPubkey(request, env)` returns null unless `peerIsProxy` holds" but peerIsProxy "accepts 127.0.0.1, ::1, ::ffff:127.0.0.1" — AB-11.14.
  - "a 0600 file is a same-uid boundary only. Every agentbox process runs as devuser, so co-resident code can read the agent key, the bridge key and the AoE daemon token alike" — AB-11.16.
  - "the loopback test authenticates the network position, not the process: anything inside the container can open its own loopback connection and forge the stamp" — AB-11.14 Debt note.
  - `role_isolation` defaults to false — AB-05.16, AB-02.24.
- Warning signs visible today:
  - The material explicitly labels this "DEBT residual" and "TODO-unified X-1" — it is a known, accepted risk.
  - The `role_isolation` flag exists specifically to close this class of bypass but ships off.
  - The `capability-scope.js` trust-classification (AB-11.12) is "declarative" with acknowledged bypasses.
- Confidence: high

- Severity: high

---

### R-2 — Host Docker socket widened o+rw every boot: container escape with host root

- Topics: AB-06.13, AB-02.24, AB-02.4
- Chain of events:
  1. Same initial compromise as R-1 (code execution as devuser).
  2. The entrypoint, with `role_isolation = 0` (shipped default), runs `chmod o+rw` on the host Docker socket at `/var/run/docker.sock` on every boot.
  3. The compromised process accesses the Docker daemon through the bound socket.
  4. It runs `docker run -v /:/host alpine cat /host/home/operator/.ssh/id_rsa` or equivalent.
  5. The process reads or modifies any file on the host, including other clients' data, the operator's credentials, and the host's Docker socket permissions themselves.
  6. The socket permission persists on the host inode across container rebuilds — only a host-side `chmod 0660` narrows it.
- Evidence:
  - "the host socket bound rw into agentbox — docker-compose.override.yml:117" — AB-06.13.
  - "flag off: chmod o+rw on the socket every boot — entrypoint-unified.sh:598-601" — AB-06.13.
  - "OPEN R2: the socket is the HOST inode, earlier boots left it o+rw, and only a host-side chmod 0660 narrows it" — AB-06.13.
  - "The Docker daemon is root on the host, so while devuser can drive it every in-container boundary is void" — AB-06.13.
- Warning signs visible today:
  - The compose file itself labels this as "risk R2, owner question Q2" — AB-06.13.
  - `role_isolation = true` would leave the socket as found, but the flag ships false.
- Confidence: high

- Severity: high

---

### R-3 — claude-cred-sync bidirectional bind: in-container process steals or replaces host OAuth tokens

- Topics: AB-05.15, AB-06.11, AB-07.11
- Chain of events:
  1. Same initial compromise as R-1.
  2. The host's `~/.claude/.credentials.json` is bound read-write into the container at `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude/.credentials.json`.
  3. The compromised process reads the file, obtaining the current OAuth access token and rotating refresh token.
  4. The refresh token grants persistent access to the client's Claude account even after the container is destroyed.
  5. Alternatively, the process writes a poisoned credentials file that the sync loop propagates to the host.
  6. The client's Claude account is compromised from the host side, outside the container's audit trail.
- Evidence:
  - "the credential bind stays writable both ways, so a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's .claude" — AB-06.11 Open.
  - "merge per-token, later expiresAt wins recursively" with bidirectional writes — AB-05.15.
  - "host ~/.claude read AND written, bound at /var/lib/agentbox/host-claude" — AB-06.3.
  - "a rotating OAuth refresh token invalidates the OTHER side's refresh token, so both copies must converge fast" — AB-05.15.
- Warning signs visible today:
  - The material labels this as an "Open" risk in AB-06.11: "only the configuration-read path was closed."
- Confidence: high

- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — NIP-98 URL tag path-only `endsWith` comparison weakens endpoint binding

- Topics: AB-10.4, AB-11.14
- Evidence: AB-11.14 states "the originator strips the query string from the signed u tag, so the comparison is path-only — verifyNip98 accepts urlTag.endsWith(url)" and passes `pathOnly = request.url.split('?')[0]` as the `url` parameter. AB-10.4 shows the check as "url tag mismatch - urlTag not equal url and not endsWith(url)".
- Failure: A NIP-98 token legitimately signed for a full URL like `https://any-host/v1/agent-events/emit` validates at management-api for path `/v1/agent-events/emit` because the u tag ends with that path, regardless of which host the signer intended. This permits cross-host token replay if the same path exists on multiple services.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: no

- Severity: medium

---

### F-02 — Break-glass bearer bounds (expiry, scope) are opt-in, default OFF

- Topics: AB-10.3
- Evidence: "both bounds default-OFF and opt-in" — AB-10.3.
- Failure: An operator sets `NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER` without configuring `NIP98_PROXY_BEARER_EXPIRY` or `NIP98_PROXY_BEARER_SCOPE`. The break-glass token then grants unbounded authority indefinitely; if leaked (e.g., via the `?access_token=` WebSocket query parameter appearing in proxy access logs), it cannot be expired by time.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, noted in the material as still tracked)

- Severity: high

---

### F-03 — WebSocket credentials passed via URL query parameter

- Topics: AB-10.9
- Evidence: "parse query access_token or bearer — proxy.mjs:960-961" and "req.headers.authorization = Nostr plus the auth param — proxy.mjs:962-965."
- Failure: The break-glass bearer or NIP-98 token appears in the URL as `?access_token=...`. This is logged by proxies, browser history, and any intermediate network device, persisting the credential beyond the WebSocket's lifetime.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, labelled in AB-10.9)

- Severity: medium

---

### F-04 — K_browser not minted; browsercontainer authenticates as the house key

- Topics: AB-06.12
- Evidence: "K_browser's pubkey is still null, g5-key-split.json:16-19, and the nip98-proxy roster still admits the house key for it, agentbox.toml:2096" — AB-06.12.
- Failure: The browser automation sidecar's Podkey vault and all browser-mediated actions authenticate under the operator's main key rather than the intended scoped `K_browser`. A compromise of the sidecar's encrypted vault yields the house key, not a narrow browser-only credential.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

- Severity: high

---

### F-05 — Hash-chained audit log is blind to tail deletion

- Topics: AB-03.12
- Evidence: "INVARIANT deletion at the tail is the one tamper mode a bare hash chain cannot see (lib/audit-chain.js:14-16)" — AB-03.12.
- Failure: An attacker with filesystem access to `YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` deletes the most recent N events from the tail. The chain verification recomputes hashes for remaining lines and reports `ok: true` because the truncated chain is internally consistent. False audit trail after an incident.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant note)

- Severity: high

---

### F-06 — `/lo/*` viewer bundle served with no authentication

- Topics: AB-03.8
- Evidence: "GET /lo/* — static viewer bundle — no auth (server.js:250-252)" — AB-03.8. "INVARIANT: /v1/* data endpoints stay authed even when the /lo static bundle is public."
- Failure: If the viewer bundle contains any client-identifying metadata, embedded state, or cached data URLs, it is served unauthenticated. An attacker who can reach the loopback-published port reads the bundle.
- Confidence: medium (the bundle itself may contain nothing sensitive, but the surface is unauthenticated by design)
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant, stated as deliberate)

- Severity: low

---

### F-07 — Mandate registry entries can be written unsigned

- Topics: AB-11.7, AB-11.9
- Evidence: "the registry write is never blocked purely by a missing relay signer — routes/mandate.js:152" — AB-11.7. AB-11.9 labels this "DIVERGENCE — an entry can sit in registry.json with no signed replaceable event behind it."
- Failure: A mandate is created while the signer is unavailable. The local registry records the grant, but no kind-30078 event exists on any relay. The local state and the sovereign state disagree about what is granted, and a consumer that verifies only on-chain sees no mandate where the local system sees one.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

- Severity: medium

---

### F-08 — CTC trajectory queue overflow silently drops events

- Topics: AB-08.13
- Evidence: "overflow beyond CTC_QUEUE_MAX IS dropped and logged as INCOMPLETE (:561-563)" — AB-08.13.
- Failure: A long-running session generates more cross-turn-correlation events than the bounded 2000-entry queue can hold between Stop events. Surplus events are dropped. The learning system and trajectory analysis operate on incomplete data without a hard failure signal.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (the code logs it but the material does not flag it as a defect)

- Severity: low

---

### F-09 — voicelink gate enabled while ADR-2151 status is proposed/none/inactive

- Topics: AB-01.1, AB-05.17, AB-08.16
- Evidence: "ADR-2151 is `decision_status: proposed`, `implementation_status: none`, `activation_status: inactive` … yet `[voicelink] enabled = true` makes `voicelinkOn` bake `voice-paned` into the package set" — AB-01.1. "yet voicelink enabled = true registers hooks on every boot" — AB-08.16.
- Failure: voice-paned records Stop and Notification events with no accepted ADR review of its security implications. A defect in the recording path (excessive capture, wrong destination, credential leakage in recorded content) ships to production without the governance gate that an accepted ADR would provide.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

- Severity: medium

---

### F-10 — System One consumers all enabled while ADR-2155 activation is staged

- Topics: AB-05.13, AB-01.1
- Evidence: "the nineteen consumer switches and the unified route are configured, not live: ADR-2155 is `accepted`, `implementation_status: complete`, `activation_status: staged`" — AB-05.13. "the running container keeps the old routing until the new image is activated."
- Failure: On the next `agentbox.sh rebuild`, nineteen consumer gates flip live simultaneously. Any defect in the untested unified routing path (wrong backend, missing fallback, unexpected cost) affects every typed-decision consumer at once, with no gradual rollout.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

- Severity: medium

---

### F-11 — Podkey pin artefact expires 2027-01-11; expiry will silently break browser identity

- Topics: AB-06.12
- Evidence: "the pin now names run 38386898958 and expires 2027-01-11 (podkey.pin:11)" — AB-06.12.
- Failure: If the pin is not re-pinned before 2027-01-11, the browsercontainer build fails on verification or (worse) the extension stops functioning at runtime, breaking browser automation flows with no graceful degradation.
- Confidence: medium (depends on whether the pin expiry causes a build failure or a runtime failure)
- Marked by authors: no

- Severity: medium

---

### F-12 — NIP-07 session secret is per-boot random; sessions do not survive proxy restart

- Topics: AB-10.2, AB-10.8
- Evidence: "SESSION_SECRET = PINNED_SESSION_SECRET or crypto.randomBytes(32).toString(hex)" — AB-10.2. "DIVERGENCE session secret is per-boot — NIP-07 sessions do not survive a proxy restart, by design" — AB-10.2.
- Failure: During an incident or deploy, the nip98-proxy restarts. Every browser session is invalidated. Operators mid-approval-flow are logged out and must re-sign with their NIP-07 extension, potentially at the worst possible moment during an incident response.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

- Severity: low

---

### F-13 — `agentbox.sh config` subcommand unreachable despite existing implementation

- Topics: AB-05.5
- Evidence: "the `config` arm of the dispatch case is unreachable: the argument loop admits only the allowlisted subcommands and `config` is not among them" — AB-05.5.
- Failure: An operator follows documentation or a runbook that references `./agentbox.sh config plan` and receives "Unknown command." They may then edit `agentbox.toml` directly without the validation that `cmd_config` would have run.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

- Severity: low

---

### F-14 — `validate-artifacts.sh` is a syntax/presence check only, not behavioural

- Topics: AB-05.8
- Evidence: "the gate is a SYNTAX and PRESENCE check, not a behavioural one — node --check and --version dominate" — AB-05.8.
- Failure: A service closure passes `node --check` but has a runtime dependency missing from its `node_modules` (e.g., an optional native module that failed to build). The container boots, passes artifact validation, and the service crashes on first real use. `/ready` may not catch it if the service is not `REQUIRED_FOR_READINESS`.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (noted as a limitation)

- Severity: medium

---

### F-15 — `/run/secrets` chowned to devuser 0700 when role_isolation is off

- Topics: AB-06.13, AB-02.24
- Evidence: "flag off: /run/secrets chowned back to devuser 0700 — entrypoint-unified.sh:396-398" — AB-06.13. "nostr.key written 0400 devuser" — AB-02.24.
- Failure: With the shipped default `role_isolation = false`, all secrets under `/run/secrets` — including the nostr bridge signing key, role secrets, and any 0400 key files — are readable by any devuser process. A compromised agent reads the bridge key and can sign nostr events as the agentbox identity, which is the identity trusted by relay allowlists and governance consumers.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, AB-06.13 describes the mechanism)

- Severity: high

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — Same-UID boundary puts the sovereign key, the session socket and journalled attribution inside every agent process
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-16.5, AB-12.6, AB-14.14
- Evidence: `role_isolation` off is the shipped default; `/run/secrets` is handed to devuser at 0700 (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:396-399`) and the bridge key is written 0400 devuser (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`); with the flag off every ROLE variable "stays in PID 1 and reaches every child" under a byte-identical environment (`entrypoint-unified.sh:432,476,1271`, `tests/runtime-contract/RC-X1-06.sh:12`); the nostr-gateway's default identity is `operator`, so it "holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" read from `AGENTBOX_PRIVKEY_HEX` et al. in that shared env (`config/nostr-gateway/gateway.cjs:148-158`, `config/hooks/lib/operator-key.cjs:58`); the session-plane socket admits uid 1000 by design and its `send` accepts **any** slug (`config/custody/session-plane-acl.json:8-11`, `services/agentbox-sessions/src/plane.rs:396-404`, `api.rs:74-76`); a same-UID process passes the loopback check and can stamp any pubkey into journalled `agent_did` (`management-api/lib/action-plane.js:222-233`, `agent-event-auth.js:98-105`).
- Failure: any compromised or prompt-injected agent reads the root operator key from the environment or key file, connects to `sessions.sock` as uid 1000 and injects keystrokes into any session including the operator's coordinator tab, captures panes via `/tabs/:n`, and forges attribution in the journal. Exfiltration of the root key makes the compromise survive container rebuilds.
- Confidence: high on every fact; the composed exploit path is inferred from stated facts.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the same-UID limit is marked (AB-16.1 N4, AB-16.7, AB-14.14 "Debt"); the composition into a full path is not marked anywhere.

### F-02 — Two independent inbox writers with two different allowlists; the JS one is runtime-env-configured
- Topics: AB-13.6, AB-13.1, AB-13.3, AB-13.7
- Evidence: AB-13.6 states as a present-tense DIVERGENCE that the JS `RelayConsumer` subscribes to the same relay and writes to the same `pods/NPUB/events/inbox/` path "with its own allowlist (`AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS`)" (`mcp/nostr-bridge/relay-consumer.js:546`, `management-api/server.js:1366-1428`), while AB-13.3 asserts the ADR-2012 invariant "no runtime mutation path — no auto-add, no fallback" with the Rust allowlist baked at nix build (`flake.nix:1892`); AB-13.1's N1 claims this was RESOLVED (writeInbox=false, Rust sole writer) — the corpus asserts both the divergence and its resolution without reconciling them.
- Failure: whenever the JS consumer writes, a pubkey the build-baked Rust admission gate rejects but the runtime env allowlist accepts still lands in the pod inbox; changing one environment variable mutates ingress authorisation without a rebuild, defeating the ADR-2012 invariant; and the material cannot confirm which writer is active on the deployed image.
- Confidence: high that the divergence and the conflict are stated; medium on the deployed writer state.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE) for the dual writer; the baked-vs-env allowlist contrast and the N1/AB-13.6 self-conflict are not marked.

### F-03 — Published 0.0.0.0 voice doors outside the sanctioned surface, and one bearer token spanning three surfaces
- Topics: AB-12.7, AB-12.3, AB-16.9
- Evidence: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by `docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate"; the Unmute backend holds `BRIDGE_TOKEN` as `KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY` (`server.mjs:608-611`, `voice/README.md:93`) and can therefore POST `/tab0/send`; custody register C3: the proxy break-glass bearer is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4"; the `/feed` WebSocket gate can only be satisfied via `?token=`/`?auth=` in the URL (`server.mjs:499,506`).
- Failure: a LAN actor reaching 8444 reaches the voice loop that drives the coordinator session — the material states no auth on the Unmute `/embed*`/`/api/*` paths; one leaked token simultaneously opens the bridge (session injection), the NIP-98 proxy break-glass, and appears in access logs via query strings; the Unmute container itself is a holder of the bridge bearer.
- Confidence: high on the stated facts; exploitability of 8444 is inferred because console-side auth is not described.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the port divergence and the token-equality note are marked; the Unmute-backend-holds-the-injection-token implication is not.

### F-04 — Break-glass bearer is a full identity bypass with unsafe defaults and placeholder attribution
- Topics: AB-16.6
- Evidence: matching the bearer bypasses NIP-98 entirely ("a full identity bypass while enabled"); "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime"; "unset scope is unrestricted"; accepted requests carry the sentinel `BREAK_GLASS_PUBKEY` so "every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder" (`config/nip98-proxy/proxy.mjs:53,523-567`); it is also accepted via `?access_token=`/`?bearer=` on WS upgrades (see AB-10.9 reference).
- Failure: while enabled (and per F-03 the token equals `BRIDGE_TOKEN`), anyone holding it exercises operator-named routes; a misconfigured deployment makes it permanent and unrestricted; every action taken under it poisons audit and governance records with a fake pubkey.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OPEN).

### F-05 — The governance and spend apparatus exists as code but almost nothing routes through it
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-15.6, AB-14.11, AB-13.16
- Evidence: the authors' own TOP OPEN RISK, only narrowed: every agent-initiated side-effect surface except `POST /v1/tasks` — direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alternate harness — "still reaches EXEC with no interceptor" (`GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:339-342`, `action-plane.js`); the `payment_settlement` zero-tolerance authority class is never passed in production, only tests (`agentbox.toml:1405`, `tests/sovereign/authority.test.js:129,248`); spend sits in "three stores that never reconcile" (`cost_cap/ledger.rs:75`, `routes/payments.js:205`, `lib/llm-marketplace.js:85`); kinds 38303/38305 are deliberately non-federated so "a peer that federates the other four kinds never learns of a denial or a revocation" (`agentbox.toml:332-333`); ADR-2087 reaches live only on a first real governance response whose producer the record does not name (`scripts/activation/adr-2087-check.sh:332-344`).
- Failure: an agent-initiated destructive or spend action executes with no approval gate and no journal entry; a revoked marketplace grant remains honoured by federated peers; no single number answers what the container has spent.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE1, DEBT, DIVERGENCE, OPEN).

### F-06 — The corpus contradicts itself on whether nightly session egress is redacted
- Topics: AB-16.11, AB-13.9, AB-13.17
- Evidence: AB-16.11 says the mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text" before the NIP-59 wrap ("one of them unredacted before wrapping"); AB-13.9 shows `redactForEgress(body)` applied before publish with fail-closed skip on redaction failure (`config/hooks/lib/egress-policy.cjs:118`, `config/hooks/nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457`); AB-13.17 says "RESOLVED ADR-2026 — the mirror and the kind-30840 digest now share ONE policy contract: … mandatory redaction … cross-checked by one fixture".
- Failure: if AB-16.11 describes the deployed hook, session content leaves the box to a public cloud worker unredacted on every Stop event; if AB-13.9 does, then a governing security document materially misstates the egress posture. Either way, sign-off on egress cannot proceed from this material.
- Confidence: high that the claims conflict; which is true is not judgeable here.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — each note is internally consistent; the cross-document contradiction is unflagged.

### F-07 — The operative secrets backup is an unencrypted ZIP; the encrypted tool is not in the running image
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12
- Evidence: register row C7 — `scripts/backup-secrets.sh` produces a workspace ZIP with "ordinary zip … NO encryption flags … The script was not run and no backup contents were inspected"; `agentbox-secret-backup` (age-encrypted, cannot write plaintext) exists but "Explicit operator invocation remains required … The active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" (G17).
- Failure: any run of the live script writes a plaintext archive of selected secrets onto the host-visible workspace bind; recovery has never been exercised; rotation/revocation for any register row is NOT IMPLEMENTED (ADR-2027 proposed/none/inactive), so a lost container plus a lost or stolen ZIP is unrecoverable key loss, including the sovereign identity.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (register note, G17, ADR-2027 status).

### F-08 — Cross-repo provenance identity lives in one append-only JSONL with no recovery path
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.5, AB-17.6
- Evidence: content-addressed crossings mint a fresh sha256-12 with "no algebraic inverse … lose the store, lose the crossing"; the durable store is a single file at `BC20_URN_MAPPING_PATH` or `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl`, last-writer-wins; the PROTOCOL-registry requires persistence, replay, round-trip and recovery receipts and "persistence, replay, round-trip and recovery receipts do not exist"; `roundTrips()` proves the algebra against a fresh in-memory store only (`bc20-provenance-bridge.js:307,346`).
- Failure: truncation, corruption or disk loss of that one file permanently and silently orphans every VisionClaw `execution`/`kg` identifier from its agentbox origin — `toAgentbox` then returns null and the crossing is unrecoverable; no backup for this file is mentioned anywhere.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, B01).

### F-09 — Records designed as evidence fail silently: chain blind to tail deletion, in-memory event ring, fail-open dream journal, unjournalled experiment actions
- Topics: AB-14.9, AB-17.1, AB-23.2, AB-14.15
- Evidence: "deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see" (`management-api/lib/audit-chain.js:13-16`); the agent-event buffer is a ring capped at 1000, "NOT a durable record", numeric ids restart at 1 per process (`agent-event-publisher.js`, `routes/agent-events.js:216-251`); "Every journalled side effect fails open" (`services/dream-engine/src/journal.rs:30`) — the ADR-2071 journal records nothing while the API is down; the EXP-B8 tick takes two outward side effects, a signed forum post and a `gh` pull request, and "records neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal" (`scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:550-606`), on a 30-minute crontab.
- Failure: after an incident the audit substrate cannot show what happened: a daily JSONL tail can be removed undetected, restarts orphan numeric event ids, dream side effects during API outages leave no record, and an unattended experiment posts to the forum and opens PRs off the record.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: mostly yes (notes and OPENs); the aggregate gap is not marked.

### F-10 — The default skill router sends operator prompts off-box, and now also synced-account skill descriptions
- Topics: AB-22.2
- Evidence: `router = jev` is the default (`agentbox.toml:1293`) and cascade is off by default (`agentbox.toml:1309`), so "a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container"; candidates "GROW past the manifest" to include claude.ai synced skills and plugin skills sent as candidate rubrics (`config/hooks/lib/skill-route.cjs:277,587-594`); the OPEN asks whether ADR-2090's egress acceptance covers those descriptions and answers only "the record notes only the behaviour".
- Failure: routine operator turns — potentially containing secrets or incident detail — are shipped to the System One proxy/vendor by default, and the candidate list now leaks the operator's personal claude.ai skill inventory, an egress the accepting ADR never considered.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, OPEN).

### F-11 — Dream nights read repository content through an external LLM by default, unattended
- Topics: AB-23.2, AB-23.15
- Evidence: `llm_provider = "zai"` is the default (`agentbox.toml:2585`); "nightly repository content leaves the LAN unless llm_provider = loom" during the 01:00-05:00 UTC unattended window; the LAN-only Loom path is opt-in (`services/dream-engine/src/llm.rs:205`).
- Failure: every night, source files from nominated repos are POSTed to z.ai with no human in the loop; a mis-scoped `scan` entry or honoured source request exfiltrates more than intended, and nothing bounds what the tree-read collects beyond the slot config.
- Confidence: high on the default and the egress; the over-broad-read scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (RESOLVED-as-deliberate-choice in AB-23.2, DEBT in AB-23.15).

### F-12 — role_isolation is a silent-degradation trap and the identity-port ACL story contradicts itself
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-14.16, AB-16.13, AB-16.1
- Evidence: under the flag the gateway "holds no key file, so it gets none and exits"; dream-engine's forum round trip is "SKIPPED, fail-open" (`governance.rs:922-926`); JunkieJarvis fails closed; the mirror falls back to a throwaway sender — all Open; AB-23.20 claims "31400 to 31405 may NEVER be granted" (`identity_port/acl.rs:65-73`) while AB-14.16/AB-16 say the loader "now admits 31400 and 31402 to an `agent_governance` key" (`acl.rs:84,304-307`) and the shipped ACL's own comment is stale (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:13`); no `agent_governance` key exists yet (ADR-2122 ER-6, `:643`); the 38 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials remain ambient in every agent process regardless of the flag (Q8 pending, `env-classes.json:92`).
- Failure: an operator flips the catalogued rebuild-class flag and loses voice control and forum governance with only warning logs; the planned W3b cutover is blocked on a key that does not exist and on an ACL whose permissiveness the corpus describes two incompatible ways.
- Confidence: high on the degradations and the contradiction; medium on operational intent.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the Opens and the acl.json drift are marked; the AB-23.20-vs-AB-14.16 conflict is not.

### F-13 — The governed ontology decision end invokes a subcommand the baked binary no longer carries
- Topics: AB-14.13, AB-22.11
- Evidence: the ADR-2108 path carries `propose_command`/`vault edit --expect` argv in the 31402 (`lib/ontology-propose.js`, `lib/elevation-publisher.js:200`); "the baked `vault 0.1.0` no longer carries `find`, `retrieve`, `tree`, `edit`, `create` or `apply`, which moved to the `ontomark` CLI" (`docs/adr/ADR-2108…:238`); only `vault propose` survives.
- Failure: an operator approving an elevation executes a `vault edit` argv that fails with unknown-subcommand — the decision half of the governed write path is broken at runtime, after the approval event has already been signed and published.
- Confidence: high that the drift is stated; the runtime breakage is inferred from it.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift); the consequence is not.

### F-14 — The session plane's own acceptance run shows blocked sessions, redactor 500s, resettable backoff, and a fail-open injection fallback
- Topics: AB-12.15, AB-12.5
- Evidence: the acceptance run "left antigravity blocked on sign-in and openrouter on a 401, and session-boundary turn events returned HTTP 500 from the beads redactor" (`docs/adr/ADR-2153…:169-172,195-198`); the restart counter is process-local so "a restart of the daemon itself forgets the hour's history and a command that dies on launch gets three fresh attempts" (`services/agentbox-sessions/src/reconcile.rs:96-98`); tab0-bridge FAIL-OPEN degrades to raw `tmux send-keys -t agentbox:0` when the plane is unreachable (`server.mjs:159-168`, ADR-044 D3), and the daemon "never refuses a dead pane" for the coordinator (`plane.rs:398-401`).
- Failure: sessions silently sit dead — the exact failure ADR-2153 was written after; a crash-looping command restarts without bound across daemon restarts; while the plane is down, injections bypass its dead-pane and conflict handling and land in whatever pane the legacy target resolves to.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Debt, FAIL-OPEN note).

### F-15 — Knowledge units can never be retired or superseded through any real surface
- Topics: AB-22.7
- Evidence: "no code path in this crate or colloquy-mcp's six verbs currently WRITES UnitStatus::Retired or Superseded; only tests set them directly. The 'signed decision' retirement door ADR-2085/ADR-2086 describe is not yet wired to an MCP verb at this revision" (`colloquy-core/src/graduation.rs:400` test only).
- Failure: the governance knowledge base accumulates forever; a superseded or dangerous unit remains servable — ranked down at best, never hidden, never removable — so the lifecycle the ADRs specify is unreachable in production and disputed content persists indefinitely.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN).

## Not judgeable from this material

1. What authentication, if any, sits in front of the published 8444/8443 voice surfaces (the Caddy config beyond the excerpt, and whether the Unmute frontend/backend authenticate callers) — required to size F-03.
2. The deployed wiring state of the inbox writers: whether `AGENTBOX_POD_INBOX_WRITER`/`writeInbox=false` is actually projected on the running image, and what `AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS` contains — required to size F-02.
3. The actual `nostr-live-mirror.cjs` revision in the running container (does it call `redactForEgress` before publish?) — resolves the F-06 contradiction.
4. Backup and durability arrangements for the `/var/lib/agentbox` state — `bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl`, `executed.json`, `LEDGER.md`, night `run-state.json` — and which events adapter implementation backs the execution journal.
5. The relay/cloud-worker half of the governance round trip (hard-delete semantics on kind-5, case subscription) which AB-23.17 itself calls "asserted, not verified", plus who is the named human expected to produce the governance response that gates ADR-2087 B7.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — agentbox production incident (custody/security class)

Scope note: every claim below is taken from the estate-review corpus at its stamped revisions (`verified_commit 6500582d…` for agentbox). Citations are the ones the corpus itself gives; nothing was verified against running code. Where a chain of events reasons past what the diagrams assert, the confidence line says "inferred".

---

### R-1 — The shipped default puts every signing key in the same uid: one compromised process becomes the operator
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-14.14, AB-17.2, AB-16.5, AB-13.5, AB-14.16
- Chain of events:
  1. The container ships with `role_isolation = false` — "the shipped default" (AB-16.1 N4; AB-16.13 OFF subgraph, "today's boot, unchanged").
  2. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill, agent and consultant child runs as `devuser` uid 1000. `/run/secrets` is handed to devuser at 0700 (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:396-399`) and the bridge key is written 0400 devuser (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`). With the flag off, capture/scrub "return at their first line, so every other ROLE variable stays in PID 1 and reaches every child" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:432`, `:476`).
  3. A workload running under devuser — a consultant vendor CLI, a ruflo orchestration child, tooling from a cloned repo — reads the operator key: `operatorKeyHex` resolves "first non-empty of AGENTBOX_PRIVKEY_HEX, AGENTBOX_BRIDGE_SK, OPERATOR_NOSTR_PRIVKEY" (`config/hooks/lib/operator-key.cjs:58`), all ambient in PID 1's environment.
  4. Holding the root secret makes the process the commander. The nostr-gateway drops every wrap whose "sealed sender != commanderPub" (`config/nostr-gateway/gateway.cjs:673`) and `adminPub = getPublicKey(rawSk)` is derived from exactly that key (`gateway.cjs:151,157`). Free-form instructions route through one bounded Sonnet C2 call into the tmux fleet; `/spawn` creates sessions; `/tab` and `/say` type into any agent window (`gateway.cjs:269-320`, `:322`, `:526`; AB-13.5, AB-13.8).
  5. Attribution is simultaneously forgeable at the API layer: a same-container process passes the loopback peer check (`agent-event-auth.js:98-105`, `:117`) and can "stamp any pubkey into the journalled `agent_did`" (`management-api/lib/action-plane.js:222-233`, the same-UID boundary, TODO-unified X-1).
  6. The session-plane socket admits uid 1000 by design (`config/custody/session-plane-acl.json:8-11`, `services/agentbox-sessions/src/api.rs:70-76`), so the process also drives sessions directly.
  7. Containment is not available mid-incident: the identity port does not run with the flag off (`services/nostr-pod-bridge/src/identity_port/mod.rs:92-96`), and under the flag the gateway, JunkieJarvis, colloquy and the mirror hook "still read ab-identity key files that devuser cannot open… so they fail closed until the W3b cutover" (`docs/adr/ADR-2122…md:244-245`) — flipping it disables forum signing and the mirror rather than isolating the key.
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4 ("each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key, and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design"); AB-16.7 role_isolation-off branch (peer read SUCCEEDS, socket ADMITTED, bridge key read SUCCEEDS); AB-14.14 Debt; AB-17.2 Debt; AB-16.5 DIVERGENCE ("out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key"); AB-14.16 OPEN1 (port clients unwired); AB-16.7 note on GOVERNANCE-capabilities item 5.
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus labels this repeatedly and precisely — "same-uid boundary" (AB-16.1 N4), "a same-container process still passes the loopback test" (AB-14.14), "the residual limit, stated in the governing docs" (AB-16.7), the W3b cutover owed (ADR-2122:244-245), and the boot rehearsal not yet run (AB-36 referenced).
- Confidence: high that the boundary is exactly as described (directly stated in four topics); the identity of the first compromised process is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Break-glass bearer: an unscoped, unexpiring shared secret that also authenticates the bridge, carried in URLs, with placeholder attribution
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9, AB-12.3, AB-12.5, AB-12.7
- Chain of events:
  1. `NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER` break-glass is read at process start (`config/nip98-proxy/proxy.mjs:53`) and accepted on the LAN-facing proxy port 9096 (`proxy.mjs:523`), including "via ?access_token= / ?bearer= on WS upgrades" (AB-16.6 DIVERGENCE note citing INGRESS-identity) — the token travels in URLs.
  2. The token value "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (AB-16.9 C3), so one leaked string is valid in two planes.
  3. ADR-2027's lifecycle is open: "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime", "unset scope is unrestricted", "durable per-use receipt is unproven"; record status "decision proposed, implementation none, activation inactive" (`docs/adr/ADR-2027…md:193-198`; AB-16.6).
  4. On match, the proxy forwards with `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` = `BREAK_GLASS_PUBKEY`, a sentinel that "is NOT a real pubkey, so every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder" (`proxy.mjs:567`, AB-16.6).
  5. The same string, as Bearer TOKEN, satisfies tab0-bridge's gate (`config/tab0-bridge/server.mjs:583`), which covers `/tab0/send`, `/nostr/*`, `/tabs*`, `/turns`, `/feed` (AB-12.3 invariant, ADR-044 finding 1) — i.e. text injection into the coordinator agent session, outbound Nostr sends, and pane reads.
  6. Post-incident reconstruction fails twice: the acceptance log records a fingerprint, not an identity ("logged with fingerprint, NOT the token", `proxy.mjs:554-566`), and the downstream identity is a placeholder, so no request can be tied to a person.
- Evidence: AB-16.6 sequence and notes (ADR-2027 requirement box; DIVERGENCE "a full identity bypass while enabled"); AB-16.9 C3 shared-value-until-Q4; `proxy.mjs:437` constant-time compare, `:533-567` checks; AB-12.3 gate; AB-12.7 DIVERGENCE that only 9096 is the sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door while 8444/8443 are published 0.0.0.0 (`docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`).
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus already names this "the least governed credential" (AB-16.6 title) and records the lifecycle as unimplemented; the shared value with BRIDGE_TOKEN is documented; ADR-2027's `verified_paths` is the empty list, so none of the custody claims are mechanically checked.
- Confidence: high on every mechanic (all directly stated); that this was the incident vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Plaintext secrets archive on a host-visible bind, with no rotation, no revocation and no confirmed custodian
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12, AB-16.13, AB-13.3
- Chain of events:
  1. `scripts/backup-secrets.sh` collects selected secrets "into a ZIP" at `workspace/secret-backups` (AB-16.9 C7). SECURITY-profiles records: "ordinary zip and unzip integrity testing with NO encryption flags and no explicit umask or chmod. Final permissions depend on the invoking environment" (AB-16.8 BackedUp note).
  2. The workspace is a host-visible bind mount — the corpus itself shows live keys living on it: the faucet treasury key at `/home/devuser/workspace/sidestr/agents/treasury.key` (`agentbox.toml:1926`, AB-16.13 tension note).
  3. The replacement age-encrypted tool exists (`services/secret-backup`, AB-16.12) but "Explicit operator invocation remains required… The active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" (AB-16.12 N2) — the plaintext path remains the operative backup.
  4. Rotation and revocation are "NOT IMPLEMENTED" for any register row (AB-16.8 RotatedProposed/RevokedProposed notes; ADR-2027 proposed/none/inactive), and every register row has "custodian UNCONFIRMED, deployed location UNCONFIRMED, rotation cadence UNCONFIRMED" (AB-16.9).
  5. Publisher revocation at the relay requires a full nix rebuild — "the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle" (AB-16.8 note; AB-13.3, `relayAllowedPubkeysCsv` baked at `flake.nix:1892`, "no runtime mutation path").
  6. Loss or theft of the archive therefore yields durable estate-wide key compromise with no rapid revocation path and no custodian to notice.
- Evidence: AB-16.8 state diagram notes (Provisioned IMPLEMENTED / BackedUp "PARTIAL and unverified… The script was not run and no backup contents were inspected" / Rotated & Revoked NOT IMPLEMENTED); AB-16.9 C7 and the U node; AB-16.12 N2, N4; AB-16.13 tension on the workspace bind.
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus flags the backup as unverified in its own words, flags the register as a starting set with zero confirmed custodians, and notes the derived HMAC keys are absent from it entirely (AB-16.9 N2).
- Confidence: medium — the script's properties are asserted by SECURITY-profiles but explicitly unverified by its authors; the theft chain is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The nostr-gateway signs as the root operator key by default
- Topics: AB-16.5
- Evidence: `AGENTBOX_GATEWAY_IDENTITY === 'gateway'` derived-child mode is opt-in via env; the default `'operator'` branch sets `sk = rawSk` — "the gateway signs AS the operator (`gateway.cjs:155`)"; DIVERGENCE note: "out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key."
- Failure: any compromise of the gateway process (or a same-uid read of the operator key) authenticates to the cloud relay and issues commands/replies under the operator identity, including NIP-42 AUTH as the operator.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-02 — Derived child keys are off the custody register and rotate without revocation
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-16.9
- Evidence: gateway/mirror children are `HMAC-SHA256(operator_sk, tag)`; "rotation is 'bump the env tag' with no revocation of the previously derived child at any relay" (AB-16.5, DIVERGENCE ADR-2027); "The two derived HMAC keys in AB-16.5 are absent from the register entirely" (AB-16.9 N2). The port returns the mirror child `secret_hex` to devuser (`port.rs:635-637`, `identity-port-acl.json:42`).
- Failure: a leaked mirror child (imported on the operator phone) keeps reading gift-wrapped session turns after "rotation", because the old child stays valid at every relay; under the flag the child secret is additionally devuser-readable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; TENSION in AB-16.5/AB-16.13)

### F-03 — Relay publisher revocation requires a full image rebuild
- Topics: AB-13.3, AB-16.8
- Evidence: `relayAllowedPubkeysCsv` is baked at nix build (`flake.nix:1892`); "no runtime mutation path — no auto-add, no fallback… Changing allowed_pubkeys requires ./agentbox.sh rebuild" (AB-13.3); "publisher revocation needs a full rebuild — the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle" (AB-16.8).
- Failure: a compromised publisher key retains relay admission and inbox-write authority for the entire build-deploy cycle while the incident is being contained.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (noted in AB-16.8 diagram and ADR-2012 Consequences)

### F-04 — The audit hash chain cannot detect tail deletion and tolerates an unverified prefix
- Topics: AB-14.9
- Evidence: "deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see" (`audit-chain.js:13-16`); records with neither hash nor prev_hash are tolerated as `legacyPrefix` while the chain has not started (`audit-chain.js:113-118`).
- Failure: an actor with file access deletes the newest daily-JSONL records (the most recent spend receipts); `verifyLines` passes on the truncated chain, or forged "pre-ADR-039" records are prepended and accepted unverified.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (tail-deletion note; prefix tolerance drawn without warning)

### F-05 — The agent-event buffer is a non-durable ring and event ids restart per process
- Topics: AB-17.1, AB-17.2
- Evidence: "eventBuffer is a ring capped at maxBufferSize 1000 — the oldest event is shift()ed out, so the buffer is NOT a durable record" (AB-17.1); "GET /v1/agent-events?id=<ref> for a BARE NUMERIC id stays process-local… because ids restart at 1 per process" (`agent-events.js:216-251`).
- Failure: a restart silently discards up to 1000 in-flight events and makes every historical numeric id ambiguous — consumers correlating by id mis-join events across process generations; post-incident forensics cannot retrieve pre-restart numeric ids.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("NOT a durable record" note)

### F-06 — BC20 crossing: byte-parity of the content digest is asserted nowhere, and store loss is identity loss
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.5, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: "JS sha12 hashes a UTF-8 string, VisionClaw content_address hashes bytes, byte-parity asserted nowhere" (AB-17.10 DIVERGENCE, PROTOCOL-registry); "for execution and kg the local is a fresh sha256-12 with no algebraic inverse. Recovery is store-dependent, so store loss is identity loss. No recovery receipt exists for the durable store" (AB-17.6); `JsonlUrnMappingStore` is last-writer-wins; `roundTrips()` proves the algebra "against a FRESH IN-MEMORY store" only (`bc20-provenance-bridge.js:307`).
- Failure: JS and Rust disagree on a digest for non-ASCII content, producing two different URNs claimed to be the same crossing; or the JSONL mapping store is lost/corrupt and every past `urn:agentbox` activity/thing identity becomes permanently unrecoverable on the VisionClaw side, severing provenance chains.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, PROTOCOL-registry)

### F-07 — Spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-15.2
- Evidence: DEBT note — the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger (`cost_cap/ledger.rs:75`), the pod sats balance behind `GET /v1/pay/balance` (`payments.js:205`), and the marketplace token_budget on a grant (`llm-marketplace.js:85`): "No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent."
- Failure: a workload that mixes tree-search reservations, pod debits and marketplace grants exceeds the operator's real budget while every individual gate reports green; the overspend surfaces only on an invoice.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-08 — The `payment_settlement` zero-tolerance class never reaches the authority gate
- Topics: AB-14.11, AB-15.6
- Evidence: "the classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance (agentbox.toml:1405) but no production path passes that actionClass. The only call sites are tests, and management-api/lib/authority.js:226 never sees it" (AB-14.11 DEBT, repeated at AB-15.6).
- Failure: a payment route settles sats with no approval receipt, no deny journal and none of the ADR-2087 machinery — an irreversible spend leaving no governance record.
- Confidence: high (gap is stated; exploitation inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-09 — One decision point for one surface: most agent side-effect paths remain ungated and some outward effects are unrecorded
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.15, AB-23.2
- Evidence: "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor — the gap is narrower, not closed" (AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1); the dream engine "RECORDS each side effect… but nothing there approves or denies" (AB-14.1, ADR-2071 Phase 1); EXP-B8 "takes two outward side effects… and records neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal" (AB-14.15 Open).
- Failure: an unattended background job performs an outward side effect — a signed forum post, an opened PR, an egress — with no approval, no journal row and no attribution until a human notices externally.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE "TOP OPEN RISK"; Open)

### F-10 — nostr-send reports success on non-delivery
- Topics: AB-12.14
- Evidence: "nostr-send.cjs is fail-open, exit 0 even on delivery failure — ok true means handed to the relay path, not delivered (`server.mjs:466-467`)."
- Failure: operator notifications (veto/question alerts, replies via `/nostr/send`) silently drop while the bridge returns `ok true`; the operator believes a message was delivered and incident response is delayed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a plain note)

### F-11 — HNSW recall degrades silently after maintenance; the harness is the only detector; concurrent rebuild corrupts
- Topics: AB-20.10, AB-20.7, AB-20.9
- Evidence: "Recall drops with no error anywhere — nothing in the query path reports it, which is why the harness is the only detector" (AB-20.10); `memory_sweep_episodic` performs bulk deletes (AB-20.7); "CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY … VERIFIED DOUBLE-INSERTION, every tuple indexed twice. Never do this."
- Failure: a sweep or bulk ingest degrades the graph; agents keep querying without error and progressively fail to retrieve stored operational memory — silent knowledge loss until someone runs the recall harness; an operator "recovering" with a concurrent build double-indexes every tuple.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (index law documented as invariant)

### F-12 — Knowledge units cannot be retired or superseded through any MCP verb; disputed units stay servable
- Topics: AB-22.7
- Evidence: "no code path in this crate or colloquy-mcp's six verbs currently WRITES UnitStatus::Retired or Superseded; only tests set them directly. The 'signed decision' retirement door… is not yet wired to an MCP verb" (OPEN note); "Disputed units are still servable and are ranked down, never hidden."
- Failure: disproved knowledge persists indefinitely and continues to be served to consuming agents; the knowledge base accumulates wrong answers with no operator verb to remove them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-13 — The raw-email tier is gated by knowledge of a public value
- Topics: AB-16.10
- Evidence: tier 2 checks a caller-supplied `nostr_pubkey` against `PRIVILEGED_NOSTR_PUBKEYS`; the operator pubkey is read from `AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX` at call time; the material's own invariant states "a Nostr PUBLIC key is the publishable half… Passing it as a tool argument is the intended design."
- Failure: any bearer-token holder — every agent process — passes the operator's well-known public pubkey and receives verbatim mail with real headers, bypassing the privacy filter; the authorized path is indistinguishable from abuse and the audit line carries only an 8-char fingerprint.
- Confidence: high on the mechanics as drawn; that this is a defect rather than accepted risk is inferred (the doc frames it as intended).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (asserted as invariant)

### F-14 — Boot-time trust seeding auto-trusts every checkout under the workspace
- Topics: AB-16.4
- Evidence: trust-seed marks trusted "workspace root plus every git checkout or worktree under it, bounded by depth" (`trust-seed.cjs:78-86`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:1801-1814`); DIVERGENCE note: "this pre-accepts a SECURITY prompt for unattended agents. It is a deliberate availability-over-confirmation trade, not a hardening measure."
- Failure: an agent clones a repository whose project-local hooks or MCP config are hostile; at the next restart trust-seed marks it trusted and the harness loads its configuration unchallenged — supply-chain execution as devuser, which per R-1 is one uid away from every key.
- Confidence: the auto-trust behavior is stated; the hostile-config chain is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-15 — Key persistence failure yields a valid but unstable agent identity
- Topics: AB-16.7
- Evidence: "persistence failure is NON-FATAL (`agent-identity.js:168-171`), so a container can run a whole session on an in-memory key that vanishes at restart. 'Key persistence failure can produce a valid but unstable identity.'" (DIVERGENCE note, citing SECURITY-profiles).
- Failure: a full disk or unwritable path at mint time leaves a session signed under a key that disappears at restart; every URN, pod datum and forum attribution for that session becomes permanently unverifiable, and the re-minted identity has no linkage to the old one.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — Production ships with every secret in every process's environment: role isolation is designed, documented, and off
- Topics: AB-36 (intro), AB-36.6, AB-36.7
- Evidence: `[security].role_isolation` ships false (`agentbox.toml:2506`); the 11 ROLE-class names (sovereign, operator, bridge, JunkieJarvis, concierge keys; break-glass bearer; session secret; `TAILSCALE_AUTHKEY`) are "captured to files and unset under the flag only" (`env-classes.json:33`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:419`), so with the flag off they stay in PID 1's environment, which "supervisord passes … to every child" (AB-36 intro). Independently, 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names stay ambient in both modes, including provider API keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (`env-classes.json:90`, `env-classes.json:92`, `env-classes.json:195`, `env-classes.json:203`, `env-classes.json:211`, `ADR-2122:178-179`). Activation requires an owner flag-on rehearsal receipt that does not exist (`ADR-2122:155-163`).
- Failure: any agent tool, hook or MCP server in the container — including a prompt-injected one — can read the sovereign Nostr identity key material variables, the management API key, the voice bridge token and use the agent's ssh-agent. One compromised tool is a full-estate identity compromise, which is exactly the scenario ADR-2122 was written to stop; the stop is not engaged.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in AB-36.7 for the flag; Debt in AB-36.6 for the 37 names) — but the compound shipped posture is presented as background, not as a standing risk

### F-02 — Host Docker socket is world-accessible and bound into the container: root on the host for anyone inside
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: "the host Docker socket inode stays world-accessible until the host chmods it. The socket is a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it" (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`); the flag-on boot "can only detect and record `degraded:docker-socket`" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:614`); the host rehearsal half "records the mode for the owner without gating on it" (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`) while ADR-2122 counts the degraded state as a failure (`ADR-2122:162`).
- Failure: any process in the container that can reach the socket inode talks to the host Docker daemon, i.e. root on the host — trivially escaping every in-container boundary in AB-36 and every secret boundary in F-01. The container-side code deliberately does not and cannot fix it; remediation lives on a host the repo does not control, with no gate enforcing it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, "Open (R2)")

### F-03 — Shipped image bakes `NOPASSWD: ALL` sudo for devuser; it is inert only because two independent guards hold
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: built with `role_isolation` off — "which is how it ships" — the image keeps devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:4045`) and bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:4059`); the same flake asserts "the setuid wrapper is gone and there is no runtime escalation path" (`flake.nix:3812-3816`). The grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships; "reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary in this topic" (AB-36.7), probed by check (f) (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:593-594`).
- Failure: a compose change that drops `no-new-privileges`, or an image variant that ships a setuid sudo, silently converts every devuser process — every agent — into root with no password. The flake's own "no runtime escalation" claim is false for the artefacts it bakes; the safety rests on a compose flag and an absence, neither enforced by a test named here.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — The dream engine's default provider is a vendor (Z.AI) and the egress register says nothing accepts the corpus leaving
- Topics: AB-24.7, AB-24.9, AB-24.14
- Evidence: the night provider defaults to zai (`agentbox.toml:2585`, `config.rs:184`), so the Loom LAN path is opt-in; the egress register row `zai-night` carries "marker no-accepted-record, nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`config/egress-policy.json:346-361`). AB-24.9 N1 records this as "RESOLVED ADR-2053 … deliberate choice" — resolved for the *documentation* question, while the register still states no acceptance exists.
- Failure: every default-configured dream-machine night ships the estate's corpus and prompts to a third-party vendor under a `no-accepted-record` marker. If any corpus content is non-public, this is an unaccepted data egress that the estate's own compliance instrument is already flagging, discovered only when someone reads the register row rather than a gate.
- Confidence: high on the facts; medium on whether this is a defect rather than a pending owner decision the register says has not been taken — inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Settlement-chain signer and spend-capable faucet keys are readable by every process today; legacy copies survive even after migration
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-36.3
- Evidence: with the flag off — "which is how it ships" — producers and faucets run `user=devuser` (`flake.nix:3107`, `flake.nix:3142`) and "key, parent credential and treasury key read straight from their at-rest paths by devuser" (`run-producer.sh:70-71`, `run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1926`); the secrets-volume chown to devuser is skipped only under the flag (`entrypoint-unified.sh:557`). The custody migration "copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (`ADR-2122:232-235`); the faucet's workspace file is retained as its "legacy twin" (`config/role-accounts.json:78`).
- Failure: any agent tool running as devuser reads the chain signer key and the faucet treasury key. With the signer key an attacker mints blocks — the "a third party can verify" property of the sealed chain collapses; with the treasury key they drain grants. Even after the operator turns role isolation on and migration runs, the devuser-readable twins remain until someone remembers to purge them by hand.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (custody; the chain is test-value today, which caps the loss but not the integrity damage)
- Marked by authors: partially — yes (Tension) in AB-34.3 for the faucet twin; the flag-off devuser-readable state in AB-34.6 is stated as plain fact, unflagged

### F-06 — The sealed chain's genesis does not commit its parent or containment, though ADR-2103 D3 says it does
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-32.3
- Evidence: D3 claims `parent`, `headerProfile`, `currencyPin`, `cashOut`, `pegConfirmations` and `refundBlocks` are "committed as a `pin:` record in the genesis coinbase" (`ADR-2103:73-74`); the seal established upstream commits only the chain id, pegs, genesisTime and signer's witness — parent, comment, signers and "every containment field" are not committed, and the pin is unbuilt pending an unfiled upstream proposal (`ADR-2103:207-208`, `ADR-2103:210-214`). Until then "the binding of parent and containment is the committed document plus its genesisHash" (`sidechain/README.md:77`), and the genesis test only checks the document's internal consistency (`sidechain-genesis.test.sh:91-93`).
- Failure: the fields that assert "this chain's parent is testnet4 and `cashOut` is false" are mutable repo content not pinned by the genesis hash. A document edit (or a fork of the repo) can present a different parent/containment claim for the same `genesisHash`, and no validator layer — the pin is unbuilt — can refuse it. The record's containment guarantees are, at the chain layer, assertions in a JSON file.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, plus the ADR's own amendment)

### F-07 — The Loom door serves a stale corpus generation and cannot be refreshed until an image rebuild
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Evidence: "the next generation `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` is built, promoted and verified, but the door still serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…`, and the `vault` 0.1.0 baked into the agentbox image predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" (`ADR-141:141-150`); the fixed source pin "reaches a running `vault` only at the next image rebuild" (`flake.nix:31-32`). The ADR-2140 boot reconcile (`hp-loom-reconcile.sh:119-120`) compares and would rebuild, but is blocked by the same stale in-image binary.
- Failure: every consumer in AB-24.1 — retrieval brain, condense, dream nights, sessions, email — grounds against yesterday's ontology for as long as the image lags, and staleness is "carried, never enforced" (`loom-domain/src/model.rs:459-462`), so answers are served without refusal. The operator's only remedy is a full image rebuild, not a data refresh.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, "corpus door behind the vault, 2026-10-02")

### F-08 — Voicelink event recording is installed and enabled while its ADR records implementation as none and activation as inactive
- Topics: AB-27.7, AB-28.1
- Evidence: ADR-2151 is `proposed / implementation_status: none / activation_status: inactive` (`ADR-2151:5-7`), yet "`provision-stacks` writes the voicelink Stop and Notification recorder hooks into every profile whenever `[voicelink].record_events` is true, and the manifest sets it true" (`stacks.rs:311-323`, `agentbox.toml:2233`); the manifest also sets `[voicelink] enabled = true` (`agentbox.toml:2226`).
- Failure: user activity (Stop/Notification events) is captured and recorded in production by a mechanism whose decision record says nothing is implemented or active. There is no accepted basis for the collection, and anyone auditing against the ADR status will conclude no voice event data exists when it does — a privacy/compliance incident of the "recording without a record" kind.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, in both AB-27.7 and AB-28.1)

### F-09 — A stale model-backend route on the HP black-holes every synthesis while `/health` stays green
- Topics: AB-24.7
- Evidence: the diagram note states, present tense: "DIVERGENCE: HP's old 192.168.2.48 is DEAD — a stale model-backend route black-holes every synthesis while /health still answers" (AB-24.7 note over the model-swap sequence; the Loom door and its consumers are at `agentbox.toml:2572-2585`, discovered nightly by `dream-machine-nightly.mjs:229`).
- Failure: total silent loss of all dream-engine synthesis on the HP path — health checks pass, the container is up, and every delegated completion disappears into a dead route. Nothing in the drawn contract distinguishes "backend reachable" from "backend route fresh", so detection depends on a human noticing empty nights.
- Confidence: medium — the fact is stated as a divergence but the material does not show whether the stale route is still configured or was historical; inferred as a live class of failure either way
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (labelled DIVERGENCE inside the AB-24.7 diagram)

### F-10 — Manifest, runtime and documents disagree as a standing condition: rebuild-class activation makes the manifest lie about what runs
- Topics: AB-24.1, AB-24.6, AB-24.7, AB-25.8, AB-26.1, AB-27.7, AB-28.2, AB-34.5
