- Evidence: the voice gate said false "after the console ran 5 days while the gate said false" (AB-27.7, `system-manifest.js:182`); the txbt4 README ships `enabled = false` while the manifest sets it true "live 2026-10-03" (`config/sidechain/README.md:188` vs `agentbox.toml:1937`); GOVERNANCE still describes a scaffolded `loom` seed and a raw `:8085` path (`GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:316-318`) and names Qwen3.8-27B while `loom_model` is empty (`GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:320` vs `agentbox.toml:2575`); ADR-2140 is accepted/complete/live while the governing document "still reads as if the generation were staged by hand" (`GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:313-315`). Multiple features are repeatedly described as "configured, staged until a rebuild, so not live" — Loom Jev (AB-24.6), Jev ontology proposals (AB-25.8), headroom evidence (AB-26.1), the system-one second eval (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1260-1263`, "live at the next image activation"). The flag-class trap is explicit: `role_isolation` was specified boot-class, is catalogued rebuild-class, and a boot-only flip ends in `degraded:devuser-sudo` (`system-manifest.js:97-98`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:651`).
- Failure: an operator editing `agentbox.toml` — flipping Jev, voice, a chain, role isolation — and rebooting believes the change took effect. Some did (voice ran ungated for five days); some cannot without a rebuild; some are described nowhere correctly. Incident response during an active event will reason from the manifest and the ADRs and be wrong in both directions.
- Confidence: high on the pattern; the individual instances are each attested
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for most instances (Drift/Tension/Open individually); no for the pattern itself

### F-11 — The prompt-egress register's evidence citations have rotted and its CI check cannot detect rot
- Topics: AB-24.14
- Evidence: the register is stamped `0919dc39a` (`egress-policy.json:51`) and its loom-door and zai-night rows cite manifest lines 2179 and 2163 and flake line 3438 (`egress-policy.json:231-232`, `egress-policy.json:355`); at this revision line 2179 is the `loom-raw` seed's `slug` key, line 2163 is empty, `[dream_machine].llm_provider` is now `agentbox.toml:2585`, and the façade default is `flake.nix:3957`. The renderer's `--check` "only tests that a cited line falls inside the file" (`render-egress-register.js:110-119`).
- Failure: the compliance artefact that says which prompts may leave, and under whose acceptance, cites evidence that now points at a slug key and a blank line. An audit or an incident review that follows the citations verifies nothing, and the CI gate stays green while the register detaches from the code entirely — the same blindness that let the register claim `zai-night` needs no acceptance (F-04).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-12 — "Grounded" answers rest on a corpus with ~9–11 percent scoped recall, and the wrong search call materialises the whole namespace
- Topics: AB-26.8
- Evidence: "ruvnet-kb and the knowledge-* namespaces are LOW-RECALL — scoped R@10 about 9-11 percent — until candidate-bounded hybrid lands" and "memory_hybrid_search materialises the whole namespace and measured about 72 s on ruvnet-kb — avoid it on this namespace" (AB-26.8 notes over the grounding hook `config/hooks/ruvnet-brain-ground.cjs:90` and `mcp/ruvnet-brain/server.js:220`; recall figures attributed to AB-20).
- Failure: the hook injects a directive to call `search_ruvnet` "before asserting"; with R@10 near 9 percent, an agent that obeys receives "grounded evidence" missing roughly nine of ten relevant items and will treat absence-of-hits as absence-of-fact. Separately, any caller that reaches for the hybrid search on this namespace pays a ~72 s full-materialisation stall per call.
- Confidence: high on the stated numbers; the production impact on answer quality is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, both notes)

### F-13 — A TUI save silently resets every hand-edited `[model_routing.neural]` key to fixed literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, contrast AB-28.3
- Evidence: only `enabled` round-trips through the flat TUI state (`tui_read.rs:127`); on write, "every OTHER key a FIXED literal (provider 'openrouter', quality_bar 0.50, cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0, privacy_tier 'public', trajectory true, assets_dir '/opt/agentbox/model-router' — `tui_sections.rs:193-200`)", so "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir … even if the operator hand-edited agentbox.toml" — unlike `[consultants.*].model`, which has a read-back-and-preserve path under ADR-2031 (AB-28.3).
- Failure: an operator who has tuned the quality bar or cost ceiling in TOML, then adjusts any unrelated setting through the TUI, has their routing configuration reverted without warning. The next night routes on the defaults; a cost-ceiling edit being silently returned to 0 is the damaging case.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-14 — code-server binds 0.0.0.0 on a shared bridge network; the loopback publish constrains only host→container
- Topics: AB-27.6
- Evidence: code-server runs `--bind-addr 0.0.0.0:8080 --auth password` (`flake.nix:2858`) and the note states the residual exposure: "still not 127.0.0.1 — the docker-compose loopback publish only constrains host->container, not bridge->container on visionclaw_network"; the password is minted at boot (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1013-1044`). `visionclaw_network` is the network the loom sidecar, gui-tools, isaac-sim and external comfyui share (AB-24.1, AB-27.10, AB-27.14).
- Failure: every container on the shared bridge — including any future sidecar or compromised neighbour — can reach the code-server port and attempt the single minted password for read/write access to `/home/devuser/workspace`, i.e. the whole working estate including any secrets stored in the workspace (F-05's legacy key twins among them).
- Confidence: high on the bind and the network topology; the exploit path is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the caveat is stated in passing inside a DOC-DRIFT note about the auth fix, not tracked as an open risk

### F-15 — Consensus-code pins are bumped on "operator acceptance", violating the pins file's own rule, and the acceptance is baked into the image
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: `upstream-pins` states its rule: bump a line "only after the chain has been restarted and a block produced on that commit" (`upstream-pins:5-8`); the `blaketestnode` line was bumped 2026-10-01 as an "operator-accepted active development head — a reason that is acceptance rather than a produced block" (`upstream-pins:10`), and "the bake now carries that same commit, so the acceptance is baked into the image as well" (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`). Separately, the spec pin moved to `2fd6114` (PR #32) and "ADR-2103 records it was not verifiable from the container" (`ADR-2103:658`).
- Failure: the block-producer's rule basis changes on operator say-so rather than on the produced-block evidence the estate's own mechanism demands, with no record that the new code ever produced a block under supervision before becoming the baked default. For a chain whose pitch is "the code that makes blocks is fixed into the container image", an unpinned-basis block or a divergence between the two chains' engines is an integrity event with no test positioned to catch it.
- Confidence: high on the facts; medium on the consequence (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension for the blaketestnode bump; the spec-pin gap is noted as OPEN in AB-34.1's bake subgraph)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Live runtime values in the deployed image** — which manifest gates (code-server, comfyui external, colloquy, the Jev consumers, the system-one second eval) and which environment values (notably `DISTILL_BACKEND_URL` for the loom sidecar, whose blankness makes the email gateway's `/v1` return 503) are actually active in the running container. Rebuild-class activation means the manifest is not evidence of runtime state; I would need the image build stamp and the rendered supervisor config.
2. **Whether the 2026-09-07 coordinated Loom bundle/server rollout happened** — the audit qualification states the stricter identity-verifying client is implemented but the live façade "still reports lexical generation 2026-08-22 versus semantic 2026-08-17, without loaded identity/embedding fields", so ADR-2075 verification may still be staged-only in production. Nothing later in the material confirms or denies activation.
3. **Backup, durability and key-loss recovery** — nothing here describes backups or recovery for host-bound state (`WORKSPACE/sidestr/*/blocks.dat`, model-router state, pods) or for the `agentbox-secrets` volume. Loss of the single 32-byte signer key (`/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/sidestr-dreamlab.key`) halts the chain permanently as designed; whether any offline copy exists is not shown.
4. **Host-side posture** — whether the host Docker socket mode has since been narrowed (F-02's remediation), and whether an owner flag-on rehearsal has been run on a *rebuilt* image after the receipt rule changed (the committed receipt `x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json` predates the rule and would now fail).
5. **opf-router redaction efficacy** — the privacy filter on port 9092 is named and reclassified (`scripts/opf-router.py:41`) but its redaction behaviour, test coverage and failure mode (fail-open vs fail-closed on unparseable input) are not described in this material, and every prompt-path guarantee leans on it.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody/data incident, twelve months on

Scope note: this reconstruction is derived only from the state documented at the verified stamps (agentbox `6500582d`, loom `5ad8608`, visionclaw `8692b12`, sidestr-rs `d8ace3a`). Nothing about the intervening year is known to me; every root cause below is a reconstruction from the documented posture and is marked inferred where I go beyond it. All citations are the ones the material itself gives; several findings are precisely about those citations having drifted, so they should be re-resolved at HEAD before acting.

---

### R-1 — Every secret in the container is ambient to every process, because role isolation shipped off and was never activated
- Topics: AB-36 (intro, 36.1–36.7), AB-34.6, AB-24.14
- Chain of events: a compromised or mistaken agent tool runs as devuser (uid 1000) — the user every long-running program uses. Compose feeds `.env` to PID 1 and supervisord passes its environment to every child, so the tool inherits `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, provider API keys, the ssh-agent socket path, and the sovereign/operator/JunkieJarvis key variables. It reads the chain signer and faucet treasury keys from their at-rest paths, signs as the estate identity, drives the payment-gated management routes, or — if the host Docker socket is reachable — mounts the host and takes the secrets volume from outside. The material's own business framing predicts exactly this: "One mistaken or compromised tool could therefore impersonate the organisation or move value."
- Evidence: AB-36 intro — "every secret in the container reached every process … a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent"; the flag "ships **false**" (agentbox.toml:2506); activation requires an owner's flag-on PASS rehearsal receipt, and the only committed receipt is STAGED with the flag false (x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4, x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:8). AB-36.6 Debt — 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names stay ambient in both modes, including provider keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (env-classes.json:90, env-classes.json:92, env-classes.json:195, env-classes.json:203, env-classes.json:211; ADR-2122:178-179). AB-34.6 — with the flag off, "key, parent credential and treasury key read straight from their at-rest paths by devuser" (run-producer.sh:70-71, run-faucet.sh:27). AB-36.7 Open R2 — the host Docker socket inode stays world-accessible and a flag-on boot only records `degraded:docker-socket` (docker-compose.override.yml:116-117, entrypoint-unified.sh:614). AB-36.4 Open — the consumer cutover is incomplete: "JunkieJarvis, the live-mirror hook, the gateway and dream-engine are owed as W3b" (ADR-2122:244-245).
- Warning signs visible today: the flag is off; the only rehearsal receipt is STAGED; the identity port's ACL declares only two keys while the design assumes more consumers; the material states the pre-change exposure persists until a rebuild-and-rehearsal that has not happened.
- Confidence: high that the exposure is real and unmitigated in the shipped state; the attribution of this incident to it is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client-derived corpus left the estate through the dream engine's default vendor provider, and the egress register that should have caught it cites lines that no longer exist
- Topics: AB-24.7, AB-24.9 (N1), AB-24.14
- Chain of events: the dream engine's night provider defaults to Z.AI, a vendor — loom is opt-in (agentbox.toml:2585, config.rs:184). Nightly runs send prompts derived from estate memory and corpus content to that vendor. The egress register's own row for this path carries the marker `no-accepted-record` — "nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (egress-policy.json:346-361) — so no governance surface ever accepted the egress. When the leak is investigated, the register turns out to be stamped at an older commit (`0919dc39a`, egress-policy.json:51) and its loom-door and zai-night rows cite manifest lines 2179 and 2163 and flake line 3438 (egress-policy.json:231-232, egress-policy.json:355), which at this revision are a seed's `slug` key, an empty line, and an empty line; the real keys have moved (llm_provider now agentbox.toml:2585, façade default now flake.nix:3957). The renderer's `--check` only tests that a cited line falls inside the file, so no gate ever noticed (render-egress-register.js:110-119). Client-confidential material in the corpus leaves; the client discovers it.
- Evidence: as cited above, plus AB-24.7's note that the provider default is deliberate ("the night provider defaults to zai … so loom is an opt-in") and AB-24.9 N1 ("the dream engine's default provider is Z.AI by deliberate choice"); the adjacent GOVERNANCE drift compounds audit confusion — it still names Qwen3.8-27B by a `loom_model` that has been empty since `878f23311` (agentbox.toml:2575, GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:320) and still describes a raw `:8085` model path (GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:316-318).
- Warning signs visible today: a register row for the *default* provider marked no-accepted-record; a register stamp more than three hundred manifest lines behind; a bounds-only citation checker.
- Confidence: high that the default and the marker are as stated; that this was the incident is inferred (the material does not state what the nightly prompts carry).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The Loom door served stale or wrong grounding at normal latency while every health signal stayed green
- Topics: AB-24.3 (audit qualification), AB-24.6, AB-24.7, AB-24.8, AB-24.11, AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Chain of events: the corpus is rebuilt and promoted, but the door keeps serving the previous generation — already the live condition on 2026-10-02: `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` promoted and verified while the door served `visionGraph@ae913f93…`, because the `vault` 0.1.0 baked into the image predates the portable-records file and cannot produce a promotable bundle (ADR-141:141-150); the pin is fixed in source only (flake.nix:31-32). The boot reconcile that should close this fails open — unreachable, deferred, refused and failed all exit 0 (hp-loom-reconcile.sh:13-15) — and ADR-141's design carries staleness rather than enforcing it: "a generation past stale_after is STILL SERVED, honestly labelled — staleness is carried, never enforced" (model.rs:459-462). Meanwhile the client-side identity verifier that would catch a mismatch cannot be activated, because the live façade still reports mixed lexical/semantic generations without identity fields and the material says "a coordinated bundle/server rollout is required before activating the stricter client" (AB-24 audit qualification). Consumers — condense, dream nights, session seeds — ground client-facing work on the stale corpus, and the scaffold can be applied to a subject the ontology does not cover, which the material establishes is "a WRONG answer, not a weak one" (ADR-139:13-16, the 2026-09-09 blockchain-`Node` serve). The deliverable is wrong, confident and fast. Health never blinks: `/health` returns 200 with an unloaded lexical index (AB-24.8 empty-floor trap) and returns 200 beside a dead model route — "a stale model-backend route black-holes every synthesis while /health still answers" (AB-24.7).
- Evidence: as cited; plus AB-24.6 — a blank `DISTILL_BACKEND_URL` yields 503s from a container whose `/health` is green.
- Warning signs visible today: the door already served a superseded generation on 2026-10-02; the stricter client is staged but un-activatable; one wrong-subject serve is already on record.
- Confidence: medium — every constituent is documented as live or staged; the sustained-stale-then-wrong-answer composition is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The setup server is unauthenticated and can rewrite the manifest and wield the management key if bound off loopback
- Topics: AB-30.2, AB-30.3
- Evidence: "red warning: unauthenticated, can rewrite config and use the mgmt key" (setup/server/src/main.rs:276); a non-loopback bind is an explicit `AGENTBOX_SETUP_BIND` opt-in (main.rs:268-269); the retired python/static tiers are still taught in quickstart.md:75, quickstart.md:80-84.
- Failure: the wizard is bound to the bridge network (or the warning ignored on a shared LAN); any co-tenant container POSTs `/api/config` repointing condense/loom/deepsec endpoints at an attacker host, or uses `/api/proxy/{*path}` with the loaded management key (main.rs:112, main.rs:193-211).
- Confidence: high that the surface is unauthenticated; exploitation inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Flag-off images bake devuser into `wheel` with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in; the guard is a single compose flag
- Topics: AB-36.7 (Tension), AB-36 intro
- Evidence: flake.nix:4045; config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47 (called at flake.nix:4059); inert only because no setuid sudo ships and docker-compose.yml:129 sets `no-new-privileges`; "Reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary in this topic" (AB-36.7).
- Failure: an image change reintroduces setuid sudo or drops `no-new-privileges` (e.g. to "fix" sudo for an ops task); every co-resident agent gains passwordless root. A boot of such an image records `degraded:devuser-sudo` rather than repairing it (entrypoint-unified.sh:651).
- Confidence: high (documented state); scenario inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: high

### F-03 — The host Docker socket remains world-accessible; the container can only detect and record the degradation
- Topics: AB-36.7 (Open R2), AB-36.1
- Evidence: docker-compose.override.yml:116-117 (bind of the host inode earlier boots widened); entrypoint-unified.sh:614 (`degraded:docker-socket` detection only); role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19 (mode recorded, not gated); role-accounts.json:6 (uid 965 reserved because "the Docker daemon is root on the host").
- Failure: any process that can reach the socket talks to the Docker API as host root, mounts the host filesystem, and reads the secrets volume and every at-rest key from outside the container — bypassing role isolation entirely even after activation.
- Confidence: high (exposure documented); exploitation inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: high

### F-04 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` re-opens writable consensus code with no bound on how long it stays set
- Topics: AB-34.1 (Debt), AB-34.6
- Evidence: run-producer.sh:84-93 — the override runs the workspace checkout at any commit, logging `SIDESTR-UNPINNED` that "warns, never refuses"; Debt note: "while the override is set, the exposure the bake closed is back, a dirty checkout runs, and nothing bounds how long it stays set."
- Failure: an operator sets the override for a debug session and forgets; a co-resident agent (everything is devuser with the flag off) edits the checkout, and the signer key seals blocks produced by modified consensus code.
- Confidence: high (mechanism stated); scenario inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: high

### F-05 — "Configured, staged until a rebuild": the running image lags the manifest on several decision-bearing gates
- Topics: AB-24.6, AB-24.13, AB-25.8, AB-26.1, AB-34.1
- Evidence: LOOM_JEV on the sidecar is "configured, live when the loom service is recreated and the new agentbox image is activated" (AB-24.6); the proposal verifier is "off in code, configured and staged until a rebuild" (AB-25.8, system_one.rs:533, system_one.rs:564-577); `compressSelected` is "staged until a rebuild, so not live" (AB-26.1 Open); the `vaultSrc` fix "reaches a running vault only at the next image rebuild" (AB-24.13, flake.nix:31-32); the spec pin `2fd6114` "was not verifiable from the container" (AB-34.1, upstream-pins:5-8).
- Failure: operators read `agentbox.toml` and the docs, believe a verifier or gate is active, and make custody, egress or answer-quality decisions against an image where it is off — the enabler behind R-2 and R-3.
- Confidence: high (divergence stated repeatedly); the specific decision harmed is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

### F-06 — The faucet treasury key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes
- Topics: AB-34.3 (Tension), AB-34.6, AB-36.3
- Evidence: with the flag off the faucet defaults to the workspace path (run-faucet.sh:27, agentbox.toml:1926); the migrate step "copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:232-235).
- Failure: any devuser process, or a host-side compromise of the bind, spends from the treasury key; faucet rate limits (20 grants/hour, agentbox.toml:1927-1928) bound but do not prevent it.
- Confidence: high (stated); consequence inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Bead payloads are compressed while the originals expire from the CCR store in 30 minutes
- Topics: AB-26.1, AB-26.2, AB-26.9
- Evidence: `[compression.slots]` `beads = true` (agentbox.toml:2471); the CCR store has `ttl_minutes = 30`, `max_entries = 1000` with LRU eviction (agentbox.toml:2462-2464); `headroom_retrieve` on an expired/evicted hash returns an error, not content (headroom-mcp-tools.js:34).
- Failure: a closed bead's payload survives only as a compressed form whose CCR sentinels no longer resolve; the work-DAG's record of what a bead carried — inputs to `spawn_complete` audit and outcome evidence — becomes unrecoverable.
- Confidence: inferred — the material does not state whether bead originals are persisted elsewhere before compression.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — A TUI save silently resets the model router's provider, quality bar and cost ceiling to fixed literals
- Topics: AB-28.10 (DOC-DRIFT), AB-29.2
- Evidence: tui_sections.rs:193-200 emits fixed literals (provider "openrouter", quality_bar 0.50, `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok` 0, …) and "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir to these literals even if the operator hand-edited agentbox.toml" — no read-back-and-preserve path exists, unlike consultants (ADR-2031).
- Failure: an operator raises the cost ceiling or quality bar in TOML; any later TUI write resets both, silently changing routing and unbounding spend.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — The stricter Loom identity client and the live façade must roll out together; nothing enforces the pairing
- Topics: AB-24 (audit qualification), AB-24.3, AB-24.13 (N4)
- Evidence: "the live façade was probed and still reports lexical generation 2026-08-22 versus semantic 2026-08-17, without loaded identity/embedding fields. A coordinated bundle/server rollout is required before activating the stricter client"; the client answers a failed verification with "empty labelled generation degradation, no fallback" (AB-24.3) and refuses mixed graph/semantic generations.
- Failure: the client ships ahead of the server and every ontology answer becomes a labelled refusal (silent capability loss across all consumers); or the server upgrades and an old client keeps serving answers whose response identity it never validated, replaying cache entries across a corpus swap.
- Confidence: medium — both halves are stated; which failure mode fires is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The dream engine sends an empty model name and depends on the door answering it
- Topics: AB-24.7
- Evidence: the supervisor exports `LOOM_MODEL` empty (flake.nix:2900) and `llm_config` passes it through as the request model (estate.rs:106), so "a loom night sends an empty model name and depends on the Loom answering an empty name with its loaded model"; the serde default `qwen3.8-27B` applies only when the key is absent (config.rs:171).
- Failure: the model behind `DISTILL_BACKEND_URL` is swapped (the advertised use of the seam, AB-24.7) to one that rejects empty model names; every loom night fails, or silently routes to a different model than the one operators believe is answering.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated as an invariant; swap behaviour inferred).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Staleness is carried, never enforced, and the boot reconcile fails open
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Evidence: model.rs:459-462 ("STILL SERVED, honestly labelled … never enforced"); hp-loom-reconcile.sh:13-15 (unreachable/deferred/refused/failed "never stop a container"); the 2026-10-02 debt already shows the door serving `ae913f93…` while `015ca2c1f` is promoted and the baked `vault` cannot produce a promotable bundle (ADR-141:141-150).
- Failure: a silent reconcile failure leaves the door on a months-old corpus indefinitely; every grounded consumer reasons over the old ontology and the wrong answers carry normal confidence and latency (the R-3 mechanism, already observed once).
- Confidence: high (each fact stated; the stale-door condition was live on 2026-10-02).
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — The egress register cites stale lines and its checker only tests file bounds
- Topics: AB-24.14
- Evidence: register stamped `0919dc39a` (egress-policy.json:51); rows cite manifest lines 2179/2163 and flake line 3438 (egress-policy.json:231-232, egress-policy.json:355), which no longer hold the cited keys; render-egress-register.js:110-119 `--check` "only tests that a cited line falls inside the file, so it cannot see the shift."
- Failure: an egress review verifies the wrong keys and approves a posture the manifest no longer has; this is the mechanism that let R-2's default-vendor row persist unexamined.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Voice events are recorded while the governing record says the capability is inactive
- Topics: AB-27.7 (Tension), AB-28.1 (Tension)
- Evidence: ADR-2151 records `proposed / none / inactive` (ADR-2151:5-7) while `[voicelink] enabled = true` and `record_events = true` (agentbox.toml:2226, agentbox.toml:2233) and `provision-stacks` writes the voicelink Stop/Notification hooks into every profile whenever `record_events` is true (stacks.rs:311-323); the console gate itself was flipped true on 2026-10-02 "after the console ran 5 days while the gate said false."
- Failure: voice-interaction events (approvals, notification content) are captured and spooled under no accepted record governing their retention or egress — an ungoverned privacy surface for anyone who used the console.
- Confidence: medium (divergence and capture both stated; harm inferred).
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — "No sixth hand-rolled client" is asserted as a rule, with no enforcement named
- Topics: AB-24.10, AB-28.13
- Evidence: ADR-2084 invariant — "hand-rolled chat-completions construction against a facade is PROHIBITED - a sixth caller takes the crate" (ADR-2084:37); the traps it centralises include the 400-to-empty truncation (web_summary/llm.rs:20, web_summary/llm.rs:63) and the verbatim/scaffold switch confusion (ADR-2084:30-33). No CI or runtime check is named.
- Failure: a new service or agent-written script POSTs `/v1/chat/completions` directly, floors no `max_tokens`, reads empty content as success, and records empty answers as data — the exact class the crate exists to end.
- Confidence: medium (rule stated; absence of enforcement inferred from the material's silence).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The grounding tier the hook directs agents to is low-recall, and its fallback search materialises the whole namespace
- Topics: AB-26.8 (DIVERGENCE)
- Evidence: "ruvnet-kb and the knowledge-* namespaces are LOW-RECALL — scoped R@10 about 9-11 percent — until candidate-bounded hybrid lands"; "memory_hybrid_search materialises the whole namespace and measured about 72 s on ruvnet-kb — avoid it on this namespace"; the hook's injected directive tells the agent to call `search_ruvnet` "before asserting."
- Failure: an agent asserts an estate fact grounded on a 9–11 percent recall tier and misses the contradicting evidence, or triggers hybrid search and stalls the turn past a minute; either yields a confidently wrong or timed-out answer.
- Confidence: medium (numbers stated; the wrong-assertion path is inferred).
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: low

---

Material also records, unflagged or lightly flagged, several smaller drifts I did not promote to findings because their failure paths are weaker or purely cosmetic: the https-bridge's `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` (a combination browsers reject — AB-30.4); the dormant hub roster still naming the deleted `precedent-bridge` (AB-28.8 N6); the Isaac Sim catalogue advertising `live` while its ADR is `proposed` (AB-27.14); the committed rehearsal receipt predating the baked-table rule it would now fail (AB-36.7); and the `blaketestnode` pin bumped on "operator-accepted" grounds rather than the produced block its own rule requires (AB-34.1). I am confident each is present as described; I am not confident any of them independently causes an incident.

## dreamlab-ai-website / critical

### F-01 — One key is both forum admin and governance publisher, and rotating it spans five sites, one of which CI cannot verify and one of which ships only on the next SPA deploy
- Topics: DW-03.3, DW-03.4, DW-03.5 (M1)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:39-72` — `visionclaw-server` (11ed6422...663c) is BOTH primary admin AND `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher; the DW-03.4 note states the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI"; DW-03.5 M1 lists that secret as "verifiable: false"; the split runbook is a four-location atomic change (`[admin].static_pubkeys`, relay `[vars]`, search `[vars]`, auth CF secret) that `deploy.yml:62-73` extends to five — `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is the operator's interim working key and the ADR-040 D3 runbook "must re-point it", but the client mirror changes only when the SPA is next rebuilt and republished.
- Failure: compromise of that single key yields full admin plus governance publishing. Conversely, a half-executed rotation (CF secret updated, SPA not yet redeployed, or a wrong value pushed via `set-worker-secrets.yml` — nothing verifies it against the TOML) leaves a removed admin silently retaining powers, or contact/signup DMs addressed to a key nobody holds.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("Open, staged" in DW-03.4; INTERIM at `deploy.yml:62-73`); the `verifiable: false` revocation gap is stated but not flagged as a defect.

### F-02 — Production encryption config cites ADR-2016, which was never written, while the governing doc still says only zone3 is encrypted
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-03.6 (M7), DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.9 (D15)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:123,137,148` set zone2/3/4 `encrypted=true` and `:149` gives zone4 `agent_keys=true`; the master gate `[encryption].enabled` (`dreamlab.toml:33,37`) is itself a three-site hand-synced mirror (M7). `IDENTITY-zones.md:180-181` still says only zone3 is encrypted. The toml cites 'ADR-2016' (`dreamlab.toml:34,149`) and DW-01.6 says the payload grew "since ADR-2015/ADR-2016 shipped" — but I7 states "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written". So one part of this very pack asserts ADR-2016 shipped while another shows it absent.
- Failure: an operator or auditor working from the governing identity doc mis-models the E2EE posture (which zones encrypt, that zone4 agents hold zone keys, that the master gate is a mirror, not a kill switch — "false suppresses every zone's flag; keys already held still decrypt"). Any change to the gate is unreviewable against intent because the decision record does not exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (BREACHED invariant, DOC-DRIFT, D15); the internal "shipped vs never written" contradiction is not itself marked.

### F-03 — Anonymous ingress has no server-side per-recipient defence: 10 events/s/IP is the only spam mitigation and the LLM-cost throttle is client-side
- Topics: DW-04.5 (T1, T3), DW-04.3
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:276,278` — kind-1059 admission is anonymous-ephemeral, recipient-gated only; `:282` — "10 events/s/IP relay limit only — no PoW/CAPTCHA/per-recipient throttle; kind 1059 bypasses content moderation"; `:284` — junkiejarvis cost abuse is mitigated by "serialised sends (one in-flight/session), client-side throttle" plus an ops kill-switch. Client-side throttles execute in the attacker's browser.
- Failure: a scripted client rotating sessions/IPs drives unmoderated kind-1059 volume into relay storage and per-wrap LLM spend until an operator manually flips `JUNKIEJARVIS_ENABLED=0`; the stored moderation backlog persists after the switch.
- Confidence: high (limits as stated; the exploitation scenario is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (threat-table T1/T3); that the LLM throttle is enforceable only client-side is unmarked.

### F-04 — Client/worker kit skew already wiped the forum once; prevention rests on two independent workflows both running, with no atomicity or rollback
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-01.6, DW-05.8
- Evidence: `BASELINE-architecture.md:121-124` — client/worker skew "wiped the forum on 2026-06-15". The mitigation is trigger design: `workers-deploy.yml` fires on `forum-config/Cargo.lock` and `KIT_REF` changes — but the client is built and published by `deploy.yml` (gh-pages, `deploy.yml:405-412`) and workers by a separate `workers-deploy.yml`; the pin moved again on 2026-10-30 (9c36b60) and the compatibility record itself warns "this pin update is not a deployment receipt" (`kit-compatibility-record.md:38-44`). No lockstep or rollback mechanism is described.
- Failure: a repin where the client deploy succeeds but the workers deploy fails or is delayed re-creates the 2026-06-15 class: the live client speaks the new kit's protocol against old workers and the forum appears wiped or corrupt; gh-pages publication has no described revert path.
- Confidence: high (incident and split workflows stated; the residual failure window is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (incident cited as the motivation); the non-atomicity of the mitigation is unmarked.

### F-05 — Anonymous-DM PII cannot be self-deleted, and the only backup path has no restore script
- Topics: DW-04.5 (T2), DW-07.5
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283` — erasure is operator-side D1 purge only; "self-service NIP-09 deletion is cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)" for contact/signup/chat messages. DW-07.5 — the nightly export is "the only backup/restore path for the forum found in this repo — there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export"; kind-40/42/0 events in the NAS copy are signed plaintext (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`); vectors are "nice-to-have", KV "rebuildable" (`forum-backup.sh:30-35`).
- Failure: an erasure request must be chased through D1 plus 14 nights of NAS exports (`KEEP_NIGHTS=14`) with no self-service path; a D1 corruption event finds exports whose restore procedure has never been exercised — recovery is improvised mid-incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — T2 sits in the threat table; the missing restore script is stated as fact, not marked as a defect.

### F-06 — One external box (agentbox) concentrates the only backup execution, the only monitoring, and the private keys that decrypt every website enquiry
- Topics: DW-07.3, DW-07.5, DW-07.8, DW-01.8, DW-04.9
- Evidence: the backup cron (`scripts/backup/crontab:6`, supercronic `[program:forum-backup-cron]`) and the nightly inbox-watch (`scripts/seo-watch/crontab:17`) both run on the agentbox; seed scripts read `JUNKIEJARVIS_PRIVKEY_HEX`/`AGENTBOX_PRIVKEY_HEX` from `agentbox/.env` (`provision-junkiejarvis-relay.mjs:4`, `probe-admin42.mjs:4`); every contact, signup and chat message is copied to the monitor key (`deploy.yml:75-80`) and the inbox-watch reads those wraps — the relay serves a wrap only to its addressee — so that box holds the monitor key's private half.
- Failure: compromise of one machine yields the key that decrypts all inbound enquiries/chats plus agent admin keys; failure of the same box silently stops both backups and the only health/alarm channel, so the next data-loss event is discovered late against stale backups.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the crontabs and key reads are stated; the aggregation is reasoning beyond the material)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Single relay, branded domains absent from DNS, raw workers.dev hosts baked into builds: one hostname event takes out forum, contact, signup and chat at once
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-04.5, DW-04.3
- Evidence: "single relay today; the mesh transport itself is designed, not shipped" (DW-04.5); branded worker domains "are not provisioned in DNS" and clients talk to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:49-61`), verified failing 2026-06-09 and "not re-checked as of 2026-08-31"; shipping the branded domains "previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`); Talk-to-AI replies depend on third-party public relays (`VITE_REPLY_RELAYS: relay.damus.io, relay.primal.net`).
- Failure: a relay-worker outage, or Cloudflare disabling the `workers.dev` hostname, removes every community function simultaneously with no second relay and no DNS failover; provisioning the documented "end-state" domains without rebuilding every client env repeats the severed-API incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, D4); the single-relay SPOF framing is unmarked.

### F-08 — The gate workflow has no push trigger, so direct pushes to main can ship with zero tests — contradicting rust-ci.yml's own header
- Topics: DW-05.1, DW-05.2, DW-05.3
- Evidence: `rust-ci.yml:4-6` claims `test-and-lint.yml` covers `forum-config/` "on every push/PR", but DW-05.2 (Drift) shows it has no `push` trigger (`test-and-lint.yml:20-30`) and runs for main only as a `workflow_call` inside deploy workflows "whose own path filters decide whether it runs at all"; the kit's own CI is `workflow_dispatch`-only with advisory clippy (`rust-ci.yml:9, 39-62`); the only CORS-assertion spec (`forum-smoke.spec.ts:404-410`) is deliberately wired into no gate; `npm audit high` is `continue-on-error`.
- Failure: a direct push to main outside the deploy path filters ships untested; kit regressions surface in production because nothing runs the kit suite automatically; a worker CORS regression goes live unflagged until a browser client breaks.
- Confidence: high (facts stated; the ungated-push scenario follows from the stated trigger logic)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the trigger mismatch is marked Drift; the composite "main pushes can ship ungated" consequence is not.

### F-09 — D1-promoted admins are invisible to the search worker, and D1 demotions never reach it
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-03.10
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:269-272` — admin status is static ∪ D1 (`whitelist.is_admin` then `members.is_admin`), but "the search-worker honours only its own `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]` value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it"; its copy is a separate static list (`search-worker.wrangler.toml:30,46`), not read from `ADMIN_KV`/`ADMIN_KV_RO` (DW-03.10).
- Failure: a moderator promoted via `/api/whitelist/set-admin` finds admin actions silently fail on search endpoints; conversely a demotion performed in D1 never reaches search, so a demoted admin retains search-side powers until someone edits the static list and redeploys.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Known gap", Gap 2)

### F-10 — Auth worker calls a third-party LLM endpoint by default with a secret whose presence is never checked at deploy time
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-04.5 (T4), DW-05.1, DW-07.4
- Evidence: since the 2026-10-30 pin, relay/search/auth workers call TypeSafe jev-latest with a `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` secret (`search-worker.wrangler.toml:33-37`, `relay-worker.wrangler.toml:16-20`, `auth-worker.wrangler.toml:61-66`); the auth worker's rationale check "is cloud by default and private only when `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY=1`" (`SYSTEM-ONE.md:13-19`) — previously refused unless local (T4); the deploy-time credential check runs for search and relay only (`workers-deploy.yml:287-299`), so "a missing key there degrades silently" (DW-05.1 Debt); `set-worker-secrets.yml:3-10` header still describes only the four auth-worker secrets (Drift). Which data fields are transmitted is stated nowhere.
- Failure: the auth worker ships calling an external endpoint with no receipt the secret exists — its checks silently return unavailable; whatever content it forwards leaves the boundary under a change with no ADR in this repo and a workflow header that misdescribes it.
- Confidence: high (facts); the data-scope concern is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Drift, Open cluster)

### F-11 — First-user-is-admin bootstrap reopens after reset-db, and the loss is reported only by a nightly job
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-04.9, DW-07.8
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259` — "first-user-is-admin bootstrap: GET /api/setup-status reports needsSetup when no is_admin=1 row exists"; `POST /api/admin/reset-db` loses the registry row and the whitelist row and "only the nightly watch reports it" (`ADR-2011:58`, `provision-website-info.mjs:19-20`); the reporting channel is the same single nightly inbox-watch on the agentbox (`scripts/inbox-watch/run.mjs:288-295`).
- Failure: after a reset-db or a restore that lands an empty `members` table, the forum sits in `needsSetup` and the first visitor — or an attacker watching for it — completes setup and becomes admin; the degraded monitor rows surface up to ~24 h later, if the watch box is up.
- Confidence: medium (bootstrap stated; the first-caller race is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (bootstrap is described neutrally; the reset-db row loss is marked Debt in ADR-2011)

### F-12 — Talk-to-AI has no request/reply correlation, an unverified identity hint, and a reply path that dies silently if relay lists drift
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation" — a late answer to a timed-out question "can read as the answer to whatever was asked most recently" (`AIChatFab.tsx` closeout, `lateForQuestion` `AIChatFab.tsx:9-19`); `identityHint` is "EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED — no proof of possession" (`AIChatFab.tsx:431-432`); replies are readable only from open third-party relays and the subset invariant is held by convention (`IDENTITY-zones.md:182-183`).
- Failure: a user receives a late reply misattributed to their latest question and acts on wrong advice; if `VITE_REPLY_RELAYS` ever exceeds the agent's publish fan-out, all replies vanish silently; a forged hint attributes messages to a visitor's key without their involvement.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (closeout comment, estate closeout note, Invariant 8)

### F-13 — Zone identity is frozen into immutable event tags and legacy cohort slugs: both known failure modes have already fired once, guarded only by comments
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-07.3, DW-06.6 (I6)
- Evidence: `required_cohorts` must stay dual-accept "until every legacy slug grant is migrated, or locked-zone members lose access — dropping either arm collapsed legacy members to welcome-only in a 2026-07-20 regression" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:178-179`, `dreamlab.toml:116-119`); the friends→minimoonoir rename "orphaned friends-* channels (kind-40 section tags are immutable), requiring a delete-and-recreate" (`reseed-minimoonoir.mjs:1-6`).
- Failure: a future zone rename or cohort cleanup repeats a precedent: members silently dumped into the public zone (access regression), or channel history orphaned and destroyed in a delete-and-recreate; neither is mechanically checked, only remembered in comments.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant 6, reseed note); that the guard is comment-only is unmarked

### F-14 — Three documents disagree about which kit commit is deployed, and the parity gate reads only the SHA, so the record can lie while green
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-05.8, DW-06.8, DW-06.9 (D9, D12)
- Evidence: live pins are `9c36b603…` in all three pin sites and `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA` (`deploy.yml:130`, `workers-deploy.yml:44`, `rust-ci.yml:20`, `kit-compatibility-record.md:30`); `BASELINE-architecture.md:117` still says `ea7d95e7…`; `README.md:65` says `8a5cf81…`; the History table marks `931898a` (beta.10) "Current (canonical…)" six releases behind (`kit-compatibility-record.md:355`); "pin-parity compares only the SHA", so "the surrounding comments can rot without the gate noticing" (DW-01.5 INVARIANT) and the branch column "could drift again unseen" (DW-05.8).
- Failure: during an incident the operator consults the compatibility record or BASELINE to determine what is live, gets the wrong commit/version/branch, and diffs or rolls back the wrong source while every gate passes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D9, D12, and the record's own notes)

### F-15 — The deploy pipeline's correctness hangs on an unstated build order, a 404-redirect shim, and an onboarding doc that miscounts the frontends
- Topics: DW-01.6, DW-01.7, DW-01.2, DW-02.5
- Evidence: the BBS client must "build after the forum merge so `cp kit/dist/*` does not grab the bbs subtree" (DW-01.6) — an ordering constraint enforced only by step placement; SPA deep links depend on the 404.html shim ("load-bearing, not incidental", `BASELINE-architecture.md:156-159`), whose React pickup must remain the external `public/spa-redirect.js` because `index.html`'s `script-src 'self'` CSP blocks inline injection (`deploy.yml:399-402`); `CLAUDE.md:14` still says "static dual-SPA" while three frontends ship (DW-01.2 Drift).
- Failure: a workflow edit that reorders the BBS build or the merge clobbers `/community/bbs` from production; dropping or renaming `spa-redirect.js` breaks every shared deep link with nothing watching it; an operator onboarding from CLAUDE.md miscounts the frontends and never learns the merge-order or shim constraints.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the shim and the CLAUDE.md drift are marked; the build-order dependency appears only as a step annotation

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Where relay events physically live and whether the nightly export covers them** — DW-03.10 calls the `dreamlab-relay` D1 "canonical" while DW-03.11 says `NostrRelayDO` "manages its own SQLite schema at runtime"; whether DO-held state is inside the D1 export or is silently unbacked-up needs the DO storage semantics and `forum-backup.sh` output.
2. **Branch protection on main** — whether direct pushes (F-08's bypass) are possible at all, and whether `ci-pass`/gate outputs are required checks, is not stated.
3. **What the TypeSafe/Jev calls transmit** — event content, metadata, or which zones' data; without the call sites, F-10's real severity (privacy vs. mere degradation) cannot be set.
4. **Whether the historical plaintext admin key (`c0bcece`, DW-07.2) is still authorised** — the authors say its liveness "cannot be decided from this repo"; an engineer must check that pubkey against every `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` mirror and the CF secret.
5. **Sanitisation of the `sed`-injected `window.__ENV__` values and the deploy path filters for content** — whether quote/`</script>` handling exists in the inject step, whether the forum/BBS HTML carries a CSP like the React shell, and whether `public/**`-only commits (workshop markdown, OG images) actually trigger a deploy.

## dreamlab-ai-website / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident, dreamlab-ai.com estate

**Summary.** A client was lost after a failure in the data/custody/security class. The exact trigger has not been conclusively isolated; the three causal chains below are ranked by fit with what this repository's own documentation records, and each is checkable against the cited files and tests. Latent defects found during the investigation follow. Where I reason beyond what the material states, the finding says so.

---

### R-1 — A kit re-pin ships a new client against old Workers, repeating the 2026-06-15 forum wipe
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-01.6, DW-05.1, DW-05.3, DW-05.8
- Chain of events:
  1. KIT_REF is repinned in the three workflow pin sites plus `forum-config/Cargo.toml`; pin-parity passes because all sites and `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA` agree (only the SHA is compared).
  2. Both `deploy.yml` (client) and `workers-deploy.yml` (Workers) fire, but they are independent workflows. Nothing in the material makes client publication depend on the Workers deploy's outcome — the client's only gate is `needs.gate.outputs.passed == 'true'` from `test-and-lint`, which contains no Workers-deploy step.
  3. `workers-deploy.yml` contains steps that hard-fail the job — "Validate required auth-worker secrets are set" and "Validate public System One credential", which fails the deploy when `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is not named in `wrangler secret list` for search/relay. A missing/expired credential, or any other worker-side validation failure, leaves the Workers at the old pin while the client built from the new kit publishes.
  4. New client, old Workers is precisely the skew that "wiped the forum on 2026-06-15"; the trigger-on-`Cargo.lock`/`KIT_REF` design is the *only* stated defence, and it presumes the Workers job succeeds.
- Evidence: skew precedent `BASELINE-architecture.md:121-124` (via DW-01.5); pin sites `deploy.yml:130`, `workers-deploy.yml:44`, `rust-ci.yml:20`, canonical `kit-compatibility-record.md:30-31`; SHA-only comparison (DW-01.5, DW-05.8); client gate composition `deploy.yml:134-149` (DW-05.3); hard-failing worker validations `workers-deploy.yml:287-297, 301` (DW-05.1); record's own warning that "this pin update is not a deployment receipt" `kit-compatibility-record.md:38-44`; pin has moved twice in three months (8a5cf81 → 9c36b60, 2026-10-30) at unchanged crate version `1.0.0-beta.16`.
- Warning signs visible today: History table still marks `931898a` "Current (canonical…)" six releases behind (`kit-compatibility-record.md:355`); README names `8a5cf81` one repin stale (`README.md:65`); prose around the pins is documented to rot on every repin without failing the gate (`deploy.yml:129` via DW-01.5).
- Confidence: high (mechanism and precedent are documented; the recurrence is inferred).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Default-on third-party egress to TypeSafe shipped by the 2026-10-30 repin, with no deploy receipt for the auth Worker
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-04.5, DW-05.1, DW-05.8, DW-07.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The 2026-10-30 repin to `9c36b60` made each kit Jev consumer's local-only switch env-driven and **off by default** (`kit-compatibility-record.md:38-44`).
  2. The overlay opted in all three server-side consumers: `JEV_TRIAGE` on the relay with a five-minute cron sweep, `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK` on search, `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK` on the auth Worker, all routed to TypeSafe `jev-latest`.
  3. The auth Worker's rationale check therefore runs in the cloud by default, private only when `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY=1`; the material states the third-party egress is "not in the threat table above" (T4, OPEN).
  4. Deploy-time credential validation covers search and relay only; the auth Worker's `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` "has no receipt", and a missing key there "degrades silently to an unavailable result".
  5. Client-side Jev knobs default off (`DREAMLAB_JEV` default off), so the operator's own client tooling signals nothing while server-side egress is on — a client's content/queries processed by the three Workers flow to a third party with no documented payload inventory.
- Evidence: `relay-worker.wrangler.toml:16-20, 99-100`; `search-worker.wrangler.toml:33-37`; `auth-worker.wrangler.toml:61-66`; `SYSTEM-ONE.md:3-5, 13-19`; DW-03.8 OPTIN node (run 41028875471, credential check search+relay only); DW-05.1 Debt (silent degradation); DW-04.5 T4 (egress absent from threat register); `scripts/lib/enquiry-topic.mjs:27-32, 86` (client-side local-only default off).
- Warning signs visible today: the asymmetry between server-side opt-in and client-side default-off; the deploy receipt gap is already recorded for the auth Worker; the record explicitly disclaims deployment receipt for the pin that carried the change.
- Confidence: high that the configuration and defaults are as described; the attribution of the client loss to this egress is inferred. The material does not state what fields are sent to TypeSafe — enumerating that payload is the first check to run.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Compromise or misuse of the unsplit visionclaw key: one key is primary admin, governance publisher, and can invoke reset-db against an unrestorable estate
- Topics: DW-03.3, DW-03.4, DW-03.5, DW-04.6, DW-04.9, DW-07.5
- Chain of events:
  1. `visionclaw-server` (11ed6422…663c) is simultaneously the forum's primary admin pubkey and the `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher — the key split is "Open, staged", not applied.
  2. The auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI"; the split is a four-location (five with the interim client `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY`) manual atomic change.
  3. Admin resolution is static ∪ D1, so D1-side demotion never removes static powers, and the search Worker honours only its own static copy.
  4. An admin can invoke `POST /api/admin/reset-db`, which destroys the website-info registry and whitelist rows and is reported only by the next nightly watch.
  5. Recovery from destructive admin action has no tested path: the nightly backup is a raw D1/R2/KV export with "no corresponding restore script", and the relay Durable Object's self-managed SQLite is not in the backup script's list at all (see F-01).
- Evidence: dual-role key `dreamlab.toml:275` (DW-01.8, DW-03.3); staged split `dreamlab.toml:39-72`, runbook reference `dreamlab.toml:54-55`, interim client key `deploy.yml:62-73` (DW-03.4); static ∪ D1 and search gap `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259-272`; reset-db row loss `ADR-2011-website-information-identity.md:58`, `provision-website-info.mjs:19-20`; backup/restore gap `forum-backup.sh:26-35` and DW-07.5; DO storage outside any SQL file DW-03.11.
- Warning signs visible today: a single key holding two trust domains is documented as deliberately held; the mirror checker covers seven groups but `[nip05]` is "still elided" (`config-mirrors.mjs:360-361`); the D1-demotion gap is recorded as "Known gap".
- Confidence: medium — the structural facts are documented; the compromise/misuse chain is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Relay Durable Object storage is absent from the nightly backup, and no restore path exists
- Topics: DW-03.10, DW-03.11, DW-07.5
- Evidence: `NostrRelayDO` "manages its own SQLite schema at runtime" with no SQL file (DW-03.11); the backup exports D1 `dreamlab-relay`/`dreamlab-auth`, R2 pods/vectors, and KV only — DO storage is not listed (`forum-backup.sh:26-35`); "there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export" (DW-07.5).
- Failure: any DO data loss (precedent: the 2026-06-15 "wiped the forum") is unrecoverable from backups; the export that exists has never been rehearsed for restore.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from omission; the material never shows a DO export.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — PII erasure is cryptographically impossible client-side and retention outlives any purge
- Topics: DW-02.7, DW-04.5, DW-07.5
- Evidence: "self-service NIP-09 deletion is cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway); erasure is operator-side D1 purge only" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`); the contact form sends PII via gift-wrap (`Contact.tsx:495-509`); the NAS backup keeps 14 nights and "the NAS copy is readable content" for kind-40/42/0 (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`, KEEP_NIGHTS=14, DW-07.5).
- Failure: an erasure request fulfilled on live D1 leaves content in the 14-night NAS chain and in every gift-wrap held by admin/monitor keys; a client or regulator audit finds the data still extant.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (threat register T2); the backup-retention interaction is not marked
- Severity: high

### F-03 — The `[encryption].enabled` master switch silently disables zone E2EE with no visible signal
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6
- Evidence: `false` "suppresses every zone's flag (keys already held still decrypt)" (`dreamlab.toml:33,37`); zones 2–4 carry `encrypted = true` (`dreamlab.toml:123,137,148`); the governing doc still says only zone3 is encrypted (`IDENTITY-zones.md:180-181`); the design cites "ADR-2016", which was never written (`dreamlab.toml:34,149`; DW-06.6 I7).
- Failure: one boolean flip ships zones that accept unencrypted content; because previously-held keys still decrypt old material, nothing surfaces the regression until new plaintext is exposed.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (I7 breach, D15)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Admin demotion is ineffective: static ∪ D1 resolution, and search honours only its static copy
- Topics: DW-03.10, DW-04.6
- Evidence: "admin status = static ∪ D1" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259-271`); "the search-worker honours only its own `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]` value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`; `search-worker.wrangler.toml:46`).
- Failure: a demoted or compromised admin demoted in D1 retains full static powers on every Worker; a newly promoted moderator cannot act on search; moderation truth diverges per surface.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("Known gap")
- Severity: high

### F-05 — `required_cohorts` dual-accept remains one edit away from re-locking legacy members
- Topics: DW-03.1
- Evidence: INVARIANT — dual-accept (zone id + legacy slug) must persist "until every legacy slug grant is migrated, or locked-zone members lose access"; dropping either arm "collapsed legacy members to welcome-only in a 2026-07-20 regression" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:178-179`; `dreamlab.toml:116-119`).
- Failure: any cohort-list cleanup repeats the silent lockout; locked-zone content becomes unreachable for affected members, reading to the client as data loss.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT with precedent)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Migrations are frozen at a single `001_init.sql` against a kit that re-pins repeatedly
- Topics: DW-03.11, DW-01.5
- Evidence: the repo's only DDL is `migrations/001_init.sql` (476 lines, 24 `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, `migrations_dir` at `auth-worker.wrangler.toml:18`); the kit pin has moved repeatedly at the same crate version with no migration versioning shown anywhere in the material.
- Failure: a kit at a new pin expects schema the frozen migration cannot produce (`IF NOT EXISTS` cannot alter existing tables — inferred from the stated DDL shape); deploy "succeeds", runtime writes fail or silently drop data.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from omission of any migration-versioning mechanism.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-07 — The compatibility record has rotted: "Current" is six releases stale and only the SHA is machine-checked
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-05.8
- Evidence: History table marks `931898a` (beta.10) "Current (canonical — matches CANONICAL_KIT_SHA…)" while the canonical is `9c36b60` (`kit-compatibility-record.md:355` vs `:30`); README names `8a5cf81` one repin behind (`README.md:65`); "pin-parity compares only the SHA, so this does not fail the gate"; the branch column drifted once before unseen (DW-05.8).
- Failure: during an incident, an operator trusts the record to establish what is actually deployed and rolls to or reasons from the wrong pin.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — E2E is ungated, kit CI is advisory and manual, and the pre-deploy gate is path-filtered
- Topics: DW-05.1, DW-05.2, DW-05.3
- Evidence: Playwright specs "deliberately NOT wired into `ci.yml`, `test-and-lint.yml` or any gate job" (DW-05.1); `rust-ci.yml` clippy is advisory and workflow_dispatch-only (`rust-ci.yml:39-62`); `test-and-lint.yml` has no push trigger and runs only where the callers' path filters admit it (DW-05.2 Drift).
- Failure: a behavioural regression (e.g. the CORS assertion at `forum-smoke.spec.ts:404-410`, the only spec of its kind) ships because nothing executes it; the gate's own header records that it previously ran no unit tests and reported unconditional success until 2026-09-05.
- Confidence: high for the wiring facts; the shipping regression is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, INVARIANT)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — `provision-kv` resolves-or-creates the shared admin namespace; a wrong create rebinds both Workers to an empty KV
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-03.9
- Evidence: the pre-job "resolves or creates a SINGLE shared `dreamlab-admin-kv`" so auth `ADMIN_KV` (RW) and pod `ADMIN_KV_RO` bind the same namespace; "a per-worker title would split it and silently drop bans/mutes" (`workers-deploy.yml:60`; DW-03.8/03.9).
- Failure: a resolution miss (rename, list/permission failure) makes the job create a fresh namespace; both Workers bind it; every existing ban and mute silently vanishes while new writes succeed — the moderation record looks healthy.
- Confidence: medium — the create-path behaviour is inferred; the silent-drop consequence of any namespace split is author-stated.
- Marked by authors: yes (split danger); the create-path risk is not
- Severity: high

### F-10 — The admin key split is a five-location manual atomic change with a live interim client key
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.5
- Evidence: split "Open, staged"; the auth-worker CF secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI"; runbook requires four locations; `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is INTERIM and "the runbook must re-point it", extending the split to five (`dreamlab.toml:39-72, 54-55`; `deploy.yml:62-73`); `[nip05]` is not compared at all (`config-mirrors.mjs:360-361`).
- Failure: partial execution leaves the old dual-role key admin-visible on some surface, or strips the new admin everywhere, silently — the checker covers seven groups but not every governed key.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-11 — Talk-to-AI replies depend on an unchecked fan-out invariant and carry no request/reply correlation
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: "the Talk-to-AI reply-relay set must remain a subset of the agent's own publish fan-out, or replies are never seen" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:182-183`); "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation" — a late answer can read as the answer to whatever was asked most recently (`AIChatFab.tsx:9-19`; `IDENTITY-zones.md:208`).
- Failure: a change to jarvis's publish fan-out silently stops all replies; or a timed-out question's late answer is attributed to the next question and the visitor acts on wrong advice.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (closeout note)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Raw `*.workers.dev` hosts are baked into the build env; branded domains are undeployed and the DNS re-cut is a standing severance risk
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-06.6
- Evidence: branded Worker domains "are **not provisioned in DNS** — the client instead talks to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env" (`deploy.yml:49-61`); "shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`); GH Pages origin-of-record invariant "until DNS is re-cut" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:173-175`).
- Failure: the long-planned DNS re-cut, or flipping `CLOUDFLARE_PAGES_ENABLED`, without updating every baked env value repeats the total API severance — contact, signup and chat all fail while the static site looks healthy.
- Confidence: high (prior occurrence documented)
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant, Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-13 — The `agent` cohort holds the zone4 E2EE key (`agent_keys=true`) across eleven agents
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-03.3
- Evidence: zone4 sets `agent_keys = true` (`dreamlab.toml:149`); eleven agents are rostered, several of them bots (`dreamlab.toml:386-412`); `website-info` was deliberately given `cohorts = []` "because the `agent` cohort would receive the zone4 key" (`ADR-2011…md:35-37`).
- Failure: any one of eleven agent-key compromises decrypts zone4 history; no zone-key rotation path is shown anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: medium — exposure is documented; compromise and the absent rotation path are inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as design, not flagged)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Talk-to-AI traffic is visible as event metadata on public third-party relays
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: replies are "readable only from open relays" `relay.damus.io`, `relay.primal.net` (`VITE_REPLY_RELAYS`, DW-04.3), and the invariant requires the agent to publish to those same public relays.
- Failure: gift-wraps are encrypted, but timing, size and recipient linkage of website visitors' questions are observable on public infrastructure — a deanonymisation/metadata disclosure for privacy-sensitive users.
- Confidence: medium — the metadata inference goes beyond what the material states.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: low

### F-15 — `reset-db` destroys the website-info registry row (and undeclared whitelist rows); detection waits for the nightly watch
- Topics: DW-04.9, DW-07.8
- Evidence: "the registry row and the whitelist row are both lost on `POST /api/admin/reset-db`, and only the nightly watch reports it" (`ADR-2011…md:58`; `provision-website-info.mjs:19-20`); `[[ingress]]`-declared recipients survive reset (`whitelist-admin-recipient.mjs:19-21`), but the registry row — whose `registered_by` already mismatches the roster (`ADR-2011…md:56-57`) — does not.
- Failure: a routine operator reset leaves the monitor identity unregistered until the next 07:15 UTC run; in the window, health reporting and the enquiry digest silently degrade.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Open)
- Severity: medium

## estate / critical

### F-01 — Anonymous public API reads exposed to the internet via the standalone Cloudflare tunnel
- Topics: ES-10.3
- Evidence: The shipped compose defaults invert every fail-closed code default: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS: ${RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1}` (docker-compose.unified.yml:121) and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS: ${RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1}` (:122) produce the "demo-open" profile with anonymous `/api` reads ON. The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted — with this demo-open posture, anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel, not just the LAN" and "the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" because the route is a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname with no local config.yml (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6). ADR-2119 leaves this standalone file unaffected (ADR-2119:64-66).
- Failure: Anyone on the internet hits the tunnel URL, gets anonymous read access to the knowledge graph API, and the operator cannot audit what routes the tunnel exposes because the mapping lives in a Cloudflare dashboard, not in the repository.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the LANGAP note in ES-10.3 marks the ADR-2119 gap as OPEN, and the CFT box describes the exposure, but nowhere is the combination of "default compose = anonymous reads + standalone tunnel = public internet + no local route audit" called out as a single named finding.

---

### F-02 — Any process inside the agentbox container can forge any agent's did:nostr identity over loopback
- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-04.1
- Evidence: The proxy-stamped `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` is accepted without a signature when the TCP peer is loopback (`agent-event-auth.js:184-186`, gated by `peerIsProxy` at `:98-106`). Both ES-02.1 and ES-04.1 note in their "Debt" paragraphs: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1), so attribution on the session and break-glass hop is bounded by the same-UID boundary." Under `role_isolation = false` (the default), "every program but bootstrap and tailscale runs as devuser, and PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of them" (entrypoint-unified.sh).
- Failure: A compromised Claude Code session, MCP server, or any other process in the container emits agent-events, task spawns or break-glass actions attributed to the operator's `did:nostr` key. VisionClaw ingests and renders these as genuinely attributed actions, and no downstream consumer can distinguish them from the real thing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("Debt" in ES-02.1 and ES-04.1), but the marker understates it — the text calls it a boundary while describing no boundary at all.

---

### F-03 — Deleting agent memory from the Solid pod leaves the embedding permanently searchable in RuVector
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9
- Evidence: `deleteAgentMemory` in `agentMemory.ts:196-204` has "no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent." The embedding row in RuVector persists and stays semantically searchable. ES-07.9 calls this "the largest erasure hole" and adds "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO."
- Failure: A user requests erasure of their agent memory. The pod resource is deleted, but the vector embedding of that memory remains in the HNSW index and returns as a search result to any future query, indefinitely. There is no mechanism to find or remove it after the fact, and no backup to restore from either.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-08.9, D5 in ES-07.9).

---

### F-04 — Forum production runs a kit with an unpatched RUSTSEC advisory; main is fixed, the edge is not
- Topics: ES-05.14
- Evidence: "forum main (1b27f79) is fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387, with k256 0.14.0, p256 0.14.0, solid-pod-rs 0.5.0-alpha.18 and nostr-bbs-core 1.0.0-beta.19, but forum production still runs kit 9c36b60, with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4, so main is fixed and the edge is not." The kit pin moved three times on 2 October but production still runs an older kit.
- Failure: The production relay worker executes a known-vulnerable cryptographic dependency. Depending on the advisory's nature, signature verification, key derivation or event validation on the live governance edge may be exploitable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the fact is stated in the prose but not given a Tension/Drift/Debt/Open marker, and the gap between main and production is not called out as a named finding.

---

### F-05 — The System One proxy is cloud-bound by default, reachable by every container on the shared bridge, and spends a vendor key
- Topics: ES-10.8, ES-01.3
- Evidence: "the System One proxy binds 0.0.0.0 inside the container when network is true (agentbox.toml:893-894), never published, so any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key" (ADR-2139:287). The unified compose arms `JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` for VisionClaw, Loom and Codescape (docker-compose.unified.yml:9,387,475), so private corpus content reaches a cloud endpoint (TypeSafe) by default. The Loom façade's own header "still justifies local-only by private corpus content" (loom jev.rs:30-34).
- Failure: A compromised container on visionclaw_network exhausts the TypeSafe API budget. Separately, the Loom sends private email corpus snippets to a cloud API because the compose default overrides the code's local-only intent, and the Loom documentation explicitly says the corpus is private.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — ES-10.8 D6 notes the spend risk as "an accepted consequence"; the privacy contradiction between the Loom header and the compose default is not marked as a named finding.

---

### F-06 — Claude Code deny rules are bypassable by a sh -c wrapper, so the "access control" governs nothing
- Topics: ES-10.10
- Evidence: "Deny rules (agentbox.toml:713) are pattern matches a sh -c wrapper evades, so it is access control, not a vault entry" (ES-10.10 D6). The `[claude_code]` block configures `bypassPermissions` default plus deny rules for `docker run/compose, ssh to machinelearn` (agentbox.toml:698-713).
- Failure: An agent instructed to run `sh -c "docker run ..."` or `bash -c "ssh user@host ..."` bypasses every deny rule. Since the container has the host Docker socket mounted and agents run with `bypassPermissions`, this is the primary guard against an agent taking unrestricted host-level action, and it is cosmetic.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (D6, "SCOPED OUT"), but scoping it out does not reduce the risk — it just acknowledges the register does not cover it.

---

### F-07 — No point-in-time backup exists for the Solid pod store; recovery is undefined
- Topics: ES-08.8
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE, no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only, docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md Known divergences, no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO." ES-07.9 adds the same for RuVector: "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists."
- Failure: Disk corruption, accidental deletion or a bad deploy destroys all pod data (user profiles, preferences, ontology contributions, inbox) and all RuVector memory embeddings with no recovery path. There is no declared RPO or RTO, so no one has committed to how much data loss is acceptable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-08.8, D5 in ES-07.9).

---

### F-08 — Three independent sats ledgers, none synced, one with a 501 deposit stub
- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-01.8
- Evidence: "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others. The host deposit path is a 501 stub and the crate version pins skew" (sovereign-settlement.md:61). The three are solid-pod-rs StoragePaymentStore/WebLedger, host FsPaymentStore (whose deposit handler returns 501), and forum pod-worker D1PaymentStore.
- Failure: A user has a balance in one ledger that another system does not recognise. Credits or debits applied in one store do not propagate. If the settlement pack activates, three divergent balances must be reconciled with no mechanism to do so.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, "the FACT" node).

---

### F-09 — Solid-pod-rs version pins diverge across the estate: three deployments, three different versions
- Topics: ES-01.1, ES-08
- Evidence: VisionClaw compiles crates.io `0.5.0-alpha.17` (Cargo.toml:245). Agentbox builds the `0.5.0-alpha.18` workspace from rev 76ab7aab (solid-pod-rs.nix:54,58). The agentbox pod guide names `0.5.0-alpha.13` (solid-pod.md:37), and native-pod-mesh.md says "Shipped at alpha.15" and "the Nix pin is v0.5.0-alpha.9" (native-pod-mesh.md:3-4). The WAC evaluation, NIP-98 handling and the new `SOLID_POD_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD` flag differ between these versions.
- Failure: A write that succeeds against the agentbox 8484 pod (alpha.18) is rejected by the embedded VisionClaw pod (alpha.17), or vice versa. The JEV cloud flag exists in one deployment but not the other. An operator reading the guide (alpha.13) has no idea what is actually running.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-01.1, Drift in ES-08).

---

### F-10 — Turning on role_isolation removes the dream governance panel's only signing path
- Topics: ES-05.15
- Evidence: The dream engine publishes its 31400 panel and 31402 cases as JunkieJarvis (governance.rs:9-10) and is listed for the W3b move onto the port (ADR-2122:244-245). The owner decided on 2026-10-18 to admit an agent-governance signer for exactly those two kinds (ADR-2122:636-641), but "no such key exists and the shipped ACL grants none" (ADR-2122:643, identity-port-acl.json:40). Under the flag the identity port refuses human-decision kinds and grants 31400/31402 only to a key that does not exist.
- Failure: The operator enables role_isolation for security hardening. The nightly dream cycle runs, produces results, but its governance panel and cases cannot be published to the forum. The human oversight loop silently breaks: no panel appears, no case is raised, and no error reaches the operator because the adapter's governance driver simply cannot sign.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("OPEN" box in ES-05.15).

---

### F-11 — The ADR-2037 release-hygiene boundary is unverified: no CI assertion confirms a shipped release binary omits dev-auth
- Topics: ES-09.5, ES-09.11
- Evidence: "no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected" (ES-09.5). The `dev-auth-release-gate` job in ci.yml asserts over Dockerfiles and entrypoints (ci.yml:143-155) but not over the compiled binary. `enforce_release_env_hygiene` is a no-op stub in dev builds (src/main.rs:169) and the dev-runtime profile is a named profile, not release.
- Failure: A build pipeline misconfiguration produces a release image compiled with `dev-auth`, enabling the Bearer dev-session-token bypass on a production deployment. The Dockerfile-level text assertion passes because the entrypoint does not name the feature, and no binary-level check catches it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-09.5, "ADR-2037 proposed, implementation_status none").

---

### F-12 — NIP-07 browser sessions are lost on every proxy restart because the session secret defaults to per-boot random
- Topics: ES-04.5
