- Evidence: "NIP98_PROXY_SESSION_SECRET defaults to crypto.randomBytes (proxy.mjs:167), so NIP-07 sessions do NOT survive a proxy restart. Intentional, but every restart forces re-authentication."
- Failure: The nip98-proxy restarts (crash, deploy, host reboot). Every browser session is invalidated. All users must re-authenticate. If the proxy restarts frequently (crash loop, OOM), users are in a constant re-authentication loop and cannot maintain a working session.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D2, "DIVERGENCE — Intentional").

---

### F-13 — The host build trap: builds launched from inside the container silently bake stale code while appearing to succeed
- Topics: ES-09.1, ES-09.2
- Evidence: "a build launched from INSIDE this container resolves bind paths against the HOST filesystem and silently bakes stale code" (ES-09.1). The container has the host Docker socket mounted, so `docker compose up` from inside "resolves HOST_PROJECT_ROOT bind-mount paths against ITS OWN (host-side) cwd/view, not the CC container path" causing a "bind source path mismatch" that produces "a silent stale-code failure."
- Failure: An agent inside the Claude Code container runs what it believes is a rebuild. The build succeeds, the container starts, the health check passes — but the running container serves stale code from image-build-time rather than the just-edited files. The agent believes its changes are deployed; they are not.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D2 "TRAP" in ES-01.5, classDef wrong in ES-09.1).

---

### F-14 — The governance rationale gate fires on no VisionClaw case: every case folds to medium and neither gate runs
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13
- Evidence: "VisionClaw's 31400 carries only a d tag and a PanelDefinition with no tier or task-property field, and its 31402 carries d, priority, category, subject-kind, subject-id and title" (events.rs:211-217, :91-101, :308-323). "so the relay folds every VisionClaw case to the advertised default, medium, and check_rationale only bites at high or critical: NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case" (governance.rs:769, wrangler.toml:61, governance.rs:525). The host gate reads "the stricter of the case's DECLARED tier and an effective tier the caller forwards" but "no VisionClaw writer records a declared tier."
- Failure: A human approves an irreversible action on a VisionClaw case without a rationale. No gate refuses it because the case folds to medium and neither the host nor the relay gate fires. The augmentation-conditions invariant ("no surface may fabricate a human's rationale") is unenforceable on the entire VisionClaw surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN in ES-05.12), but the full consequence — that the invariant is structurally unenforceable on an entire subsystem rather than just "open" — is not stated.

---

### F-15 — The ADR ratchet stopped enforcing after 2026-10-20 and now only reports
- Topics: ES-09.12, ES-09.15
- Evidence: "after ADR_RATCHET_UNTIL, default 2026-10-20 (:36), it reports only" (scripts/adr-ratchet.sh:36). The same end date is carried in both the host and agentbox copies (agentbox/scripts/adr-ratchet.sh:36). Separately, "at visionclaw f223bbd40 FIFTEEN host records fail this gate, each naming the governed path that moved under it. The gate works, and what it is reporting is a re-verification backlog" (ES-09.23).
- Failure: The mechanism that prevented undecided ADRs from accumulating has silently become advisory. The fifteen already-failing records will grow because nothing blocks a merge that adds more. Decision records governing security, custody and protocol choices become increasingly unreliable as their verification claims go stale.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the date expiry is stated in ES-09.12 as a parenthetical note; the fifteen stale records are marked "DEBT" in ES-09.23; the combination (enforcement ended + backlog growing + no replacement) is not called out.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual network exposure**: whether the standalone cloudflared tunnel (F-01) is currently deployed and routing to a live backend, or merely present as a file in the repository. I would need to inspect the running Cloudflare dashboard configuration and the deployed containers.

2. **Whether the RUSTSEC-2026-0387 advisory is exploitable in the forum relay worker's specific usage of k256**: the material names the advisory but does not describe its attack surface. I would need the advisory text and the forum's call sites for the affected functions.

3. **The actual contents and sensitivity of the RuVector embeddings**: F-03 assumes the embeddings carry personal or sensitive data that warrants erasure, but I cannot confirm what data is actually stored there from this material alone.

4. **Whether any process has actually exploited the same-UID attribution gap (F-02)**: I would need audit logs from the agent-events journal showing whether any forged-pubkey events have been observed.

5. **The current state of the fifteen stale ADR records**: whether the paths they govern have changed in ways that invalidate the decisions themselves, or only in ways that require re-verification of the same conclusion. I would need to diff each record's verified_paths from its verified_commit to HEAD.

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem — data/custody/security incident, client lost

**Method note.** The material below is the estate corpus (ES-01…ES-12, ES-90) at its stamped revisions (visionclaw `8692b12b`, agentbox `6500582d`, and the sibling stamps in each file's `verified_commit`). It predates the incident and does not name it. The three R items are the failure mechanisms this corpus shows to be live, unmitigated and client-costing if triggered, ranked by likelihood; every citation is the path:line form the corpus itself gives and should be resolved against the stamped revisions before acting. Where a chain of events sequences steps the corpus does not explicitly order, that is flagged under Confidence.

---

### R-1 — Client data deletion never propagates: pod deletes leave RuVector embeddings live, searchable and re-servable, with no reverse tombstone, no logged secondary write and no consistent restore
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-07.1, ES-08.8
- Chain of events:
  1. A client requests erasure; the operator deletes agent memory through the client surface (`SolidPodService.deleteAgentMemory:363` → `agentMemory.ts:198` → `ldpClient.deleteResource:206`).
  2. The DELETE succeeds on the pod (204; 404 treated as success, `ldpClient.ts:210`) and the operator confirms deletion.
  3. The call graph never reaches RuVector: "agentMemory.ts:196-204 has no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent" (ES-08.9).
  4. The embedding row persists and "stays semantically searchable" (ES-07.9 D5); the deletion path "does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence", violating Invariant 6 of `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` (dropped secondary writes must be logged, not swallowed).
  5. The surviving rows are not merely internal: the memory cloud is a read-only external surface that "samples the store and queries its HNSW index" (`src/services/memory_cloud_service.rs:6-13`, ADR-2133, live), so deleted content can be re-surfaced after erasure was confirmed.
  6. Remediation fails too: "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO" (ES-07.9 D5); the pod store likewise has "no point-in-time backup… backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only" (ES-08.8).
- Evidence: ES-08.9 (whole diagram, "Largest erasure hole" framing is ES-07.9 D5's); ES-07.9 D5; ES-08.8 FATAL note; ES-07.1 MCL read-only reader; `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` Known divergences and open items.
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus itself names this "Largest erasure hole" and records that "No joined key-to-row lookup or live post-delete search was performed in this audit" — the gap is documented and unmeasured.
- Confidence: high — the gap is an unconditional property of the shipped code path; only the timing of a client deletion request is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A routine agentbox rebuild batch-activates staged cloud-egress consumers, moving private memory, scaffold and governance-card content off-LAN by default
- Topics: ES-07.3, ES-06.6, ES-05.7, ES-08.13, ES-09.8, ES-01.3, ES-06.1
- Chain of events:
  1. Multiple System One consumers are enabled in the manifest and compose but are boot-projected/baked, hence "staged rather than live": memory rerank (`agentbox.toml:910`, `system_one.rs:444`, ADR-2155), loom Jev with `LOOM_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:391-397`, esp. `:395`), governance rubric (`agentbox.toml:902-903`, `:918`), pod advisories (`agentbox.toml:921`, `flake.nix:1538`, `system_one.rs:578-602`).
  2. One `./agentbox.sh rebuild` activates all of them; no per-consumer egress review exists.
  3. Once live: memory search sends "the query and capped value snippets of the shortlist… cloud TypeSafe by default" (`memory-jev-rerank.js:16-18,107`; `[system_one].local_only` is empty, `agentbox.toml:895`); the Loom's gate/rank/support run against the cloud-reaching agentbox proxy (`loom/crates/loom-facade/src/routes/mod.rs:383-384,495-512`); the governance card text "reaches the selected backend, TypeSafe by default" (`governance-evidence-rubric.js:118`); the pod reranks listings through the proxy (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5130`, `jev.rs:153-181`).
  4. This contradicts the system's own privacy invariants: "the Loom… delegates ONLY to a LAN/local model… so mail content never leaves the LAN" (ES-06.1 INV2), and the façade/pod headers "still justify local-only by private corpus content" (`loom jev.rs:30-34`; `solid-pod-rs jev.rs:24-33`).
  5. Result: client-derived queries, memory snippets and card text leave the LAN to a third-party endpoint with no consent gate — a privacy breach at the next rebuild.
- Evidence: as cited above; ES-09.8 SYS1 (shared anchor merged into VisionClaw, loom and codescape environments, `docker-compose.unified.yml:2-11,14,22,387-395,475,505`); ES-01.3 Open ("takes effect only when the new agentbox image is activated… the running container still dials loopback").
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus disagrees with itself at the same stamps — ES-09.8 records the ADR-2148 close-out as LIVE on 2026-10-30 ("both activation conditions… happened"), while ES-01.3, ES-06.6 and ES-07.3 still describe their consumers as staged pending the same rebuild. Nobody can tell from the docs whether the batch already fired.
- Confidence: medium — the activation mechanism and the invariant contradiction are documented; the breach manifestation is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The shipped stack runs demo-open with a total, peer-agnostic auth bypass, and the standalone Cloudflare tunnel can front it to the internet with no repo-reviewable config
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-10.2, ES-10.8, ES-09.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Code defaults are fail-closed (`rbac_gate.rs:135-141` `unwrap_or(false)`; ownerless boot refused at `main.rs:809-819`), but the shipped compose inverts them: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:121-122`) and `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` (`:105`) — "Net shipped posture… = demo-open" (ES-10.3).
  2. The dev image builds with `BUILD_FEATURES` defaulting to `gpu,ontology,dev-auth` (`rust-backend-wrapper.sh:42`), and on "a dev or dev-auth build with VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1", `dev_full_bypass_active()` (`auth.rs:116`) is true: "BYPASS IS TOTAL — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check, across REST verify_access, the settings extractor and the WS handshake. Peer-agnostic BY DESIGN" (ES-10.6).
  3. The dev service publishes ports 3001 and 4000 (`docker-compose.unified.yml:225-226`).
  4. The standalone cloudflared "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted — with this demo-open posture, anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8`); the route is a dashboard Public Hostname with "no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (`:5-6`) and is "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit" (`:6-8`).
  5. ADR-2119 "leaves this standalone file unaffected… VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan therefore does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host" (ES-10.3 LANGAP; ADR-2119:64-66).
  6. The profile-intent guard only refuses on non-debug artefacts; "Only a debug build logs and continues" (`security_profile.rs:657-661`, ES-10.2) — and the dev artefact is exactly that class. An internet-side visitor or LAN peer obtains anonymous reads and, on the dev build, full dev-admin writes to client graph data.
- Evidence: ES-10.3 NET, DRIFT1, CFT, LANGAP; ES-10.6; ES-09.5 ("no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected", ADR-2037 proposed/none).
- Warning signs visible today: the corpus's own summary of the shipped posture; the unreviewable tunnel mapping; the total-bypass flag defaulting to 1 in compose.
- Confidence: medium — every element is documented; the incident requires the tunnel running or hostile LAN reach, which is inferred.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — The TXO stand-in deposit is a reachable free-money oracle
- Topics: ES-08.12, ES-03.12
- Evidence: "the TXO stand-in deposit is DELETED, not left default-off: it credits sats for any parseable TXO URI guarded only by a replay key. A chain-settled estate must not keep a reachable free-money oracle" (ADR-2008:67-71); status is proposed/partial — the released crate still carries it, the rust-bitcoin port being "landed on main, unreleased" (ADR-2008:119-158).
- Failure: any holder of a parseable TXO URI mints unbacked sats into a did:nostr-keyed ledger; if any consumer ever treats the ledger as value (the settlement pack proposes exactly that), a counterparty is settled in invented credit.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — PROPOSED D6 with the free-money-oracle rationale)

### F-02 — Hand-rolled Bitcoin crypto ships in the released solid-pod-rs
- Topics: ES-08.12
- Evidence: "the released crate… HAND-ROLLS it: BIP-341 scripts, the TapSighash and key-path Schnorr on raw k256, with big-endian mod-n scalar arithmetic written out by hand" (ADR-2008:22-26); the port that retires it is unreleased.
- Failure: a nonce-derivation or scalar-arithmetic error in hand-written Schnorr leaks a key or mutates a transaction; because the crate is also the pod's payment rail, one bad signature is a direct custody loss or locked funds.
- Confidence: high that the released code is as described; any specific flaw is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — the record ranks the port highest priority for exactly this reason)

### F-03 — Three unsynced sats ledgers over non-atomic payment state
- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-03.11
- Evidence: "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others" (`sovereign-settlement.md:61`); "non-atomic payment state is fixed FIRST… a precondition rather than a follow-up" (ADR-2008:72-75); the `payment_settlement` authority class "currently GATES NOTHING" (`economy-loop.md:270`).
- Failure: two ledgers disagree on the same did:nostr balance; a settlement made against one is double-claimed against another; there is no ledger of record to arbitrate.

- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence + Open)

### F-04 — Forum production runs the vulnerable crypto kit while main is fixed
- Topics: ES-05.14, ES-11.4
- Evidence: "forum main (1b27f79) is fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387, with k256 0.14.0… but forum production still runs kit 9c36b60, with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4, so main is fixed and the edge is not" (ES-05.14; pin at `workers-deploy.yml:44`); `kit-pin-guard.yml` enforces five-file pin *agreement*, not freshness, and the pin "kept moving" (ES-11.4).

### F-05 — Building from inside the container silently deploys stale code with green health checks
- Topics: ES-09.1, ES-09.2, ES-01.5
- Evidence: "a build launched from INSIDE this container resolves bind paths against the HOST filesystem and silently bakes stale code. Build only from the host shell" (ES-01.5 D2); "the trap: container starts, health check passes, edits never take effect" (ES-09.1); the container has the host Docker socket mounted (ES-09.1 ENV-FACT).
- Failure: a security or data-handling fix is edited and "deployed" from inside the Claude Code container; binds mis-resolve; the service keeps serving pre-fix code while `/api/health` stays 200, and the client-facing defect continues.

- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Trap)

### F-06 — Same-UID forgery surface: any in-container process can stamp any did:nostr, with role_isolation off and secrets in PID 1's plaintext environment
- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-04.1, ES-04.5, ES-10.10, ES-10.12, ES-06.3
- Evidence: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1)" (`agent-event-auth.js:36-39`); "the ACL authorises a UID, not a process" (`session-plane-acl.json:6`, `api.rs:32`); "role_isolation = false, the shipped default… PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of them" (ES-10.12); "SOPS never executed… .env is PLAINTEXT today" (ES-10.10 D5); bridge key "legacy env fallback REMAINS" (ES-04.5 K1).
- Failure: one compromised or misbehaving process in the container reads `.env` for the management API key and the email-gateway bearer token, emits agent-events and journal entries under any operator `did:nostr` via the loopback proxy-header path, and drives break-glass raw-email reads (ES-06.3) — attribution and custody are void inside the boundary.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt + Drift + Open)

### F-07 — The host Docker socket stays world-writable even after the isolation fix
- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-01.5
- Evidence: "the socket is the HOST inode, already widened by earlier boots. Skipping the chmod does not narrow it; only a host-side chmod does" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:598-621`, registered as Q2/R2 in AB-36); the override mounts the socket and `group_add 965` lets the container drive docker without sudo (ES-01.5 N2).
- Failure: with role_isolation on, the boot records `degraded:docker-socket` and continues; anything reaching the still-`o+rw` host socket gets effective host root; nothing enforces the owed host-side chmod.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-08 — The System One proxy spends the vendor key for any container on the shared bridge, and the port gate is structurally blind to it
- Topics: ES-10.8, ES-01.3, ES-09.8
- Evidence: "the System One proxy binds 0.0.0.0 inside the container when network is true (`agentbox.toml:893-894`), never published, so any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key, an accepted consequence (ADR-2139:287)"; "the gate reasons about PUBLISHED ports and is structurally blind to a container-internal 0.0.0.0 bind on a shared bridge" (ADR-2062, proposed); all sidecars share the one bridge (ES-01.5 N1).
- Failure: a compromised sidecar (browsercontainer, gui-tools, voice, xr) dials `agentbox:8094` directly, burns the cloud vendor key and exfiltrates prompts; no gate detects the bind because none inspects listeners.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / PROPOSED, "accepted consequence")

- Evidence: "VisionClaw backup-secrets.sh still writes ordinary ZIP with an integrity check, without explicit encryption or permission hardening" (ES-10.10 D2, `scripts/backup-secrets.sh`); the hardened agentbox Rust backup "does not replace the legacy script or prove deployed adoption".

- Topics: ES-03.6, ES-03.5

**Verification caveat for the reviewing engineer.** The corpus itself warns which of its claims are unexercised: the ES-08.9 erasure gap has had "no joined key-to-row lookup or live post-delete search performed"; the custody register is a "proposed governing surface" with every custodian and rotation cadence "UNCONFIRMED" (ES-10.10); and fifteen host ADR records fail their own staleness gate at the stamped revision (ES-09.23), so any doc line cited here should be re-read at HEAD before it is relied on.

## knowledgegraph / critical

### F-01 — The privacy/publication gate (invariants 1 and 9) was retired with the archived pipeline; no equivalent is stated anywhere in the live build
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: I1 ("vc:public is the ONLY publication gate — strictly boolean, re-checked in every output stage") and I9 ("No public artefact contains a private identifier — visibility.py at every output, release gate re-scans") are still stated present-tense at `BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:186-219`, while that same document's own header says the invariants "do not assert that the archived workflow remains active" (`BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:29`). The enforcers — `visibility.py:133 is_private_iri` and the release-gate re-scan — moved to `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/` on 2026-09-22 (`README.md:64`). The live build's stated gates are the domain contracts (`visionGraph/.github/workflows/publish.yml:309-313`) and the oo-cert closure check (`publish.yml:158-163`); neither is a private-identifier scan. Authoring also moved from Logseq `vc:public` flags to an Obsidian vault with "typed frontmatter" (`README.md:46`) — the flag convention itself changed.
- Failure: a vault note containing private identifiers has a missing or mistyped flag under the new frontmatter; ontomark publishes it to narrativegoldmine.com and the Quartz `/notes/` mirror; with I9's output-side re-scan retired, nothing catches it — a silent public leak from an 8,138-page personal knowledge vault.
- Confidence: medium — this is an absence (no visibility gate appears in any live path); ontomark could implement one invisibly to this material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — KG-07.1's Drift marks the tense contradiction only.

### F-02 — An external workflow is the sole writer of this repo's published output; nothing in this repo verifies what lands on gh-pages
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-01.1
- Evidence: the only deploy step is `peaceiris/actions-gh-pages, external_repository=knowledgeGraph` (`publish.yml:515-523`); this repo "no longer authors or builds the corpus" and is the deploy target (`README.md:13-15`), and "nothing here computes it any more" (`README.md:87`). The last workflow inside this repo was scoped `permissions: contents: read` at workflow and job level (`archive/github-workflows/build.yml:6-8`) — the only writer is now entirely outside it. The same run then dispatches corpus-sync to VisionClaw with the source sha (`publish.yml:525-531`), so whatever lands propagates into the reasoned store agents query.
- Failure: a compromised or misbehaving run in visionGraph (or in the third-party action, or via any credential that can trigger it — see F-03) replaces the public corpus; this repo has no gate, test, or revert path inspecting gh-pages; VisionClaw then ingests the bad corpus and the Loom/MCP tools ground agents on it.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from topology; no verification step is mentioned on either side).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-03 — "Agents never push to visionGraph main" is now false, and push-to-main publishes to production end-to-end
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-05.3
- Evidence: the earlier invariant text "Agents never push to visionGraph main — publish.yml runs only on the owner's push or by hand" is superseded: the header now runs on push to main or by hand, with estate deployment authorised 2026-10-11 (`publish.yml:17-18`). A push to main triggers the full chain: ontomark build (`publish.yml:127-135`) → domain gates (`publish.yml:309-313`) → gh-pages deploy to narrativegoldmine.com (`publish.yml:515-523`) → corpus-sync dispatch into VisionClaw (`publish.yml:525-531`). No approval environment or review gate on that path is stated.
- Failure: an agent or automation holding write on visionGraph main publishes unreviewed corpus changes to the public site and into the live reasoned graph; operators still trusting the stale invariant do not treat agent pushes as publish events.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the authorisation consequence is drawn, not stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (KG-05.5) marks the doc staleness, not the security implication.

- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3

- Failure: a vault edit silently drops, duplicates or renames classes; ontomark builds it, oo-cert validates the inference closure (not class-count intent), and the site plus VisionClaw republish — the check built to catch exactly this drift no longer runs anywhere.

### F-08 — The markdown primary fetch misses by construction; production prose falls back to raw.githubusercontent.com
- Topics: KG-04.4, KG-05.3
- Evidence: pageService requests `api/markdown/${title}.md` in TITLE form (`pageService.ts:133`) while the build writes a SLUG-form mirror "that pageService.ts never requests" (KG-04.4); on primary 404 it falls back to gh-pages raw markdown (`pageService.ts:134`); `isValidMarkdown`'s heuristics (reject HTML content-type, sessionStorage bodies, DOCTYPE) exist because the static host 404s missing paths to index.html.
- Failure: page prose is served from GitHub raw rather than the deployed generation — it can lag or diverge from the deployed corpus and breaks independently under rate-limiting or a branch move; an error page passing the three heuristics renders as page content. (Severity depends on whether the diverged shipping tree shares this pageService — see Not judgeable.)
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — DIVERGENCE (KG-04.4) marks the form mismatch; the fallback consequence is unmarked.

### F-09 — Scope caps are a runtime-only contract; corpus growth turns the graph view into a thrown RangeError
- Topics: KG-04.3, KG-04.2, KG-05.3
- Evidence: MAX_NODES 1500 / MAX_EDGES 4000 / FOCUS_MAX 300; `assertScope` throws RangeError on any over-budget scope (`explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:144-155`); "caps are named CONTRACT, not tuning — construction… must be rejected, never silently truncated" (KG-04.3); T2 ego tiers are client-derived (KG-04.2). The live pipeline's stated gates are domain contracts (`publish.yml:309-313`) — no tier-size check against these caps is listed.
- Failure: the vault grows one domain tier past 4000 edges, or one hub's ego exceeds FOCUS_MAX 300; ontomark publishes it; every visitor to that graph hits a construction-time RangeError — a data-dependent, per-page outage with no build-time signal.
- Confidence: medium — runtime behaviour is asserted; the absence of a build gate is inferred from the gate list.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

- Marked by authors: no.

### F-12 — The governing documentation systematically describes the retired system; incident responders cannot trust it

- Failure: a layout change in v0.2.2 or in ontomark's writer drifts the 24-byte record; the fixture is not shown to run against the deployed writer/reader pair, so tier data misparses on the live site — the exact failure the fixture existed to prevent.

- Severity: medium

### F-15 — One workflow, one pinned binary, one external release asset: no in-repo path to rebuild, update or roll back the site
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: "one vault, one build — the sole producer is the pinned ontomark release, a run that cannot fetch it fails" (KG-05.5 note1, framed as INVARIANT); this repo is the deploy target only (`README.md:13-15`); gh-pages is written solely by the external workflow (`publish.yml:515-523`).
- Failure: the ontomark release asset is removed or the visionGraph workflow is disabled (action permissions, quota, archival) → the site is frozen indefinitely; there is no in-repo build to regenerate it and no stated rollback procedure for gh-pages; recovery requires maintainers of a different repository and a third-party tool.
- Confidence: high on the topology; the recovery gap is inferred from the stated invariant.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — KG-05.5 note1 states the fail-hard behaviour as an INVARIANT; the loss of any in-repo recovery path is unmarked.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether ontomark or any publish.yml step enforces an equivalent of I1/I9 — a strictly-boolean publication flag re-checked at every output stage and a private-identifier scan of every artefact. I need ontomark's source/flags and the complete gate list beyond the domain contracts.
2. The shipping explorer's fetch contract: whether visionGraph's WasmVOWL pageService requests the SLUG-form mirror and retains the raw.githubusercontent fallback — F-08's production impact depends entirely on this.
3. Integrity pinning of the `vowl-wasm`/`ontomark-graph` tarball dependencies (lockfile hashes), and the scope and type of the credential peaceiris uses for `external_repository` deploys into this repo.
4. Build-time enforcement of MAX_NODES/MAX_EDGES/FOCUS_MAX against ontomark's tier output, and whether the 183-byte golden fixture executes against the deployed writer/reader pair (ontomark + v0.2.2).
5. visionGraph vault content licence and the rights chain behind COMMERCIAL.md's data-licence track; also corpus-sync failure handling — whether a failed post-deploy dispatch leaves VisionClaw stale with any retry or reconciliation.

## knowledgegraph / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody / security class)

Basis: the KG-01…KG-07 distillation at verified commits `knowledgegraph 1edcfc9`, `visionGraph 7b0875f`, `visionflow 4d361b5`, plus the drift markers recorded in it. Root-cause attribution is ranked by support in the recorded state, not by an established timeline; every claim below carries the citation the material gives and is checkable against the code. Where I reason past the material I say so.

---

### R-1 — The privacy/publication gate was retired with the archived pipeline; the new sole-producer path has no documented visibility gate
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3, KG-01.1, KG-06.4
- Chain of events: A non-public note is authored in the visionGraph Obsidian vault (typed frontmatter — a different convention from Logseq's `vc:public` boolean). `publish.yml` runs `ontomark validate --vault all` then `build --vault all --with-markdown-mirror --certificate-out` (publish.yml:127-135, 165-171). The gates the material names on this path are the oo-cert closure check (publish.yml:158-163, 210-227) and the space/Earth/supply-chains domain contracts (publish.yml:309-313) — no visibility or private-IRI step appears anywhere in the new path. Deploy goes via `peaceiris/actions-gh-pages` with `external_repository=knowledgeGraph` (publish.yml:515-523) to narrativegoldmine.com and raw.githubusercontent.com. A private identifier reaches the markdown mirror or data bundle and is public.
- Evidence: Invariant 1 (`vc:public` is the ONLY publication gate, strictly boolean, re-checked in every output stage) and Invariant 9 (no public artefact contains a private identifier — `visibility.py` at every output, release gate re-scans), BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:186-219. The enforcing code is archived: `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/visibility.py:133` (`is_private_iri` consulted by every exporter) and the release-gate re-scan (KG-07.2). The baseline self-describes as historical and says its invariants "do not assert that the archived workflow remains active" (BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:29). ontomark is the ONLY producer (publish.yml:91-97; README.md:56). The archived tree is "not built from, not accepted into" (README.md:15,58). The only documented pending-review convention for machine-written pages (`public:: false, pending-review`, ontocast.md:39-41) is itself archived.
- Warning signs visible today: KG-07.1's recorded Drift (present-tense invariants over retired enforcement); KG-05.3's DIVERGENCE (governing build-and-gates.md:1-3 still claims six active gates); KG-01.1's invariant that this repo no longer builds anything.
- Confidence: medium — the retirement of the enforcing code is stated; that ontomark/publish.yml lack an equivalent gate is inferred from the material's silence and must be verified in ontomark's frontmatter handling and the full workflow step list.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Three corpora of record: the frozen default-branch `dist/data/` (7 Aug 2026) is still a documented ingest source while gh-pages and the VisionClaw store serve later generations
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-06.2, KG-06.1, KG-05.3
- Chain of events: Client work is grounded through agentbox's Loom facade / ontology-bridge MCP tools, which ground against VisionClaw's WHELK-reasoned store, never this repo's Turtle (KG-06.2). That store is fed either by the CorpusSource default — the visionGraph vault directly (ADR-2114) — or by the legacy GitHubSyncService path that fetches markdown + Turtle from this repo over GitHub (ecosystem.md:87-88; the KG→VC edge in KG-06.1). The GitHub path reads the default branch, whose committed `dist/data/` snapshot is the 7 August 2026 generation, explicitly "not the served one" (README.md:87). Deliverables are produced from a corpus months stale relative to narrativegoldmine.com; the client cross-checks against the live site and finds contradictions.
- Evidence: KG-05.5 bullet (snapshot date and mismatch, README.md:87); KG-06.2 sequence and its "Secondary path, not default" note; the 258,200-triple count pinned in the docs for that frozen artefact (KG-06.2); ADR-2013's parity trigger — Loom `/health`, VisionClaw `/api/ontology/classes`, `vault build --stats` agreeing at a named commit — is dated 2026-10-30 and, per the material, staged and unmet (ADR-2013:12).
- Warning signs visible today: the drift bullet exists verbatim; the class-count parity control that would catch vault-vs-published divergence is recorded as not yet activated.
- Confidence: medium — the frozen snapshot and both ingest paths are stated; which path fed the client's deliverable is beyond the material (inferred).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The audited, patched explorer never ships: production runs a diverged tree, so a client-visible fix or security patch lands nowhere
- Topics: KG-04.6, KG-04.1, KG-07.1 (Invariant 2), KG-01.1
- Chain of events: A defect is reported on narrativegoldmine.com (e.g., mishandling of domains present only in the shipping tree, or an NGG1 parse mismatch after an ontomark writer change). Engineers work in `knowledgeGraph/explorer/modern`, because BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:14 and architecture/explorer.md:10 point at an in-tree `explorer/rust-wasm/src/ngg1.rs` reader. The fix lands and passes tests in this repo. Nothing deploys it: this repo's build.yml is retired and inert and "never builds explorer/" (KG-04.6); the shipping SPA is built from `visionGraph/publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern` by publish.yml:410. The client-visible defect persists; the trees also disagree on the shared reader (v0.1.1 here vs v0.2.2 shipping) and on domains (6 vs 9), so even a ported fix needs re-verification against a tree documented only as VG-*.
- Evidence: KG-04.6 in full — HPKG `explorer/modern/package.json:20` v0.1.1 vs SPKG `WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:25` v0.2.2; DOMAIN_SLUGS six (`explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:73-80`) vs nine (`WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:76-86`), diverged since 2026-10-01; BUILDYML "RETIRED, INERT … never builds explorer/". KG-04.1 note1 DOC-DRIFT on the phantom crate. Invariant 2 pins writer + both readers to a 183-byte golden fixture — but the writer is now ontomark and one reader ships only from the other tree. Explorer is "retained not deployed" (README.md:68,96).
- Warning signs visible today: the standing version and domain divergences; docs directing work at a tree that cannot reach production.
- Confidence: high that changes to `knowledgeGraph/explorer` cannot reach production (stated); medium that this was the incident mechanism (inferred).
- Severity: high

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

### F-01 — Production edge runs known-vulnerable cryptographic dependency (RUSTSEC-2026-0387)
- Topics: NF-01.4, NF-06.18
- Evidence: The material states "Production is not fixed: the edge still runs kit `9c36b60`, which resolves crypto-bigint `0.5.5` and k256 `0.13.4`" while main is on "k256 and p256 are `0.14.0`" after the RUSTSEC bump. The kit-compatibility record confirms "the edge kit pin is now 9c36b60" (`../dreamlab-ai-website/docs/architecture/kit-compatibility-record.md:30`).
- Failure: A remotely exploitable crypto vulnerability (RUSTSEC severity) is live on every signature verification, key derivation, and encrypted-zone operation the relay performs. Any advisory with a proof-of-concept against crypto-bigint 0.5.5 or k256 0.13.4 compromises the entire trust model: event signatures, NIP-44 conversation keys, passkey-derived identities.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, NF-01.4)

### F-02 — SSRF guard is denylist-only by default, has no wall-clock timeout, and the template never sets the allowlist
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`) … and the template does not set it" (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13` declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN). Separately: "there is NO wall-clock timeout – only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3).
- Failure: A user submits a preview URL that resolves to an internal Cloudflare or metadata endpoint via DNS rebinding. The denylist-only guard cannot catch the rebound address. Without a timeout, a slow-loris external server holds Worker invocations open indefinitely, burning CPU-time budget. Both vectors are live on any default deployment.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE acknowledged in-code, NF-07.6 N4)

### F-03 — NIP-42 relay-tag check fails open when RELAY_URL is blank, and the template ships blank
- Topics: NF-03.2
- Evidence: "own_relay_url unset or blank SKIPS the relay-tag check only. This fails OPEN on the defence-in-depth check … so the gate can roll out before RELAY_URL is configured (`nip42.rs:168-172`) – RELAY_URL ships blank (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:51`)." A console error is logged on every AUTH while blank (`nip42.rs:230`).
- Failure: A kind-22242 AUTH event minted for relay A is replayed to relay B (which also has blank RELAY_URL). The challenge check still passes if the challenge was issued by B, but the relay-binding defense-in-depth is gone. An attacker who obtains any valid AUTH event for a user can authenticate as them on any relay that ships the default template.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (explicitly described in material, NF-03.2)

### F-04 — Governance READ endpoints have no membership check; reviewer aggregate reconstructable from public reads
- Topics: NF-06.9, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-2: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23`). KF-2a: "the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:24`).
- Failure: Any nostr key holder (not a forum member) calls GET /api/governance/decisions and GET /api/governance/cases, harvesting every broker case, decision outcome, rationale and reviewer identity. The admin-gated /api/governance/reviewers endpoint's data is fully reconstructable by joining public case and decision reads, defeating its access gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-2, KF-2a)

### F-05 — ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read by the relay but declared in no wrangler.toml; a deploy that omits it silently disables all zone encryption
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8
- Evidence: "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4). NF-08.8 N7 confirms: "A second lockstep gate, ENCRYPTION_ENABLED, follows the same own-binding pattern across relay and clients but is undeclared in every wrangler.toml."
- Failure: An operator follows SETUP.md, deploys the relay with encrypted zones configured in forum.toml, and assumes posts in those zones are encrypted. The gate defaults off. Plaintext is accepted into "encrypted" zones because is_encrypted returns false. Users posting sensitive content believe they have confidentiality they do not have. Discovering this requires reading source code, not documentation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, NF-08.2 N4)

### F-06 — Case-sensitive pubkey comparisons in core self-review guards vs case-insensitive handling everywhere else
- Topics: NF-06.15, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-6: "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:29`). NF-06.15 N1 confirms: "NIP-98 accepts case-insensitive hex and returns event.pubkey verbatim, and the event id is recomputed over that string, so the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify." The registry was fixed (NF-06.15) but "left open in the core aggregate."
- Failure: An agent registers with lowercase pubkey and has a case pending. The same agent, using a NIP-07 extension that presents the key in mixed case, signs a 31403 decision on its own case. The self-review guard (`CaseError::SelfReview`, `governance.rs:1257`) compares case-sensitively and does not fire. The agent decides its own case, defeating the separation the protocol exists to enforce.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-6)

### F-07 — ADMIN_PUBKEYS declared in no auth or relay template; the relay template's own comment asserts the opposite of what the code does
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-10.7
- Evidence: "ADMIN_PUBKEYS is DECLARED in exactly one wrangler template – the search worker (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`) – yet is read by the auth worker (`admin.rs:71`) and the relay (`auth.rs:185`). The relay template asserts the opposite: there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/ (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21`)." NF-10.7 S2: "A by-the-book deployment has no static admin bootstrap."
- Failure: An operator deploys following SETUP.md, which never lists ADMIN_PUBKEYS. No static admin exists on relay or auth. The operator cannot administer the forum through the API surface: no whitelist management, no agent provisioning, no governance decisions. The relay template comment actively misleads anyone auditing it. Recovery requires source-diving to discover the variable the templates deny exists.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT NF-08.7 N4; deployment divergence NF-10.7 S2)

### F-08 — Per-IP rate limits are volatile in-memory state lost on every DO hibernation, making them ineffective as a defense
- Topics: NF-11.1, NF-11.2
- Evidence: NF-11.1 lists "rate_limits : RefCell HashMap String Vec f64" as volatile in-memory state. NF-11.2 states on hibernation: "sessions map, next_session_id and rate limits are gone, but sockets and their sid/ip tags survive." No persistence or external store for rate limits is mentioned.
- Failure: Cloudflare hibernates the Durable Object after a period of inactivity (seconds to minutes). An attacker sends a burst of requests, waits for hibernation, sends another burst. Each wake starts with a clean rate-limit map. The documented 20 req/60s per IP limit (NF-03.4 step 1) is a speed bump, not a control. A patient or distributed attacker faces effectively no admission rate limit on the WebSocket relay.
- Confidence: high (inferred from explicit statement that rate limits are lost on hibernation, combined with no mention of persistent rate limiting)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Moderation and ban gates key on the raw signing pubkey even when the write gate already resolved a device key to its owner
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-4/KF-9: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:27`, `:44`). NF-03.4 step 9 says suspension/silence is "checked for the device key AND its owner" but step 10 says ban/mute is keyed on "the principal" which per step 6 is the resolved owner for device keys but "never for a gift wrap."
- Failure: A banned user's device key is banned, but they mint a fresh device key under the same owner. The write gate resolves to the owner (not banned), and the ban gate keys on the raw new device key (not in the ban list). The banned user publishes freely through a rotating device key. Similarly, a muted user gift-wraps content that bypasses mute enforcement entirely because a gift wrap has no resolvable author at that gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-4, KF-9)

### F-10 — NIP-40 expiration: the admission and sweep paths disagreed; the fix is at HEAD but production runs an older pin
- Topics: NF-10.11, NF-11.13, NF-01.4
- Evidence: KF-1: "the NIP-40 expiry sweep and the serve path disagree on a malformed expiration tag, and the reap side wins" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:22`). The mechanism is fixed at HEAD: "admission refuses a malformed or past expiration (`filter.rs:375`) and the sweep's SQL now skips non-numeric values (`cron.rs:419`)." However, NF-01.4 confirms production runs kit `9c36b60`, not HEAD (`1b27f79`).
- Failure: On the production edge, an event with a malformed expiration tag is admitted (pre-fix admission behavior) and then silently deleted by the retention sweep (pre-fix reap behavior). A user's post disappears without a tombstone, deletion event, or notification. No client-side evidence remains that it ever existed. The fix exists in the repository but is not deployed.
- Confidence: high (fix exists at HEAD; production confirmed on older commit)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-1; marked as "MECHANISM FIXED at HEAD, row not yet retired")

### F-11 — The auth worker executes the full D1 schema bootstrap on every single request with no isolate latch
- Topics: NF-02.1, NF-03.3, NF-08.5
- Evidence: NF-03.3 N1: "the auth worker runs it at the top of every request (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/lib.rs:163`)." This is explicitly contrasted with the relay, which latches `SCHEMA_READY` once per isolate. NF-08.5 N1 confirms the auth worker's bootstrap is idempotent but run per-request.
- Failure: Every passkey login, NIP-98 verification, governance API call and username claim pays the cost of executing every CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS statement against D1 before routing. Under load, this multiplies D1 operations by the number of auth requests. A transient D1 outage causes every auth request to fail at the bootstrap stage before even reaching a rate limit or CORS check, turning a database blip into a total auth outage.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (described as a factual contrast in NF-03.3 but not flagged as a defect)

### F-12 — NIP-29 group metadata kinds 39000-39002 are accepted from admin clients rather than relay-key-signed (spec violation, still live)
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-10.3, NF-12.6
- Evidence: O2 (STILL LIVE): "NIP-29 group metadata 39000-39002 accepted from admin CLIENTS rather than relay-key-signed. Acknowledged TODO `nip_handlers.rs:1300`, admin gate `nip_handlers.rs:1307`. Spec drift." NF-12.6 note: "The relay admits 39000-39002 from admin CLIENTS while these builders exist for relay-key signing."
- Failure: Any admin client publishes a kind-39000 group metadata event that other NIP-29-compliant software interprets as authoritative relay-signed group state. Because it is actually client-signed, the relay cannot prove provenance to federated peers, and a compromised admin browser session can rewrite group membership metadata that downstream consumers trust as relay-attested. The mismatch means any NIP-29 interop is broken by design.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Anomaly O2, STILL LIVE, NF-10.3)

### F-13 — The search worker template ships the only real, non-placeholder admin pubkey in any wrangler.toml
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7
- Evidence: "This worker is the ONLY wrangler template that declares ADMIN_PUBKEYS, yet the auth and relay workers both read it … It is also the only template shipping a non-placeholder value where every other template ships example.com or a zeroed key (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`)." NF-08.7 N5: "the search worker ships a REAL non-placeholder pubkey as its default admin – the only non-generic value in any template."
- Failure: An operator deploys the search worker from the template. The hardcoded pubkey becomes the sole admin for /ingest. If the key belongs to a kit developer, that external party permanently controls the search index of every deployment that does not override the variable. If the key is orphaned, no one can ingest. Either way, a production search index is bootstrapped under authority the operator did not choose.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, NF-07.2 N3, NF-08.7 N5)

### F-14 — Production runs PAY_ENABLED=true while the withdrawn dormancy assumption named three evidence-free value paths as defects
- Topics: NF-04.9, NF-04.11
- Evidence: "The shipped template has PAY_ENABLED 'false' (`nostr-bbs-pod-worker/wrangler.toml:31`). DRIFT: production sets it 'true'" (NF-04.9). NF-04.11 N2: "the routes were triaged as dormant, but production sets PAY_ENABLED true – the three evidence-free value paths named in the amendment are the defect this journal closes." The journal fix exists at HEAD but production runs an older pin.
- Failure: On the production edge, a user deposits coins to a frozen deposit address. The credit path that runs is one of the three evidence-free value paths the journal was built to close. Without the journal, a balance can be created or raised without chain evidence, or a deposit can fail to credit with no recovery record. Users lose money or the pod operator is exposed to a balance-injection attack.
- Confidence: medium (the fix is at HEAD; whether production has the specific defect depends on the gap between 9c36b60 and 1b27f79)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT, NF-04.9; NF-04.11 N2)

### F-15 — The mesh federation gate only restricts clients listed in MESH_ALLOWED_REMOTE_DIDS; anyone unlisted bypasses it entirely
- Topics: NF-03.13
- Evidence: "A local client whose pubkey is not in the remote-DID list bypasses this check entirely (`nip_handlers.rs:1173-1175`)." The gate is `is_mesh_peer AND NOT is_federated_kind_allowed to blocked` — it only fires when the peer IS recognized as a mesh peer.
- Failure: The gate is documented as the boundary that controls which kinds cross a relay federation boundary. An attacker with a whitelisted pubkey (but not in the remote-DID list) publishes restricted kinds (DMs, moderation events) that the mesh gate was designed to contain. The gate is inert for anyone it doesn't explicitly name, providing no positive enforcement — only opt-in restriction for declared peers. Combined with MESH_ALLOWED_REMOTE_DIDS shipping empty, the gate is doubly non-functional.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the logic description; the actual kinds blocked may be caught by other gates in the pipeline)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The actual CORS implementation on the auth worker.** The material says "origin = the ACTUAL request URL, not EXPECTED_ORIGIN" and mentions `with_cors(resp)` but never specifies whether the CORS allowlist reflects any origin, uses a fixed list, or reads ALLOWED_ORIGIN. Without seeing the `with_cors` function I cannot determine whether cross-origin credential leakage is possible.

2. **Whether the relay text search returning ciphertext for encrypted zones constitutes an information leak.** The material says "the relay match is on ciphertext, which relay_text_search discards client-side" but does not specify what NIP-50 LIKE matching against NIP-44 v2 base64 could reveal about encrypted content patterns to a non-client observer.

3. **The actual NIP-44 v2 conversation-key derivation security at the pinned k256 0.13.4 version.** The material confirms the version gap but not which specific RUSTSEC-2026-0387 attack primitive applies or whether any proof-of-concept exists that would be exploitable in a Workers runtime.

4. **Whether the ActivityLedger's in-memory coalescing window can lose trust-promoting activity data on DO eviction in a way that prevents a legitimate promotion.** The material says "a DO eviction can drop at most one window of pending reads" but does not state whether the trust sweep's ~6-month inactivity gate amplifies this into a wrongful demotion for a low-activity user.

5. **The concrete behavior of `wrangler deploy` against a D1 database_id of all zeros.** The material says the auth-worker RELAY_DB is "a ZERO placeholder that every deployment must override" but does not state whether the deploy fails loudly, silently connects to nothing, or connects to another tenant's database if the ID happens to be valid for another account.

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — nostr-rust-forum / dreamlab deployment

**Incident class:** confidential client-community data (governance case content, member search queries, and zone content represented to members as end-to-end encrypted) left the operator's control boundary. The three root causes below are ranked from defects this material itself records as live at the verified commit; every claim is checkable at the cited path:line.

---

### R-1 — Advisory AI flags ship ON and cloud-by-default, so case data and member queries leave to a third-party endpoint
- Topics: NF-08.8, NF-06.19, NF-07.9, NF-02.2
- Chain of events:
  1. All three worker templates ship the System One advisory flags on: relay `JEV_TRIAGE=1`, search `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK=1`, auth `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK=1`.
  2. The locality switches (`JEV_SEARCH_RERANK_LOCAL_ONLY`, `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY`) default **off**, so the remote path is allowed by default; endpoints resolve to TypeSafe `jev-latest` via a `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` Worker secret.
  3. Every semantic search with the flag on sends the query text and up to ten public snippets out of the Worker; every rationale-check call sends the case row's title, subject, summary, effective tier and task properties.
  4. The website overlay deployed Workers from this pin (run 41028875471), so the shipped-on configuration reached deployed code paths.
  5. A client audit or DPA review discovers that moderation/ontology case content and member search behavior transited an external AI vendor with no consent path and no measured benefit; the client terminates.
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:32-36`, `nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:53-58`, `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:11-13`; `docs/jev-option.md:27-32` (manifest ON, cloud-allowed), `:38` (query + ten public snippets leave), `:11` (no measured uplift, unfitted thresholds); `rationale_check.rs:38`, `:303` (locality default off), `:279` (case fields read and sent); NF-02.2 Open (flag ON in the committed template, cloud by default since the 2026-10-30 pin); NF-06.19 N2 records the overlay deploy from this pin.
- Warning signs visible today: the flags are already ON in committed manifests while the docs call the feature a "proposed research candidate"; locality opt-out exists but defaults to allowing the cloud; NF-06.19 explicitly says no governed path depends on it — yet the data leaves regardless.
- Confidence: high that the egress path is default-on and deployed; **inferred** that the reviewer's draft rationale text is in the rationale-check payload (the material lists case fields; the route's purpose implies the draft is checked) and that this specific egress cost the client.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is undeclared and defaultless, so "encrypted" zones silently run in plaintext the relay stores and the operator can read
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8, NF-08.9, NF-05.16, NF-03.12, NF-09.4
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator marks zones `encrypted = true` in forum.toml; ZONE_CONFIG projects to the relay env and the client `window.__ENV__`.
  2. `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is read from env but declared in **no** wrangler.toml `[vars]` in any worker and has no documented deployment default.
  3. The deploy omits it; the relay's `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT public` evaluates false, so the ciphertext shape-check gate never fires.
  4. The client's own gate (`encryption_enabled()`, dormant per NF-05.16 N3) is likewise off when its env lacks it, so `ZoneWriter::prepare` passes plaintext through — while the member still sees the zone flagged encrypted in the rendered config.
  5. Plaintext kind-42 events are admitted (no encrypted-zone refusal applies), stored in relay D1, and fall under the relay's NIP-50 text search, which matches content and where only zk-tagged (encrypted) hits are discarded client-side — plaintext hits are returned to any searching member unless the search path applies the per-event viewer gate, which the material does not show (F-11).
  6. A support interaction reveals the operator can read a "family"/"business" zone's contents server-side, breaking the stated guarantee that "the relay holds no zone key"; enabling the flag later does not encrypt stored history.
- Evidence: `zone_config.rs:86` (read), NF-08.2 N4 (DOC-DRIFT: declared in no wrangler.toml, no documented default, "silently leaves encryption OFF"); NF-08.8 N7; `zone_config.rs:133` (effective-encryption formula), `:208-223` + `nip_handlers.rs:1458-1464` (shape gate that therefore never runs), `:204-207` (no-zone-key guarantee); `store.rs:480-491` (client writer), `zone_crypto/mod.rs:99` + `app.rs:796-800` (dormant client gate); `global_search.rs:725-760` and `:733-742` (search matches content; only zk-tagged discarded).
- Warning signs visible today: the doc-drift note itself names the exact failure ("a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF"); SETUP.md never lists the variable; the client and relay gates are independent and can diverge from operator intent.
- Confidence: high that the silent-off mechanism exists exactly as documented; **inferred** that a given production deploy was in the off state.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The production edge still runs the pre-advisory crypto stack: RUSTSEC-2026-0387 fixed on main, never rolled forward
- Topics: NF-01.4, NF-06.18, NF-09.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Main at `1b27f79` bumps k256/p256 to 0.14.0 and solid-pod-rs to `0.5.0-alpha.18`, with Cargo.lock resolving crypto-bigint 0.7.10 / k256 0.14.0.
  2. Production is pinned to kit `9c36b60`, resolving crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4.
  3. The pin advances by hand (kit-compatibility-record) and deploys are per-crate wrangler runs — no automated roll-forward — and the bump additionally needs the wasm32-only `getrandom` 0.4 `wasm_js` wrinkle, which is exactly the friction that makes pin-lag persist.
  4. Twelve months on, the advisory is exploited or surfaces in a client security review against the still-pinned edge; the affected code paths are the estate's signature/encryption spine (event verify, NIP-44, gift wrap).
- Evidence: NF-01.4 Drift paragraph with `Cargo.toml:104-105`, `:185`, `nostr-bbs-core/Cargo.toml:60`; `../dreamlab-ai-website/docs/architecture/kit-compatibility-record.md:26`, `:30`, `:38`; `SETUP.md:178` (per-crate deploy); NF-12.2 / NF-12.4 (what sits on the crypto stack).
- Warning signs visible today: the material records the production/main crypto split explicitly at the verified commit.
- Confidence: high that the unfixed advisory is live in production at the stamp; **inferred** that the advisory is exploitable in these paths — the material does not state its severity.
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — The search-worker template ships a REAL admin pubkey as its default
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-10.6
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41` ships a real 64-hex pubkey — the only non-placeholder value in any template; `/ingest` is the only mutating route and is admin-gated (`lib.rs:720`, `auth.rs:86`); ADMIN_PUBKEYS is declared in no other template (NF-08.7 N4, DD3).
- Failure: any deploy that does not override the value gives a third party admin on `/ingest`: they can insert public labels/vectors that surface in members' search results as legitimate-looking hits linking `/go/:event_id` (in-forum phishing / content injection), and retract the operator's vectors via `allowPrivate` (`lib.rs:254`).
- Confidence: high (facts asserted); the unmodified-deploy step is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — NF-08.7 N4/N5, NF-10.6 DD3)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Governance read endpoints verify a signature but no membership; the reviewer aggregate is reconstructable
- Topics: NF-10.11, NF-02.6, NF-02.2
- Evidence: KF-2/KF-2a (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`): NIP-98 verification with no membership check makes "authenticated effectively anonymous," and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read; case/decision/role list routes sit in the `require_authed` tier (NF-02.6, `lib.rs:529`, `:555`, `:588`; read endpoint `governance_api.rs:619`).
- Failure: any key holder harvests a client's confidential case titles, summaries, rationales and outcomes — a direct exfiltration surface independent of R-1.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — "operator decision, taken once for both")
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively
- Topics: NF-10.11, NF-06.6, NF-06.15
- Evidence: KF-6 (`known-findings.md:29`): core's guards compare case-sensitively while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim; the identical defect class was fixed in the agent registry (`governance_api.rs:108`) but left open at `governance.rs:1709` and `:1806`.
- Failure: an agent publishes a 31403 for its own case using a mixed-case rendering of its pubkey; `CaseError::SelfReview` never fires and the agent decides its own case; the same bypass defeats the original-signer check on supersession.
- Confidence: high (mechanism asserted).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-6)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Device-key revocation is silently inert when the flag is off while the mapping is known
- Topics: NF-02.7, NF-03.4, NF-10.7
- Evidence: NF-02.7 DIVERGENCE note — with the gate off "a known device to owner mapping is IGNORED and revocation has no effect at AUTH"; both templates ship `"false"` (`auth wrangler.toml:69`, `relay wrangler.toml:29`); gate enables only on exact `"true"` (`devices.rs:112`); revoke writes `revoked = 1` (`devices.rs:477`).
- Failure: device keys registered while enabled, flag later unset (rollback/redeploy); a compromised device key keeps authenticating, and the operator's incident response — `/api/devices/revoke` — succeeds HTTP-wise while changing nothing at the relay.
- Confidence: high (mechanism asserted); the key-compromise precondition is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — NF-02.7 divergence note; NF-10.7 S4)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — SSRF guard is denylist-only without `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, has no wall-clock timeout, and leaks readers to Google
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-07.5, NF-07.7, NF-10.7
- Evidence: in-code acknowledgment (`ssrf.rs:13`): no resolve-then-pin primitive, denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable without the allowlist (`ssrf.rs:100`, `:114`); the template declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (`preview wrangler.toml:13`); no wall-clock timeout exists (NF-07.6 N3); every preview embeds a google.com favicon (`parse.rs:172`).
- Failure: an attacker posts a link whose hostname rebinds after first resolution, or one that stalls — the preview worker fetches an attacker-chosen target or holds the isolate with only redirect/size caps bounding it; separately, every reader's IP is disclosed to Google per rendered preview.
- Confidence: high that the gaps exist (the code says so); exploitation is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/DIVERGENCE acknowledged in code; NF-10.7 S3)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The cross-stack derivation parity proof runs in CI but does not gate merges
- Topics: NF-09.1, NF-09.3, NF-10.2, NF-10.7
- Evidence: the drift-gates job runs `identity-vector-parity.mjs` (`ci.yml:293`, `:296`) but is absent from the `ci-pass` needs list (`ci.yml:306-314`); `derive_subkey` is declared a byte-for-byte cross-stack contract with agentbox's JS mirror (`keys.rs:254`, `:479`, `:484`; `identity-vector-parity.mjs:6`).
- Failure: either side changes the HMAC input; the parity job flags it on the branch but nothing blocks merge; Rust and agentbox then derive different subkeys from the same root — cross-stack identity/wallet divergence and custody-bound actions taken under the wrong derived identity.
- Confidence: high (non-gating asserted); the divergence consequence is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — NF-10.7 S6)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Relay OK certifies storage only; the "applied" loop has no consumer half
- Topics: NF-03.10, NF-06.11, NF-10.8, NF-11.10
- Evidence: ADR-2010 ledger is proposed/partial/staged (`ADR-2010:5-7`); the relay explicitly says its OK does not stand as the last word when projection did not commit (`nip_handlers.rs:1630-1642`); acceptance still awaits the owner's first high-tier 31403 at projection-committed (`ADR-2010:157`); consumer implementations remain separate and unwitnessed (NF-11.10 N4/N5).
- Failure: an admin approves a consequential action; the 31403 is stored and projected, OK returned — but the consuming system never applies it, and no `applied`/`not_applied` record is ever written; the client acts on an approval that never took effect while everyone watched the relay's OK.
- Confidence: high (the open loop is asserted); the incident scenario is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension/Open — NF-03.10 tension, ADR-2010 staged)
- Severity: high

### F-08 — `broker_decisions.superseded_by` exists only through ensure_schema, not in any migration
- Topics: NF-10.11, NF-08.6, NF-06.11
- Evidence: KF-13 (`known-findings.md:48`): the column is created only by the live bootstrap (`lib.rs:765` area), not by migration 0002 (`migrations/0002_governance.sql:39`); both use IF NOT EXISTS, so a migration-created table is never altered.
- Failure: a D1 provisioned from the migration set keeps the older table shape; the supersession UPDATE fails at runtime, so a superseded decision remains the effective one while the UI shows the newer decision — silent governance-state divergence.
- Confidence: medium (author-asserted gap; the runtime-failure path depends on table-shape divergence an engineer should confirm).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-13)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Payment D1 is the only live ledger; the chain view that would make balances reconstructible is not built
- Topics: NF-04.11, NF-04.9, NF-08.4
- Evidence: live system reads balance from D1 (`pay_ledger.rs:412`) with credits journalled against chain evidence (`pay_ledger.rs:110`); the height-stamped chain-derived view is "not yet built" (`ADR-2012:48-52`); production sets `PAY_ENABLED true` against a template `"false"` and an amendment that triaged the routes as dormant (`ADR-2012:35-40`; `wrangler.toml:31`).
- Failure: D1 loss, corruption or a bad manual UPDATE destroys member balances; the journal names outpoints but no rebuild tooling exists — paid credit becomes unspendable with no recovery path.
- Confidence: medium (exposure asserted; the loss event is inferred).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/PROPOSED)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — The forum client has two relay resolvers with divergent hardcoded fallbacks
- Topics: NF-05.7, NF-10.3
- Evidence: anomaly O8 re-verified STILL LIVE: `relay.rs:1045` reads `window.__ENV__` inline instead of the shared helper (`utils/relay_url.rs:151`), and the two fallbacks diverge (`relay.rs:22` vs `utils/relay_url.rs:9`).
- Failure: a bundle served without env injection splits its traffic across two different default hosts — signed events published to one relay while REST/auth calls target another; if either fallback is outside the operator's control, member content and tokens are delivered to a third party.
- Confidence: medium (divergence asserted; the wrong-host consequence depends on what the fallbacks are, which the material does not state).
- Marked by authors: yes (anomaly register O8, NF-05.7 and NF-10.3)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — NIP-50 text search is not shown to apply the per-event viewer gate that REQ delivery does
- Topics: NF-05.14, NF-03.14, NF-11.14
- Evidence: REQ delivery runs `authorize_event` per event against the resolved ViewerContext (`nip_handlers.rs:1883`) and broadcast re-checks per session (`broadcast.rs:148`); the NIP-50 handler is a separate surface (`profiles.rs:251`, advertised at `nip11.rs:207`) and no diagram in this material shows it applying zone/protected-read gating; the client discards only zk-tagged hits (`global_search.rs:733-742`), which does not bound cohort-restricted plaintext.
- Failure: if unguarded, a member extracts the content of a Locked/family-zone post via a text query unique to it, bypassing the read-cohort lattice.
- Confidence: low — this is an absence-of-evidence suspicion raised for verification, not an asserted defect; the material's diagrams simply do not cover the search path's authorization.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-12 — The upstream-absorption canary exercises only three of the five NIPs its own doc claims
- Topics: NF-01.5, NF-09.8, NF-10.6
- Evidence: DD5: the module doc names a five-feature matrix nip04/nip19/nip44/nip59/nip98 (`canary lib.rs:10`) but only three smokes exist (`:35`, `:56`, `:89`); the same doc misnames the crate (`:14` vs package `nostr-bbs-upstream-canary`).
- Failure: a PASS is read as full-coverage evidence; a deliberate module deletion proceeds; upstream NIP-04/59/98 behavior differs and legacy DM unwrap or REST auth breaks in production after absorption.
- Confidence: high (coverage gap asserted); the post-absorption breakage is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift DD5)
- Severity: high

### F-13 — One beta channel carries five kit versions, and the exact-pin invariant has become a caret
- Topics: NF-01.1, NF-01.4, NF-04, NF-10.1
- Evidence: path-dep versions span beta.19/16/15/10 (`Cargo.toml:188-194`) with a stale beta.3 comment (`:187`); ADR-2007 and BASELINE invariant 6 require an EXACT solid-pod-rs pin (`ADR-2007:30`, `BASELINE-architecture.md:200`) against the declared caret `0.5.0-alpha.18` (`Cargo.toml:181-185`); ADR-2012 still speaks of "the exact pin at Cargo.toml:155" (`ADR-2012:76-80`).
- Failure: an upgrade moves one consumer of a shared surface (whitelist SQL constants, admin parse rule, gift-wrap shapes) but not the others; cross-crate schema/wire drift compiles green and mis-authorizes or corrupts at runtime.
- Confidence: medium (facts asserted; runtime corruption is inferred).
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence/Tension — NF-01.1 N2/N3, NF-01.4, NF-04 tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — The auth worker re-runs its full D1 bootstrap on every request
- Topics: NF-02.1, NF-08.5, NF-10.11
- Evidence: bootstrap at the top of every request, no isolate latch (`auth lib.rs:163`, `schema.rs:19`); KF-3 records ~75 DDL statements per request on this worker while the relay now latches once per isolate (`relay lib.rs:200`, `:222`).
- Failure: sustained traffic exhausts D1 quotas → all auth-worker routes (passkey login, NIP-98 verification for every admin surface) fail at peak — an effective lockout of the admin plane and a cost event.
- Confidence: high (per-request execution asserted); quota exhaustion is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift KF-3)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — Moderation gates after the write gate key on the raw signing pubkey; gift wraps have no author to resolve
- Topics: NF-10.11, NF-03.4, NF-03.6
- Evidence: KF-4/KF-9 (`known-findings.md:27`, `:44`): the write gate resolves device-key→owner but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve; gift wraps are deliberately excluded from ban gating (`nip_handlers.rs:56-65`). Note the tension: NF-03.4 step 9 states suspension/silence is "checked for the device key AND its owner" — the material does not reconcile which downstream gates (step 10 ban/mute, step 11 trust) resolve the owner.
- Failure: a sanctioned member publishing through a path whose effective principal is not the raw banned key passes the moderation gates the sanction was meant to enforce.
- Confidence: medium (defect author-asserted; its exact reach versus step 9 is unresolved in the material and needs code check).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-4/KF-9)
- Severity: medium

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The still-running VisionClaw producer ships the unfixed SEV-1 defects: fail-open redactor and body-only credential scan
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT: "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires" — S1.1 is the redactor that "failed OPEN on a regex compile error" (`vault/src/projection.rs:230`) and S1.2 the "body-only scan, frontmatter published" (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160,196`). OM-05.5 shows this job is live and publishing: `cargo build --release --locked -p vault` then `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge --out output/vault` (`ontology-publish.yml:167-175`), feeding `pack-pod-resources.py` (`:247`).
- Failure: a regex compile error in VisionClaw's redactor publishes private prose verbatim; a credential placed in a frontmatter value (title, sources) on a public page is published because only the body is scanned. Both defects were rated SEV-1 when found in the port; the original still runs in production.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL, OM-00.3)

### F-02 — Held-back page identities escape the redaction marker set: the narrow `_misc`-top-level rule feeds masking, the wide any-depth rule feeds staging
- Topics: OM-02.5, OM-03.3, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `excluded_identities_of` (which feeds "the collision check and the marker set", `ir/projection.rs:543,569`) uses `is_unpublished` — `_misc` at top level only (`input/page.rs:641`, `ir/projection.rs:114,231`); staging and `clean_vault` use `held_back`, which applies `UNPUBLISHED_DIRS` at any depth plus `misc` (`build/publish.rs:134,138-148`, ER-18). OM-02.5 n5 and OM-03.3 state the mismatch as the divergence that remains after 95208e8. The authors also leave it **Open** whether `misc` belongs in `HELD_BACK_DIRS` at all (OM-00.1).
- Failure: a page held back at `subdir/_misc/page.md` (or any-depth `misc`) is withheld from `publish/`, but its id/slug/title are absent from the private-identity marker set, so its name passes unmasked into other pages' published prose, indexes and Turtle — exactly the leak class the marker redactor exists to close. The collision check likewise cannot see it.
- Confidence: high (the rule mismatch is explicit; the exact leak population is slightly ambiguous because the material describes `held_back` two ways — see F-13)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-02.5 n5 / OM-03.3; Open, OM-00.1)

### F-03 — Publication is not atomic: `publish_out` is written after `promote`, and the swap itself is two renames with only error-driven rollback
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-00.2
- Evidence: OM-03.5 n2 (DIVERGENCE): "`write_to` runs AFTER `promote`, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree … the two no longer one generation" (`build/publish.rs:356`, `build/mod.rs:1018-1020`). `swap_in` is two renames with rollback "on error" only (`build/mod.rs:306-327`) and begins by clearing a stale `.publication-retired-PID` directory (`:306-312`); the module doc still says "promote atomically" (`build/mod.rs:12`) while the `promote` doc no longer claims it (`:409-410`) — OM-00.2 F5.
- Failure: a crash between the two renames leaves no `out` at all, with the only copy of the previous publication in a hidden retired directory that the next successful promote deletes first rather than restores — the site is down until a successful rebuild and the recovery copy is destroyed by routine operation. Separately, any failure or crash in `write_to` serves the site mixed generations: new data bundle, old published markdown (or the reverse).
- Confidence: high for the torn-generation behaviour (stated); the crash-window chain is inferred from the stated step order — no crash recovery is described anywhere
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — yes (DIVERGENCE) for the ordering; the crash window and stale-retired deletion are unmarked

### F-04 — ADR-OM-009 says *every* published closure carries a derivation certificate; the flag defaults to off and the build then writes and checks nothing
- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Evidence: the ADR's own title is "every-published-closure-carries-a-derivation-certificate" (`docs/adr/ADR-OM-009-…md:3-5`), but "`certificate_out` defaults to absent and the build writes and checks nothing then" (`build/mod.rs:730`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:401`) — marked **Open** at OM-03.8. The C3 file listing "names no certificate output" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`, OM-04.1 NC).
- Failure: any invocation without `--certificate-out` — a library caller of `run_with`, a future workflow — publishes a bundle whose closure carries no warrant, in direct contradiction of the accepted decision record; consumers cannot distinguish warranted from unwarranted generations from the artefacts alone. (visionGraph currently passes the flag, `publish.yml:165-171`, so the estate's main path is covered.)
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, OM-03.8; DRIFT on C3, OM-04.1)

### F-05 — `--vault all` silently degrades to a knowledge-only publish when the working folder is missing
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: `sibling_vault(Working)` loads the working vault "only if that directory exists" (`build/publish.rs:365-376`, `build/mod.rs:604-605`); contrast the layout's `root_of`, which *refuses* a role it lacks with `MissingVault` exit 2 (`profile.rs:561-576`, `main.rs:451-452`) — two opposite policies for the same condition. OM-05.5 n3: "ontomark silently accepts a knowledge-only build", which visionGraph works around with an external check that both `pages/` and `working/` exist (`publish.yml:189-195`).
- Failure: `working/` is accidentally moved, renamed or unmounted; `ontomark build --vault all` exits 0 and publishes a tree with no working content. Every consumer without visionGraph's directory check accepts a silently incomplete publication as a good generation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — stated inside an INVARIANT note (OM-05.5 n3), not flagged as a defect
