### F-06 — The compat flag `--no-redact-private-links` disables held-back publication too, not just link redaction
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-00.2
- Evidence: OM-02.3 n1 (DIVERGENCE): "the compat path never forces `misc/` pages private (`held_back` is only applied in the link path `ir/projection.rs:616`); a public `misc/` page stays public there, while `stage_redacted` skips it (`build/publish.rs:220`)" — kept as S3.4 for byte-parity, pinned by the conformance run that passes the flag (`conformance.rs:230,245`).
- Failure: an operator passes the flag to "match legacy bytes" — its name promises only that link redaction is off — and the bundle's Turtle, indexes and page API now contain classes and content-derived data from pages the publication rules deliberately hold back, while their markdown is simultaneously absent: a publication-boundary bypass and an internally inconsistent bundle.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (conditional on a non-default flag whose name understates its effect — that hazard is unmarked)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-02.3 n1; KEEP, S3.4)

### F-07 — `content_digest` does not cover the attribution roster, and the roster and dirty-check read the "ontology" directory through two different roots
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.2
- Evidence: OM-03.6 N1 (DIVERGENCE): the roster "shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to `content_digest`" (`build/mod.rs:680-685` vs `:963-983`); N3: dirtiness is hard-coded to `repo_root/ontology` (`:933`) while the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` (`attribution.rs:32`). OM-03.2 **Debt**: on the flat layout the vault root *is* `repo_root` (`profile.rs:514-518`), so the roster is looked up one directory above the repository.
- Failure: an attribution correction is rebuilt and republished under an unchanged `content_digest`, so any digest-keyed consumer (C3 documents digest-keyed caches, OM-06.5 L4) never invalidates and serves the wrong attribution indefinitely; independently, the `dirty` stamp can be computed over a directory that was not the one read, or the roster silently picked up from above the repository — a false provenance stamp on a published generation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.6 N1/N3; Debt, OM-03.2)

### F-08 — `retrieve`, `tree` and `edit` read only the first vault under `--vault all`, contradicting the documented meaning of `all`
- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.2
- Evidence: OM-05.1 n2 (DIVERGENCE): the `vault_kinds` doc says `all` means both and `validate`, `gate`, `conflicts`, `find` loop every vault (`migrate_cmd.rs:64-68`, `main.rs:650,686,745,901`), but "retrieve, tree and edit call `.next()` on the loaded vaults, so under `all` they read knowledge alone" (`main.rs:923-926,948-951,971-974`).
- Failure: `ontomark retrieve --vault all <page>` answers "not found" for a working-only page and the user re-creates it in knowledge (a duplicate); `edit --vault all` modifies the knowledge twin while the working copy silently diverges — a wrong-vault write performed under the flag's documented meaning.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-05.1 n2)

### F-09 — One malformed frontmatter file aborts the entire load, halting every verb and the whole publication pipeline
- Topics: OM-01.1, OM-01.3
- Evidence: OM-01.1 note2 (presented as INVARIANT, not as a defect): "the first malformed frontmatter or unreadable file aborts the whole load — `Page::parse` errors propagate" (`input/page.rs:494`). Contrast the census, which counts unterminated fences as `invalid_input` rather than aborting (`ir/projection.rs:123-126`).
- Failure: a single author's YAML typo anywhere in thousands of pages makes `build`, `validate`, `gate`, `conflicts`, `find` and `edit` all fail until that one file is fixed — no skip-and-report path exists for parse errors (only walk-level skips are reported). One bad file is a full-outage lever over publication.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — stated as an INVARIANT; the availability consequence is not flagged

### F-10 — Every page is read twice (census, then load), so a concurrent edit is classified on one text and published from another
- Topics: OM-03.3
- Evidence: OM-03.3 n2 (DIVERGENCE): "each page is still read twice, census then load, so a file edited between the two is classified on one text and parsed from another, a time-of-check gap" (`ir/projection.rs:118` vs `input/page.rs:493`). The digest hashes the loaded text (`build/mod.rs:963-983`), not the text the census gated.
- Failure: an editor saves mid-build: the census validates a text where `public` is a bool, the load parses an edited text where `public` is the string `"yes"` — the strict rule then makes the page silently *private* and it is omitted from the published bundle with no refusal and no report. The build is also non-reproducible against either snapshot.
- Confidence: high (the window is stated; no locking is mentioned anywhere)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.3 n2)

### F-11 — Three wikilink resolvers with different semantics: fragment and `.md` handling diverges, so fragment links publish wrong IRIs
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-01.5
- Evidence: `PrivateLinks::resolve` strips a `#fragment` and `.md` before lookup (`build/redact.rs:219,227`); `Corpus::build` "passes the target straight to `names.get`" (`ir/model.rs:338`), so "a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string" (OM-02.1 n2); a third case-sensitive resolver without a slug key exists in `shacl.rs:1201-1207`. OM-01.5 note3 adds the `target_slug` vs `slugify` fork (`input/slug.rs:65-75` vs `ir/model.rs:342`).
- Failure: any public wikilink with a fragment — `[[Term#Section]]` — fails the NameIndex lookup and publishes the nonexistent IRI `…/term-section` into Turtle, indexes and backlink keys; consumers resolve dead IRIs, and the shacl export disagrees with the corpus on the same link.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-02.1 n1/n2, OM-01.5 note3)

### F-12 — Hardcoded relation tables shadow the vocabulary: a vocabulary-added relation yields dangling Turtle references and mislabelled WebVOWL edges
- Topics: OM-04.2, OM-04.6, OM-04.5
- Evidence: the stub list is "a hardcoded array of 13 property names" (`turtle.rs:446-460`) beside the vocabulary-driven declarations (`turtle.rs:678-691`); "a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed" (OM-04.2). WebVOWL maps relations from a hardcoded `REL_MAP` (`webvowl.rs:30-42`) while Turtle takes IRIs from the vocabulary (`turtle.rs:656-673`) — "the two agree only while the vocabulary does" (OM-04.6). Four hand-kept orderings of the same twelve relations exist (`indexes.rs:30`, `page_api.rs:18`, `ir/model.rs:36`, `webvowl.rs:30`).
- Failure: the vocabulary legitimately adds one relation; the build succeeds, but `ontology.ttl` emits triples whose external targets receive no `skos:Concept` stub (undeclared object IRIs in the published ontology), WebVOWL drops or mislabels those edges, and the order-sensitive consumers miss the key — a config-evolution correctness break with no gate refusing it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.2 / OM-04.6 / OM-04.5)

### F-13 — The register contradicts itself on what C3 lists and on what `held_back` does
- Topics: OM-04.1, OM-03.7, OM-00.2, OM-03.4, OM-03.3
- Evidence: OM-04.1 says `api/pages/_domain-index.json` is "not in C3" (`mod.rs:811`), while OM-03.7 N2 says "C3 now lists it" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:123`) and OM-00.2 F10 records it as "RESOLVED in the C3 text" — two chapters give opposite conformance answers at the same verified commit. Separately, OM-03.4's staging node describes `held_back` as "`_misc` top level, `misc` at any depth" (`build/publish.rs:220`) while OM-03.3 and OM-00.1 H2 say it applies `UNPUBLISHED_DIRS` *at any depth* plus `misc` (`build/publish.rs:138-148`) — the same function is specified two ways.
- Failure: an engineer auditing C3 conformance, or fixing F-02, works from whichever chapter they read first and reaches the opposite conclusion; the actual population of held-back pages cannot be determined from this material, which also changes F-02's blast radius.
- Confidence: high (the contradictions are verbatim)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Two producers with an unfinished repin: ADR-2136 is partial, the `vault` source pin was never done, and a 0.6.0 image coexists with in-tree builds
- Topics: OM-06.5, OM-05.5, OM-00.3
- Evidence: ADR-2136 status is `proposed / partial / inactive` (`ADR-2136-…md:5-7`); its close-out "found the released `ontomark` 0.6.0 baked into the running image beside `vault`, but not Decision 1's repin of the `vault` source" (`:97-102`). VisionClaw builds its own crate in-tree (`ontology-publish.yml:167-175`) and still carries S1.1/S1.2/S1.5/S2.3 (OM-00.3 EXT). ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires that job "gated, so it keeps running".
- Failure: the two published ontologies (site via pinned ontomark; pod resources via unpinned in-tree vault) diverge in fixes and behaviour — a security fix lands in one producer and not the other, and nothing in the toolchain detects the drift because the repin that would have enforced it was never completed.
- Confidence: high on the stated status; the divergence consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (STATUS, OM-06.5; EXTERNAL, OM-00.3)

### F-15 — Machine-scope refusals are all-or-nothing, and knowledge→working links are published unresolved
- Topics: OM-06.2, OM-06.1
- Evidence: the directional twin rule refuses the *entire* build when one public working page twins a private knowledge page (`ir/projection.rs:725-731`, tested at `machine_scope.rs:386`) — exit 2, "nothing is written". And by design "edges from knowledge to working are not [resolved] … working links are one-directional in v1" (OM-06.2), so knowledge-record links aimed at working pages go out unresolved.
- Failure: one stray agent-authored public note in `working/` that happens to share a name with a private knowledge page (the `Web3` case that forced the amendment, `machine-bundle-brief.md:44-49`) blocks the whole nightly machine bundle — the downstream agentbox pipeline is starved until a human edits content. Independently, published machine page documents contain link targets that resolve to nothing.
- Confidence: high on the behaviour (tested); the pipeline-blocking consequence is judgment
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: partly — the twin rule and one-directional links are marked as designed and pinned by tests; the whole-bundle availability impact is unmarked

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether the pinned release v0.6.0 (visionGraph, `publish.yml:48-51`) contains the 8d654d1 SEV-1 fixes and the ADR-OM-009 certificate path.** The version-to-commit mapping is never shown; if 0.6.0 predates 8d654d1, the live site publisher still has the fail-open redactor and coercing public gate.
2. **Which hold-back rule link redaction itself uses.** The material never states whether `PrivateLinks::is_private` consults `is_unpublished` (narrow) or `held_back` (wide) for link targets — this determines whether F-02's leak extends to *links* to deep-held pages, not just prose masking.
3. **Concurrency and locking around the vault during builds.** No statement about file locks, git locks, or editor behaviour; F-10's real-world severity depends on how often mid-build edits occur.
4. **Crash-recovery semantics of the publish swap.** Whether anything at startup checks for or restores `.publication-retired-PID` / `.publication-PID` directories, and the filesystem's rename-pair behaviour on power loss — F-03's worst case hinges on this.
5. **The credential patterns themselves.** The 11 `CredentialKind` regexes (`input/secrets.rs:115-157`) are not shown, so the false-negative rate on real credential formats — and the safety of excluding `$`-prefixed env references — cannot be assessed.

## ontomark / premortem

# Post-mortem — publication-boundary failure in the ontology pipeline

**Incident class:** a data/custody/confidentiality failure at the publication boundary of the ontology corpus, twelve months after register stamp 07f4c1c. The register and topic files below already contain, in their own words, the unresolved faults this post-mortem ranks. Every claim carries the citation the material gives; anything I reasoned beyond the material is marked *inferred*. Where a diagram's ordering was not asserted, I have not assumed it.

---

### R-1 — The legacy VisionClaw producer kept running with the unfixed SEV-1 credential and redaction gates
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-05.5, OM-06.5
- Chain of events: (1) ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires VisionClaw's build job "gated, so it keeps running" (`ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment-one-producer-explicit-projections.md:11`). (2) The job builds the in-tree `vault` crate, not ontomark (`ontology-publish.yml:167-168`), and runs `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge` whose output "feeds pack-pod-resources.py" (`ontology-publish.yml:172-175, :247`). (3) That crate still ships S1.1 and S1.2 "unchanged until VC-60 retires" (OM-00.3 EXT): the credential scan is body-only while the render publishes frontmatter (inherited at `vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160, :196`), and the prose redactor fails open on a regex compile error (inherited at `vault/src/projection.rs:230`). (4) Twelve months on, ADR-2136's close-out confirms the repin never happened: "the released `ontomark` 0.6.0 baked into the running image beside `vault`, but not Decision 1's repin of the `vault` source" (`ADR-2136…md:97-102`). (5) A token pasted into a frontmatter value of a public knowledge page — or any redactor regex failure — publishes to the Pod resources, unscanned and unredacted.
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT box; S1.1/S1.2 provenance rows; OM-05.5 VC subgraph (`ontology-publish.yml:167-175, :247`); OM-06.5 STATUS (ADR-2136 `proposed/partial/inactive`, `ADR-2136…md:5-7, :97-102`); S1.5 and S2.3 also still live in that binary.
- Warning signs visible today: the register itself states the faults are shipped unchanged and the retirement is gated; two producers with duplicated rule sets (see F-12) guarantee drift between the fixed and unfixed copies.
- Confidence: high — the faults, the live job, and the unfinished repin are all stated; the triggering edit is inferred but is exactly the shape S1.2 was written from.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The publish/ markdown tree is link-redacted only; identity masking never runs on it
- Topics: OM-02.4, OM-03.4
- Chain of events: (1) Redaction has two steps: PrivateLinks rewrites wikilinks and frontmatter links structurally *before* `Corpus::build`; the Redactor masks identity strings (private ids, names, IRI projections, `ir/projection.rs:558-583`) *after* it (OM-02.4). (2) `stage_redacted` is handed only the links (`build/mod.rs:633`, `build/publish.rs:200`), and `render` writes "frontmatter re-rendered, links redacted, **body verbatim**" (`build/publish.rs:169-175`). (3) The lexical mask *is* applied to record fields and re-applied to the markdown mirror (`build/mod.rs:886-891`) — but the mirror is flag-gated (`build/mod.rs:842, :917`) while publish/ is "the markdown that is published" (`build/publish.rs:1`). (4) With `--publish-out quartz/content` (visionGraph, `publish.yml:165-171`), the client-facing site is the publish tree. (5) A private page's name appearing in a public page's plain prose — not as a wikilink — is masked in the data API and the mirror but ships verbatim on the public site.
- Evidence: `build/publish.rs:169-175` and the `stage_redacted(vaults, links)` signature vs `ir/projection.rs:558-583` and `build/mod.rs:886-891`; the two surfaces' differing treatment is stated, not conjectured.
- Warning signs visible today: the mirror note calls its second redaction pass "a second body-cleaning pass whose only reason is the Tally" (OM-02.4 note over PL) — no equivalent pass exists for publish/; nothing in the material shows the Redactor reaching `render`.
- Confidence: medium — the differential treatment is stated; that masking was *intended* to cover publish/ is inferred. Verify by checking whether `render` receives the `ProseRedactor`.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — `publish_out` is swapped *after* the bundle is promoted: a failed write leaves a bundle whose marker hashes files that were never published
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Chain of events: (1) With `publish_out`, publish files are recorded as external artefacts and hashed into the root marker (S2.5 fix, `build/mod.rs:773-778, :1008-1016`; test `s2_5b…register_sev2.rs:286`). (2) `run_with` then promotes the bundle via `swap_in` (`build/mod.rs:1018`, `:304-327`) and only afterwards calls `publish::write_to`, which stages beside the target and swaps (`build/publish.rs:341-356`, `build/mod.rs:1019-1021`). (3) `write_to` fails (permissions, disk, path). (4) The CLI maps the leftover anyhow error to exit 1 (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:871`) — but the bundle at `out` is already the new generation. (5) Result, exactly as the register states: "a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation" (OM-03.5 N2) — and the new bundle's root marker asserts sha256s of publish files that are not the ones on disk.
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N2 (`build/publish.rs:356`, `build/mod.rs:1018-1020`); N3 (external artefacts stamped, `build/mod.rs:361-363`); the promote ordering is asserted in the OM-03.1 sequence.
- Warning signs visible today: the register carries this as a DIVERGENCE today; every visionGraph deploy uses `--publish-out`, so any transient failure at that step triggers it.
- Confidence: high — the mechanics and ordering are fully stated.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — `--vault all` silently publishes knowledge-only when the working folder is missing
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: `sibling_vault(Working)` loads the working root "only if that directory exists" (`build/mod.rs:604-605`, `build/publish.rs:365-376`) and returns None silently; visionGraph had to add its own guard — "treats the staging tree as wrong unless both `pages/` and `working/` exist, because ontomark silently accepts a knowledge-only build" (`publish.yml:189-195`).
- Failure: a renamed or unmounted working directory at build time yields exit 0, a green marker, and a published site missing every public working page. Only visionGraph's external check catches it; any other consumer ships an incomplete corpus.
- Confidence: high (stated); incident path inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a consumer invariant, not flagged as a defect)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Every page is read twice (census, then load); classification and payload can come from different bytes
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-02.5
- Evidence: "each page is still read twice, census then load, so a file edited between the two is classified on one text and parsed from another, a time-of-check gap the digest no longer widens" (`ir/projection.rs:118` vs `input/page.rs:493`, OM-03.3 N2).
- Failure: an edit landing between the two reads makes `api/census.json` (`build/mod.rs:812`) describe read #1 while every artefact describes read #2; a `public: true`→`public: "true"` edit in the window either silently drops the page to private or aborts after census passed — published counts contradict the shipped corpus.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium on the concrete consequence.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.3 N2)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — A library caller can stage the same vault under both publish trees
- Topics: OM-03.2
- Evidence: "sibling_vault never compares its kind with vault.kind, so a library caller with vault_root = working plus with_working_publish stages the same vault twice" (`build/publish.rs:365-376`, OM-03.2 N3); the CLI cannot reach it (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:795-803`).
- Failure: working content published under both `publish/tree/pages` and `publish/tree/working` — working notes labelled as knowledge on the public site, pages double-counted in the digest.
- Confidence: high (stated); reachability library-only.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.2 N3)
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — The compat projection publishes held-back pages into every machine artefact
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-03.3, OM-00.1, OM-00.5
- Evidence: "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private … a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it" (`ir/projection.rs:616` vs `build/publish.rs:220`, OM-02.3 n1); the hold-back rule exists in two shapes — `is_unpublished` (top-level `_misc` only, `input/page.rs:47, :641`) vs `held_back` (`_misc` and `misc` at any depth, `build/publish.rs:134, :138-148`) — and the census and `excluded_identities_of` use the narrow one (`ir/projection.rs:114, :231`); kept as S3.4 (OM-00.1 H4, OM-00.5 OPEN2); the conformance test normalizes the flag (`conformance.rs:245`).
- Failure: a build with `--no-redact-private-links` (or profile `redact_private_links: false`) — e.g. someone replicating the conformance invocation — publishes `misc/` and nested `_misc` content into the search index, `api/pages`, TTL, OKF and graph tiers (all emitters read the projected corpus, OM-04.1) while publish/ omits it: an inclusion invisible to any publish-tree check.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (KEEP / Open — OM-00.1 H4 and the "Open" misc question; OM-00.5 OPEN2)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Three wikilink resolvers disagree on fragments, `.md` suffixes and dashed slugs
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-01.5, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `PrivateLinks::resolve` strips `#fragment` and `.md` (`build/redact.rs:219, :227`); `Corpus::build` resolve does not — "a fragment link … is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string" (`ir/model.rs:338`, OM-02.1 n2); curated links take `target_slug` verbatim, keeping leading/doubled dashes, while the resolve fallback trims them (`input/slug.rs:65-75` vs `ir/model.rs:342`, OM-01.5 note3).
- Failure: in the compat path a `[[Page#section]]` link becomes a dangling `ns/page-section` IRI in the published Turtle (every target kept, `turtle.rs:337-342`), silently dropped by NGG1 and WebVOWL (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298, :309-310`, `webvowl.rs:66,118`); dashed slugs yield two identity derivations for one page, so backlink keys and `by_class_slug` can miss or conflate.
- Confidence: high on the divergence; medium on the scenario.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-02.1 n1–n3, OM-01.5 note3)
- Severity: medium

### F-06 — The SHACL resolver is a third, divergent name matcher
- Topics: OM-00.1, OM-02.1
- Evidence: `Resolver::page` has "no slug key, case-sensitive id then lower-cased title and alias" (`shacl.rs:1201-1207`), sharing no NameIndex with the other two resolvers (OM-02.1 n1).
- Failure: SHACL export validates a different target mapping than the build publishes; shape results pass or fail spuriously against the real corpus.
- Confidence: high on the divergence.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-00.1 L3)
- Severity: low

### F-07 — Turtle's hardcoded 13-relation stub list silently dangles vocabulary-added relations
- Topics: OM-04.2, OM-00.1
- Evidence: "Hardcoded 13-name object-property list: undeclared ngm targets become skos:Concept stubs" (`turtle.rs:446-483`); "a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed" (`turtle.rs:446-460` vs `declare_object_properties` `turtle.rs:678-691`).
- Failure: a fourteenth relation added to `vocabulary.yaml` publishes dangling object IRIs with no refusal; explorer tiers drop the edges; the TTL accumulates unresolvable references.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.2)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — WebVOWL's hardcoded relation map diverges from the vocabulary-driven Turtle IRIs
- Topics: OM-04.6, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `REL_MAP` maps `related-to`→`skos:related` etc. from a hardcoded table (`webvowl.rs:30-42`) while "Turtle takes each relation IRI from the vocabulary" (`turtle.rs:656-673`); "the two agree only while the vocabulary does."
- Failure: a renamed or added relation makes `ontology.json` and `ontology.ttl` — both published as C3 artefacts — disagree about the same property.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.6)
- Severity: low

### F-09 — Attribution changes the published bytes but not `content_digest`, and is read from a second root
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.2
- Evidence: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest" (`build/mod.rs:680-685` vs `:963-983`, OM-03.6 N1); dirtiness reads `repo_root/ontology` (`build/mod.rs:933`) while the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` (`build/attribution.rs:32`, N3); on a flat layout the vault root *is* the repo root (`profile.rs:514-518, :549-550`), so the roster is looked up one directory above the repository (OM-03.2 Debt).
- Failure: an `attribution.yaml` edit republishes different Turtle/page-API bytes under an identical `content_digest`, so digest-keyed consumer caches serve stale attribution indefinitely; on flat vaults the roster path is outside the repo and absent from `read_paths`, so changes there neither dirty-flag nor digest.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, OM-03.2; DIVERGENCE, OM-03.6 N1/N3)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — `retrieve`, `tree` and `edit` read only the first vault under `--vault all`
- Topics: OM-05.1
- Evidence: "all means BOTH for validate, gate, conflicts, find and build … retrieve, tree and edit call `.next()` on the loaded vaults, so under all they read knowledge alone" (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:923-926, :948-951, :971-974` vs `migrate_cmd.rs:64-68`, OM-05.1 n2).
- Failure: `edit --vault all` silently applies a guarded write to knowledge only, or `retrieve` returns the knowledge copy while working is newer — a decision made on stale data with no warning.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-05.1 n2)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — `repair` defaults to `--vault all` while every other verb defaults to knowledge
- Topics: OM-05.1
- Evidence: "repair defaults to `all` while validate, gate, conflicts, build and the query verbs default to knowledge, so the same word means a different scope per verb" (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:152, :169, :182, :382`); repair fences/bodies are write operations (`main.rs:1128-1214`, `check/repair.rs:390`).
- Failure: an operator habituated to the knowledge default runs `ontomark repair bodies` without `--check` and rewrites working-vault note bodies they never intended to touch.
- Confidence: high on the defaults; the operator error is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (stated, unlabeled)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — The furniture-folder list is written twice across the two producers
- Topics: OM-00.1
- Evidence: `EXCLUDED_DIRS = [templates, bases, assets, journals]` (`input/page.rs:58`) and VisionClaw's `FURNITURE_DIRS` "with the same four names" (`../project/crates/vault-core/src/page.rs:50`), flagged as Debt since ADR-OM-007.
- Failure: a new furniture folder added to ontomark's profile only leaves the still-running VisionClaw pipeline treating it as pages — internal templates published to Pod resources.
- Confidence: high on the duplication; scenario inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, OM-00.1)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The credential scanner's classes exclude `$` and cover only 11 kinds
- Topics: OM-01.4
- Evidence: "no credential character class contains a dollar sign, so env references are not matches" (`input/secrets.rs:106-113`); the pattern set is 11 `CredentialKind` variants (`input/secrets.rs:115-157`), scanned at `check/validate.rs:305, :323` and `build/publish.rs:228, :240`.
- Failure: a credential containing `$`, or any format outside the 11 kinds, on a public page passes both validate and staging and ships in publish/, the frontmatter render and the mirror.
- Confidence: high on the exclusion (stated deliberate); exploitability inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as an invariant)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — `Report.unsatisfiable` and `Census.rejected` are constants published as signals
- Topics: OM-03.7, OM-02.5, OM-00.2
- Evidence: `Report.unsatisfiable` "is always empty on success, because a non-empty list already refused the build" (`build/mod.rs:157-161, :1035`); `Census.rejected` is the constant 0 and the `conflicting_` counter is dead (`ir/projection.rs:61-62, :148, :172`).
- Failure: monitoring or JSON consumers keying on either field get a constant — the signal can never fire, and any alerting built on it is vacuous (compounded by F-02 for the counts that do vary).
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (OM-00.2 F4 "constant by design"; DIVERGENCE, OM-02.5 n4)
- Severity: low

### F-15 — The derivation certificate is opt-in, so an uncertified closure can publish by default
- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Evidence: "`certificate_out` defaults to absent and the build writes and checks nothing then" (`build/mod.rs:730`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:401`, OM-03.8 Open); ADR-OM-009 decides "every published closure carries a derivation certificate" (`ADR-OM-009…md:14`); the C3 artefact listing names no certificate output (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`, OM-04.1 NC).
- Failure: a new or edited pipeline drops `--certificate-out` — nothing refuses, no parity check runs, and a closure publishes with no warrant despite the ADR decision; today only visionGraph's habit passes the flag (`publish.yml:165-171`).
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, OM-03.8; Drift, OM-04.1 NC)
- Severity: medium

## sidestr-rs / critical

Findings ranked by the damage they would do in production. Citations are the path:line references the material itself gives.

### F-01 — The live chains are produced by a patched upstream JS engine that the Rust parity ladder never validates
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.4
- Evidence: ADR-0001:27 makes the pinned reference the compatibility contract, and CI runs the Rust workspace against references checked out at fixed revisions (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`, `:99`, `:207`) with no patch step mentioned. But the live producer is "the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" plus `patches/peg-script.patch` and a baked upstream PR #32 (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`, `:150-153`, `:162-165`). The producer therefore differs from the oracle by at least one local patch and one upstream change whose presence in the CI pins is unstated.
- Failure: every Rust component (mirror, faucet, any future native producer or validator) is proven compatible with the *unpatched* reference, not with the binary actually producing `sidestr:dreamlab` and `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4`. A patch-induced behavioural difference makes Rust components reject valid blocks or accept invalid ones. The estate's central evidence claim — parity to the reference — is about a program that is not the one running, and one JS engine is the sole producer for both chains.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the patch and pins are stated; whether CI applies the patches is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the SR-01.4 Open covers "native producer not deployed", not the patch/parity gap)

### F-02 — Published Hitch 0.1 lets a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf; 0.2 strands those channels
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Evidence: "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`); version-1 snapshots are refused outright (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`) and "such a channel must be closed with 0.1"; the business line repeats "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version."
- Failure: any counterparty still running published 0.1 can broadcast an old commitment and immediately sweep its own revocation output, defeating the penalty path and taking the delayed funds. An operator inheriting 0.1 channels must keep the vulnerable version in service to close them, because 0.2 refuses the snapshots. There is no migration path, only a shutdown path through known-bad code.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — disclosed as history and as an "Invariant", with no risk marker

### F-03 — No host exists for Hitch; every money-bearing reaction depends on an unbuilt host honouring an unenforceable contract
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: the kernel performs no IO; the host must supply wallet funding, kind-23600 relay I/O, atomic snapshot storage with restore-on-failed-save (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`, `:49`, `:105`), chain lookups for `on_spend` (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`), broadcasting and republishing claims after six blocks (`chain.rs:578`), persistence of ForwardRecords (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:108`), and the full retry/timeout scheduler (`mod.rs:70`, `:121`; `route.rs:13`). "No estate host supplies the complete service boundary" and ADR-2111 is proposed, unimplemented, inactive (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-...md:95`; `../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:103`).
- Failure: penalties, HTLC claims and sweeps are time-boxed (the tick goes to chain at `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` before expiry, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`). A missing, slow, or non-atomic host means missed expiry windows and lost channel funds; a lost snapshot — which contains the signing key (`snapshot.rs:22-24`) — means the channel can never be reacted to at all. The library cannot enforce any of this; a partially built host is the realistic failure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-04.5)

### F-04 — Peg authorisation on the live chains is single-signer and exists only as a local patch
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: "`patches/peg-script.patch` keeps single-signer peg announcements" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`, `:162-165`) — the peg rule for the running chains is n-of-one and lives in a locally maintained diff against the pinned engine.
- Failure: one key — or one compromised host holding it — can authorise peg movements on the live chains. The rule is invisible to upstream review and to the Rust parity ladder (F-01), so nothing but the patch enforces it. Carried into a value-bearing chain, this is a direct custody single point; today it is latent only because the chains are test-only.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — "single-signer" is stated; the key's custody and the patch's exact semantics are not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Governing decisions are still proposed while chains are already sealed and produced
- Topics: SR-01.3, SR-01.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: ADR-2012 ("sidestr settlement is ecosystem canon") is recorded as proposed with activation inactive (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-...md:115`, `:130`), yet it is listed under `governing:` in the SR-01, SR-02 and SR-03 front matter. ADR-2111 is proposed and unimplemented (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-...md:95`); adoption is an open board row (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:103`, `:116`). Meanwhile two chains are sealed and produced (`chain.json:5`; `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:11`).
- Failure: production runs ahead of governance. An operator inherits live artefacts whose canon, activation and adoption decisions can still be rewritten or withdrawn; what "level 1" sealing commits the estate to is not yet fixed by any accepted decision, so responsibility for the sealed chains' behaviour is legally and operationally unanchored.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — statuses are disclosed but the governing-while-proposed contradiction carries no marker

### F-06 — "Sidechains are the sole value instrument", but both live sidechains are test-only experiments
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-03.1
- Evidence: ADR-2096 is cited as the governing sole-value-instrument policy (`../project/agentbox/docs/adr/ADR-2096-...md:116`, and in SR-03's front matter); the business lines state `sidestr:dreamlab` "is still a test-only chain" and `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` is a BLAKE2b testnet4 research chain (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:11`; `chain.json:5`).
- Failure: the estate's value policy has no value-bearing instantiation. Taking production responsibility means either putting production load on testnet4 experiment chains (one of which depends on a BLAKE2b parent variant) or having nothing to operate. The policy statement and the estate state cannot both be true of a production system.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (adjacent Open rows exist; the policy-vs-reality gap itself is unmarked)

### F-07 — Market refunds pay "half a sat each", which the integer-sat ledger cannot represent
- Topics: SR-02.3, SR-02.2
- Evidence: the refund transition is "redeem paired outcomes at half a sat each" (SR-02.3 state diagram, anchored at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:138`); the surrounding crate is integer-valued — "integer constant-product boundary" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/pool.rs:213`) and checked integer tallies in assets (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:160`).
- Failure: redeeming a YES+NO pair implies 0.5-sat payouts. With whole-sat accounting these are either unrepresentable (outputs below the unit), rounded (systematic value loss on odd collateral), or the pair pays out less than was locked. Any of these is a correctness or value-loss defect the moment markets activate.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the diagram label is stated; the arithmetic conflict is reasoned from it)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — sidestr-evm deliberately diverges from the reference's commit semantics, carving an exception out of the compatibility contract
- Topics: SR-03.2, SR-01.2
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), while ADR-0001:27 makes the reference the contract and says Rust-only greenness proves nothing.
- Failure: the reference's prepare-time commit is observable behaviour; a Rust follower or future producer that discards staged state where the reference retains it can diverge on refused-block, reorg, or producer-sequencing paths — exactly the paths fixtures may under-cover. The "contract" now has an exception recorded only in a crate doc comment, not in an ADR.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — called "deliberate" in prose, with no Tension/Drift marker and no decision record

### F-09 — Asset conservation is only as strong as the mint policies other rules supply
- Topics: SR-02.2, SR-02.1
- Evidence: the asset rule "delegates exceptional minting to a policy supplied by the pool and market views" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:160`; `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:34`); the claim "one conservation rule stays authoritative" holds exactly when every composing rule's policy is correct, and the compositor installs rules in a fixed order that future rules join (`overlays.rs:65`).
- Failure: any overlay later added to the pipeline that supplies a permissive or buggy mint policy can authorise inflation *through* the "authoritative" conservation rule. The invariant is compositional and, on the material shown, only exercised against the two in-tree policies.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Binary markets hinge on an ungoverned resolver
- Topics: SR-02.3
- Evidence: resolution advances on "resolver proof within expiry plus grace" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:202`, `:260`); nothing in the material describes who holds the resolver key, its rotation, dispute handling, or what the proof attests.
- Failure: the resolver single-handedly allocates all locked collateral between YES and NO holders. A compromised, coerced or lost resolver key either mis-resolves (theft by outcome choice) or forces every market into refund after expiry plus grace (liveness loss for all participants).
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the resolver's authority is stated; the absence of governance is an absence of statement)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The producer's persistence fail-stop exists only if upstream PR #32 stays baked into the deployment pin
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: "The baked spec pin now includes upstream PR #32, so an append or index-write failure latches `SIDING_PERSISTENCE_FAILED` and the producer exits before advertising or extending an unsaved tip" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`).
- Failure: a safety property — never advertise an unsaved tip — is contingent on deployment configuration. Re-baking the image at a different revision, or upstream refactoring the PR away, silently removes the latch and reintroduces tip-loss-on-crash. Nothing at build time fails to reveal the regression.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Sealed, rule-less chain documents make every SR-02/SR-03 capability unactivatable; the upgrade path is sealing another chain
- Topics: SR-02.4, SR-03.4, SR-01.4
- Evidence: dreamlab's document "reaches `containmentDigest` without a `rules` field" and is sealed at level 1 (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28`, `:5`); the second chain was sealed to accommodate a BLAKE2b parent (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:11`); assets/pools/markets and EVM are Open-inactive (SR-02.4, SR-03.4).
- Failure: no rule change can ever reach an existing chain. Each activation — markets, EVM, a native producer's semantics — requires sealing a new chain and migrating users and any held value; the estate already runs two sealed chains and will accumulate them, with no described mechanism for moving value between sealed chains.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — sealing semantics beyond the document fields are not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-02.4 and SR-03.4) — but only for inactivity; the chain-proliferation consequence is unmarked

### F-13 — The mirror and faucet serve only dreamlab; dreamlab-txbt4 shows a producer and nothing else
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: the live-boundary diagram draws `M --> D` and `F --> D` only (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111` area); `D2` (`sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4`) receives only the producer edge.
- Failure: if the diagram reflects reality, the second chain has no mirror (no independent following of its data) and no faucet. A producer-only chain is one crash away from having no readable state at all — the one-instance-vs-two gap applied to chains.
- Confidence: low (inferred from the diagram's drawn edges; the material may simply omit them)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Marked Drift: ADR-0001 says four reference repositories; CI pins six, and one pin has already moved
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: ADR-0001:27 says CI pins "all four reference repositories" versus `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14` pinning six (spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef, teller); Reef was repinned `648487a` → `2bd3cb8` (`ci.yml:25`).
- Failure: the decision record that defines the compatibility contract misdescribes the contract, so readers audit the wrong set. A moving oracle also makes "compatible" time-indexed: anything validated against the old Reef pin has no current parity claim.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, SR-01.2)

### F-15 — Marked Tension: an off-curve point is silently dropped where Hitch replies with a reject
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04.1
- Evidence: `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63` records the departure as deliberate and tested; the reference's proof-of-possession check (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:773`, `:785`) is where Hitch rejects.
- Failure: a Rust peer and a Hitch JS peer disagree on protocol progress for the same malformed input: the reference side's reject path fires, the Rust side just waits. In a mixed deployment — and the reference engine is what actually runs in this estate — a malformed announcement stalls a channel until host timeouts, which do not exist yet (F-03).
- Confidence: high (the departure is stated; the stall consequence is inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, SR-04.5)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether CI applies `patches/peg-script.patch` and upstream PR #32 when checking out the references — this decides how large F-01 and F-04 actually are.
2. The unit and rounding rules for market refunds — whether a sub-satoshi unit or a minimum even collateral exists — needed to close F-07.
3. Any Hitch host design beyond the proposed ADR-2111, and the intended custody arrangements for snapshot storage (which holds channel signing keys) — needed to size F-03.
4. Whether `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` has its own mirror, faucet, or redundancy — needed to confirm or dismiss F-13.
5. The reserve's cross-attestation semantics: whether any mechanism binds a credit to a single use across attestations at different tips, and who holds the attestation signing key — the duplicate refusal shown is intra-attestation only, and nothing consumer-side exists yet.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: sidestr estate incident (client loss)

Scope note: every finding below is checkable against the cited path:line in the distillation material. Where a failure chain goes beyond what the material states, it is marked inferred.

---

### R-1 — A Hitch host was deployed without the full service boundary, and time-critical duties were never executed
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04.2, SR-04.3, SR-04.4
- Chain of events: the estate builds its first Hitch host — wallet funding, kind-23600 relay I/O, snapshot storage, chain lookups, broadcasting — but implements the I/O surface thinly and leaves the scheduler (retries, proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts) unimplemented or ticked only on incoming traffic. A channel opens and carries a client's funds. A close lands on-chain, or an HTLC nears expiry: the kernel only acts when ticked — the machine's tick takes a claimable HTLC to the chain `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` blocks before expiry, and `on_spend`/`after_close` only run when the host reports the spend. No tick, no report: the to_local delay or HTLC expiry passes, the counterparty sweeps, the client's balance is gone. Alternatively the host skips persisting the ForwardRecord/snapshot at the right moment and a revealed preimage is lost, making the on-chain claim impossible.
- Evidence: the kernel performs no IO and the host owes wallet funding, relay I/O, atomic snapshot storage, chain watches, broadcasting and the scheduler (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:70`, `:105`); the router expects the host to retry retryable actions, Hitch itself tries twenty times (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`); tick-driven HTLC claims (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`); close handling and republication are host-fed (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`, `:364`, `:578`); atomic persistence before sending anything returned (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`); 0.2.0 "widened" the boundary (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`).
- Warning signs visible today: the material's own Open says no estate host supplies the complete service boundary; ADR-2111 is proposed and unimplemented (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-...md:95`); adoption is an open board row (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:103`, `:116`).
- Confidence: high that the duty gap exists and is the dominant risk; the concrete loss sequence is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Source capability was treated as live enforcement: client value recorded where no chain rule exists
- Topics: SR-01.3, SR-01.4, SR-02.4, SR-03.4, SR-05.3
- Chain of events: over twelve months the eight published crates and oracle-green CI make the estate's capability surface look operational. A client is onboarded against `sidestr:dreamlab` (or `dreamlab-txbt4`) with issued assets, pool positions, market outcome tokens or bridged reserve units tracked by an agent's bookkeeping. The sealed chain document reaches `containmentDigest` with no `rules` field, so none of these are chain-enforced. A bug or a counterparty moves the underlying UTXOs; the client's recorded balance has no consensus recourse, and the settlement the client believed final does not exist on any rule the chain validates. The same boundary failure occurs operationally if the Rust engine is described or trusted as the running producer when the live producer is still the patched upstream JavaScript engine.
- Evidence: "the live estate chain has no rules field and therefore offers none of them" (SR-02, For the business; `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28`); EVM unpublished with no `evm` rule (SR-03.4 Open); reserve unfunded with no live bridge rule (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0003-...md:32`); activation cannot be promoted by source evidence (SR-01.3 invariant; `docs/adr/ADR-2012-...md:115`, `:130`); "native producer not deployed" and the Open to deploy and prove one first (SR-01.4); publication, source completeness and deployment tracked as separate facts (SR-01, For the business).
- Warning signs visible today: every business section repeats "source capabilities only"; ADR-2012 remains proposed while two chains are already sealed and produced.
- Confidence: high that the boundary is real and load-bearing; the client-onboarding scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A channel opened under Hitch 0.1 was exploited or stranded through the broken single-party revocation key
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Chain of events: an early host trial opens channels under 0.1. The 0.1 construction "let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf" — the penalty path is preemptable by the cheater. A counterparty broadcasts an old commitment and sweeps its own revocation output before the client's penalty can land; the client loses the balance owed in the latest state. Alternatively, the estate upgrades to 0.2 mid-channel: 0.2 refuses the version-1 snapshot, so the channel can only be closed with 0.1; if the 0.1 watcher has been decommissioned, a unilateral close goes unseen until the delays expire and the counterparty sweeps.
- Evidence: the flaw and its fix (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:52`, `:45`); the 0.2 two-party key (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:740`, `:11`); the 0.2 oracle explicitly tests that an owner's self-spend of its revocation leaf is refused (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/tests/oracle.rs:465`); only version-2 snapshots restore and a 0.1 channel "must be closed with 0.1" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`; SR-04, For the business).
- Warning signs visible today: a breaking release, un-restorable prior snapshots, no stated migration tooling, and the business-facing warning that earlier-version channels need earlier-version closure.
- Confidence: medium — the flaw is documented; the exploitation requires a 0.1-era channel to have existed, which is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Off-curve points are silently dropped where Hitch replies with a reject
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: the port refuses a point off the curve at deserialisation and drops it without a `reject`, where Hitch answers with a reject at the proof-of-possession check — recorded as deliberate (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`).
- Failure: a peer sending a malformed point gets no protocol response; its state machine waits, the update/revocation handshake stalls, and an in-flight HTLC times out. The silent drop is indistinguishable from relay loss, so the host cannot diagnose it.
- Confidence: high that the divergence exists (stated); the stall scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — ADR-0001 says four reference repositories; CI pins six
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: ADR-0001 claims CI pins "all four reference repositories" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27`) while the workflow pins spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`).
- Failure: an engineer implementing to the ADR omits the Reef or teller oracles; a compatibility break in `sidestr-wallet`'s BIP 21 handling or the reserve JCS bytes ships undetected.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-03 — EVM commits only on applied blocks where the reference commits at prepare
- Topics: SR-03.2
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`); staged state is discarded on refusal or sequencing-only runs (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:62`).
- Failure: if the reference implementation ever runs beside the Rust node on an activated chain, a refused or sequencing-only block leaves one side with committed state and the other with none; the next state root diverges and the chain splits.
- Confidence: medium (departure stated; split inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Producer persistence failure: pre-patch unsaved-tip advertisement, and today a latched single-producer halt
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: the baked pin includes upstream PR #32 so an append or index-write failure latches `SIDING_PERSISTENCE_FAILED` and the producer exits "before advertising or extending an unsaved tip" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`) — implying the unpatched behaviour advertised unsaved tips. One supervisor runs producer, mirror and faucet (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`).
- Failure: under the earlier pin, a write failure advertised a tip that was never saved; on restart the chain truncated and settlements against the vanished tip were unrecoverable. Today, the latch halts the sole producer while the mirror keeps serving the stale tip; clients read it as current and accept payments that never confirm.
- Confidence: medium; the pre-patch behaviour and mirror staleness are inferred from the wording.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — The peg-script patch keeps single-signer peg announcements
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: `patches/peg-script.patch` "keeps single-signer peg announcements" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:162-165`); no rationale or key-management statement accompanies it.
- Failure: peg authorisation rests on one key; its compromise or misuse moves pegged value unilaterally. The patch also means the deployed producer deviates from the validated reference set.
- Confidence: low (effect stated; threat inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Exact-byte eviction leaves same-txid and re-admission semantics undefined
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: eviction removes the exact serialised transaction so a witness-repaired same-txid form is not removed (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:759`; `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/tests/eviction.rs:16`); nothing states that a transaction evicted only because of in-block rule context is re-admitted later, nor how two serialisations of one txid coexist in the mempool.
- Failure: a transaction valid in the next block but invalid in this block context is dropped permanently; the client's funds appear stuck until manual resubmission, or two same-txid forms oscillate in the mempool.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Set-aside states bind HTLCs until revocation or funding spend
- Topics: SR-04.2
- Evidence: on collision the lower key's update stands and the other signed state is kept as an alternative "whose HTLCs stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent by something else" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:20`; `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:561`); the router's tick responds with a protective force close when only a set-aside state binds (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:570`, `:578`).
- Failure: a peer that provokes collisions and then never revokes that state number pins the HTLCs indefinitely; the only exits are the peer's revocation or the router's force close, which converts the grief into on-chain cost and channel death for the client.
- Confidence: medium (binding stated; withholding adversary inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Channel snapshots contain complete key material with only an advisory protection note
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the snapshot contains the signing key, revocation basepoint, per-state secrets and preimages (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`); "snapshot storage must be protected as wallet key material" is a documentation-level requirement (`:24`).
- Failure: a host persisting snapshots through ordinary service storage, backups or logs leaks total channel control; whoever holds the snapshot can spend every commitment, penalty paths included.
- Confidence: medium (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Crash window between sending the downstream add and persisting the ForwardRecord
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the router records a forward before the downstream add and the host persists the record the `Forward` action carries (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:108`, `:173`); the host must persist the full recovery boundary atomically "before sending anything the call returned" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`); preimages live only in the snapshot (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`).
- Failure: the host crashes after the add is on the wire but before the record and snapshot land; on restart the forward and any revealed preimage are unknown, the upstream HTLC cannot be settled, and the on-chain claim needing the preimage cannot be built — the value goes to timeout or the counterparty.
- Confidence: medium (duties stated; crash window inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Refund path pays "half a sat each"
- Topics: SR-02.3
- Evidence: the refunding state redeems paired outcomes "at half a sat each" (SR-02.3 diagram, note `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:138`).
- Failure: sub-satoshi amounts are unrepresentable on the UTXO ledger; unless collateral per pair is constrained to even values (unstated), refund outputs are dust-truncated or stranded and client collateral is lost in refund scenarios.
- Confidence: low (wording only; no enforcing code cited).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — JCS canonicalisation spread across three implementations
- Topics: SR-05.2, SR-01.2
- Evidence: canonical bytes are held to `serde_jcs` and to the teller's `jcs` by a cross-check suite (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:60`; teller pinned as JCS oracle at `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:30`); RFC 8785 leaves number- and escaping-format edges where implementations diverge.
- Failure: signer and verifier canonicalise differently; the digest mismatches and signed attestations are voided — or two accepted canonical forms let the same credit statement back two mints.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Credit replay keys are delegated per adapter
- Topics: SR-05.1
- Evidence: each credit is identified by an "origin-appropriate replay key" chosen by the adapter (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:436`; `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-bridge-liquid/src/lib.rs:17`), with no stated strength requirement.
- Failure: a sibling adapter picks a weaker key; the same origin deposit credits twice across two attestations (or is wrongly deduped), and a future bridge rule mints twice against one reserve credit.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Fixed 1 sat = 1 gwei mapping with unstated rounding
- Topics: SR-03.1
- Evidence: "One sat maps to one gwei inside the overlay" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:5`); withdrawals are valid only when the same block's coinbase pays the required sats (SR-03.1 invariant).
- Failure: a withdrawal whose gwei amount is not an integral number of sats rounds somewhere unstated; an off-by-one between the EVM root's obligation and the coinbase's satoshis breaks the block invariant or shaves value.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Overlay ordering is consensus but enforced only by compositor convention
- Topics: SR-02.1
- Evidence: the invariant says overlay order "cannot be rearranged as an implementation detail" (SR-02.1), yet the order is realised by the compositor installing assets before pool before markets (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:1`, `:65`); no chain-document attestation of the composition is stated.
- Failure: a host or alternate client inserting a new rule (e.g., the future bridge) in a different position validates a different consensus — mints authorised before conservation is checked, shares or outcome tokens double-issued.
- Confidence: medium (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The ecosystem canon decision remains proposed while chains operate
- Topics: SR-01.3
- Evidence: VisionFlow records the decision proposed, implementation partial and activation inactive (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-...md:115`, `:130`) while two chains are sealed and produced (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:11`; `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:5`).
- Failure: settlement-finality semantics that clients rely on are unratified; a later accepted revision changes them retroactively and strands transactions that were treated as final under the proposed text.
- Confidence: medium (status stated; harm inferred).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — The production edge tier resolves ACL failures as "no policy", the exact escalation ADR-2005 closed
- Topics: SP-10.6, SP-04.3, SP-09.7
- Evidence: SP-04.3 states the invariant: Invalid or Unavailable policy outcomes DENY, never inherit, because "collapsing Invalid or Unavailable back into `Ok(None)` re-opens the escalation where a malformed RESTRICTIVE policy is silently replaced by a permissive inherited one" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/resolver.rs:275`, `may_inherit` at `:153`). SP-10.6 D5 then records: "ADR-2005: the edge ACL resolver is out of repo and still treats a failed or invalid read as a miss" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/resolver.rs:265`), and SP-09.7 confirms the edge in production "still runs kit 9c36b60, whose manifest asks alpha.14 and whose lock holds alpha.17".
- Failure: on the actually-deployed wasm edge, a storage read error or malformed ACL on a restrictive policy is treated as absent, inherits the ancestor's permissive policy, and private resources are served. The fail-closed invariant the repo advertises does not hold where the edge runs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE register D5 in SP-10.6)

### F-02 — Access-token verification does not check WebID↔issuer binding unless the caller remembers a second call
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-05.16
- Evidence: "the issuer binding is a separate call. verify_access_token does not check that the webid profile lists iss as solid:oidcIssuer (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:833`), so without verify_webid_issuer_binding an unrelated IdP can assert any WebID" — the binding helper is "caller-invoked" (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:984`). No topic shows any caller in this estate invoking it.
- Failure: any consumer of the library's default token path (or a server route built on it) accepts a token from an attacker-run IdP asserting the victim's WebID; WAC then evaluates the attacker as the owner.
- Confidence: high that the gap exists in the default path; the material never shows whether the shipped server verifies OIDC tokens at all (its LDP auth is NIP-98/dev-bearer, and `oidc_enabled` defaults false) — so production exposure via this repo's binary is unconfirmed.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D1b in SP-05.8)

### F-03 — Every serialisation guard is process-local: replay, payments and trail locks all break under a second replica or a restart
- Topics: SP-05.5, SP-07.15, SP-10.4, SP-07.12
- Evidence: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-05.5, `solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay.rs`); "PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK is process-wide… Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock" (SP-07.15, `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:208`); "trail and epoch locks are in-process and held across network calls" (SP-10.4 W8, `CHANGELOG.md:251`); the git-mark and epoch machinery sits in the same process (SP-07.2/SP-07.7).
- Failure: scale horizontally or restart mid-flight: replayed NIP-98 events verify for up to 120 s after every restart; two replicas concurrently mutate the ledger/order book/pool with lost updates; two anchors can race the same trail head. The alpha.13 "fix" for the payment race (SP-10.8 F4) is in-process only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (W3/W4/W8 windows, DIVERGENCE in SP-07.15)

### F-04 — The full value-handling route family ships enabled in every default binary while the project's own README says do not carry value through it
- Topics: SP-07.14, SP-07.15, SP-02.9
- Evidence: "the whole /pay/* surface is registered with the SAME gating as /pay/.info — always on, with no payments feature flag (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5262`). The README's status section says not to carry value through these routes until the audit findings are fixed" (SP-07.14 N2); SP-07.15 records the README audit line "non-atomic payment state" as "a reproduced critical finding".
- Failure: an operator runs the stock binary — `.deposit`, `.buy`, `.sell`, `.swap`, `.withdraw`, `.withdraw-sats` are live on day one, with no flag to compile them out, on a surface the project itself declares unsafe for value; combined with F-03, any second replica reactivates the critical race.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-07.14)

### F-05 — The deployed fleet carries an ignored, unassessed RUSTSEC advisory, and the pin record's "lockstep" claim is false
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-01.11, SP-09.4
- Evidence: production edge "still runs kit 9c36b60, whose manifest asks alpha.14 and whose lock holds alpha.17 with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4" (SP-09.7 NF) while RUSTSEC-2026-0387 (crypto-bigint 0.5.5) is ignored with "exposure unassessed" (`../solid-pod-rs/deny.toml:57-63`, `ci.yml:191`); agentbox's Nix pin moved to alpha.18 but "the running container still on the old pin" (version unnamed, `../project/agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:54-58`); the host manifest says its alpha.17 pin is "in lockstep with nostr-rust-forum" (`../project/Cargo.toml:224-225`) while forum main is alpha.18.
- Failure: the artifact actually serving traffic carries an advisory this repo has formally decided not to assess; during an incident, the lockstep claim sends a responder to patch the wrong consumer, and the agentbox running version is unknown even to this record.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension/Open bullets in SP-09.7; DEBT in SP-01.11)

### F-06 — Busy-port "shift" converts a bind failure into a silently running second writer over the same store
- Topics: SP-02.7, SP-07.15, SP-05.5
- Evidence: on `AddrInUse` the server retries up to 11 attempts and then "Bound: warn port busy — shifted listener (`main.rs:206`)… the actual port is re-read from local_addr, so base_url reflects the shift" (SP-02.7). Nothing in any topic describes an out-of-process or filesystem lock on the data root; all serialisation is in-process (`lib.rs:208`, replay cache, trail/epoch locks).
- Failure: during a rolling restart or supervisor overlap, a second server process binds a shifted port and serves the same storage concurrently — the process-local replay, payment and trail locks of F-03 now protect nothing, with only a log warning. The feature that exists to help local dev actively manufactures the two-writers-one-store case in production.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the shift behaviour and the per-process locks are both stated; the overlap scenario is my construction from them.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Crash between a payout debit and its receipt leaves the user debited, unmoved and explicitly unreversed
- Topics: SP-07.15
- Evidence: recovery semantics: "A definite 404 reverses the recorded payout via reverse_payout, an intent with no payout receipt is RETAINED with a warning — the ledger cannot restore a debit it has no receipt for (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:242`)".
- Failure: a crash (or an ambiguous chain lookup that is neither a definite 404 nor a confirmed broadcast) in the window after the ledger debited the user and before the payout receipt was committed leaves the balance permanently down, the funds never sent, and recovery designed not to reverse it — per-user manual ledger surgery with no queue of affected users.
- Confidence: medium — the window is implied by the stated recovery asymmetry; its exact width is not shown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the behaviour is described in a note but never flagged as Debt/Drift/Open.

### F-08 — The record contradicts itself on whether MCP's "reproduced CRITICAL" finding is closed
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8
- Evidence: SP-08.14: "the README's status section records 'anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass' as a reproduced CRITICAL finding and says to keep MCP disabled." SP-10.8 F2: the same finding is "CLOSED in 0.5.0-alpha.13", and "The README no longer lists these findings or says to keep MCP disabled (`../solid-pod-rs/README.md:175`)". Both describe the README at the same commit.
- Failure: an operator cannot decide from this material whether enabling `--mcp` (the surface an agent-driven deployment will want) re-exposes an anonymous-read/WAC-bypass or not; if SP-08.14 is the current reading, enabling it is a direct data-disclosure incident.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; which side is true is not judgeable here.
- Severity: medium (endpoint is default-off) — high if the SP-08.14 reading is current.
- Marked by authors: no — each note is individually sourced, but the conflict between them is flagged nowhere.

### F-09 — New pods cannot anchor at all, the error misleads, and the one anchoring path depends on a single public testnet explorer
- Topics: SP-07.3, SP-07.20, SP-07.11, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "nothing outside the tests calls GitmarkAnchorer::genesis (`mempool.rs:1400`), and no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file (`trail_store.rs:354`). A new pod therefore has no way to reach the git-mark branch from this repository" (SP-07.3 N5, SP-07.20 N2); DRIFT: `_prov/anchor` still answers "no pay-token / trail not minted" (`prov.rs:501`) though `build_anchorer` no longer requires one (`prov.rs:195`); "ONE URL, no fallback chain" with `DEFAULT_MEMPOOL_URL` the public mempool.space testnet4 explorer (`mempool.rs:61`), and trail/epoch locks "held across network calls" (SP-10.4 W8).
- Failure: a new deployment's headline provenance claim (Bitcoin-anchored history) is unreachable; an operator who wires it manually depends on one third-party explorer, defaults to anchoring to testnet unless configured, and when it fails is told to mint a pay-token that is no longer required.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium — a missing feature and an operability trap, not a data-loss path.
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN N5, DRIFT N4, DIVERGENCE in SP-07.11)

### F-10 — Sustained anchor failure silently and permanently drops commits from the epoch accumulator
- Topics: SP-07.7
- Evidence: "Past 4 times the epoch size a commit is no longer accumulated, but the close is still attempted (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:289`)" and "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4066`)".
- Failure: a mempool outage longer than 4× the epoch size means every git commit after that point is never added to any epoch; when anchoring recovers, the accumulated batch anchors only the early commits and the rest are permanently unanchored — no error, no header, no log that any commit was dropped from the accumulator.
- Confidence: medium-high — direct consequence of the two stated mechanics.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — both mechanics are stated plainly; the combination is never flagged.

### F-11 — Crash windows leave mismatched body/meta pairs and unmarked writes, against an advertised atomic-storage contract
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.3, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "the rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (`solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111`); the 2026-08-19 audit records "filesystem writes still violate the advertised atomic-storage contract" (SP-06.4); "write-then-mark is NOT atomic — a crash between the storage put and the git commit leaves an unmarked resource (`lib.rs:1518`)" (W1).
- Failure: power loss mid-write serves a body under a stale ETag/content-type meta (breaking conditional requests and conneg), and stored resources exist with no provenance mark — the receipt makes the window observable but nothing closes or reconciles it automatically.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-06.4; W1/W2 in SP-10.4; audit F5)

### F-12 — Every `acl:agentGroup` grant denies silently unless a group resolver is wired, and the material never shows one being wired
- Topics: SP-04.15, SP-04.1
- Evidence: "NoGroupMembership — always false, the server default (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:28`)"; the invariant note: "an unwired GroupMembership resolver… makes every acl:agentGroup clause deny silently — a server that forgets to pass a real resolver… loses group grants without any error." SP-04.1 shows the server calling `evaluate_access_ctx_with_registry` (`evaluator.rs:234`) but no topic shows a group resolver being passed anywhere in the server.
- Failure: a pod owner who grants access via `acl:agentGroup` (the normal way to share a folder with a list of people) finds every member locked out — including themselves if they relied on a group — with no error, no log, and no denial metric (see F-14).
- Confidence: medium — the class diagram states the default is what the server uses; whether any call site overrides it is not shown.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the failure mode is described as a generic invariant warning, not flagged as affecting this server.

### F-13 — A default deployment exposes no metrics endpoint and counts only two subsystems
- Topics: SP-10.12
- Evidence: "Prometheus export is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE (`metrics.rs:4`)… a bare solid-pod-rs-server exposes NO metrics endpoint at all. There is no /metrics route"; "Only TWO subsystems are counted — SSRF blocks and dotfile denials. WAC denials, replay rejections, quota refusals and provenance failures are logged but never counted"; "the counters are also OPT-IN: without a with_metrics call… a deployment that never wires one records nothing."
- Failure: in production you cannot see authorisation-failure rates, replay storms, quota exhaustion or provenance outages except by scraping logs — and a stock deployment emits neither counters nor an endpoint. Every security-relevant rate in this system is invisible exactly when you need it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the Prometheus scope is marked DIVERGENCE; the two-counter gap and opt-in wiring are stated without a marker.

### F-14 — The IdP's default user store is in-memory: turning the IdP on wipes every account on restart
- Topics: SP-05.18, SP-05.16, SP-10.8
- Evidence: "UserStore trait plus InMemoryUserStore (`solid-pod-rs-idp/src/user_store.rs:70`)" is the only implementation shown; the flow depends on SessionStore and `take_code` single-use codes (`session.rs:185`); SP-10.8 F3 says the forged-identity finding is "mitigated by not exposing the optional IdP router" — i.e. the mitigation is that nobody runs it.
- Failure: an operator who wires the IdP (the `axum-binder` feature exists precisely so they can) gets a login system whose accounts, credentials and sessions all evaporate on every restart — users cannot log in again, and password-change/reset state is gone.
- Confidence: medium — the in-memory default is stated; durable alternatives are never shown.
- Severity: medium (high if the IdP is enabled)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — CI runtime-tests none of the paths an operator will actually run, and skips coverage on pull requests
- Topics: SP-09.10, SP-09.2, SP-09.11, SP-01.13
- Evidence: "the git feature end-to-end — the CGI integration tests sit behind with-git-binary (`crates/solid-pod-rs-git/Cargo.toml:48`)"; "the tls feature — no matrix row builds it (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/Cargo.toml:136`)"; "no job starts the binary, exercises a real cache, or crosses a restart"; "coverage now runs only on main pushes… a pull-request run reaches green without the 60 percent gate having run" (`ci.yml:201`, `:347`); fuzz and bench are scheduled-only outside ci-required (`ci.yml:292`); examples are never built by CI (SP-01.13 N3).
- Failure: regressions in the provenance/git path, TLS builds, examples, or anything restart-dependent reach main undetected — the subsystems this system is chosen for (git marks, provenance) are exactly the ones with no runtime CI evidence.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-09.10; notes N2B in SP-09.2 and N2 in SP-09.11)

### F-16 — AGPL-3.0-only is inherited by anyone who links or embeds, which is the stated integration pattern
- Topics: SP-01.1, SP-08.16
- Evidence: "workspace.package.license = AGPL-3.0-only (`../solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:17`) — every crate above inherits it, so a consumer that statically links CORE or a sibling (e.g. VisionClaw's embedded pod, VC-26) inherits the same obligation."
- Failure: an operator who embeds the core or server into their own product (the pattern the estate's own consumers use) inherits network-copyleft source-disclosure obligations for their combined work; treating the crate as an ordinary MIT/Apache dependency produces a compliance incident discovered late.
- Confidence: high that the obligation is stated; the legal scope is the standard AGPL reading, not asserted by the material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — stated as fact in an EXTERNAL note, never as a risk.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether any code path in `solid-pod-rs-server` (LDP, MCP, proxy) verifies OIDC access tokens, and if so whether it calls `verify_webid_issuer_binding` — F-02's production exposure turns on this.
2. Whether the server passes a real `GroupMembership` implementation into `evaluate_access_ctx_with_registry`, or ships with `NoGroupMembership` — F-12 turns on this.
3. Which side of the MCP-critical contradiction (SP-08.14 vs SP-10.8) reflects the README and code at the verified commit — F-08.
