4. The concrete version and feature set of the currently running agentbox container ("the old pin" is unnamed), and whether any deployment in the estate runs two or more replicas against one storage — F-03/F-05 are latent or live depending on this.
5. Whether any out-of-process lock (file lock, lease) guards the data root — the material never mentions one, and the port-shift split-brain scenario in F-06 assumes its absence.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: solid-pod-rs production incident (custody loss on the `/pay` surface)

**Scope.** Twelve months after the distilled commit (`09ec90f`), a production pod lost a client after value movements on the `/pay` surface could not be reconciled with on-chain state. The investigation could not reduce the failure to a single trigger; the three candidates below are ranked by likelihood against the code as cited. Every claim carries the citation the distillation gives; where I reason beyond the material I say so.

---

### R-1 — Non-atomic payment state: a second server process over one pod store double-spends the ledger
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.18, SP-07.14, SP-02.1, SP-02.7, SP-10.4
- Chain of events:
  1. A second `solid-pod-rs-server` process comes up against the same data root — a restart race where the old process is still inside its 30 s graceful shutdown (`main.rs:485`, `:489-497`), a supervisor starting a replacement before the first exits, or deliberate scale-out. The busy-port logic does not fail here: on `AddrInUse` it warns and serves on a shifted port (`main.rs:206`, `:213`), so the second process silently becomes a live server over the same storage.
  2. Both processes open the same `FsBackend`. Every `/pay` mutation is serialised only by `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK`, a process-wide mutex (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:208`); the storage API has no cross-resource CAS primitive (SP-07.15 note).
  3. Two withdrawals (or a withdraw and a swap) land on different processes. Both read the same ledger document, both apply their debit, both persist — last write wins, one debit is lost. The replay guard `check_replay` → mutate → `record_replay` is check-then-act under the same process mutex, so a replayed action across processes also executes twice.
  4. The trail and epoch locks are equally in-process and held across network calls, so two processes can also build on the same anchor head (SP-10.4 W8, `CHANGELOG.md:251`).
  5. The stored ledger is the authority (SP-07.18: `write_ledger` on `PaymentStore` is still the authority), so the client's balance now exceeds what was ever deposited and the excess is paid out. No reconciliation primitive exists.
- Evidence: the lock is process-wide with the README audit line recording "non-atomic payment state" as a reproduced critical finding (SP-07.15 note); "two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:204`, SP-10.4 W4); ADR-2008 D7 makes fixing this a precondition of the port and nothing states what replaces the lock (SP-07.18 Open); the whole `/pay/*` surface is registered with no feature flag (SP-07.14 N2, `lib.rs:5262`) while the README says not to carry value through these routes.
- Warning signs visible today: the reproduced-critical audit line; W4 and W8 recorded as open windows; the port-shift behaviour that turns a collision into a second live instance rather than a crash; "do not carry value through these routes until the audit findings are fixed" (SP-07.14 N2).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Deposits are credited on unconfirmed anchors that never confirm
- Topics: SP-07.9, SP-07.17, SP-07.19, SP-07.11, SP-07.12
- Chain of events:
  1. A depositor presents an MRC20 body at `POST /pay/.deposit`; issuer and ticker binding pass because the trail is anchored on the pod's configured issuer key (`handlers/pay.rs:497`, `:549`).
  2. `verify_mrc20_anchor` walks every mark via `MempoolLookup` — and since alpha.12 "a head still in the mempool is still accepted (intact, not verified)" (`solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:1034`, SP-07.9 N4). `verify` answers with `is_intact`, which "also accepts a head still in the mempool" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:856`; `solid-pod-rs/src/blocktrail.rs:849`).
  3. `credit_by_outpoint` credits the caller's ticker balance — the only credit path (`handlers/pay.rs:655`; `payments.rs:450`).
  4. The credited balance is spent through the `/pay` write routes before the anchor confirms.
  5. The anchor transaction is double-spent or evicted and never confirms. The credit's on-chain basis disappears; the ledger number stands. The amount on each mark was never checked on this path (`blocktrail.rs:1068`, SP-07.19 note), and the truth source for `TxInfo` was a single external mempool endpoint — by default the PUBLIC mempool.space testnet4 explorer, one URL, no fallback (`mempool.rs:61`, SP-07.11 divergence note).
- Evidence: acceptance of unconfirmed heads is stated twice (SP-07.9 N4, SP-07.19 notes); amounts unchecked stated at `blocktrail.rs:1068`; credit-by-outpoint as the sole credit at `payments.rs:450` and `handlers/pay.rs:655`; the public-endpoint default at `mempool.rs:61`.
- Warning signs visible today: the alpha.12 "Changed" entry itself; ADR-2007's recorded divergence (one URL, public default, no fallback); the README's "do not carry value" line (SP-07.14 N2).
- Confidence: high (the unconfirmed-credit behaviour is stated; the spend-out step through the live `/pay/.buy`, `.withdraw`, `.withdraw-sats` routes is the material's own tension — SP-07.18 N3 records these token routes still live)
- Severity: high

### R-3 — WebID asserted by an unrelated IdP: issuer binding is not part of token verification
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-05.7, SP-04.15, SP-04.14
- Chain of events:
  1. A consuming surface verifies Solid-OIDC access tokens through `verify_access_token`, the convenient wrapper (`oidc/mod.rs:741`).
  2. The wrapper performs the crypto, `aud` and WebID extraction but does **not** check that the webid profile lists `iss` as `solid:oidcIssuer`; issuer binding is a separate, caller-invoked `verify_webid_issuer_binding` (`oidc/mod.rs:833`, `:984`).
  3. An attacker-operated IdP issues a valid ES256 token whose `webid`/URL-shaped `sub` names the victim's WebID. Signature, `aud solid` (`mod.rs:921`) and the attacker's own DPoP proof all verify.
  4. The extracted WebID is fed to WAC as the agent; every ACL naming that WebID matches on the exact-WebID clause (`wac/evaluator.rs:120`).
  5. The attacker reads and writes the victim's pod under the victim's identity.
- Evidence: SP-05.8 D1b — "without verify_webid_issuer_binding an unrelated IdP can assert any WebID" (`mod.rs:833`); the challenge advertises DPoP and Bearer realms (`lib.rs:1160`), so some surface accepts these tokens.
- Warning signs visible today: the divergence is recorded in the DIVERGENCE register (SP-10.6, alongside D2/D3 which are "documented and pinned by tests, not fixed"); discovery/verifier drift on EdDSA shows the OIDC surface already carries a known crypto-policy mismatch (`mod.rs:549` vs `:184`).
- Confidence: medium — the library gap is stated; whether the operator's consuming path omitted the binding call is inferred, not shown
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Body and `.meta` sidecar are two separate atomic renames; a crash leaves a mismatched pair
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "the rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (SP-06.4 DIVERGENCE note; `storage/fs.rs:111`; SP-10.4 W2). The ETag is a SHA-256 stored in the sidecar (`fs.rs:107`).
- Failure: after a crash, a resource is served with another version's ETag, size and content type; conditional requests and downstream caches validate or serve the wrong bytes, propagating corrupt data to clients silently.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-02 — A default server build records zero provenance marks; deletion is permanent
- Topics: SP-01.7, SP-07.2, SP-10.5
- Evidence: "with the server's empty default feature set this whole function is the no-op shim (`lib.rs:4110`), so a default build records ZERO marks" (SP-07.2 N3); server default = `[]` (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/Cargo.toml:127`).
- Failure: an operator runs the default binary believing the README's "every write is a git-mark commit"; a client resource is deleted and there is no history to recover from — unrecoverable data loss presented as a versioned store.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-03 — Write-then-mark is not atomic; a crash leaves a stored, unmarked resource
- Topics: SP-10.3, SP-10.4, SP-07.5
- Evidence: "the three steps are still not atomic" (SP-10.3); W1 at `lib.rs:1518`; the receipt "makes the write-then-mark window observable — it does not close it."
- Failure: a crash between `storage.put` and the git commit leaves a resource with no provenance; the client's provenance guarantee silently does not hold for that write.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-04 — The replay store's fail-closed refusal is attacker-fillable: verified junk events exhaust capacity and lock out all authentication
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.1, SP-10.10
- Evidence: verification runs before the replay check (`lib.rs:636` then `:641`, SP-05.1); at capacity the store "REFUSE[s] the NEW credential" with `CapacityExhausted` (`replay.rs:275`); `DEFAULT_MAX_SIZE` 10 000, TTL 120 s (`replay.rs:82`, `:78`).
- Failure: an attacker with their own keypair mints ~10 000 unique valid kind-27235 events inside the TTL; every legitimate credential is then refused (returns None → anonymous → 401) for up to two minutes, repeatably — an authentication denial-of-service using the fail-closed design itself. The ADR-2006 note considered floods of *replays*, not floods of *fresh* events.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the verify-then-record order and the refusal are stated; the fill rate needed is arithmetic)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Known supply-chain advisories carried as exceptions, one with unassessed exposure in the auth path
- Topics: SP-01.11, SP-09.4, SP-10.8
- Evidence: RUSTSEC-2026-0387 (crypto-bigint 0.5.5 via openidconnect) is ignored with "the oidc feature is exposed and unassessed until openidconnect moves to the 0.14 line" (`deny.toml:57-63`, SP-01.11 DEBT); the README "no longer states the gate PASSES" (`README.md:175`, `:178`).
- Failure: a vulnerability in the unassessed advisory is exploited through an OIDC-enabled deployment twelve months after the exception was recorded with no assessment.
- Confidence: high (the debt exists; exploitability unassessed)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-06 — Anchor and deposit verification trust a single public third-party endpoint, defaulting to public testnet4
- Topics: SP-07.11, SP-07.12
- Evidence: "DEFAULT_MEMPOOL_URL is the PUBLIC mempool.space testnet4 explorer (`mempool.rs:61`)"; "ONE URL, no fallback chain" (ADR-2007 divergence, SP-07.11); an unconfigured operator cannot tell from the logs which chain anchors are written to beyond the one-time startup record.
- Failure: a production pod quietly broadcasts and verifies against testnet4 through a public rate-limited API — anchors land on a chain with trivial security, and explorer outages or wrong data feed `TxInfo` directly into custody decisions (`transaction_exists`, `mempool.rs:456`).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-07 — Epoch anchor failures are swallowed; provenance promises are silently unmet
- Topics: SP-07.7, SP-07.6, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (`lib.rs:4066`, SP-07.7 note); REC-11: no pod-wide `_prov` enumeration exists (`handlers/prov.rs:749`).
- Failure: a client on `AnchorPolicy::Epoch` believes commits are Bitcoin-anchored; the batch anchors repeatedly fail and nobody — caller or operator — is told. There is also no way to audit the gap after the fact.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-08 — GitmarkAnchorer has no production on-ramp, and the anchor endpoint's error message misdirects
- Topics: SP-07.3, SP-07.20
- Evidence: "nothing outside the tests calls `GitmarkAnchorer::genesis` (`mempool.rs:1400`), and no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file (`trail_store.rs:354`)" (SP-07.3 OPEN); the handler still answers "no pay-token / trail not minted" while `build_anchorer` no longer requires one (`handlers/prov.rs:501` vs `:195`, SP-07.3 N4).
- Failure: a new pod sold Bitcoin-anchored provenance can never start a trail, and the operator troubleshooting it follows an error message describing a requirement that no longer exists.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-09 — The git NIP-98 policy is a broad, replayable, body-unbound token
- Topics: SP-05.3, SP-06.12
- Evidence: `GitLenient` — "method `*` plus URL-prefix" (`nip98.rs:93`); "the git bridge also passes body = None, so a push body is NOT hash-bound" (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/auth.rs:148`); one token "covers every smart-protocol sub-request" (`auth.rs:154`).
- Failure: a single intercepted git credential replays for any read under the repo URL prefix for the full freshness window, with no body binding at all. (Server-side push refusal currently caps the write half of this — that refusal is one owner decision old, ER-28.)
- Confidence: high (facts stated; impact partially inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The record contradicts itself on whether the MCP bypass is closed
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8
- Evidence: SP-08.14 says the README records "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" as a reproduced CRITICAL finding and says to keep MCP disabled; SP-10.8 says F2 is "CLOSED in 0.5.0-alpha.13" (`CHANGELOG.md:198`, `:207`).
- Failure: an operator following the stale README leaves MCP permanently disabled (loss of function) or, worse, a reviewer treats the close as covering surfaces the alpha.13 fix did not touch — the two statements cannot both be current, and nothing in the material says which surfaces the close was verified against beyond the named path canonicaliser.
- Confidence: high (the contradiction is in the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-11 — Default CORS echoes every Origin, and the acl:origin gate is off by default
- Topics: SP-06.10, SP-02.3, SP-01.6, SP-04.8
- Evidence: "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (`lib.rs:3505`, SP-06.10 N2); wildcard+credentials "degrades to echoing the concrete Origin" (`security/cors.rs:241-243`); "acl-origin is NOT in any default feature set... a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely" (SP-04.8 divergence).
- Failure: a pod deployed with no CORS config echoes any Origin alongside credential-bearing headers, and nothing at the WAC layer looks at Origin either — cross-origin abuse from any credential-holding browser client is undistinguished from same-origin use.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-12 — Discovery does not advertise EdDSA though the verifier accepts it (invariant 6, broken in the shipped direction)
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.1
- Evidence: "the verifier accepts EdDSA (`oidc/mod.rs:549`) and discovery does not advertise it" (`mod.rs:184`) — "the one currently BROKEN in the shipped direction" (SP-10.1 N).
- Failure: a relying party builds its accepted-alg policy from discovery, excludes EdDSA, and diverges from the pod's own verifier — tokens one side accepts the other rejects, or an alg the operator believed excluded is in fact honoured.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-13 — Quota reservation is documented only on PUT; POST and PATCH lifecycles show no reservation step
- Topics: SP-03.3, SP-03.4, SP-03.5, SP-06.6
- Evidence: the PUT sequence includes `reserve_quota_for_size` (`lib.rs:2652`) and `finish_quota_reservation` (`lib.rs:2680`); the POST (SP-03.4) and PATCH (SP-03.5) sequences contain no quota step at any point. This is absence in the diagrams, not an assertion — flagged as a suspicion, per the check-against-code instruction.
- Failure: if the reservation is PUT-only, quota is bypassable by writing via POST or PATCH; if it exists but a crash lands between write and `finish`, the reservation leaks and the pod's usable quota silently shrinks until an operator runs `quota reconcile`.
- Confidence: low
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — `open_edit: true` removes authorisation from the edit route entirely, and the route always compiles
- Topics: SP-08.18
- Evidence: "with [open_edit true] no authorisation check runs at all and any caller may edit any repo" — default false at `config.rs:131`, warning at `lib.rs:217`, gate at `web_edit.rs:226`; `ApiEdit` is routed unconditionally (`router.rs:279`).
- Failure: an operator sets `open_edit` for convenience; any anonymous caller commits arbitrary file changes to any repo on the forge. The warning is a log line, not a barrier.
- Confidence: high (conditional on the flag)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-15 — The running agentbox container is still on the old pin; configured fixes are not live
- Topics: SP-09.7
- Evidence: the Nix pin is "now version 0.5.0-alpha.18... CONFIGURED in agentbox's tree and taking effect when the new agentbox image is activated, the running container still on the old pin" (`../project/agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:54-58`); every server release from 0.4.0-alpha.3 to 0.5.0-alpha.12 was **yanked** after the alpha.13 security release (`CHANGELOG.md:181`).
- Failure: whatever the old pin is, if it predates alpha.13 the running production container serves a yanked build carrying the COPY and glob authorization flaws that alpha.13 closed — the fixes exist in the tree but not in the process taking traffic.
- Confidence: medium (the stale running pin is stated; the old pin's version is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — The `/api/health` public allowlist is prefix-based, so unauthenticated callers can start the MCP relay and read its logs
- Topics: VC-03.6, VC-01.7, VC-01.12
- Evidence: `required_level` returns `None` (public) for any path with segment prefix `["api","health"]` *before* method classification (src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:56-60, sequence at :262). The same prefix contains mutating and log routes: POST `/api/health/mcp/start` → `start_mcp_relay` and GET `/api/health/mcp/logs` → `get_mcp_logs` (src/handlers/consolidated_health_handler.rs:481-482, registered per VC-01.12). Method is only considered *after* the allowlist branch, so "public read" exemptions apply to POSTs.
- Failure: anyone who can reach the port POSTs `/api/health/mcp/start` and repeatedly spawns/restarts a relay process (resource exhaustion), and reads `LogQuery`-filtered relay logs without any identity — log contents are not shown to be sanitized.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the allowlist and the routes are both documented; nobody connects the two)

### F-02 — `/ws/client-messages` authenticates on token *presence only*; any non-empty string subscribes to the client-message stream
- Topics: VC-04.25
- Evidence: the handler reads the Bearer token, rejects only an *empty* token with 401 (client_messages_handler.rs:131-142), then `ws::start` and a 100 ms interval drains `app_state.client_message_rx` to the socket as `{type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}`. The material states plainly: "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes." ADR-2090 fixed exactly this pattern for `/ws/mcp-relay` (VC-05.12) and `/multi-mcp/ws` (VC-05.13) by resolving through `get_session`; this route is not mentioned in that fix.
- Failure: an unauthenticated client presents `Authorization: Bearer x` and receives the process-global client-message broadcast — a standing data-disclosure channel no gate covers (`/ws/*` sits outside the `/api` scope, so RbacGate never runs).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — DOC-DRIFT flags only the comment/code mismatch, not the presence-only check itself

### F-03 — The pay handler debits the caller's balance and never delivers the resource
- Topics: VC-04.27
- Evidence: `pay_resource_handler` computes a per-endpoint cost, calls `store.debit(&did, cost)` and returns 200 with `X-Balance`/`X-Cost` headers — but "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)". Mounting is unconditional and live once `PAY_ENABLED=true` (VC-09.13).
- Failure: with payments enabled, every authenticated request to `/pay/{resource}` takes sats and returns nothing; the deposit side is separately a 501 stub, so users cannot even top up to be wronged — but any pre-existing balance is consumed silently.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a note; the adjacent deposit stub is the only DIVERGENCE-marked item)

### F-04 — Dual-store persistence has no cross-store transaction, no reload consistency and no restore correctness
- Topics: VC-01.1, VC-05.6, VC-05.7, VC-07.1
- Evidence: marked DIVERGENCE (BASELINE 2026-09-04 persistence closeout, VC-01.1): "shared Oxigraph ownership establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness." Decisions write to two stores in sequence — SQLite `record_decision` then Oxigraph `append_derived_summary` (VC-05.7), with only a warn on the second — and the decision provenance path appends quads in one Oxigraph transaction while the decision row lives in SQLite (VC-05.6).
- Failure: a crash between the two writes leaves a decision with no provenance (or vice versa); any restore from backup that lands Oxigraph and SQLite at different points yields decisions whose provenance chains reference nonexistent rows, permanently corrupting the append-only audit trail the governance model rests on.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — Full dev-admin bypass is peer-agnostic and compiled into every debug/dev-auth artefact, including the standard dev-runtime profile
- Topics: VC-03.11, VC-08.9, VC-09.4
- Evidence: `dev_full_bypass_active()` grants every request `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY` dev-admin with "no signature/token/peer check" (src/utils/auth.rs:116-123), deliberately peer-agnostic per ADR-2039. The normal dev launcher builds profile dev-runtime which "keeps debug-assertions" (Cargo.toml:328-331, VC-08.9), and the boot-time profile assertion only *warns and continues* for debug builds even when `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` is present (VC-09.4, `may_bind_listener` security_profile.rs:388).
- Failure: any dev-runtime or dev-auth container reachable beyond localhost (LAN mis-exposure, port-forward, misconfigured ingress) is a total authentication bypass for every caller — no credential of any kind is checked. The three substituted gates (compile-time, opt-in, boot refusal) all pass by construction in that deployment.
- Confidence: high (mechanics); the exposure itself is deployment-dependent
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2039 records the peer-agnostic design as intended; the residual exposure risk is not flagged)

### F-06 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local: single-use tokens are reusable across replicas
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03.5
- Evidence: replay claim happens in a process-global `REPLAY_CACHE` mutex (src/utils/nip98.rs:221); the note states "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)". The freshness window is 60 s and the cache TTL 120 s — but only within one process.
- Failure: with two instances behind a load balancer (or during a blue/green overlap), the same signed kind-27235 event is verified fresh on each instance within its 60 s window — every "single-use" REST signature and WS-upgrade signature can be spent once per replica.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT note acknowledging process-local scope)

### F-07 — Rate limits are constructed per worker, so every documented per-minute cap is up to 4× weaker than stated
- Topics: VC-01.3, VC-03.12, VC-04.23
- Evidence: the worker factory "runs the closure once per worker — .workers(4)" (src/main.rs:972-1260, VC-01.3), and the limiters are constructed inside it (`RateLimit::per_minute(60)` at main.rs:1144; the `/graph` 600/min at graph/mod.rs:1524; workspace 60/min at workspace_handler.rs:33-34). `RateLimitState` lives behind one `Arc` *per constructed instance* (rate_limit.rs:84-98) — nothing shares state across workers.
- Failure: the `/api/settings` "60/min" is effectively up to 240/min, `/graph` reads up to 2,400/min, workspace up to 240/min; any replica adds another multiple. Brute-force and scraping budgets computed from the documented caps are wrong by ≥4×.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the per-worker closure construction and per-instance Arc; the material does not state a shared store)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — No request-size guard is registered on the live request path
- Topics: VC-03.1, VC-04.19
- Evidence: DEBT note in VC-03.1: `src/middleware/validation.rs` was deleted and `RequestSizeLimit` (default cap 16 MiB, src/utils/validation/middleware.rs:17, mod.rs:14) "is not registered by any .wrap() in src/main.rs:1048-1051". VC-04.19 confirms the sanitizers are reachable only via the dry-run `/api/validation/test/{type}` route, never automatically.
- Failure: oversized bodies hit extractors/handlers with no middleware ceiling; combined with the public `/api/client-logs` route (allowlisted, 1,000 entries per request but no byte or rate bound stated), an unauthenticated caller can drive memory/CPU and disk (`/app/logs/client.log`) pressure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-09 — The KG watchdog polls a `/api/health` route that first-registration-wins gives to a different handler than the composed health check
- Topics: VC-01.7, VC-01.10, VC-01.11, VC-08.1, VC-08.2
- Evidence: VC-01.7 notes the collision: "`/api/health` also collides with api_handler::config's own /health route… first registration wins". `api_handler::config` is registered sixth (src/main.rs:1163) with `GET /api/health → health_check` (api_handler/mod.rs:109); the consolidated `unified_health_check` group registers later (main.rs:1171-1191). Only `unified_health_check` is documented to compose `get_degraded_reason` into a `status` (VC-04.16); the watchdog's `health_verdict` marks DOWN only on transport failure or `status == "unhealthy"` (liveness_harness.rs:505-507).
- Failure: if the winning `health_check` returns 200 with a benign/absent `status` regardless of subsystem degradation, `kg_backend_up` can never transition DOWN and `CANARY-VC-RESA-KG` can never fire on real outages — the flagship liveness canary is wired to (at best) an undocumented handler.
- Confidence: medium (route winner is asserted by the material's own ordering rule; the winning handler's response shape is not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the collision is noted; the watchdog consequence is not

### F-10 — Supervision escalation permanently stops the graph subsystem; nothing restarts the supervisor
- Topics: VC-02.4, VC-02.5, VC-02.6, VC-02.7
- Evidence: after `restart_count` exceeds `max_restarts` (5 within 300 s, graph_service_supervisor.rs:481-493), strategy `Escalate` — explicitly "top of the tree" after ADR-2045 — calls `ctx.stop()` (:843-855). VC-02.6: this is "the only explicit stop… there is no drain timer and no registration gate on the live path"; children "stop when [their] last Addr clone drops"; no code path restarts a stopped supervisor, and `RestartAllActors` itself clears all four child Addr slots, discarding in-memory graph state.
- Failure: a flapping child (e.g. transient kg_repo errors) for ~5 minutes escalates to a dead supervisor; every graph read/write routed through it then fails with mailbox errors, all clients lose the position stream, and recovery requires a process restart. Positions and any unsupervised in-memory state are lost on every OneForAll restart too.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the stated state machine and the absence of any restart path)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Settings PUT returns 200 when persistence fails, so acknowledged settings are silently lost on restart
- Topics: VC-06.3
- Evidence: after propagating to actors, the route calls `set_setting("physics", …)` and on failure only warns — "warn only — the response still succeeds" (settings_routes.rs:667-673). The in-memory actor state and GPU now hold the new values while the durable row does not.
- Failure: a full/locked SQLite during a settings-change window: every client gets 200s, sliders "stick" live, then a restart silently reverts to the last persisted snapshot; sessions disagree with storage and there is no retry or reconciliation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Settings writes are an unguarded read-merge-write of the full snapshot: concurrent PUTs to different sections lose updates
- Topics: VC-06.1, VC-06.2, VC-06.3
- Evidence: each PUT does `send(GetSettings)` (one full snapshot, settings_routes.rs:527), merges its section into `full_settings`, then `send(UpdateSettings { settings: full_settings })` (:577) — two separate actor messages. The actor serialises individual messages, not the read-modify-write pair across requests.
- Failure: two clients PUT different sections concurrently: both read S0, each writes S0+own-change, the second write clobbers the first's section; both receive 200. No version check or per-section merge on the write path is shown.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the two-message pattern; no compare-and-swap or lock is documented)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — GPU compute is mandatory with no CPU fallback anywhere; GPU loss silently freezes the position stream
- Topics: VC-10.10, VC-10.2, VC-04.1, VC-12.6
- Evidence: "CPU physics fallback not implemented - GPU compute is mandatory" (physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:585-587, warn once); `ComputeForces` drops the frame and returns Err when no context (force_compute_actor.rs:1807-1822), errors logged "every 300th skip"; clustering/components/stress-majorization hard-fail; the end-note states "no actor now degrades to a CPU implementation". Boot itself degrades open on init timeout (resource_supervisor.rs:457-460) and `GET /api/graph/positions` 503s when `gpu_addr` is None (graph/mod.rs:632).
- Failure: a driver/PTX failure at boot (fallback PTX stale, ISA splice accepted but rejected only at driver load — VC-12.5/12.6) leaves a "healthy" server serving topology with positions that never advance; the only signals are sparse logs and `telemetryLive:false`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the no-fallback policy is stated as design and the coverage unevenness is marked DIVERGENCE; the availability consequence is not flagged

### F-14 — The human-approval journey is two disconnected producers writing indistinguishable rows; REST decisions are never signed
- Topics: VC-05.17, VC-05.7, VC-05.8, VC-07.9
- Evidence: marked DIVERGENCE — no handler sends a message to `ElevationActor`/`DecisionElevationActor` (grep finds zero references); the REST path always writes `decision_event_id:None` (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:543-561) while the actor path writes the signed event id (elevation_actor.rs:1749); the inbox "projects EITHER kind of row into the SAME BrokerCase shape, indistinguishable to the bridge". BASELINE itself: "Current source review does not certify a complete human-approval journey" (BASELINE-architecture.md:359).
- Failure: an operator or auditor reading the broker inbox cannot distinguish a forum-panel-signed human decision from an automated agentbox REST decision; HITL-precision KPIs (VC-04.14) and any compliance claim built on this table count unsigned machine decisions as human judgment.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-15 — The shipped Compose defaults turn on the Jev typed-decision client with cloud routing allowed, for dev *and* production
- Topics: VC-09.16, VC-01.2, VC-05.8, VC-08.3
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:21-22` defaults `VISIONCLAW_JEV=1` and `VISIONCLAW_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` (library default off), `BACKEND_LOCAL=0`, routed through the shared agentbox proxy (agentbox:8094, jev-latest) — recorded as accepted/complete/live (ADR-2148). Items documented as *local-only* (e.g. broker `PROPOSAL_TRIAGE`, VC-05.8; `HEALTH_TRIAGE` on degraded status, VC-08.3) then "take the route of the backend agentbox selects" (VC-09.16), with a public TypeSafe API as a possible endpoint when a key is set.
- Failure: broker-case content and degraded-state summaries leave the estate to whatever model the proxy selects, with no data-classification gate shown; a production operator who never read ADR-2148 gets LLM egress of case data by default.
- Confidence: medium (defaults and routing are stated; the sensitivity of the exact payloads and the proxy's actual backend selection are not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as CONFIGURED, not as a risk)

## Not judgeable from this material
1. Whether the `health_check` handler that actually serves `/api/health` (per first-registration-wins) emits a `status` field reflecting degraded state — decides F-09's full impact on the watchdog and canary.
2. Persistence of identity and secret state across restarts: the NostrService session store's backing (role of `REDIS_URL`, nostr_service.rs:149) and whether `ProtectedSettings::default()` (VC-06.6) has any durable store for API keys and tokens.
3. Whether the XR/Godot client sends an `Origin` header on WebSocket upgrade — release builds return 400 "Origin header required" when it is missing (http_handler.rs:139), which would break the XR client entirely in production.
4. What `app_state.client_message_rx` actually carries and which legitimate clients consume `/ws/client-messages` — needed to size F-02's disclosure impact.
5. Deployment topology: whether more than one replica is ever run (rate limits, replay cache, sessions all assume one) and whether any dev-runtime/dev-auth artefact is network-reachable outside localhost in practice.

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-mortem — production incident resulting in loss of a client

Scope of record: the architecture distillation verified at `visionclaw` commit `8692b12ba987ecb87165566beb7c320a2446e4b4` (agentbox `6500582dfae23bf8a38a6942916d208154e43625`). Every claim below carries the citation the record gives; where I reason past what the record states, I say so. The three root causes are ranked by how few preconditions each needs to fire. All findings are independently checkable against the cited files and tests.

---

### R-1 — `/ws/client-messages` hands the outbound client-message stream to any caller presenting any non-empty string as a token
- Topics: VC-04.25, VC-03.2, VC-05.12, VC-05.13
- Chain of events:
  1. A caller opens `GET /ws/client-messages` with `Authorization: Bearer` followed by any non-empty value.
  2. `websocket_client_messages` (client_messages_handler.rs:106) reads the header token; an empty or missing token answers 401 (:131-142), but a present token is never resolved against `NostrService` — no `get_session`, no `validate_session`.
  3. `ws::start(ClientMessagesWs::new(app_state), ...)` upgrades; the socket answers `{type:init, status:connected}`.
  4. `start_message_stream` runs every 100 ms and drains `app_state.client_message_rx`, text-forwarding `{type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}` to the socket.
  5. The socket stays alive by answering the 30 s heartbeat pings (stop only after 90 s without a pong), so the stream is indefinite.
  6. Other users' client messages — content plus session and agent identifiers — are disclosed to an unauthenticated party.
- Evidence: VC-04.25 states the token's "CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (client_messages_handler.rs:131-142), against the file's own comment claiming it "allows but logs unauthenticated connections" (:114-116). The sibling upgrades were fixed for exactly this class: ADR-2090 made `/ws/mcp-relay` (mcp_relay_handler.rs:513-519, VC-05.12) and `/multi-mcp/ws` (multi_mcp_websocket_handler.rs:851-856, VC-05.13) resolve the token through `get_session`; ADR-2058 touched this same file for the `?token=` carrier (client_messages_handler.rs:145) without adding content validation. The route sits in the plain upgrade block (src/main.rs:1111-1119) with no scope-level auth wrap.
- Warning signs visible today: the comment/code mismatch the authors already flagged as DOC-DRIFT on this handler; two prior presence-only bypasses (ADR-2090) in the same upgrade family; the route was deliberately moved early in registration (src/main.rs:1130, VC-01.10) and allowlisted (rbac_gate.rs:56-60), confirming it is expected to be reachable without the gate.
- Confidence: high — the defect is stated outright; nothing about the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — `/pay/{resource}` debits the caller's balance and returns success while never forwarding to or performing any resource
- Topics: VC-04.27, VC-09.13, VC-01.13
- Chain of events:
  1. `PAY_ENABLED=true` activates the `/pay` routes, which are mounted unconditionally under `solid-pod-embed` and merely inert until then (src/main.rs:1097-1105 / :1105, VC-04.27, VC-09.13).
  2. An authenticated caller (NIP-98 pubkey → did) requests `GET /pay/{resource_path}`.
  3. `cost_for_endpoint` applies the per-endpoint tier — inference ×10, image-gen ×100, analytics ×5 of the base cost.
  4. `FsPaymentStore.debit(&did, cost)` succeeds; the handler answers `200 {resource, charged, balance}` with `X-Balance`/`X-Cost` headers.
  5. The handler then stops: "this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)" — no inference run, no image generated, no analytics call.
  6. The balance sits in a host-owned local ledger (`PAY_LEDGER_DIR`, `FsPaymentStore`); the deposit route is a 501 stub ("Contact the server operator for manual funding", pay_handler.rs:487-492) and no refund or reversal path appears anywhere in the record.
- Evidence: VC-04.27's stub note and the deposit divergence note; ADR-2111 remains decision_status proposed / implementation_status none, re-qualified 2026-10-01 so that "FsPaymentStore and /pay/* stay host-owned" (ADR-2111 doc :38, :83, :95-96).
- Warning signs visible today: the charge-without-delivery stub is live code on an always-mounted surface; the ×100 image-gen tier amplifies each occurrence; there is no automated top-up (deposit 501) and no reversal mechanism.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism (stated verbatim); that `PAY_ENABLED` was set in the incident deployment is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — `PUT /api/settings/physics` answers 200 even when the persistence write fails, so accepted client configuration silently reverts on restart
- Topics: VC-06.2, VC-06.3, VC-06.1, VC-06.7
- Chain of events:
  1. Client PUTs a physics patch; `merge_physics_patch`, `validate_physics_settings` and `validate_for_gpu` all pass.
  2. `UpdateSettings` to `OptimizedSettingsActor` succeeds — the actor's in-memory state now holds the new values.
  3. GPU propagation, `ConfigureStream`, `ForceResumePhysics` and the settings broadcast all run, so the change is visibly live.
  4. The explicit durable write, `set_setting("physics", ...)` (settings_routes.rs:667-673), runs last; on failure it is "warn only — the response still succeeds".
  5. The route returns 200 with the new `PhysicsSettings` JSON (:691); every read until restart returns the new values from the actor.
  6. On any restart (deploy, crash, supervisor `Escalate` ctx.stop — VC-02.4), settings load from the SQLite store (VC-06.7, VC-07.7): the old values. The client's accepted change is gone and no error was ever surfaced to the caller or the operator.
- Evidence: VC-06.3's warn-only branch and the 200 at :691; the store is the restart source of truth (SqliteSettingsRepository flat dotted-key rows, VC-06.7; `LoadAllSettings` reads it, VC-07.7).
- Warning signs visible today: the same SQLite settings layer already forced the hot-reload watcher off for causing "database deadlocks" (VC-06.1, app_state.rs:1110-1113), so transient `set_setting` failures are plausible; success is nowhere conditioned on durability on the flagship write path.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; that this was the incident is medium — it requires a transient persist failure plus a restart, both ordinary events.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — A client acknowledges a position chunk it silently dropped inside the 16 ms frame gate, so the server records wrong nodes as delivered
- Topics: VC-13.7, VC-14.10, VC-13.4
- Evidence: `handleBinaryFrame` "returns normally" when a frame arrives within 16 ms of the last accepted one (wsClient.ts:26-28) and the frame never reaches the worker; the ack is sent after `updateNodePositions` resolves, which it does (store/websocket/binaryProtocol.ts:392-408). ADR-2141 decision 5 requires a chunk that was not applied not to be acknowledged (ADR-2141 doc :32). Both VC-13.7 and VC-14.10 label this TENSION.
- Failure: the coordinator's `ClientStream` commits those nodes as held (position_stream.rs:225-240) and excludes them from planning until they move past `streamMoveThreshold` — on a settled graph they never do, so the client renders stale positions indefinitely while the server believes delivery is complete.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — No request-size or content-type guard exists anywhere in the middleware chain
- Topics: VC-03.1
- Evidence: VC-03.1's DEBT note — `validation.rs` was deleted, and `RequestSizeLimit` (16 MiB cap, utils/validation/middleware.rs:17, utils/validation/mod.rs:14) "is not registered by any .wrap() in src/main.rs:1048-1051". The only size bound in the record is per-route (client-logs' 1000 entries).
- Failure: an oversized body to any `/api` route is buffered by extractors with no global ceiling; memory/CPU exhaustion takes down all four workers.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — Shared-graph passwords travel as a URL query parameter
- Topics: VC-04.11, VC-01.3
- Evidence: `GET /api/graph-export/shared/{id}?password=` (graph_export_handler.rs:194 area) and the password checked from that query; `Logger::default()` wraps every request (src/main.rs:1048).
- Failure: share passwords land in access logs and in any intermediary that records URLs; possession of a log grants share access. The log-line capture is inferred — actix's default logger records the request line including the query; the record only asserts the query carrier and the logger.
- Confidence: medium (inferred for the log capture; the query carrier is stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — `/api/client-logs` is a public, unthrottled, unauthenticated write into server logs
- Topics: VC-03.6, VC-04.25, VC-01.10
- Evidence: the path is on the RbacGate public allowlist (rbac_gate.rs:56-60); the only bound is `MAX_LOG_ENTRIES` (1000) per request (client_log_handler.rs:39 area); entries append to `/app/logs/client.log` with caller-controlled session ids and content.
- Failure: forged telemetry and correlation records pollute the same log family the canary and KPI evidence draws on, and unbounded request volume grows the file without limit.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Replay protection and session state are process-local; any second replica defeats NIP-98 single-use
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03.5, VC-03.12
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" on the REPLAY_CACHE (nip98.rs:221); sessions (`NostrService`) and rate-limit state (`RateLimit`'s Arc RwLock HashMap) are likewise in-process.
- Failure: with two replicas behind one address, a captured kind-27235 token verifies on the replica that did not claim its id; single-use, expiry enforcement and rate budgets all split. The record does not state a multi-replica deployment exists — the scaling step is inferred.
- Confidence: high for the limitation; the deployment scenario is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — A misconfigured local corpus silently falls back to GitHub and the graph is rebuilt from the wrong source
- Topics: VC-01.2, VC-07.13, VC-09.11, VC-04.3
- Evidence: "a misconfigured local source logs an error and falls back to GitHubSource rather than failing boot (corpus_source/mod.rs:157-171)"; ingestion then drives `AddNodesFromMetadata`, which VC-04.3 records as a full destructive bulk reload (re-fetch, re-process, rebuild).
- Failure: a `VAULT_ROOT` typo in production serves and persists the remote corpus as if it were the client's vault — wrong nodes, wrong edges, wrong provenance — with only a boot-time error log.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Restore correctness and cross-store consistency were never established for the shared Oxigraph + SQLite persistence
- Topics: VC-01.2, VC-07.1
- Evidence: the persistence closeout divergence — shared Oxigraph ownership "establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness" (BASELINE 2026-09-04, cited at VC-01.2).
- Failure: any DR restore reassembles the Oxigraph store, the per-domain SQLite files and in-memory actor state from different points in time; decisions whose provenance quads lived only in the lost Oxigraph snapshot become unreconstructible.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-08 — A decision persists in SQLite even when its provenance append fails, and the request still reports success
- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.6, VC-01.2
- Evidence: `repo.record_decision` commits first (:561); `append_derived_summary` failure sets `writeback_committed=false` and only warns ("DEGRADED"); `DecideResponse{success:true}` is returned regardless. Trace reads walk `dl:caused`/`dl:precedentFor` edges in the store (decision_handler.rs) — edges that were never written.
- Failure: the ADR-2016 append-only provenance chain silently loses edges per decision; a later `/decisions/{urn}/trace` or `/api/trace` returns a truncated history with no marker that anything is missing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

- Failure: health-triage state, proposal triage and similar local-only payloads leave the host to whatever endpoint the proxy selects, without an operator having set any cloud variable.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; classifying it as a defect is inferred

- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — On a divergent physics frame the server broadcasts the last-known-good layout as though it were current
- Topics: VC-13.3, VC-04.1
- Evidence: a bad frame (NaN/Inf, out-of-range coordinates or speed) sends `UpdateNodePositions` carrying "the last-known-good layout, display-projected the same way" (force_compute_actor.rs:2166-2189); after `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_BAD_FRAMES=5` the breaker halts stepping (:2192-2201); no frame tells clients the data is stale (`telemetry_live` covers only the settlement summary, VC-04.1).
- Failure: clients reason over a frozen graph presented as live positions — silently wrong interaction targets and derived conclusions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The PTX ISA downgrade is a text splice, so an unsupported instruction only surfaces as a runtime module-load failure
- Topics: VC-12.5, VC-12.6, VC-10.10
- Evidence: "the rewrite is a declared-ISA text splice only; it does not prove every instruction is supported by that ISA, only a real driver load settles that (ADR-2030 Consequences)"; and no CPU fallback exists on any physics path (VC-10.10 — ComputeForces drops frames, SSSP and stress majorization hard-fail).
- Failure: a CUDA/driver update in the field makes `Module::from_ptx` fail at GPU actor initialisation; physics and the position stream go dark with hard errors and no degradation path.
- Confidence: high for the statement; the field failure is inferred

### F-12 — Worker-local middleware and handler state split rate limits and the shared-graph registry across four workers
- Topics: VC-01.3, VC-01.4, VC-03.12, VC-04.11
- Evidence: the App closure "runs the closure once per worker — .workers(4)" (src/main.rs:972-1260, :1265) and the app_data registry is built inside it (src/main.rs:1057+); `RateLimit::per_minute(60)` is constructed inside that same closure (src/main.rs:1144), and its Arc sharing covers clones of one instance (rate_limit.rs:84-87), not four independently constructed ones; `GraphExportHandler`'s web::Data holds the "shared_graphs map" and rate limiter (VC-04.11).
- Failure: a share created by a POST on worker 1 returns 404 from a GET served by worker 2; every configured per-minute budget is effectively ×4, widening brute-force and share-guessing windows. Where `GraphExportHandler`'s Data is constructed is not stated — that step is inferred.

- Marked by authors: no

- Topics: VC-03.1

- Severity: medium

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose inverts the fail-closed RBAC defaults: ownerless boot, public reads, and Editor for every unassigned key
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-22.1
- Evidence: Code is fail-closed — `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:135-141`) and boot refuses to start ownerless unless `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` is set (`src/main.rs:809-818`). The shipped compose sets both open: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` on at `docker-compose.unified.yml:132` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` with a `:-1` default at `:133`, so "an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor" (`src/services/role_store.rs:359`, default Editor unless `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=viewer`).
- Failure: Deploy with the supplied compose: any holder of a nostr key gets Editor (mutating) rights on gated `/api/*` routes with no Owner assignment and no per-user role table entries; all safe-method reads are public. The fail-closed code never runs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — the "CORRECTED ADR-2070" note states the inversion and says the open posture "stays" as ADR-2027's demo default

### F-02 — Agent-event ingest accepts asserted, unsigned pubkeys: provenance attribution and KPI trust metrics are spoofable
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-25.3
- Evidence: "SECURITY: ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified - the wire carries no sig field (`agent_events/provenance.rs:29-38`)" and classification is purely on hex well-formedness (`provenance.rs:66-72`). The same events are persisted by the KPI tap with `agent_did` (`src/adapters/sqlite_kpi_repository.rs:334`, `:245`) and feed trust_variance/HITL (`src/services/kpi_compute.rs`).
- Failure: Any process or agent behind the agentbox session token publishes events naming another agent's DID; the victim's KPI trajectory, Trust Variance and provenance crossings are silently corrupted — the governance numbers the ACSP loop decides on become attacker-influenced with no audit signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SECURITY note)

### F-03 — One nostr privkey shared by publisher, bridge and all three ACSP panel identities; no delegation proof
- Topics: VC-24.8, VC-24.11
- Evidence: "bridge bot keypair VISIONCLAW_NOSTR_PRIVKEY shared by both publisher and bridge (`nostr_bead_publisher.rs:18`, `nostr_bridge.rs:12`)"; the bridge "re-sign[s] under bridge keypair" with "NIP-26 delegation not wired, service-signing only (`nostr_bridge.rs:29,65,169`)"; and "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret" (`src/app_state.rs:1308-1315`, `elevation_actor.rs:1056`, `decision_elevation_actor.rs:521`).
- Failure: Compromise of the single shared secret lets an attacker publish bead provenance events, re-sign forum messages, and act as any of the three panels (Elevation, DecisionElevation, decide-route projection) — there is no key separation and no cryptographic way to distinguish which process signed an event. Impact inferred from the stated key sharing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the key sharing is described as a "trust boundary", not flagged; only the NIP-26 gap carries a DIVERGENCE marker

### F-04 — Production secrets live in a plaintext `.env` while the SOPS rollout exists only as a proposal
- Topics: VC-22.1
- Evidence: Credentials row maps to ".env plaintext filesystem"; a 43MB `scripts/sops` binary dated 2026-05-09 exists but "no .sops.yaml, secrets.enc.yaml or sops-env.sh exists" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:113`); ADR-2104 is PROPOSED, "execute the SOPS rollout or formally withdraw ADR-109".
- Failure: Every secret (agent key, nostr privkey, GitHub PAT, API keys) sits unencrypted on the host and travels into ad-hoc backups/container images with no rotation or encryption story; the governing doc records it as accepted interim state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED ADR-2104 note)

### F-05 — `deleteAgentMemory` leaves the vector-store embedding row alive and searchable, and the server can now read it too
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.1
- Evidence: The Pod delete "never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint - RuVector is external Postgres reached only via MCP, no delete-propagation hook exists" (`client/src/services/solidPod/agentMemory.ts:191-198`); "embedding row persists and stays searchable (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`)"; and the server gained "a direct read-only RuVector client (tokio-postgres … crate visionclaw-memory-cloud)" so "the embedding row this path leaves behind has a second consumer (`Cargo.toml:143-145`)".
- Failure: A user's erasure request succeeds on the Pod and silently fails on the vector store; deleted memory content remains retrievable by similarity search indefinitely — an erasure/privacy incident that reports success to the user.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The KG inclusion gate contradicts its governing document: a `public: false` page with a class marker is still published, and `working/` is ingested by default
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12, VC-21.1
- Evidence: `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:236-238` promises every `knowledge/` page with valid frontmatter is a node with "no `owl-class` bypass and no OR", but the code is the disjunction `self.public || self.owl_class.is_some()` (`crates/visionclaw-domain/src/vault/mod.rs:162-163`, reached via `github_sync_service.rs:2114`), and VC-21.12 records "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy". Separately, `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:244` says `working/` is never ingested while the local source default base paths are `knowledge,working` (`corpus_source/local.rs:21`) and a working page is kept as its own node (`github_sync_service.rs:2156`).
- Failure: An author marks a draft `owl-class: urn:...` while keeping `public: false` in the belief the doc's promise holds — the page, its node and its wikilink edges are ingested into the publicly-served graph; the same happens to scratch notes under `working/`. Silent privacy/custody leak governed by a document that says otherwise.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (two Drift entries and a DIVERGENCE note)

### F-07 — A forced full sync destroys runtime-governed ontology writes, and Oxigraph has no backup to restore them from
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-22.11, VC-20.7
- Evidence: The rebuild does "CLEAR GRAPH assert then INSERT DATA" and "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`, `:1026`)"; those additions "are NOT … in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph - only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it". The same topic records "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup" (ADR-2103 PROPOSED).
- Failure: Operator (or `POST /api/admin/sync?force_full=true`, or `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1`, or the `sync_corpus` binary) triggers a full sync after weeks of accepted `add_owl_class`/`add_axiom` writes; every one of them is silently deleted and is unrecoverable — data loss of governed, signed work with no restore path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated as a plain note, with no Debt/Drift/Open marker on the loss itself

### F-08 — Client acknowledges position chunks it dropped: the throttle skip acks a frame that was never applied
- Topics: VC-30.10, VC-32.12, VC-32.8
- Evidence: "handleBinaryFrame returns without applying when under 16ms have passed since the last frame (`wsClient.ts:26-27`), updateNodePositions then resolves and the chunk is acknowledged (`binaryProtocol.ts:393`,`:407`), while ADR-2141 decision 5 acknowledges a chunk only after it has been applied." Server-side, `acknowledge(seq)` makes "the chunk's nodes … the client's held state" (`src/actors/position_stream.rs:225`) so they are not resent until re-displaced.
- Failure: A burst of frames above ~60fps: every throttled chunk is acked-but-dropped; the server records those node positions as delivered and stops sending them. Those nodes render at stale positions indefinitely (until they move more than `streamMoveThreshold` again) while the server believes the client is current.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-09 — Clustering silently substitutes a different algorithm on GPU failure and the fabricated clusters are persisted
- Topics: VC-15.1, VC-18.4, VC-15.13
- Evidence: On "gpu_manager_addr absent OR GPU call Err", `generate_cpu_fallback_clustering` (`clustering_handlers.rs:271-293`) returns agent-based or label-propagation clusters; the flow then unconditionally sends `WriteClusterAnalytics{clusters}` (`clustering_handlers.rs:69`) so `write_cluster_id_from_assignments` persists them. VC-18.4 states the intent: "a non-zero total_cpu_fallbacks means the zero-fallback intent has been violated" — and the only signal shown is the process-global counter, with no per-result fallback flag on the clustering response.
- Failure: GPU actor down or restart: `/analytics/clustering/run` requests for spectral/kmeans/louvain all return heuristic CPU partitions with no request-level indication, and those fabricated `cluster_id` assignments overwrite `node_analytics` — downstream UI and analytics act on structure that was never computed as requested.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the intent-violation signal is marked (VC-18.4 INVARIANT note); the substitution-and-persist path in VC-15.1 is drawn without any marker

### F-10 — The ADR-2110 rationale gate is bypassable for any case unknown to the store
- Topics: VC-24.3
- Evidence: "OPEN: a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated (`handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508`)"; the gate is `gate_tier(declared, effective)` at `:508` and a stub row is only created after the gate (`:537`). The in-code scope note still claims effective_tier has not landed (`:308-314`) while ER-7 forwarding is live.
- Failure: A bridge holding the shared agent key decides a first-contact high-risk case with an empty rationale: the 20-character requirement is silently skipped, the case is fabricated as a stub, and an unattributed-approve or attributed approve can proceed to Oxigraph writeback.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN and TENSION notes)

### F-11 — Feature flags are process-global, unpersisted, and updated by wholesale struct replace
- Topics: VC-18.2
- Evidence: `*flags = request.into_inner()` (`feature_flags_handlers.rs:29`) with "WHOLESALE REPLACE - no field merge, a partial POST silently resets omitted flags"; `FEATURE_FLAGS` is a process-global `Lazy<Arc<Mutex>>` (`analytics/state.rs:15`); `ontology_validation` "is the ONLY flag read as a real GATE" (`api_handler/ontology/mod.rs:332`); OPEN ADR-2059 keeps it "process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users".
- Failure: One power user POSTs `{"sssp_integration": true}`; the omitted `ontology_validation` is reset to its deserialized default for the whole process — the only ontology-validation gate flips without anyone intending it, and the change evaporates on restart so the pre/post states differ invisibly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN ADR-2059)

### F-12 — Two SSSP stacks with divergent semantics: the GPU path returns no paths (parent always −1), one route's `algorithm=Sssp` silently breaks contract, and the material contradicts itself about which routing is live
- Topics: VC-15.6, VC-15.7, VC-15.9
- Evidence: GPU `ComputeSSP` "publish distances to node_sssp map, parent=-1 always (no predecessor array)" (`shortest_path_actor.rs:309-329`), while the CPU `ComputeShortestPaths` path "does return reconstructed hop paths" (`graph_state_actor.rs:825-841`, marked DIVERGENCE). In VC-15.9 the same `/analytics/pathfinding/path` endpoint returns a path for Astar/Bidirectional/Semantic but a distances-only `SSSPResult` for Sssp. `GPUManagerActor::Handler-ComputeShortestPaths` and `GraphAnalyticsSupervisor::Handler-ComputeShortestPaths` are unreachable (DEAD CODE, `gpu_manager_actor.rs:804`, `graph_analytics_supervisor.rs:552`). And within VC-15.6 itself, the diagram shows the route sending `ComputeSSP` directly to `data.shortest_path_actor` while the RESOLVED ADR-2053 note in the same diagram says "the route now sends ComputeSSP to data.gpu_manager_addr" — the two descriptions of the live path disagree.
- Failure: A consumer switching `algorithm` to `Sssp` on the point-to-point endpoint silently gets no path (parent −1) where every other algorithm returns one; an engineer debugging SSSP using the sequence diagram inspects the wrong actor path because the diagram and its own resolution note conflict.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no for the parent=-1 semantics and the diagram/note contradiction; the dual-stack fact and dead handlers carry DIVERGENCE/DEAD CODE markers

- Topics: VC-20.3

- Failure: A large or pathological ontology change makes `reasoner::assert` run unbounded inside `run_post_sync_reasoning`; the sync task (including the boot-triggered one) stalls with no watchdog, and the checksum cache only helps once a run has completed — the first run on the new ontology is the unbounded one.

- Severity: medium

- Topics: VC-32.1
- Evidence: "DRIFT: PROTOCOL-registry.md:306-309 says the Authorization header is now the only accepted carrier in a release build. http_handler.rs:151-169 also accepts the signed Sec-WebSocket-Protocol NIP-98 subprotocol, and that is the carrier this client uses in every non-dev build (index.ts:165-169)"; additionally "BASELINE-architecture.md:297 still lists the query token as an open divergence, written before ADR-2058 landed".

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Authentication posture of the standalone interaction REST surfaces — `POST /constraints/define|apply|remove`, `/layout/*` (`constraints_handler.rs:14-18`, `layout_handler.rs:310-316`) are never shown behind a scope wrap, unlike the `/analytics` twin routes; I need the route registration in `main.rs` and any middleware to know whether they are open.
2. Soundness of `validate_read_only_sparql` (`ontology_handler.rs:820`) as the single gate for LLM-generated and user SPARQL — I need the implementation and Oxigraph's parser behavior to know whether a mutating form can be expressed without the blacklisted keywords.
3. Whether clustering/DBSCAN responses carry any per-request CPU-fallback indicator to clients — the diagrams show only the process-global telemetry counter; the response schemas would settle whether F-09's substitution is visible per call.
4. The actual production deployment facts that size F-01/F-03/F-04: which compose/env file production runs, whether an Owner and per-user roles are assigned, whether `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` / `VISIONCLAW_NOSTR_PRIVKEY` are shared across environments, and key rotation practice.
5. Operational cadence and restore capability for F-07: how often full syncs run in practice, and any restore drill results or Oxigraph snapshot mechanism not covered by ADR-2103/ADR-2017 (which the material says does not exist for Oxigraph).

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — production data/custody incident (written twelve months after stamp `8692b12ba987ecb87165566beb7c320a2446e4b4`)

This document ranks the three failure modes the as-built material already documents as most likely to produce a client-costing data, custody or security incident, followed by fifteen further latent defects found while investigating. Every claim carries the citation the material gives; anything I reasoned beyond the material is labelled "inferred". Where a diagram does not state ordering or concurrency, I have not assumed it.

---

### R-1 — Erasure is structurally incomplete: Pod deletes never reach RuVector, and provenance retains personal data forever
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.4, VC-22.10, VC-22.1
- Chain of events: A client invokes agent-memory erasure (`SolidPodService.deleteAgentMemory`). The Pod-side `.jsonld` resource is deleted (2xx or 404 both count as success), the UI reports the memory deleted — but no code path anywhere calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint. The embedding row persists in external Postgres and **remains searchable**. Independently, every event that ever touched that agent lives in `GRAPH_PROVENANCE`, which is append-only by invariant (no DELETE/DROP/CLEAR ever issued, `provenance_emitter.rs:32-33`) and where "retraction only ADDS `dl:validTo` and deletes nothing" (`provenance_writer.rs:218,478-502`). Twelve months later the client (or a regulator) demonstrates the "deleted" memory still surfaces in semantic search and in provenance traces; the operator cannot truthfully certify erasure.
- Evidence: `client/src/services/solidPod/agentMemory.ts:191-198`, `client/src/services/solidPod/ldpClient.ts:206-212`; "DIVERGENCE: no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`)"; "this Pod-side delete never calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint - RuVector is external Postgres reached only via MCP, no delete-propagation hook exists in this path". The exposure worsened: the server now holds a **direct read-only RuVector client** (`visionclaw-memory-cloud`, `Cargo.toml:143-145`), so the orphaned row has a second, in-server consumer. On the provenance side, ADR-2102 (crypto-shredding or explicit out-of-scope declaration) is **PROPOSED only** — no erasure record, no store acknowledgements, no retryable partial erasure exists today (VC-22.4, VC-22.10 closing notes).
- Warning signs visible today: the DIVERGENCE label in VC-22.6; the DRIFT note about the second RuVector consumer; ADR-2102 sitting at `proposed`; the VC-22.10 note that the five-store erasure design is proposed, not built.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The shipped compose inverts the code's fail-closed RBAC: any unassigned nostr key becomes Editor
- Topics: VC-22.9
- Chain of events: The code fails closed — `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (`rbac_gate.rs:135-141`) and boot refuses to start owner-less without `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` (`main.rs:809-818`). The **shipped** `docker-compose.unified.yml` sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` on (`:132`) and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` with a `:-1` default (`:133`), so production runs ownerless. Nostr key generation is permissionless, so an unassigned pubkey — any newly created identity — resolves through `effective_role` to the default **Editor** role (`role_store.rs:359`, `:366`), and a mutating/gated route only demands `ReadOnly`-level clearance (VC-22.9 flow). An anonymous party generates a key, connects, and performs authenticated mutations; with public reads enabled, `GET /api/graph/data` passes through with no auth at all (`rbac_gate.rs:172-175`, `:264-269`). The material records this as ADR-2027's deliberate demo default that "stays" — in production it is a privilege escalation to any passer-by.
- Evidence: "the CODE fails closed … The shipped compose inverts both (`docker-compose.unified.yml:132`, `:133`, with the `:-1` default), so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`)"; `main.rs:797`, `:802`; `role_store.rs:363-364` (power-user fallback to Admin), `:369-371` (lookup error → Viewer, the only fail-closed arm).
- Warning signs visible today: the compose lines themselves; the CORRECTED ADR-2070 note explicitly stating the inversion; the fact that the only mitigation (`RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=viewer`) is an env var the shipped compose does not set.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup and its provenance graph is restore-only: one storage failure destroys signed custody records permanently
- Topics: VC-22.7, VC-22.11, VC-22.4, VC-22.10
- Chain of events: `scripts/backup-sqlite.sh` treats only the four SQLite WAL databases as REQUIRED/OPTIONAL members (`backup-sqlite.sh:75-76`). The Oxigraph store — including `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` — has **no snapshot mechanism at all**; ADR-2103 (point-in-time snapshot, declared RPO/RTO, restore drill) is PROPOSED and unimplemented, and the VC-22.11 closeout keeps the bullet standing: "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup". The provenance graph is explicitly **restore-only because it cannot be re-derived** (VC-22.7 ADR-2103 note; VC-22.10 note that signed PROV-O attribution "stays in GRAPH_PROVENANCE", `github_sync_service.rs:1034-1041`, and is deliberately not re-materialised from the corpus). A single volume/disk/corruption event on the Oxigraph data directory loses the append-only, hash-chained custody history — broker decision attribution, assertion versions, activity records — irrecoverably, even though the SQLite decision rows survive. The operator can no longer prove who approved what.
- Evidence: `backup-sqlite.sh:75-76`, `:242-245` (manifest scope); ADR-2103 PROPOSED note in VC-22.7 and VC-22.10; VC-22.11 DriftNote "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup - that bullet stands"; append-only invariant `provenance_emitter.rs:32-33` verified by `append_only_verified` test (`provenance_emitter.rs:908`).
- Warning signs visible today: an entire class of write-master store absent from the backup contract by design; the restore-only property stated three times; no restore drill ever recorded.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### F-01 — A force-full-sync silently destroys all governed runtime ontology writes
- Topics: VC-22.10
- Evidence: `rebuild_assert_graph` does `CLEAR GRAPH` over the **entire** assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`, `:1026`); "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph - only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it after the next rebuild". `rematerialise_decisions` re-derives only `dl:DecisionRecord` quads (`github_sync_service.rs:1042-1048`, `:1066`, `:1094`).
- Failure: operator sets `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` (routine recovery); every runtime ontology addition since the last corpus derivation vanishes with no error and no provenance trail to rebuild from.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

ADR-2101 adds that to DecisionElevationActor only" (VC-24.5). Publish at `elevation_actor.rs:1633`, persist at `:1644`.

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Dev-mode LAN auth bypass is the DEFAULT in compose, not opt-in
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-33.3
- Evidence: ADR-2108 "flipped the default from :-0 to :-1 on 2026-09-08, so launch.sh up dev boots an ARMED backend and the operator opts OUT with VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0" (docker-compose.unified.yml:116). When armed, "Every request granted as DEV_MODE_PUBKEY... LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (src/utils/auth.rs:203-218). The material itself notes "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod - sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115).
- Failure: anyone who copies the compose file or shares a .env with the dev var gets a server that accepts all writes from any network peer with no credential.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (the ADR is cited but the inverted-default risk is called "sharper" in passing, not tracked as a defect)

### F-02 — Codescape main port runs OpenAccess and mounts ten source repositories read-only
- Topics: VC-39.7, VC-39.2
- Evidence: "the main port runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open" (codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124) and "port 3001 /codescape/ is LAN-published in dev by owner decision" (nginx.dev.conf:63-65). The compose service mounts "read-only views of the estate scene and ten member repositories" (docker-compose.unified.yml:492, :538-619).
- Failure: an unauthenticated LAN peer reads the source code of every project in the estate through the Codescape service.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, R1)

### F-03 — Codescape /ws/presence accepts a Schnorr signature without verifying it
- Topics: VC-39.7
- Evidence: "the shim accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label" (codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:20-23). Any connecting client can claim any DID.
- Failure: an attacker impersonates any agent in a shared room by claiming their DID in the presence handshake, poisoning co-presence display or any downstream logic that trusts the DID.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, R2)

### F-04 — Compose ships RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor, contradicting the binary's fail-closed default
- Topics: VC-33.6
- Evidence: "structural default RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=false src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:141 unwrap_or(false), but docker-compose.unified.yml:132 sets RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 in the dev/unified compose service. An unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor via UserRole::default_authenticated()" (src/models/rbac.rs:68-70, src/services/role_store.rs:188). "compose ships public reads open and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor by default, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed (no public reads) — two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env."
- Failure: any authenticated Nostr key gets Editor (write) access without assignment; a compose-file deploy leaks all reads to anyone who can sign a NIP-98 event.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Client RBAC gating is a single boolean, not the server's four-tier role lattice
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-33.1
- Evidence: "this is the ENTIRE client-side gating surface — a binary power-user flag, not the server's UserRole lattice (Owner > Admin > Editor > Viewer)... The client reads only the is_power_user bit of its own whoami answer, never the role. All real enforcement happens server-side per-request in RbacGate and a rejected write surfaces only as a 401/403 after the fact" (VC-33.6).
- Failure: a user with Viewer-only server permissions sees enabled edit controls for fields whose server-side level is WriteGraph or Admin; every attempt fails at the server with no pre-emptive disablement, producing a UI that promises writes it cannot deliver and log noise indistinguishable from attack.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — XR client sends NIP-98 authenticate and never reads the acknowledgement
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4
- Evidence: "the client sends the NIP-98 authenticate message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal" (transport.rs:117). "The client has no Authenticated state. NIP-98 authenticate is sent after Connected and its acceptance is never read back" (VC-36.4).

- Topics: VC-34.1
- Evidence: "PUT fails (network/5xx) → EP-->>ASM: settingsRetryManager.addFailedUpdate(path, value, message)" (endpoints.ts). The material describes the queueing but never describes when (or whether) settingsRetryManager retries, expires entries or surfaces stuck failures to the user.

- Severity: medium
