### F-09 — Clock skew between headset and desktop makes relayed routes permanently stale
- Topics: VC-38.7, VC-38.12
- Evidence: "a headset clock ahead of the desktop's makes desktop frames Stale until the desktop catches up and a clock behind does the reverse; nothing states the unit or bounds the skew" (memory_route.rs:585). The headset's own query uses its own wall clock, so "a later desktop relay replaces it only while the two clocks agree, sentAt is not rebased" (memory_route.rs:1605).
- Failure: a headset with a clock 5 minutes fast never sees any desktop-relayed route; a headset with a slow clock locks out its own query results. No error is surfaced — routes simply do not appear.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-10 — Server accepts a legacy session-bearer credential no client ever constructs
- Topics: VC-33.9, VC-33.3
- Evidence: "ADR-2009 legacy session-bearer fallback (UUID minted at login, plain-equality check against token_expiry) is a SERVER-side acceptance path in src/utils/auth.rs and src/services/nostr_service.rs:500. No client code in this tree ever constructs an X-Nostr-Token session header" (VC-33.3). "ADR-2009 lists authInterceptor, restClient, endpoints, ldpClient and contextLoader as client dependents of this realm, but none of them builds an X-Nostr-Token header" (VC-33.9).
- Failure: the acceptance path is live server code that takes a credential from a dead client generation; if a token UUID leaks or is brute-forceable (no hashing described, "plain-equality check"), it grants access with no client-side minting to constrain its shape.
- Confidence: medium (the material does not state whether tokens are cryptographically random or predictable)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT-adjacent)

### F-11 — Rejected position frames are not acknowledged, forcing the server into a rebuild loop
- Topics: VC-36.6, VC-39.3
- Evidence: "a rejected frame was already decoded and sanitised, then dropped with warn! and NOT acknowledged, so the server rebuilds the chunk after its ack timeout" (binary_protocol.rs:2247-2251). The server side: "oldest unacked for 2 s or more → Recover, window cleared, keyframe forced" (codescape-server/src/wire.rs:152-155).
- Failure: a client whose watermark is stuck (e.g., after a partial disconnect where FreshnessGate::reconnect never fires because the transport reconnects before poll sees the Disconnected event) silently drops every frame; the server forces keyframes every 2 seconds indefinitely, saturating the link and burning CPU on the server with no circuit-breaker.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the material describes both sides but does not assert this specific loop)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount" (NodePositionContext.tsx:13).

Similarly in the voice path: "the outcome-changing threshold is unset in Compose... which accepts nothing automatically, so neither Jev call alters a turn yet" (VC-35.7, docker-compose.unified.yml:25).

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **NIP-98 replay cache scope** — whether the single-use replay cache (ADR-2002, src/utils/nip98.rs) is per-process, shared across replicas, or backed by a store; multiple server instances behind a load balancer would each have their own cache, enabling cross-instance replay.

2. **settingsRetryManager internals** — the actual retry schedule, persistence mechanism and user-facing failure surface of the queued failed updates in VC-34.1; the material names it but never opens it.

3. **Session token generation** — whether the legacy UUID session bearers accepted by ADR-2009 are cryptographically random (v4) or predictable (v1); this determines whether F-10 is a theoretical or practical exploit.

4. **Backup and persistence of /vault and Solid pod data** — the material shows the data paths but says nothing about backup, replication or crash-consistency of the Solid/JSS store or the vault mount; a data-loss risk assessment needs this.

5. **Actual production deployment topology** — whether the docker-compose.unified.yml posture (RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1, VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE default 1, Codescape OpenAccess) is what runs in production or whether a separate production compose exists that overrides all three; the material only describes one compose file.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — production incident (client loss)

Scope note: the register below is reconstructed entirely from the as-built diagrams VC-33 to VC-40 and the ADR/estate markers they cite. Every claim carries the citation the material gives; where I reason past the material I say "inferred". The three R findings are ranked root-cause hypotheses; the F findings are latent defects noticed during the investigation, each independently checkable against the cited paths.

---

### R-1 — The auth bypass is armed by default and, once armed, checks no peer at all
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-33.3, VC-39.2
- Chain of events: the operator runs the unified/dev compose, which since ADR-2108 supplies `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` unasked (docker-compose.unified.yml:116); the variable is accepted as `1|true|TRUE` and `dev_full_bypass_active()` returns true (src/utils/auth.rs:116-121); on the dev/dev-auth build the hygiene hook is an empty stub (project/src/main.rs:169) and `verify_access` returns `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY` for every request with "no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (src/utils/auth.rs:203-218); the request reaches the server from a non-loopback peer through the dev nginx on port 3001, which the material documents as LAN-published for `/codescape/` (nginx.dev.conf:63-65); the attacker obtains full admin writes — settings, graph, memory cloud, speech socket (the other dev-bypass sites at src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25, src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66, src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165) — and destroys or exfiltrates data.
- Evidence: VC-36.15 states verbatim that ADR-2108 "flipped the default from :-0 to :-1 … boots an ARMED backend and the operator opts OUT with VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0"; that the bypass is "peer-agnostic" (src/utils/auth.rs:203-218); and the caveat that "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115). The release binary refuses to boot with the var present (project/src/main.rs:136, :161, exit 2), so the incident requires a dev/dev-auth binary — exactly what the default-armed compose runs. The speech socket's dev bearer, by contrast, keeps the loopback triple gate (src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:187-213), showing the stricter pattern exists and this path lacks it.
- Warning signs visible today: the register already calls the default "ARMED"; the peer-agnostic bypass is written down as fact; ADR-2108's own rationale (the HP headset is never a loopback peer) demonstrates non-loopback peers reach this server routinely.
- Confidence: high that the posture is exactly as described; the specific exposure path (which locations on 3001 reach the visionclaw service) is inferred beyond the documented `/codescape/` publication.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The shipped compose grants write access and public reads to any freshly generated Nostr key
- Topics: VC-33.6
- Chain of events: the deployed compose sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1` (docker-compose.unified.yml:132-133); identity requires no registration — any NIP-07 extension generates a key and the client signs per-request NIP-98 (VC-33.1, VC-33.2); the server resolves an unassigned authenticated pubkey to Editor via `UserRole::default_authenticated()` (src/models/rbac.rs:68-70, src/services/role_store.rs:188, 196-198); an unknown or malicious actor's first-ever key can read the whole corpus (public reads) and write graph/settings (Editor), including ownerless resources; the graph is vandalised or confidential corpus content is read before anyone notices.
- Evidence: VC-33.6's own DIVERGENCE note: "compose ships public reads open and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor by default, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed (no public reads) — two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env" (src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:135-141 `unwrap_or(false)` vs docker-compose.unified.yml:132). The client has no role model, only the binary `isPowerUser` flag, and "all real enforcement happens server-side per-request … a rejected write surfaces only as a 401/403 after the fact" (VC-33.6), so nothing pre-warns the operator. `/broker/inbox` and whoami are reachable by any signed-in user (src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:164-166).
- Warning signs visible today: the divergence between binary default and shipped env is already flagged; the material documents that the client's entire gating surface is one boolean.
- Confidence: high that the posture is as stated; that the unified compose is what served production is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The Nostr key is a single point of custody: stealable from browser storage, losable with a one-copy file, and the Solid pod is bound to it with no recovery path
- Topics: VC-33.4, VC-33.9, VC-33.8, VC-33.5
- Chain of events: (theft) the passkey-derived key exists as `nostr_privkey` in sessionStorage and `_localKeyHex` in memory during the session — logout's wipe list proves the storage locations (nostrAuthService.ts:460-464, :448-454); an XSS payload or malicious extension reads it and signs NIP-98 as the victim indefinitely, inheriting whatever role R-2 assigns; (loss) an authenticator without PRF hits `downloadKeyBackup()`, which "generates a random key and forces a file download, marking it the ONLY copy" (passkeyService.ts:349-377); the file is lost; the WebID is `did:nostr:hex-pubkey` and every pod operation "re-derives podUrl/webId … from the current NIP-98 identity" (SolidPodService.ts:171-206, client/src/__tests__/agent-pod/pod-provisioning.test.ts:104) — the pod (graph views, preferences, agent memory) is orphaned permanently; no rotation or recovery mechanism appears anywhere in VC-33.
- Evidence: VC-33.9's `LocalPrivateKeyMaterial` ("in-memory only, never sessionStorage") sits beside the sessionStorage key list that logout clears, so the hex key does transit sessionStorage during the session (nostrAuthService.ts:460-464); VC-33.4's fallback note "the ONLY copy"; VC-33.8's "WebID identity is bootstrapped FROM the Nostr pubkey … not via a browser OIDC redirect flow" and "No separate WebID session token is cached client-side".
- Warning signs visible today: the identity-bearing-type inventory itself — one key, browser-resident, pod-bound, no server-side identity record.
- Confidence: high on the mechanisms; the concrete incident (theft vs loss) is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Codescape's main port runs OpenAccess while executing commands
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.5, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "the main port runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open" (codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124, codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:71); typed commands execute on a blocking thread (codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:354-357); port 4100 is loopback-published by default (docker-compose.unified.yml:641) and reached via nginx with trust enabled.
- Failure: anyone who can reach 4100 (or a mis-published peer) runs commands, triggers egress to ASR/TTS/Loom with chosen text, and reads the mounted repositories.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, until P6)

### F-02 — Proxy trust with an empty peer list trusts every peer, and the last X-Forwarded-For hop becomes the rate-limit key
- Topics: VC-39.6
- Evidence: the decision tree sends "peer list empty? → yes, any peer → ON" (proxy.rs:271); config only rejects the reverse combination "--trust-proxy-from without --trust-proxy" (codescape-server/src/config.rs:237-239); under trust, the rate-limit key is the LAST X-Forwarded-For hop (proxy.rs:295-308).
- Failure: an operator passes `--trust-proxy` with no list — no boot error — and every client can forge X-Forwarded-For, rotating rate-limit keys to unbounded request rate on the open port, and influence forwarded host/prefix used to build NIP-98 URLs.
- Confidence: medium (branch is asserted; the misconfiguration is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — The Codescape presence shim accepts Schnorr signatures unverified; the claimed DID is only a label
- Topics: VC-39.7, VC-39.2
- Evidence: "/ws/presence shim accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label, bounds 64 members per room, 256 rooms" (codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:20-23, :65-67).
- Failure: an attacker claims any agent's DID in a presence room and spoofs co-presence/attention, misdirecting human decisions in a shared scene.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — The XR client sends NIP-98 authenticate and never reads an acknowledgement; there is no Authenticated state
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4
- Evidence: "the client sends the NIP-98 authenticate message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal" (xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:117); the state machine note "The client has no Authenticated state" (VC-36.4); the socket opens anonymous and useless, with the topology request sent before Connected (transport.rs:104-108).
- Failure: a rejected or mis-signed key silently degrades every write to the write-denied notice (VC-36.15) while the read stream continues, so the operator cannot distinguish key rejection from server fault, and an unauthenticated session keeps receiving the position stream.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-05 — Jev is live with cloud egress enabled while its threshold accepts nothing: voice transcripts leave the estate for zero behavioural benefit
- Topics: VC-35.7, VC-39.2, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "the outcome-changing threshold is unset in Compose (docker-compose.unified.yml:25), which accepts nothing automatically, so neither Jev call alters a turn yet, and activation is live" (speech_socket_handler.rs:404, src/services/jev_voice.rs:99); "ALLOW_CLOUD=1 sends the utterance via the proxy to the selected backend" (docker-compose.unified.yml:7-11, agentbox.toml:892); the Compose flag is on (docker-compose.unified.yml:21); threshold defaults to 1.0 (codescape-server/src/voice/jev.rs:166-171).
- Failure: confidential spoken content is shipped to a third-party backend with no compensating capability — a pure privacy/custody exposure discovered by a client audit.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the breach consequence is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-06 — The XR signing secret is passed on the ssh launch command line
- Topics: VC-36.16, VC-36.10
- Evidence: the deploy ceremony's launch call is `XR_BACKEND_WS=... XR_NOSTR_SECRET=<hex> godot --path xr-client …` (VC-36.16); a real key is required in practice because an ephemeral signer "cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin" (graph_scene.gd:477-479, docs/XR-client.md INVARIANT 6 via VC-36.10).
- Failure: the hex secret persists in the operator's shell history, the ssh session, and the process environment on the headset host, readable by anyone with that foothold — full signing identity theft.
- Confidence: medium (command shown in the material; exposure avenues inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — VisionClaw's settings assistant executes LLM-derived settings changes with no stated validate-before-execute gate
- Topics: VC-34.19, contrast VC-39.5
- Evidence: the un-keyworded path does `dispatchToSettingsLLM(command)` → `POST /bots/settings-command {command, settingsContext}` and "agent applies changes back through /api/settings/*" (VC-34.19); Codescape states the contrasting invariant explicitly — "a reply that fails validation is spoken as text, never executed" (codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:323-337) — and no equivalent is stated for VisionClaw; the ungoverned voice path also lands in the settings assistant when no agent DID is bound (speech_socket_handler.rs:868).
- Failure: a hallucinated or misheard command mutates shared physics/visual settings globally, persisted server-side, with only the after-the-fact RBAC check of R-2 standing behind it.
- Confidence: medium (absence of a stated gate; the execution path is stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The server still accepts the ADR-2009 legacy session-bearer realm that no client constructs
- Topics: VC-33.3, VC-33.9
- Evidence: "ADR-2009 legacy session-bearer fallback (UUID minted at login, plain-equality check against token_expiry) is a SERVER-side acceptance path in src/utils/auth.rs and src/services/nostr_service.rs:500. No client code in this tree ever constructs an X-Nostr-Token session header" (VC-33.3); the `LegacySessionBearer` class note (VC-33.9).
- Failure: any leaked minted UUID (log line, backup, cache) replays until expiry against a realm nothing in the tree exercises or tests; regressions in it are invisible.
- Confidence: medium (acceptance stated; exploitability inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT-adjacent note)

### F-09 — The .env sharing hazard: the armed default can escape the dev service, or brick the release boot
- Topics: VC-36.15
- Evidence: "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115); release refusal to boot, exit code 2, when the var is merely present (project/src/main.rs:136, :161); "still not in the *common-environment anchor and not in the production service" (docker-compose.unified.yml:109-112).
- Failure: (a) a shared .env plus a dev-auth binary runs armed outside compose — compounding R-1; (b) the var leaks into a prod .env and the release binary refuses to start — an availability outage at deploy time.
- Confidence: high (the mechanism is stated; the leak is the inferred trigger)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — The Codescape container mounts ten host repositories from an absolute default path while its port is OpenAccess
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "the estate mounts default to an absolute host volume path, overridable only by CODESCAPE_ESTATE_ROOT" (docker-compose.unified.yml:564); read-only views of the estate scene and ten member repositories (docker-compose.unified.yml:492, :538-619); F-01's OpenAccess.
- Failure: on a host where the absolute path resolves differently (or is overridden wrongly), the wrong trees are mounted read-only into a server whose main port has no authentication — silent source exfiltration.
- Confidence: medium (mount and open port stated; the misresolution is inferred)

### F-11 — Settings writes fail silently: a 401 raises only a CustomEvent, failures park in a retry manager
- Topics: VC-34.1, VC-33.1
- Evidence: "PUT returns 401 → window CustomEvent settings-auth-failed"; "PUT fails (network/5xx) → settingsRetryManager.addFailedUpdate(path, value, message)" (VC-34.1); the auth service clears a stale session when the extension does not reappear within 5 s (nostrAuthService.ts:373-378), after which every PUT 401s.

- Confidence: medium (paths stated; the silent-loss outcome inferred)

- Topics: VC-33.9

- Topics: VC-35.12

- Topics: VC-35.12
- Evidence: "Kokoros, Whisper-WebUI and xinference are UNTRACKED symlinks at the repo root, gitignored at .gitignore:227-229 and absent from .gitmodules … All three dangle … xinference is DIFFERENT — it has live compose consumers (docker-compose.unified.yml:160, :405, agentbox/docker-compose.yml:86)".
- Failure: the estate calls an inference endpoint with no pinned source or provenance to audit; endpoint substitution or model swap is undetectable from the repository, and embedding/inference traffic can be silently redirected.

### F-15 — memoryRoute ordering compares unrebased desktop `sentAt` against the headset's own wall clock
- Topics: VC-38.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: "a headset clock ahead of the desktop's makes desktop frames Stale until the desktop catches up and a clock behind does the reverse; nothing states the unit or bounds the skew" (Open note, VC-38.8); "sent_at is the headset's own wall clock, so a later desktop relay replaces it only while the two clocks agree, sentAt is not rebased" (xr-client/rust/src/memory_route.rs:1605, xr-client/scripts/memory_cloud_layer.gd:491).
- Failure: any clock skew makes the relayed route permanently Stale (or the headset's own route displaced); the feature silently never draws — availability, not data loss.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

## visionflow / critical

### F-01 — Replay protection for signed requests is process-local and evaporates on restart or a second replica
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Evidence: The NIP-98 verifier keeps a "process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation" (`nip98.rs:164`), and the cache "is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (`nip98.rs:210`). The panel itself states "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof." So replay protection exists only within one process's lifetime and one instance.
- Failure: VisionClaw restarts (deploy, crash, OOM) or a second replica is added; the seen-event-id memory is empty; a captured signed request replayed after the restart or against the other replica validates again — replayed governance or broker actions across the estate's auth boundary.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — disclosed as a limitation note in the panel, not tracked as Debt/Open

### F-02 — The copyrighted-source leak guard matches only root-level `.txt`, so its own stated threat passes one directory down
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: The stated threat: "The book's transition chapter reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo, so the check runs on tracked state" (`copyright-guard.yml:3`). The authors' own divergence note: "the guard polices only ROOT-LEVEL *.txt by regex. A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely." The allowlist names exactly two files (`copyright-guard.yml:34-45`).
- Failure: a contributor commits book source as `notes/ch9.txt`, `docs/sources/chapter.txt`, or any non-`.txt` file; the only automated copyright control passes; infringing text lands in the public repo and is one `cp -r` away from the published artefact.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VF-05.9)

- Topics: VF-05.14, VF-06.15, VF-06.12, VF-03.1
- Evidence: `check-augmentation-citations.cjs` "is no longer local-only by default: it sends excerpts of sibling checkouts to the shared proxy's selected backend (TypeSafe) unless VISIONFLOW_JEV_CLAIMS_LOCAL_ONLY=1 pins it local" (`scripts/check-a

## visionflow / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody / security class)

Scope note: the only evidence available is the diagrams-as-code corpus stamped at `verified_commit 4d361b58…` and the sibling revisions its frontmatter names. Everything below is grounded in a claim that corpus makes, with its citation; where I reason past the corpus I say "inferred". Where a diagram does not state order or concurrency, I do not treat it as asserted.

---

### R-1 — Replayed NIP-98 authentication accepted after restart or on a second replica
- Topics: VF-08.2, VF-08.7
- Chain of events: (1) A client-scoped request arrives authenticated by a signed NIP-98 event. (2) The verifying process validates the signature and records the event id in a single-use cache "atomically after validation" (`nip98.rs:164`). (3) That cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself (`nip98.rs:210`). (4) The process restarts, is redeployed, or a second replica serves the request — neither has the id. (5) The same captured event is replayed and validates again. (6) The replayed request performs an operation against the pod/broker in the client's name; the audit trail shows two legitimate-looking accepted requests. That production runs more than one process is inferred; the corpus does not state replica counts.
- Evidence: process-local cache with atomic id recording (`nip98.rs:164`); "declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself — see VC-03" (`nip98.rs:210`); "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof" (VF-08.2); the 2026-09-07 review "did not certify a live federation, image, hardware session or replica-wide authentication contract" (VF-08 preamble); the pod-tier matrix "is a capability guide, not proof that every tier implements the full library surface or shares its replay store" (VF-08.7).
- Warning signs visible today: the limitation is recorded verbatim in the compatibility view before any incident; nothing in this corpus claims a restart-surviving or replica-wide replay control.
- Confidence: medium — the single-process limitation is stated; the multi-process production path is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Sovereign-corpus divergence recurs with no detector; agents ground on a stale or disagreeing ontology
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-03.5, VF-03.11
- Chain of events: (1) ADR-2013 exists because four doors disagreed about one corpus and "no one can say which number is right" (`ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:22-26`). (2) The record is accepted / partial / **staged** — activation requires Loom `/health`, VisionClaw `/api/ontology/classes` and `vault build --stats` to report the same class count at a named commit. (3) Nothing polls those three counts; the trigger is a `review_trigger` no mechanism detects (`ADR-2013…:12`; VF-01.15 Open). (4) The baseline's 2026-10-12 note already diverges from the ADR's own D3: Ontomark is the producer of the shared build while VisionClaw's publish build job is "retired behind gates" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:53`) — a gated job that can re-enable a second publisher; ADR-2013 is unamended (VF-01.15 Tension). (5) agentbox consumes the corpus as a local read-only mount and Loom serves its bundle (`ADR-2013…:52-59`), so a stale or divergent bundle grounds agent decisions. (6) A client receives decisions or classifications made against a corpus whose counts disagree with the vault — the exact defect Invariant 8 defines (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340`). (7) Compounding: the vault repo `jjohare/visionGraph` is unreadable to the nightly collector without `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN` (VF-03.5), confirmed unreadable in the committed snapshot (`estate-health.json:658`), so repo-side corpus breakage is invisible to estate health.
- Evidence: as cited; "a divergence is a defect, not a rounding difference" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:335-336`; `ADR-2013…:72-75`); the original defect included Loom serving from "a stale bundle" (`ADR-2013…:22-26`).
- Warning signs visible today: the missing detector is an explicit Open item; the producer/consumer tension is recorded and unamended; the corpus repo's health row is unreadable in every snapshot.
- Confidence: medium — the detector absence and the tension are stated; recurrence and client impact are inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Custodial single-signer settlement operating ahead of its accepted canon
- Topics: VF-01.14, VF-01.11
- Chain of events: (1) ADR-2012 records exactly one financial substrate — DreamLab's sidestr sidechains (`ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:48-50`) — and remains proposed / partial / inactive (`:5-7`). (2) Custody is stated plainly: the root chain is LEVEL 1 with **one signer we operate** and is **custodial**; the k-of-n federation is rung 2 of the hardening path, "not reached" (`:84-87`). (3) Activation requires the owning substrate's deployment receipt (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:319-321`), but nothing enforces it: the compatibility matrix "still carries no settlement row" (`ADR-2012…:159-160`) and the drift counter carries no settlement axis. (4) This surface has previously published capability ahead of build-out — Lightning/NWC rail claims were live on the site until withdrawn 2026-10-02 (`ADR-2012…:51`, `:169`; `website/static/index.html:630`, `:1125`). (5) The settlement plumbing is active in the registry and roster (teller and sidestr kinds at `event-kind-registry.md:64-67`, `:79`; eight crates at `roster.json:73-80`), while agentbox still produces `sidestr:dreamlab` with upstream JavaScript and no overlay (`ADR-2012…:113-128`). (6) If settlement served a client under this posture, the single operated signer is a single point of failure: its compromise or loss moves or strands client ledger state with no federation to bound it.
- Evidence: as cited; P6's "no regulatory claim moves" (`:91`) is a posture statement, not a control.
- Warning signs visible today: one-signer custodial root recorded in the record itself; ratification explicitly partial; a documented history of premature public claims on this exact surface.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the custody posture and missing enforcement are stated; that client-facing settlement ran before acceptance is inference from surrounding activity, not a fact in the corpus.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Cross-repo fixture parity is structurally unverifiable in CI
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-05.6, VF-07.3
- Evidence: wiring sibling checkouts with a deploy key "is blocked on credentials that cannot be provisioned from the tree — so every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (`fixture-drift.yml:19`); the script's default canonical path "was REMOVED… Unqualified runs therefore exit 2, never 0" (`check-fixture-drift.sh:82`, `:93`); a compared release manifest "names WHICH corpus but not WHICH consumers matched" — `consumers` is always an empty array (`generate-release-manifest.sh:205`).
- Failure: a protocol-level change in one repo (kind semantics, auth event shape) drifts from the canonical corpus; no CI run ever compares consumers; the mesh interoperates on mismatched fixtures and misinterprets or drops governance/auth events in production.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — labelled DIVERGENCE and DOC-DRIFT)

### F-02 — The graded augmentation matrix is checked only by whoever remembers to run a local script
- Topics: VF-05.13, VF-05.11
- Evidence: the gate "resolves every citation against SIBLING checkouts, so it can only ever run locally"; `run-all.sh` is "wired to NO workflow" and reachable only via `package.json:17`; the suite "silently skips the real-checkout assertion on a hosted runner" (`augmentation-citations.test.cjs:77`; `tests/gates/run-all.sh:24`).
- Failure: a cell is raised to `measured` on a citation that does not exist or no longer resolves; nothing goes red; a client decision relies on an augmentation condition the substrate does not meet.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift and Debt)

### F-03 — Self-description counts are fail-open on every path that matters
- Topics: VF-02.8, VF-05.4, VF-05.3, VF-02 (invariants)
- Evidence: ADR-2005 is deliberately partial — two of four axes unenforced (`ADR-2005-drift-counter-allowlist-substrate-sourced.md:46`); an unavailable source blocks only that axis, never the gate (`drift-counter.mjs:266`); on a runner without the agentbox checkout "this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8); the tool-count axis's truth source `ontology-bridge.js` was deleted by ADR-2013 (`ADR-2013…:82-88`) and the README site was retired because "the bridge is retired in current Agentbox" (`allowlist.json:32`) — the axis now reads from a deleted file and degrades to unavailable; the page's CUDA figures "rest on review, not on a gate" (VF-02, no CUDA axis).
- Failure: the public site asserts capability counts that have silently drifted; a client contracts against figures no gate checks and that the fail-open counter cannot catch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — DIVERGENCE and DRIFT labels)

### F-04 — The copyright guard can be bypassed by directory depth or extension
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: "the guard polices only ROOT-LEVEL *.txt by regex. A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely" (VF-05.9); the guard exists because "the book's transition chapter reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (`copyright-guard.yml:3`).
- Failure: a copyrighted source file lands one directory deep, or under any non-`.txt` name, and is pushed to the public repo; the leak is a data/legal incident discovered externally.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — An autonomous night engine with a fabricated-evidence precedent and an unenforced human gate
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9
- Evidence: the 2026-09-07 operator audit found "no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03 or 09-04 candidates… the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters… the candidate halves are not [genuine]" (`LEDGER.md:20`); "nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape… no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9); `autoMerge false`, the branch prefix and the labels are "declarations the external engine honours" (`dream.config.json:77-78`, `:82`).
- Failure: a fabricated or unreviewed `dream/` candidate is merged — directly, or by a human trusting receipts the engine controls; deploy.yml's gates check the artefact, not the honesty of the evidence for it.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the precedent and the missing enforcement are stated; recurrence is inference.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — DIVERGENCE; the audit is an operator ledger row)

### F-06 — The dream ledger cannot prove absence, so engine actions are not fully auditable
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04 (Debt)
- Evidence: "No row at all for 09-14 through 09-26 — thirteen nights with no verdict of any kind… the silence cannot be told apart from rows that were written and lost" (`LEDGER.md:28-29`); rows reach main only by human commit (`f3d54a0`, `e52dba7`); "no mechanism in the ledger records why a night is absent" (`LEDGER.md:44-51` note).
- Failure: during incident response the record of what the nightly engine observed or changed is incomplete or unreconstructable; a faulty or malicious night is indistinguishable from a skipped one.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-07 — CI verdicts can mask real reds as `none` or `exempt`
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.11
- Evidence: the collector reads "one unfiltered page of 100 runs"; a busy repository "pushes its default-branch runs off it, and the row then reads none rather than its real state" (`estate-health.mjs:609`, `:614`); knowledgeGraph "moved from red to exempt by rule rather than by a passing run" and "no mechanism records why a row flips" (`estate-health.json:11`; `estate-health.json:480`, `:500-501`).
- Failure: a genuinely broken repo presents `none` or `exempt` ("no CI by design") in the snapshot the site renders and the dream cycle reads; hypotheses and client-facing health claims are formed on a masked state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, both)

### F-08 — The corpus repository is invisible to estate health, permanently
- Topics: VF-03.5, VF-03.11, VF-01.15
- Evidence: `jjohare/visionGraph` is cross-owner; the workflow's repo-scoped token gets a 404 and without `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN` "the row lands unreadable" (`estate-health.yml:86`); confirmed live: "summary.unreadable is 1" (`estate-health.json:16`; row at `:658`); the local-PAT trap means a readable local snapshot "is NOT evidence that the CI path works" (`estate-health.yml:92`).
- Failure: the vault holding the canonical corpus breaks, is renamed or force-pushed; nightly collection reports nothing about it, and no count-parity detector exists to catch the consequence (VF-01.15 Open).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (flagged as "THE ONE KNOWN HOLE" and "TRAP")

### F-09 — A red estate still publishes
- Topics: VF-03.2
- Evidence: "A red snapshot still deploys: no publication gate in deploy.yml consults estate health" and the workflow "deliberately does not fail on check's non-zero exit" (`ADR-2008-estate-health-collected-by-ci-read-by-the-dream-cycle.md:61`).
- Failure: substrates are failing while the public site and canon describe a healthy estate; clients act on a posture that was red at publication time.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as deliberate design, not flagged as a defect)

### F-10 — The Jev review sends sibling-checkout excerpts off-host by default since the System One unification
- Topics: VF-05.14, VF-06.15, VF-03 (since-last-stamp)
- Evidence: `check-augmentation-citations.cjs` "is no longer local-only by default: it sends excerpts of sibling checkouts to the shared proxy's selected backend (TypeSafe) unless `VISIONFLOW_JEV_CLAIMS_LOCAL_ONLY=1`" (`check-augmentation-citations.cjs:13-16`, `:96-106`; `docs/jev-option.md:12`); agentbox projects `VISIONFLOW_JEV=all` into service and shell environments (ADR-2155, image activation pending at stamp); the citations it reviews span sixteen repositories including a private one (VF-06.12: "two of them unfetchable here (a private repo, a local-only clone)").
- Failure: cited lines from a private sibling leave the estate to a backend whose trust boundary the corpus does not state; exposure is discovered only after the fact. Whether "the shared proxy's selected backend" is inside the operator's boundary is not stated in the material.
- Confidence: medium, inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact, "LIVE NOW", with no risk flag)

### F-11 — The contact-form transport is an unpinned vendored blob handling prospective-client messages
- Topics: VF-02.3, VF-02.2
- Evidence: `js/nostr-contact.js` is "a 57 KB vendored minified transport, rebuilt in dreamlab-ai-website and committed here, not compiled here" (`nostr-contact.js:1-2`); the form "gift-wraps the message (NIP-17) in the browser and publishes it to the DreamLab relay, nothing is stored by this site" (`contact.js:5-6`, `:112`); the asset gate checks presence, size and hashes the local copy, with no upstream pin to dreamlab-ai-website (`website-assets.mjs:177-197`).
- Failure: a compromised or quietly-changed rebuild in dreamlab-ai-website ships here as an ordinary content change; prospective-client correspondence is exfiltrated or altered, and no gate in this repo can detect the substitution because the receipt records rather than compares against external truth.
- Confidence: low, inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Applied-decision state is not correlatable end to end
- Topics: VF-08.4
- Evidence: "Complete correlated applied-decision proof remains open"; the agentbox journal "Does not mediate all tools or nightly egress"; "Relay acknowledgement does not prove external application" (all VF-08.4).
- Failure: a decision is signed, accepted and projected but never applied — or applied twice along a path the journal does not mediate; canon records a client-affecting decision as done while substrate state disagrees, and no receipt exists to settle it.

### F-13 — `verified_commit` can silently degrade to working-tree evidence
- Topics: VF-08.1
- Evidence: "A repository present in `sources:` but missing from that map resolves its citations against the working tree without complaint — a silent source-identity gap, not a verified stamp" (`compatibility-matrix.md:8`, per VF-08.1).
- Failure: a cross-estate claim is stamped verified against an unpinned local checkout; after an incident, re-checking the citation at the claimed revision shows different lines and the evidence chain cannot be reproduced.

- Topics: VF-02 (Debt), VF-07 (Debt)
- Evidence: `build-receipt.json` "is gitignored… exists in no commit and the four citations into it are read from the working tree whatever revision this topic declares; the numbers they carry are true of one local build, not of a revision" (`website/.gitignore:8`); same for `texput.log` (`.gitignore:50`).
- Failure: incident forensics cannot establish which exact tree any historical deploy published — the receipt that hashes the published artefact was never versioned.
- Confidence: high

### F-15 — Two dream evaluators still exit 0 after printing FAIL
- Topics: VF-04.4, VF-04.10
- Evidence: "The meta-tags and structured-data scans are unchanged and still exit 0 after printing their FAIL marker" (`dream-meta-tags-scan.sh:9`, `dream-structured-data-scan.sh:9`); the sibling scripts were fixed for exactly this assumption on 2026-10-12 (`4159e08`), showing the exit code had already once been read as the verdict.
- Failure: any consumer that reads exit status instead of the last-line sentinel — a future workflow step, a wrapper, the engine after a contract change — sees green on a failed meta/structured-data scan and publishes a page failing those checks.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact; the Drift note covers only the resolved scripts)

## visiongraph / critical

### F-01 — Cross-repo credential custody: long-lived tokens, no rotation rules, one token shared by two binary fetches
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-03.6, VG-04.1
- Evidence: VisionClaw's checkout of the private cross-account repo "needs ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_TOKEN" because `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot read it (`ontology-publish.yml:65-84`); visionGraph's workflow fetches both digest-pinned binaries (ontomark, oo-cert) "with the same `ACCESS_TOKEN`" and "neither pin has a recorded rotation rule" (`publish.yml:117`, `publish.yml:215`, marked Open); the deploy pushes to an external repo `DreamLab-AI/knowledgeGraph` (`publish.yml:515-523`) using a credential the material never names; and the corpus has a prior incident where "a live credential leak reach[ed] a build artefact" (`publish.yml:495-499`).
- Failure: any of these tokens expiring, leaking, or being revoked silently stops or hijacks all three pipelines; a compromised `ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_TOKEN` gives private-corpus read access to anything that can run a workflow in VisionClaw; there is no documented rotation procedure to execute during response.
- Confidence: high on the facts; medium (inferred) on the exposure surface — who holds write access to which repo is not shown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — rotation-rule absence is marked Open (VG-03.5); the cross-account token exposure itself is unmarked.

### F-02 — Retracting a page from the site does not retract it from the pod: `public || owl-class` vs `public` alone
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1, VG-04.1
- Evidence: the VisionClaw gate is `public || owl_class.is_some()` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`; `CLAUDE.md:10-11` after `bc4845c34`), while "the site publisher reads public alone" (`CLAUDE.md:10-11`, VG-01.3); the contract itself warns "changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`, Divergence note).
- Failure: an author unpublishes a sensitive page by removing `public: true`; the page retains a governed `owl-class`; the site drops it but VisionClaw's pod release keeps ingesting and serving it on the Solid pod. The retraction is invisible to the site pipeline.
- Confidence: high — both gate semantics are stated; no other mechanism removing owl-classed pages is shown anywhere.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — as a Divergence note (VG-01.3 note2); the concrete retraction-leak consequence is not spelled out.

### F-03 — Same source sha, two different producer programs: pinned ontomark vs vault built from HEAD
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.5
- Evidence: visionGraph pins the ontomark CLI by tag and sha256 (`publish.yml:48-51`, `publish.yml:98-117`); VisionClaw's job "builds `vault` from VisionClaw's own checkout" at default branch (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146`, `167-168`); the claim that ontomark "reproduces the vault verbs, flags and outputs byte for byte" exists only as a workflow comment the topic "cannot re-check" (`publish.yml:91-97`); ADR-VG-002's release manifest binding producer identity is proposed/inactive (VG-05.2 B1).
- Failure: a change landed in `vault`'s main (or an ontomark release fix) makes the pod ontology differ from the site built from the *identical* source sha; consumers correlating site and pod by `source_sha` get different graphs, and no manifest exists to detect the mismatch.
- Confidence: high on the divergence; medium (inferred) that outputs would actually differ.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Tension (VG-04.1).

### F-04 — Decision record says the space/Earth roots are private drafts "not deployed"; the gate requires them published
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: ADR-VG-004:35 says "the two roots exist as private draft classes" and "the changes are not deployed"; the gate fails unless each root is in the public search index (`check-space-domains.mjs:23-24`) and "every accounted identity public draft" (`check-space-domains.mjs:39-73`); the workflow deploys on push (`publish.yml:309-310`, `publish.yml:515-523`).
- Failure: either the record is false — draft classes were published to narrativegoldmine.com to satisfy the gate without the ADR being updated — or the gate cannot pass and every deploy fails. An owner relying on "not deployed" to keep drafts private is being misled by the governing record.
- Confidence: high on the contradiction; which side is true is not determinable from the material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (VG-01.7 note3, VG-03.5).

### F-05 — The 2026-09-07 boundary cases (truthy string flags, private-ancestor leakage, IRI divergence) were audited against a pipeline that no longer exists
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Evidence: B2 ("'false' as a Python truthy value"), B3 (private-ancestor leakage into derived exports) and B4 (asserted/inferred IRI divergence) are reproducible boundary cases (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`); the audited Python publisher is deleted (ADR-VG-003); ADR-VG-001 remains proposed/inactive and its acceptance conditions are "NOT certified by that retirement" (`ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:18-21`); the "57 passed" result is explicitly "NOT a fresh census" (VG-05.3).
- Failure: `public: "false"` as a string, or a private ancestor of a public page, behaves differently in the Rust/ontomark path than in the audited path; nothing in the current gate set (artifact presence, ≥1000 floors, domain counts) tests inclusion semantics, so a private page ships and every check is green.
- Confidence: medium — the cases are stated unresolved; current-parser behavior is unknown (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — as unresolved/Drift notes; the "never re-verified against the replacement parser" implication is unmarked.

### F-06 — The gates cannot detect under-production: `--vault all` omission and fixed 1000-item floors both pass green
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: omitting `--vault all` "silently falls back to knowledge-only and every later gate still passes green" (`publish.yml:152-157`, stated as a note); the substance check is three fixed floors of 1000 — search-index entries, api/pages count, ontology.ttl line count (`publish.yml:293-302`) — added because the retired Python pipeline "emitted an empty bundle … and still exited 0" (`publish.yml:91-93`); a halved corpus or a missing working/ vault clears all three.
- Failure: a refactor drops the flag, or a producer regression drops most pages; CI stays green; narrativegoldmine.com silently loses the entire working vault or most of the corpus, and VisionClaw then publishes the same reduced graph from the "validated" sha.
- Confidence: high on the stated behaviour; the triggering edit is scenario.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — stated as an invariant note (`publish.yml:152-157`); the combined "gates pass green on under-production" reading is not flagged as a defect.

### F-07 — The supply-chain domain gate silently drops its identity checks when the catalogue is absent
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: "catalogue identity checks skipped when the catalogue is absent" (`check-supply-chain-domain.mjs`, cited at VG-03.5 SUPPLY).
- Failure: a producer change or naming change omits the catalogue artefact; the domain-8 gate runs green minus its identity assertions; an incomplete or wrong-identity Supply Chains domain ships to production with no red build.
- Confidence: medium-high — behaviour stated, consequence inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-08 — Deploy-trigger drift: pushes to the explorer source or static/ ship to production, contradicting the README
- Topics: VG-01.1
- Evidence: README lists `knowledge/**`, `quartz/**` and `ontology/**` as the publish triggers (`README.md:130-131`); the workflow also fires on `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/**` and `static/**` (`publish.yml:24-30`); "a push to main deploys to narrativegoldmine.com" (`CLAUDE.md:16`).
- Failure: an editor treats explorer or static changes as inert, pushes WIP to main, and a half-finished SPA deploys live; the concurrency group serialises deploys but does not prevent this one.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (VG-01.1 note3).

### F-09 — Production change authority is contradictory: CLAUDE.md forbids agent pushes, a workflow comment authorises them
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: invariant 7: "agents NEVER push this repo — a push to main deploys to narrativegoldmine.com" (`CLAUDE.md:16`, VG-05.1); the workflow header says the owner authorised estate deployment "during the ontomark resumption on 2026-10-11" and the authorisation "is recorded in a comment, not in a governing document" (`publish.yml:17-18`, marked Open).
- Failure: during an incident, one executor following CLAUDE.md refuses or delays estate pushes while another following the workflow header performs them; who may push to a live-deploying branch is undefined exactly when it matters.
- Confidence: high on the contradiction.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Open (VG-03.1).

### F-10 — ADRs marked "staged" are already enforced by every deploy, and one's own activation trigger has already fired
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-04.1, VG-01.7
- Evidence: ADR-VG-008 activation is `staged` (`ADR-VG-008:5-7`) while its gate runs on every deploy (`publish.yml:312-313`); ADR-2147 is `staged` (`ADR-2147:7`) while both pipelines run the certify steps (`publish.yml:158-163`, `publish.yml:210-227`, `ontology-publish.yml:176-208`); its `review_trigger` — "the `vault certify` step exiting 0 on a published generation" (`ADR-2147:13`) — is what every run does.
- Failure: the status registers describe live gating mechanisms as not-yet-live; if either ADR is later rejected per its staged status, deploys break or a running mechanism loses its warrant; audit and rollback decisions made from the records are wrong.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Tension (VG-03.5, VG-04.1); the fired-trigger point is implied, not marked.

### F-11 — corpus-sync provenance has a main-branch fallback and no reconciliation if the dispatch is lost
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1
- Evidence: source repo resolves "inputs, then client_payload, then the jjohare/visionGraph default" (`ontology-publish.yml:30`); the exact-sha payload exists only when sent by publish.yml after a successful deploy (`publish.yml:525-531`); no retry, re-dispatch or pod/site reconciliation path appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: a manual or re-run of ontology-publish.yml without inputs builds visionGraph@`main` — content the site pipeline never validated — and publishes it to the pod; conversely, a failed dispatch after a successful deploy leaves the pod permanently stale with nothing that detects the site/pod divergence.
- Confidence: medium-high on the fallback (resolution order stated); medium (inferred from absence) on the no-reconciliation gap.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-12 — Single point of failure: one pinned release fetched with one token, no fallback producer, no rotation runbook
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-03.1
- Evidence: "a run that cannot obtain the pinned release fails, because nothing else produces the site's artefacts" (`publish.yml:100-105`, `publish.yml:41-51`); both binaries are fetched with the same `ACCESS_TOKEN` (`publish.yml:117`) with no recorded rotation rule (Open); there is deliberately no build-from-source fallback.
- Failure: the release asset is unavailable, the tag is yanked, or the token expires/is suspended — every deploy fails indefinitely, the site and the pod both stop updating, and no documented alternate producer or rotation procedure exists to recover with.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; the outage scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — fail-closed is stated as an invariant; rotation absence is marked Open; the aggregate SPOF is unmarked.

### F-13 — Contradiction: the runner "cannot reach the LAN pod", yet the deploy job curl-PUTs directly to SOLID_POD_URL
- Topics: VG-04.1
- Evidence: "pull model, not push — a GitHub-hosted runner cannot reach the LAN pod — no self-hosted runner is registered" (`ADR-2106:27`, deploy-jss condition `ontology-publish.yml:360`); yet deploy-jss performs `curl -X PUT SOLID_POD_URL + JSS_PUBLIC_PATH/visionflow.ttl…` in a loop (`ontology-publish.yml:401-420`).
- Failure: either the record is stale or the pod's write endpoint is internet-reachable behind a token; if the latter, the ADR's security premise is false and a leaked pod credential allows arbitrary overwrite of the published ontology. The material never reconciles the two statements.
- Confidence: low-medium — inferred; JSS_PUBLIC_PATH may be a separate public relay.
- Severity: medium (high if the pod write path is truly exposed)
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-14 — Two divergent copies of the shipping explorer, neither usable as the other's replacement
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.2, VG-06.4, VG-06.5
- Evidence: 39 of 108 shared files differ; the shipping tree pins vowl-wasm 0.2.2 while the sibling pins 0.1.1 (`WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:25` vs `explorer/modern/package.json:20`); the sibling has a `LOOM` export the shipping tree lacks and lacks the basename routing the shipping tree has (VG-06.4); nine domains vs six (`scope.ts:75-86` vs `scope.ts:72-80`); the sibling is "built by nothing in that repo" (KG-04.6).
- Failure: an incident requiring an explorer revert or hotfix has no second buildable copy; anyone building the public knowledgeGraph copy gets an older engine, no sub-path routing and three missing domains; because edits strand in both directions, fixes silently accumulate on the wrong tree.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Debt/Drift/Divergence across VG-06.

### F-15 — The governing document layer systematically describes the retired system in the present tense
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.4, VG-01.5, VG-05.1, VG-06.3, VG-01.7, VG-01.6
- Evidence: `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16` still says the publisher "reads JSON-LD Page vc:public" (a rejected format, `README.md:50`), `:39` present-tense `public_projection.py`, `:41` a scoped stylesheet whose file was deleted — all covered only by a historical banner (`:3-8`); `CLAUDE.md:23` says the pin is v0.5.0 while `publish.yml:49` pins v0.6.0; `vault.toml:30,71` names `vault build` as producer; ADR-VG-003:16-18 names the `vault` CLI as the only parser; `vocabulary.yaml:134,344-345` say six roots against nine listed (`:350-353`); `README.md:104-106` names 3 of 6 working bases; `CAPABILITIES.md:24` measures the deleted `python -m pipeline.build`.
- Failure: this contract is the governing document every topic cites; an operator or agent following it during an incident reintroduces the rejected format or the retired producer (caught only by the consumer-contract test at `publish.yml:88-89`), pins the wrong release, or validates against the wrong parser. The doc layer cannot be trusted as an operating procedure.
- Confidence: high — every item stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — individually marked Drift/DOC-DRIFT; the systemic "governing layer unreliable" reading is unmarked.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether ontomark and `vault` actually produce byte-identical output — the parity claim at `publish.yml:91-97` references a parity corpus this material does not contain.
2. The current parser's behaviour on the B2–B4 boundary cases (string `public: "false"`, private ancestors, IRI representation), including Quartz's own JS truthiness for the `public` key in `ExplicitPublish`.
3. The scopes, storage and audit trail of the secrets (`ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_TOKEN`, `ACCESS_TOKEN`, the external-repo deploy credential) and whether any rotation runbook exists outside `publish.yml`.
4. Whether the current deploy to gh-pages is atomic for simultaneous live readers, and whether any rollback, deletion or interrupted-activation scenario has ever been exercised on the current stack (ADR-VG-002's demos remain undone).
5. The actual size of the deployed site against the 1000 MB gate — the 961 MB asset store suggests thin headroom, but no total is given — and the network topology of the pod/JSS write path (F-13).

## visiongraph / premortem

# Post-mortem — candidate root causes and latent defects

Basis: the topic set VG-01 through VG-06, verified against `visiongraph` @ `7b0875f2`, `visionclaw` @ `8692b12b`, `knowledgegraph` @ `1edcfc93`. Everything below is drawn from those citations; where a chain requires reasoning past the material it is labelled inferred. Every finding should be re-verified against code before action, per instruction.

---

### R-1 — Unpublication is per-consumer: removing `public: true` removes a page from the site but not from the knowledge graph while `owl-class` remains
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1, VG-04.1, VG-05.2
- Chain of events:
  1. A page carrying client-sensitive content has frontmatter `owl-class` set (it is an ontology class) and no `public: true`, or `public: true` is removed to unpublish it.
  2. The operator or agent verifies removal where they can see it: the site. `/notes/` is gated on typed `public: true` (`../visionGraph/README.md:142-145`; `quartz/README.md:25-26` `ExplicitPublish({ key: "public" })`). The page disappears from the site. Removal looks confirmed.
  3. But the ingest gate in code is `PageMeta::is_kg_included = public || owl_class.is_some()` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`), and VisionClaw's documented interface has always been `public` **or** `owl-class` (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`, VC-21). The page is still in the ontology projection.
  4. The next deploy dispatches `corpus-sync` with the exact source sha (`publish.yml:525-531`); VisionClaw rebuilds its ontology release from that sha (`ontology-publish.yml:89-95`) and PUTs the artefacts to the Solid pod (`ontology-publish.yml:401-420`). The private page is now live on the pod and in the VisionClaw release.
  5. Nothing detects the divergence: the four consumers have deliberately distinct inclusion policies, and the contract itself states "changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer" (VG-01.3 note2, `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`).
- Evidence: the code gate (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`); the 17-day window in which the agent rules wrongly read `public` alone as the KG gate, 2026-10-01 to `bc4845c34` 2026-10-18 (VG-01.3, VG-05.1); agentbox has a further distinct private-page projection (VG-01.3); ADR-VG-001, which would give every input an explicit disposition under a named policy and require boolean publication metadata, is still `proposed`/`partial`/`inactive` (`docs/adr/README.md:7-8`, VG-05.2 A1–A2); ADR-VG-002's requirement to *demonstrate deletion* is likewise inactive (VG-05.2 B2).
- Warning signs visible today: the bolded DIVERGENCE note in VG-01.3; the correction history of invariant 1 (VG-05.1); identity separation mandated (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:14`) with no release manifest binding consumers to one generation.
- Confidence: high — the gate behaviour and the consumer divergence are stated, not inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — One source sha, two producers: the pod is built by an unpinned `vault` from VisionClaw's default branch while the site is built by digest-pinned `ontomark`; parity is asserted only in a workflow comment
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1, VG-03.5, VG-05.4
- Chain of events:
  1. A deploy succeeds; the dispatch pins the *corpus* revision (`publish.yml:525-531`) but not the *converter*.
  2. visionGraph's publisher runs the released `ontomark` CLI pinned by tag and sha256 (`publish.yml:48-51`). VisionClaw's job builds `vault` from its own default-branch checkout (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146`, `ontology-publish.yml:167-168`). If the dispatch payload's sha is absent, the corpus checkout itself falls back to `main` (`ontology-publish.yml:89-95`).
  3. A behaviour-changing commit lands on VisionClaw `main` — e.g. an export or inference change touching how private ancestors are rendered (boundary case B3, unresolved). The site, built by the pinned `ontomark`, is unaffected.
  4. The next dispatch builds the *same source sha* through the *different producer*; the pod release diverges from the site silently. The only parity evidence is a comment claiming byte-for-byte reproduction "against a parity corpus" (`publish.yml:91-97`), which the material explicitly says it cannot re-check (VG-04.1 Tension).
  5. The retired safety layer makes this worse: the 2026-09-07 "shared boundary" — `public_projection.py` with typed preflight and known-private-reference filtering before inference and every export stage (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:39`) — belonged to the deleted Python publisher (VG-05.4 note3). No equivalent filter is cited for `ontomark` or `vault`.
  6. Both pipelines re-check with the same pinned `oo-cert`, but the certificate covers the classifier's derivation closure (`publish.yml:158-163`) — it certifies *derivation*, not *inclusion*. A graph that wrongly includes a private page still certifies green.
- Evidence: the VG-04.1 Tension verbatim ("the site and the pod release can be produced by different producers from the same source sha"); the `or main` fallback (`ontology-publish.yml:89-95`); the parity comment's unverifiability (`publish.yml:91-97`); the historical banner retiring the contract's implementation descriptions (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8`); B3 "private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports" recorded as an open boundary case with "NO real private-page disclosure established" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`).
- Warning signs visible today: the Tension is labelled as such today; ADR-2147 is `staged` while the publish jobs already run the certify steps (`docs/adr/ADR-2147-...md:7`, `:13`).
- Confidence: medium — the producer divergence and the retired filter are stated; the behaviour-changing `vault` commit is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Credential material reaches the pod channel: the only artefact secret-scan guards `www`, and the VisionClaw/pod pipeline has no cited scan at all
- Topics: VG-03.6, VG-04.1, VG-03.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Precedent exists: the corpus once had a live credential leak reach a build artefact (`publish.yml:495-499`, referencing `docs/migration-2026-09-22/credential-redaction.md`).
  2. The defence added is `check-secrets.sh www` (`publish.yml:503-504`) — it scans the site tree only, and exists precisely because `ontomark validate` guards only the source (VG-03.6 drift note).
  3. The pod channel is a separate pipeline: `vault build --vault knowledge` → `pack-pod-resources.py` → `curl -X PUT` of `visionflow.ttl`, `context.jsonld`, `ontology.jsonld`, `index.jsonld` to `SOLID_POD_URL` (`ontology-publish.yml:167-174`, `:242-247`, `:401-420`). The material cites no secret scan anywhere in that chain.
  4. A credential string enters a `knowledge/` page's frontmatter or body (pasted into a research note, or into an `owl-class` page). If `ontomark` excludes it from the site projection, it never reaches `www`, `check-secrets.sh` passes on a tree that does not contain it — and `vault`'s exports serialise it into TTL/JSON-LD that is PUT to a publicly readable pod.
- Evidence: `publish.yml:495-504`; the validate-vs-artefact split (VG-03.6); the absence of any scan step in the cited VisionClaw job sequence (VG-04.1); the R-1 gate means `owl-class`-only pages flow to the pod while being absent from `www`.
- Warning signs visible today: the redaction incident is cited in the workflow itself; the scan's scope (`www` only) is stated; the pod PUT loop is external and unverified by any gate in the material.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the scan gap is stated by omission, the leak path is a constructed chain from stated parts.
- Severity: high
