## visiontouch-rs / critical

Scope note: "production" here means attended operation of the force-feedback arm (Calibration Studio, Mass Lab) plus the evidence and legal posture that surround it. Ranking below is by incident potential: physical/legal first, then evidence custody, then correctness, then operability and maintainability.

### F-01 — No hardware watchdog: a host freeze or process kill leaves the last motor offsets driving the arm
- Topics: VT-02, VT-02.5, VT-03.4
- Evidence: VT-02 business text: "It cannot protect against the computer itself freezing or the process being killed, because no timer lives in the hardware that anyone has established." VT-03.4 note: "expiry is only evaluated when the worker runs, there is no hardware watchdog (crates/device/src/lib.rs:59-60 and docs/spec/12-force-feedback.md:91-92)". Neutral-command delivery exists only via graceful shutdown/close/Drop (crates/hal/src/device.rs:357-378).
- Failure: The process is armed at up to 2.0 N / 300 counts (Studio rung), the host scheduler stalls or the process is SIGKILLed. Every software interlock — expiry, heartbeat, hold — lives in the frozen process. The arm holds the last written offsets until a human physically intervenes; nothing in the system times out.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the watchdog absence is marked DEBT in VT-03.4; the freeze/kill consequence in VT-02 prose is stated without any marker.

### F-02 — A failed shutdown clears the pending sequence, so Drop can never resend neutral packets
- Topics: VT-02.5
- Evidence: VT-02.5 note: "DEBT: end_force_session clears the pending sequence even when shutdown failed, so Drop cannot resend neutral packets, it only retries the port release (crates/hal/src/device.rs:345 and crates/hal/src/device.rs:326-328)". Higher layers only make the error outrank others (VT-02.5 prose).
- Failure: The two zero-packet shutdown fails halfway (one neutral packet lost). The sequence is cleared, Drop's retry path is structurally unable to resend, and the connection is unhealthy so no in-place retry exists. The arm keeps the last offsets — potentially the rung's full command — while the software reports only "reconnect needed". Recovery requires a fresh connect plus re-registration and re-climb, during which the arm is still loaded.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VT-02.5)

### F-03 — On Wayland a latched Space hold keeps a powered attempt running after focus loss
- Topics: VT-08.1
- Evidence: VT-08.1 note: "DEBT: on Wayland a held Space can stay latched after focus loss because no key-up or focus event arrives, only the 250 ms heartbeat and Stop remain (apps/calibration/src/main.rs:323-330)". The heartbeat is bumped by the UI thread every frame (apps/calibration/src/main.rs:214-215), and an unfocused window can keep rendering.
- Failure: Operator holds Space on a wall (time bound 180 s per apps/calibration/README.md:58) or lift attempt, an alt-tab or notification steals focus, no key-up arrives, and the window keeps rendering so the heartbeat stays fresh. Neither hold-released nor ui-heartbeat-stale fires; the attempt runs to its class time bound with the operator believing release stopped it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VT-08.1)

### F-04 — The entire force ladder has only ever run against a simulated plant; the first owner session is the first real test
- Topics: VT-08 (intro), VT-08.2, VT-08.6
- Evidence: VT-08 intro: "ADR-0003 is accepted and complete but its activation is staged until an owner session exists, so everything below is verified against a simulated plant only." VT-08.6 N1: the simulator arms instantly while the real device runs 13 ms of handshake sleeps "so arming latency is never exercised headless". N2: smoke passes `held true`, so release and stale-heartbeat interlocks "are covered only by controller unit tests, not by the end-to-end run". VT-08.2 prose: "Three powered sessions on 2026-10-16 produced no perceptible force."
- Failure: The first attended sitting exercises real arming latency, real I/O cadence against the 25 ms gap guard (VT-08.1 OPEN) and real gains simultaneously. Every "verified" property in the ladder is untested in the configuration that matters, and the operator's hand plus a hand-typed gauge (see F-10) are the only instruments.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN/staged in VT-08.6 N4; DEBT N1; TENSION N2)

### F-05 — Millimetre-tolerance interlocks are judged by a position model with centimetre-scale error and an unchecked unwrap assumption
- Topics: VT-03.2, VT-08.3, VT-08.4
- Evidence: VT-03.2: "Expected error at the tip is centimetres" and the angle gains are "a black-box fit, physical accuracy is unestablished (docs/spec/12-force-feedback.md:35-41)"; DEBT: "unwrapping assumes less than half a counter revolution between samples, nothing checks it (crates/device/src/lib.rs:196-197)". Against that: return check "capture within 2 mm, spread of captures within 2 mm" (apps/calibration/src/registration.rs:16), excursion and 50 mm/s speed interlocks (apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:53-70), and a historical phantom 50 mm/s stop from cadence alone (P-0065, VT-08.3 N2).
- Failure: The 2 mm return gate passes or fails on model error rather than physical pose; the excursion interlock during a force step mis-measures by centimetres; a sampling gap large enough to violate the half-revolution assumption wraps a counter and flips the computed position, so a spring force points the wrong way at up to the current rung cap. The safety interlocks are inside the noise floor of the sensor model they depend on.
- Confidence: high (both facts stated; the failure is their direct arithmetic combination)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the fit accuracy (EXTERNAL) and unwrap (DEBT) are marked; the combination with mm-tolerance interlocks is not.

### F-06 — Thermal attempts run up to 660 s at 1.0 N with no held-key stop
- Topics: VT-08.3
- Evidence: VT-08.3 note: "DEBT: a thermal attempt has no hold, only a Confirm every 30 s with a 10 s grace, so release is no longer a stop and the arm relies on Confirm and the 660 s bound (apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:26-28 and apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:61)". README time bounds include 660 s (apps/calibration/README.md:58).
- Failure: The operator's hand is in the arm for eleven minutes of continuous commanded force with no dead-man control; releasing the hand or stepping away stops nothing. A missed or fumbled Confirm costs up to 10 s of grace on top. This inverts the "held attempts with operator interlocks" principle the rest of the ladder is built on.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VT-08.3)

### F-07 — The clean-room separation rule was violated: decompilation entries name a coding agent in the specification role
- Topics: VT-01.3
- Evidence: VT-01.3 note: "DIVERGENCE: PROVENANCE requires decompilation by the spec team never the implementer (docs/clean-room/PROVENANCE.md:20) while both entries name a coding agent in the specification role (docs/clean-room/DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:42 and :82)". Both D-0001 and D-0002 exist and their findings shaped the spec (VT-01.3 flow).
- Failure: The one control that keeps implementer knowledge separate from decompiled knowledge has already failed, twice, in the only two decompilation events so far. The method section of the planned paper (VT-07.1: outputs include "an academic paper") and the legal position rest on a control the project's own log contradicts. If the same agent that analysed the binary also produced Rust, the clean-room claim is unsupportable as documented.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VT-01.3)

### F-08 — The legal trigger "any decompilation re-opens the review" has fired twice; the review was never re-opened
- Topics: VT-01.4
- Evidence: VT-01.4 note: "DIVERGENCE: T3 has fired twice (D-0001 and D-0002) but the position document is unchanged (docs/legal/POSITION.md:46 vs docs/clean-room/DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:11)". Trigger T3 is "any decompilation of a vendor binary (docs/legal/POSITION.md:46)".
- Failure: The project is operating outside its own documented legal process. Its own records show the mandatory review trigger occurred on 2026-10-16 and the position document was not revisited; if the posture is ever challenged, the documented diligence is contradicted by the repository itself.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VT-01.4)

### F-09 — Attempt evidence is written only at finish; a crash destroys the record of what the arm was commanded to do
- Topics: VT-08.1, VT-08.5
- Evidence: VT-08 intro: "demand zeroed on the stopping tick, output disarmed, and only then evidence written". VT-08.1: "EV->>EV: score, then OutputOff token, then write CSV and JSON (apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:990-994)". The ADR's activation trigger is "a committed owner record" (VT-08.6 N4), i.e. this evidence.
- Failure: The process dies mid-attempt (see F-01 for how). The attempt's telemetry — command levels, forces, interlock state, the very data an incident investigation needs — exists only in memory until finish and is lost exactly when something went wrong. ADR-0003's staged activation also depends on a committed record that a crash silently prevents.
- Confidence: high (the write-at-finish ordering is stated; that telemetry is held in memory until then is the direct implication)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Rung unlocking is gated by hand-typed gauge digits with no plausibility check in the scoring formula
- Topics: VT-08.2, VT-08.5
- Evidence: VT-08.2: scoring is purely "Over above 1.2c plus 0.05, Pass from 0.8c minus 0.05, None under 0.05 N, else Under (apps/calibration/src/protocol.rs:380-390)". Readings are typed by the operator; the only validation shown is unit checking (apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:286 unit_allowed; protocol.rs:394-403). No range, repeat-reading or cross-check appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: At the 0.1 N rung the gauge reads 1.10 N (gain badly wrong); the operator types 0.11 — a dropped decimal — which lands in the Pass band [0.03, 0.17]. The 0.25 N rung unlocks on false data and the arm delivers roughly 2.75 N nominal-equivalent before the next, correctly typed reading catches it. The one instrument that stands between the model and reality is transcribed by hand and trusted verbatim.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the formula is stated in full and contains no plausibility term; absence of any other check is an absence-based inference)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The Over lockout is per-connection only: a reconnect wipes an over-delivery verdict and the climb restarts
- Topics: VT-08.2
- Evidence: VT-08.2: "only a reconnect clears Over: Session::reconnect forgets everything (apps/calibration/src/protocol.rs:528-530)"; business text: "Disconnecting forgets everything, so each sitting starts from the bottom." N5 records that ADR, spec and code agree the lock "lasts the whole connection" — nothing persists across connections.
- Failure: An Over verdict means the measured force exceeded 1.2× command plus 0.05 N — the model's gain is demonstrably unsafe on this arm. The operator unplugs and reconnects: Over is gone, and the same wrong gains face the same ladder again the same afternoon. Evidence of each Over exists per session, but no cross-session stop, flag or halt exists anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated; the risk framing is unmarked)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (N5 marks the per-connection invariant as agreed design, not as a bypass risk)

### F-12 — ADR-0002's continuous-force cap is relied on by the decision record and enforced by no code
- Topics: VT-03.1
- Evidence: VT-03.1 note: "DEBT: CONTINUOUS_FORCE_N is documented as the cap for content and ADR-0002 relies on it (docs/adr/ADR-0002-layered-software-force-envelope.md:38) but no code reads the constant, only crates/device/src/lib.rs:50 mentions it". The 10 s >1.0 N budget exists only in the Studio's controller (apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:14 and :519-527).
- Failure: Any consumer other than the Studio — Mass Lab today, anything built on the SDK tomorrow — can hold its default 0.5 N indefinitely with no duty-cycle enforcement at all. The safety argument in the accepted ADR cites a mechanism that does not exist; a reviewer trusting ADR-0002 over the code audits a phantom control.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VT-03.1)

### F-13 — Primary observation evidence lives outside the repository on one host, and the recorder silently continues after a failed write
- Topics: VT-06.2
- Evidence: VT-06.2: "Capture files ... land under the ignored vendor-original directory, so the raw logs never enter the tree". Note: "DEBT: a failed log write only prints a diagnostic and the run carries on with an incomplete capture (tools/port-recorder/src/lib.rs:279-281)". The P-NNNN rows that anchor the paper and the spec cite this evidence (VT-01.1).
- Failure: Disk fills or a permission error mid-capture: the reference run continues, the JSONL is silently truncated, and facts derived from the tail of the session rest on evidence that was never completely written. Separately, the raw captures backing every OBSERVE row exist only on the owner's machine in an ignored directory — a single disk failure erases the primary evidence for the clean-room claim, with no copy in the repository by design.
- Confidence: high (both facts stated; the custody exposure is their direct combination)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the failed-write behaviour is marked DEBT; the single-host custody exposure is not.

### F-14 — The OutputOff token is ignored by every writer; the output-off-for-evidence rule hangs on one call site
- Topics: VT-08.5
- Evidence: VT-08.5 note: "DEBT: every writer takes the OutputOff as `_off` and never reads it, so the guarantee rests on the one confirm call site (apps/calibration/src/evidence.rs:490 and apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:495)". ADR-0003 requires output off for readings (docs/adr/ADR-0003-staged-force-verification-ladder.md:52-53).
- Failure: The type-level guarantee advertised in the intro ("an OutputOff token making the order a type rule") is decorative: a future writer — a new evidence file, a control-socket path — can accept the token without the confirming call and compile cleanly, writing evidence while motors are armed. The rule degrades silently under maintenance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VT-08.5)

### F-15 — The canon's verification stamp contradicts its own record: ADR-2017's verified_commit predates the commit that added the ADR
- Topics: VT-07.1
- Evidence: VT-07.1 note: "DOC-DRIFT: ADR-2017 verified_commit 4a8d4b5 predates the commit that added the ADR (1e3bdd0) (docs/adr/ADR-2017-haptic-input-device-is-a-supporting-study.md:10)". Related N2: the same decision is "accepted, complete, live" in the study repository but "accepted, implementation none" in the canon (ADR-0006:5-7 vs ADR-2017:5-6). The ADR staleness check is gate step G1 (scripts/quality-gate.sh:11).
- Failure: A verification commit older than the file's introduction certifies nothing about the current text — the estate's own provenance mechanism (verified_commit, gate G1) vouches for a state that cannot have contained the content it stamps. Anyone trusting these stamps across the estate, including the cross-repo ADR-2017 anchors read "at VisionClaw HEAD" (VT-07.3), inherits a false assurance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, VT-07.1; the lifecycle divergence separately marked)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Write atomicity and durability of evidence files.** Whether `session.json` rewrites and attempt CSV/JSON writes use temp-file-and-rename and fsync; a crash mid-rewrite could corrupt the session record that F-09 does not already lose.
2. **Arm-side physical protections.** Whether the hardware has current limits, mechanical stops or breakaway springs that bound delivered force independently of the 300-count software clamp; the material establishes only that no hardware *timer* exists.
3. **Studio signal handling while armed.** Mass Lab's worker handles SIGINT/SIGTERM via a flag (apps/mass-lab/src/pointer.rs:257-259); nothing is stated about the Studio's behaviour on signals or crash-while-armed beyond the Drop path undermined by F-02.
4. **Stillness enforcement in Mass Lab registration.** The Studio gates registration on a still pen (apps/calibration/src/registration.rs:40); the Mass Lab flow in VT-04.5 shows no such check, but diagram omission is not assertion about `apps/mass-lab` code.
5. **Retention and concurrency of evidence and the control socket.** Where `captures/calibration/` lives, whether it is backed up, and what a second Studio instance or a post-crash restart does about a control socket that is "never unlinked" (apps/calibration/src/control.rs:167-169).

## visiontouch-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: visiontouch-rs / VisionClaw haptic input path

Incident class assumed for this review: a custody, data-integrity or security failure arising from the visiontouch-rs study or its planned VisionClaw integration. Every claim below carries the citation given in the material; where the causal story goes beyond what the material states, the confidence line says "inferred". Nothing below relies on diagram order or concurrency that the material does not assert.

---

### R-1 — The planned device-to-client bridge is loopback, unauthenticated by design so far, and feeds an existing, untyped message path
- Topics: VT-07.2, VT-07.3, VT-07.4, VT-07.1
- Chain of events:
  1. Twelve months on, the "only planned integration" is built: pose and stylus button enter VisionClaw via "a local bridge on the device host to the client over loopback, never via the server" (docs/adr/ADR-2017-haptic-input-device-is-a-supporting-study.md:31).
  2. The bridge is new code with no governing record — ADR-2017 leaves it "to a future VisionClaw record" (docs/adr/ADR-2017-haptic-input-device-is-a-supporting-study.md:39) — and today no bridge crate exists (../visiontouch-rs/Cargo.toml:2).
  3. The OPEN question of authenticating the bridge "like any other client input" (:39) is not resolved in the material and has no enforcement anywhere cited.
  4. A web page or local process connects to the loopback endpoint and emits the existing drag messages: nodeDragStart with node id and start position (client/src/features/graph/hooks/useGraphEventHandlers.ts:150), throttled nodeDragUpdate with timestamp (:64), nodeDragEnd (:255), which the server routes straight to position-update handlers (src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/message_routing.rs:52-60). No new server message type is needed, so no new server-side validation exists.
  5. Node positions in the client graph are overwritten by an unauthenticated source; the corruption propagates on sync; the client's data integrity is compromised and the client is lost.
- Evidence: loopback-never-via-server design (ADR-2017:31); the OPEN authentication gap (ADR-2017:39); the full drag-message route exists and is exercised by mouse and SpacePilot today (VT-07.3 sequence, with the EXTERNAL note that "nothing in VisionClaw reads this device"); the arm side supplies only a per-connection reset-relative position (crates/device/src/lib.rs:460-466), so the bridge must invent a frame and, for the XR path, a ray direction (VT-07.4 N2, xr-client/rust/src/selection.rs:76 vs crates/device/src/lib.rs:241).
- Warning signs visible today: the OPEN note itself; no bridge crate in the workspace (Cargo.toml:2); governance ambiguity between the two repositories (ADR-0006 accepted/complete/live in the study repo vs accepted/none/inactive in canon, VT-07.1 N2); the VisionClaw settings already use the word "haptic" for an unrelated VR feature (client/src/features/graph/services/interactionModes/vrArMode.ts:113), inviting scope and trust confusion.
- Confidence: medium (inferred) — the gap is stated as a future requirement; the incident assumes the bridge was built without closing it.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The clean-room guarantee is convention-only, and its two structural controls have already failed: the implementer/spec wall broke, and the legal re-open trigger fired twice without action
- Topics: VT-01.1, VT-01.2, VT-01.3, VT-01.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The method is "enforced by convention and commit order, not by CI: only the 'no vendor artefact tracked' step is mechanical" (VT-01 developer summary); BASELINE states invariants 2, 3 and 12 are process rules by review (docs/BASELINE-visiontouch-rs.md:49, cited at VT-01.2).
  2. Both decompilation entries were made by "a coding agent in the specification role", while PROVENANCE requires decompilation "by the spec team never the implementer" (docs/clean-room/PROVENANCE.md:20 vs docs/clean-room/DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:42 and :82, VT-01.3 DIVERGENCE). The wall between the person who reads vendor code and the person who writes ours is therefore already breached in the record.
  3. The exclusion of DECOMPILE findings from the paper is held only by prose: "no docs/spec file cites P-0036 or P-0041, the exclusion is held by the research notes" (docs/research/overload-and-calibration.md:9 and :52, VT-01.3). Nothing structural prevents a paper paragraph from inheriting a spec paragraph that descends from a DECOMPILE row, against the CDPA s50B(3) posture (docs/clean-room/POLICY.md:8, docs/legal/POSITION.md:33).
  4. Rules 6 and 7 (no verbatim vendor strings or tables, no vendor trademarks) are "judgement only" — the gate scans file extensions and paths alone (scripts/quality-gate.sh:38, .gitignore:7-13).
  5. Trigger T3, "any decompilation of a vendor binary", has already fired twice (D-0001 and D-0002) and "the position document is unchanged" (docs/legal/POSITION.md:46 vs docs/clean-room/DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:11, VT-01.4 DIVERGENCE). A rights-holder contact (T4) then finds decompile-derived text in the paper, or a verbatim string in a tracked file that the extension scan passed; the private-evaluation posture is void and the publication or installation client withdraws.
- Evidence: the four citations above; additionally the ledger itself has already drifted ("Initially empty" header vs D-0001 at :11 and D-0002 at :52, docs/clean-room/DECOMPILATION-LOG.md:3, VT-01.3 DOC-DRIFT), and DESIGN rows are absent from both the class table and the paper-eligible list (docs/clean-room/PROVENANCE.md:14-20 vs docs/legal/POSITION.md:33, VT-01.1 N2), so 24 rows of derivation have no defined paper status.
- Warning signs visible today: two DIVERGENCE notes and one DOC-DRIFT note in VT-01 alone; a verification regime whose only mechanical step is a filename scan.
- Confidence: high (the control failures are stated); the publication-incident course is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Evidence capture and records degrade silently: the recorder continues after failed log writes, no gate step tests the recorder, and Studio evidence can carry commit "unknown" behind a token nobody reads
- Topics: VT-06.2, VT-06.4, VT-08.5
- Chain of events:
  1. During an owner capture session a write to the recorder's JSONL log fails (disk, permissions); "a failed log write only prints a diagnostic and the run carries on with an incomplete capture" (tools/port-recorder/src/lib.rs:279-281).
  2. Capture files land under the ignored vendor-original directory and "the raw logs never enter the tree" (VT-06.2 after-text), so the repository holds no second copy.
  3. The recorder's own check script "is run by hand and no gate step runs it" (AGENTS.md:53 and tools/port-recorder/README.md:11 vs scripts/quality-gate.sh:35, VT-06.4 N2), so a regression in the recorder's write path is not caught by CI or the local gate.
  4. Later, the Studio owner sitting runs outside a git checkout, so every AttemptRecord records software.commit from a run-time `git rev-parse HEAD` that "falls back to unknown outside a checkout" (apps/calibration/src/evidence.rs:534-544).
  5. When the first real force figures are challenged, the evidence chain cannot be reproduced: truncated captures, unknown producing-binary provenance, and the output-off-before-write ordering guaranteed only by "the one confirm call site", since "every writer takes the OutputOff as _off and never reads it" (apps/calibration/src/evidence.rs:490 and apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:495). The claim collapses and is withdrawn.
- Evidence: the four cited notes; also session.json is rewritten after each change and attempt JSONs are rewritten when readings arrive (VT-08.5), multiplying the writes that the unread token is supposed to gate.
- Warning signs visible today: all three defects are already marked in the material; the gate's own description limits it to "software claims only" (VT-06.4 after-text), so nothing anywhere checks evidence completeness.
- Confidence: high (the defects are stated); the incident course is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The continuous-force cap is dead code
- Topics: VT-03.1
- Evidence: "CONTINUOUS_FORCE_N is documented as the cap for content and ADR-0002 relies on it" (docs/adr/ADR-0002-layered-software-force-envelope.md:38) "but no code reads the constant, only crates/device/src/lib.rs:50 mentions it".
- Failure: any consumer other than the Studio (which enforces its own 10 s over-1.0 N budget at apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:14) arms with limits near the 2.0 N ceiling and holds force indefinitely; the documented 1.0 N continuous cap never applies.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-02 — Encoder unwrap accepts any delta and a wrong unwrap is permanent for the connection
- Topics: VT-03.2
- Evidence: "unwrapping assumes less than half a counter revolution between samples, nothing checks it" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:196-197); continuous counts accumulate via wrapping_sub (:202).
- Failure: a fast motion exceeds half a counter range between two samples; the count jumps by the complement; position is silently offset by a full revolution per channel, with no flag, for the rest of the connection — corrupting registration-relative pose, the speed interlock and any downstream mapping.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — Request expiry runs only when the worker ticks; no watchdog exists anywhere
- Topics: VT-03.3, VT-03.4
- Evidence: "expiry is only evaluated when the worker runs, there is no hardware watchdog" (crates/device/src/lib.rs:59-60 and docs/spec/12-force-feedback.md:91-92); the 25 ms age check sits inside tick (crates/device/src/lib.rs:390-394).
- Failure: the worker stalls after a nonzero demand was written; the last motor offsets persist on the device with nothing on host or hardware timing them out.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-04 — On Wayland a released hold can stay latched true, defeating the hold-released stop
- Topics: VT-08.1, VT-08.7
- Evidence: "a held Space can stay latched after focus loss because no key-up or focus event arrives, only the 250 ms heartbeat and Stop remain" (apps/calibration/src/main.rs:323-330); the hold interlock is at apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:496-500.
- Failure: the operator releases the key or loses focus; held remains true; an attempt continues without a held key for as long as the 250 ms heartbeat stays fresh; this also stresses the socket's consent model, which relies on hold-release being observable (apps/calibration/src/control.rs:420-421, apps/calibration/src/main.rs:172-185).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-05 — A failed shutdown clears the pending sequence, so Drop cannot resend neutral packets
- Topics: VT-02.4, VT-02.5
- Evidence: "end_force_session clears the pending sequence even when shutdown failed, so Drop cannot resend neutral packets, it only retries the port release" (crates/hal/src/device.rs:345 and :326-328).
- Failure: the first shutdown zero packet errors; the arm stays armed with its last offsets and no code path retries the neutral write; recovery depends entirely on the operator reconnecting, which VT-02.5 says higher layers demand but cannot compel.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — Liveness survives re-registration, so force channels are not re-proved at a new pose
- Topics: VT-08.2, VT-08.4
- Evidence: re-registration "clears checks, signs and rungs but keeps liveness and Over" (apps/calibration/src/protocol.rs:758-769, specifically :762-765).
- Failure: the operator re-registers at a different physical pose; the ladder still treats all three channels as proven-live from the earlier pose; a channel that only misbehaves in the new geometry is first discovered during a force step.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the retained state is stated; the geometry dependence is reasoning)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-07 — The thermal attempt has no hold; key release is not a stop
- Topics: VT-08.2, VT-08.3
- Evidence: "a thermal attempt has no hold, only a Confirm every 30 s with a 10 s grace, so release is no longer a stop" (apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:26-28 and :61); thermal is capped at 1.0 N (apps/calibration/src/protocol.rs:684-692) and bounded at 660 s (apps/calibration/src/README.md:58, apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:61).
- Failure: the operator releases the key expecting the attempt to end; it continues for up to 30–40 s, or to 660 s if Confirm input is absent or latched (see F-04), at the 1.0 N cap.
- Confidence: high (stated); compounding with F-04 is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-08 — A hand-typed Celsius reading is live input to an interlock while output may be on
- Topics: VT-08.3, VT-08.5
- Evidence: submit_reading refuses every non-Celsius reading while an attempt runs, "the one exception is the casing temperature, which feeds the thermal interlock" (apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:1014-1018 and :1030-1033; apps/calibration/README.md:68-71); ADR-0003 does not name the exception (:52-53 vs README:70-71).
- Failure: a mistyped casing temperature under-reports; the thermal interlock's only environmental input is wrong, and over-continuous-cap accrues on model force alone (apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:519-527).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-09 — Same-user socket clients have the full trust of the local interface
- Topics: VT-08.7
- Evidence: "the socket is not a boundary against code already running as the owner, same-user clients have the trust of the local interface" (docs/spec/17-local-control.md:30); the queue holds 8 jobs applied at 4 per frame (apps/calibration/src/control.rs:28, :171, :200-217); Prepare reaches app.request (apps/calibration/src/control.rs:410-422).
- Failure: any script or process running as the owner prepares powered attempts or floods the job queue, racing the operator's UI; the only barrier is the blocked-flag hold semantics, which depend on hold-release being observable (see F-04).
- Confidence: high (the trust model is stated); exploitation is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-10 — The effects crate documents the wrong coordinate frame
- Topics: VT-05.1, VT-05.3
- Evidence: "positions are said to be relative to the calibrated centre" (crates/effects/src/lib.rs:6) but the model frame is the registered reset pose (docs/adr/ADR-0004-reset-pose-registration.md).
- Failure: a caller feeds positions in a calibrated-centre frame; every effect evaluates at a systematically offset origin — contacts, pops and detents fire in the wrong place, silently, because the guard still limits magnitude.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-11 — Invalid effect steps return a quiet zero frame
- Topics: VT-05.1
- Evidence: non-finite input or dt outside 0–25 ms "gives an all-zero frame and no state change" (crates/effects/src/lib.rs:232-234), and the material's own gloss: "An invalid step returns a zero frame the caller should treat as a fault, not a quiet success."
- Failure: a NaN position (e.g. from F-02) or a stalled loop's dt propagates into step; effects report zero force indistinguishable from free space; the fault is never surfaced.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The headless path never exercises arming latency, stale heartbeat or release
- Topics: VT-08.1, VT-08.6
- Evidence: the simulator "arms instantly while the real device runs a zero-only handshake of 5 ms plus four 2 ms preambles, 13 ms of sleeps, so arming latency is never exercised headless" (apps/calibration/src/sim.rs:326-332 vs crates/hal/src/device.rs:252-275); smoke passes held true and heartbeat age 0, so the stale-heartbeat and release interlocks are covered only by controller unit tests (apps/calibration/src/smoke.rs:38, apps/calibration/src/main.rs:164, apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:495-496).
- Failure: a regression in arming or in the release/heartbeat interlocks passes cargo test and the smoke run and is first met on the real arm during the owner sitting.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-13 — The estate promises 6-DoF; the SDK delivers three coordinates and no orientation
- Topics: VT-07.2, VT-07.4
- Evidence: ADR-2017 says the lab wants a 6-DoF input device (docs/adr/ADR-2017-haptic-input-device-is-a-supporting-study.md:23) but the SDK exposes a 3-coordinate position and the wrist counters stay uncalibrated counts (docs/spec/12-force-feedback.md:14-15); PointerInput needs a ray and "a ray direction must be chosen or calibrated" (xr-client/rust/src/selection.rs:76 vs crates/device/src/lib.rs:241).
- Failure: the integration is built to the ADR's wording; a ray direction is invented from position history or the unestablished wrist counters are read as orientation; selection hits wrong targets.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the capability gap is stated, the misuse is reasoning)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-14 — The never-preload-into-root rule is a README line, not a mechanism
- Topics: VT-06.1, VT-06.3
- Evidence: "never preload reference software into a root process, loader variables are set after the launcher" (tools/port-recorder/README.md:34-35); the launcher's own escalation path is sudo -n as the same user (scripts/with-port-access:19).
- Failure: an operator exports LD_PRELOAD before invoking the launcher, or the ordering regresses; the vendor reference program runs recorded under root, and capture files and the vendor-original directory become root-owned, breaking the attended-session custody assumptions.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the rule is stated, its enforcement strength is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The servo loop never catches up, and slow I/O quietly stretches the period toward the gap guard
- Topics: VT-08.1, VT-08.3
- Evidence: "the servo period is a fixed 2.4 ms sleep, a slow real I/O round trip stretches the interval toward the 25 ms gap guard" (apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:1279, apps/calibration/src/controller.rs:36); "the loop never catches up after an overrun, the real interval is measured and the 25 ms gap interlock judges it" (apps/calibration/src/worker.rs:1322-1324).
- Failure: on a loaded host every round trip pushes the measured interval close to, but under, 25 ms; no stop fires, the servo rate collapses, and attempts proceed at a cadence nothing validated.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — both boundary facts are stated, the steady-state collapse is reasoning)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

## vowl-wasm / critical

### F-01 — `loadOntology` silently truncates classes past `max_classes`
- Topics: VW-02.1, VW-02.2
- Evidence: VW-02.2 states the `ParserConfig` cutoff is "enforced at src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64: a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected." The `loadOntology` chain (VW-02.1, src/bindings/mod.rs:56-70) maps only parse/validate/build errors to `JsValue`; truncation is not an error, so nothing reaches JS.
- Failure: an ontology above the cap loads "successfully" with classes (and their edges) dropped. Users reason over an incomplete visualization; no error, warning or count discrepancy surfaces anywhere in the pipeline.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — No advisory/licence check on the release path; `cargo-deny` runs weekly only
- Topics: VW-05.1, VW-05.3
- Evidence: VW-05.1 places `cargo-deny` only in CI on "push main / PR / weekly cron" (ci.yml:3-12), with the comment that "a green build can go red without a code change" (ci.yml:10). The full release sequence VW-05.3 (release.yml:28-173) enumerates version assert → inventory verify → wasm-pack build → test gates → metadata pin → notices → pack → publish, with no cargo-deny or advisory step.
- Failure: an advisory publishes Tuesday; a tag pushed Wednesday builds and ships the browser bundle with the vulnerable dependency. Nothing fails until the next Monday cron, after the artifact is immutable on npm.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from the absence of the step in the material's own enumeration of release.yml
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — `#!deny(unsafe_code)` vs the `unsafe` SIMD kernel, which no CI job ever compiles
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-03.4, VW-05.1, VW-05.2, VW-04.5
- Evidence: VW-01.1 cites src/lib.rs:136-137 `#!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)`. VW-03.4 shows `unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()` at src/layout/simd.rs:386 (target_feature simd128) plus a wasm32 runtime probe `is_simd_available()` (src/layout/simd.rs:516) implying the kernel is meant to execute. The only wasm32 build in CI uses `--features ngg1,interaction` (VW-05.1 W32) and so does the bundle (VW-04.5); the feature matrix (VW-05.2) checks features natively only — so wasm32+`simd` is never built anywhere described. The coincident-node invariant (VW-03.4) explicitly names only "the scalar leaf path" and "the CSR tick", omitting the SIMD kernel.
- Failure: Either wasm32+`simd` fails to compile against the crate-level deny (the feature is dead and the runtime probe is unreachable), or an undocumented `allow(unsafe_code)` ships an unsafe SIMD kernel that no pipeline has ever compiled or tested — a lane-math or coincident-point bug there corrupts positions or traps at runtime, and the advertised crate-wide unsafe ban is not actually in force. The analogous wasm32×`parallel`/`debug-serde` combinations are likewise unvalidated.
- Confidence: high that the two statements conflict as written; the production consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Zero-copy `positionsPtr` is valid only until wasm memory grows
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-04.2, VW-04.6
- Evidence: `positionsPtr()` returns a "raw pointer into wasm.memory.buffer" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:84) and the worker constructs `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)` (VW-04.6). The "no copy at all" invariant (VW-04.2) holds only if no wasm allocation grows linear memory afterwards; the material never states the pointer is re-fetched or the view rebuilt, while `loadCsr`, `reheat`, `pinNode` and string-returning calls all occur on the same instance.
- Failure: any allocation that grows memory detaches the cached buffer — the worker throws on the next frame read (or reads moved memory on non-detaching engines), freezing or killing the visualization exactly when allocations happen (drag-reheat, reload).
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the hazard follows from the stated design; the worker's re-fetch cadence is unstated
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — ADR-001 is recorded "implemented" while the contract it exists for is not delivered
- Topics: VW-01 (audit qualification), VW-02.2, VW-04.8
- Evidence: The audit qualification says ADR-001 is "accepted, implemented for NGG1 ownership, active" while its own caveat is that "the plugin, the TypeScript reader consolidation and the wider acceptance evidence are not claimed"; VW-04.8 N2 marks the same items OPEN (ADR-001:89-93). Meanwhile the Rust-side markdown parser, validator and loader were deleted (VW-02.2; ADR-001:25-26; README.md:204-205), leaving `StandardParser` as the only ingest here.
- Failure: The consolidation that justified deleting functionality does not exist: TypeScript readers and the Obsidian plugin parse the same sources independently of this crate's `StandardParser`, so the two sides can disagree (on truncation, empty labels, domain strings) and render different graphs for the same vault — with no gate tying them together.
- Confidence: high (stated); the reader-divergence consequence is the direct implication
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — audit-qualification caveat and OPEN marker at VW-04.8 N2

### F-06 — Binary ingest path has no size cap; the only guard is a comment tuned for ≤1,500 nodes
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-03.9, VW-03.10
- Evidence: The only stated size guard anywhere is `max_classes` on the JSON path — and it silently truncates (VW-02.2). The NGG1 path parses arbitrary bytes and allocates positions/velocities of len n*2 with no stated cap (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:369-371, VW-03.9); `SimConfig` is only "commented as tuned for the scoped tier of at most 1,500 nodes" (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:82, VW-03.10) — a comment, not a check.
- Failure: A corrupt or oversized producer tier loads without error into a worker whose physics constants are wrong for that scale — either an OOM or a minutes-long settle in the render worker, or a silently bad layout. The two ingest paths also protect oppositely: JSON silently drops data, binary accepts everything.
- Confidence: medium — stated asymmetry plus absence of any stated cap on the binary path
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — The worker's permissive lookups and `optionalMethod` silently mask binding regressions
- Topics: VW-04.5, VW-04.6
- Evidence: VW-04.6: the worker resolves exported names "permissively (snake_case per Rust, with camelCase fallbacks)" (physics.worker.ts:33-37) and binds `pinNode`/`unpinNode`/`reheat` via `optionalMethod`, whose "message handlers return silently when the loaded package lacks them" (physics.worker.ts:126-128). The worker pins the npm package independently (VW-04.6), so worker and package versions can skew.
- Failure: A package regression, rename, or version skew that drops a method produces no error anywhere — drag pinning (or any optional capability) just stops working in production with no signal. This defeats the d.ts symbol gate, which only runs at build time in CI, not against the version the deployed worker actually loads.
- Confidence: high — the silent-fallback behavior is stated; the masking consequence is direct
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — No panic hook and `panic = "abort"`: every Rust panic is an opaque trap
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-05.5
- Evidence: `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:157/166, VW-01.5) — so no hook exists. The release profile sets `panic=abort` (Cargo.toml:90-95, VW-05.5) and the published bundle is built from it.
- Failure: Any panic (index bug, slice math in the SIMD kernel, an unwrap in parsing) becomes an "unreachable executed" trap that aborts the module — in the worker, the entire physics/render loop dies with no stack, no message and no recovery; production triage starts from a bare trap.
- Confidence: high — both facts stated; consequence is direct
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — NGG1 format custody sits in an external 0.x crate behind a glob re-export
- Topics: VW-01.4, VW-03.6
- Evidence: The codec is `ontomark-ngg1` 0.1.0 from crates.io (Cargo.toml:45-46); `src/ngg1.rs` is one `pub use ontomark_ngg1::*;`; and the byte layout "cannot be re-read from this repository at 89d1a0a" (VW-03.6, marked Open). A 0.x dependency carries no stability promise, and a 0.1.x patch is picked up automatically by any non-`--locked` build (release is `--locked`, VW-05.3; the CI test jobs shown are not).
- Failure: An upstream 0.1.x change to header/stride semantics — or any rename propagated through the glob re-export — silently alters binary-format handling this repo can no longer verify from source. The golden-fixture test catches only the cases ontomark encoded; CI-tested dependency versions can differ from the ones the release publishes.
- Confidence: high for the statements; the drift mechanism is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the byte-layout verification gap is marked Open (VW-03.6); the 0.x pin and glob re-export risks are unmarked

### F-10 — Two independent physics engines and two independent Barnes-Hut quadtrees
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.7
- Evidence: The authors' own DEBT note (VW-03.5): "ForceSimulation and CsrSimulation each carry their own Barnes-Hut quadtree … a fix to one does not reach the other" (src/layout/quadtree.rs:126, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:160-169). VW-03.7 confirms they deliberately share no trait.
- Failure: A numerics fix — e.g., to the coincident-node nudge or the theta test at src/layout/quadtree.rs:300 — lands in one engine only. Main-thread `WebVowl` layouts and worker `NggExplorer` layouts of the same data diverge, surfacing as "the explorer renders differently than the editor" with no test tying the two implementations together.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VW-03.5)

### F-11 — No verification of the published bundle in a real browser or deployed consumer
- Topics: VW-01 (audit qualification), VW-04.5, VW-04.6, VW-05.1
- Evidence: The audit qualification states the passing run "covers native logic, not optional ngg1, interaction, browser loading or a deployed consumer" and that the 136-test count "was not re-run at 89d1a0a". The only browser-adjacent checks are a Node test (`node --test tests/ngg1_wasm.test.mjs`, ci.yml:241-242) and d.ts symbol presence (VW-04.5); the actual consumer lives in another repo with separate governance (VW-04.6).
- Failure: The bundle's first execution in an actual browser worker happens in production. Environment-specific behavior — the SIMD runtime probe (src/layout/simd.rs:516), worker APIs, memory growth — is unexercised until then, so F-03/F-04-class defects are discovered by users.
- Confidence: high — the coverage limits are stated
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes, disclosed as an audit-qualification caveat, though not flagged as a defect

### F-12 — Licence-notice completeness of the already-published 0.2.0 tarball is unconfirmed and uncorrectable in place
- Topics: VW-05.3, VW-05.6
- Evidence: VW-05.6's final line: "The earlier statement that the 0.2.0 tarball carried only the project and WebVOWL licences is not restated at this pin and is not confirmed here." Immutability rules (release.yml:86-90; tools/release-inventory README:18-22) mean a wrong notice in a published tarball is corrected only by a separate companion asset.
- Failure: If the shipped 0.2.0 bundle's THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md was incomplete, the artifact on npm is permanently non-compliant; consumers who never fetch the companion asset redistribute it without required attributions.
- Confidence: medium — the material explicitly declines to confirm either way
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — the authors flag the claim as unconfirmed

### F-13 — Contradiction over whether any bundle-size budget is enforced
- Topics: VW-04.5, VW-05.4, VW-05.5
- Evidence: VW-04.5 CHECK3 describes the bundle gate as covering "real wasm module, size budget, exact frozen JS API" (ci.yml:233-239). VW-05.4 says the <1.5MB target is "aspirational annotations in the source, not enforced by any CI assertion", contrasting only "the bundle-symbol check in VW-04.5, which IS enforced". The two statements about the same CI lines disagree on whether a size budget exists.
- Failure: If size is ungated (VW-05.4's claim), nothing stops the bundle — built with `wasm-opt = false` (VW-05.5) and grown by the 0.2.0 ngg bindings — from blowing past the worker's load budget. If it is gated, VW-05.4 misleads maintainers about what protects them. Either way, one document is wrong about a release gate.
- Confidence: high that the texts conflict; medium on which is correct
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Hit-testing uses a fixed 20.0 radius and fails silently on malformed input
- Topics: VW-04.3
- Evidence: radius=20.0 is "a fixed constant in the binding, not read from per-node visual size", with the source's own comment "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node" (src/bindings/mod.rs:425-426); malformed arrays produce `console.warn` + `None` (src/bindings/mod.rs:407-413), indistinguishable to the caller from a genuine miss.
- Failure: At zoom levels where rendered nodes are not ~20 world units, clicks select the wrong node or nothing; caller-side input-shape bugs surface as "clicks randomly don't work" with only a console warning.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low

### F-15 — Release-inventory guard runs only on tags, so `main` drifts and releases fail late
- Topics: VW-05.1, VW-05.6
- Evidence: VW-05.6 N2 (marked TENSION): "the guard runs on tags and manual dispatch only, so on main a source edit can leave docs/release-inventory.json stale until the next release attempt fails". Corroborated by ci.yml:50-52: the `release-notices` job "deliberately does not run its verify against the reviewed sources" on `main`.
- Failure: Every release following mid-week source edits fails at inventory-verify with a hash mismatch, converting releases into a fix-the-inventory-then-retry ritual. Under release pressure, the documented escape is updating hashes — with only the README's own warning (tools/release-inventory/README.md:18-22, "never to bypass a failed check") standing between a rushed hash bump and shipping unreviewed notices.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, VW-05.6 N2)

## Not judgeable from this material

- Whether `PinManager` is enabled by default and whether any binding can enable it. The frozen surface (VW-04.1) lists `pinNode`/`unpinNode`/`isPinned`/`resetPins`/`getPinnedCount` but no `enablePinning`; `pin_node` returns `InteractionError("Pinning is disabled")` when disabled (VW-02.8). If the default is disabled with no enable path, the entire pin API on `WebVowl` always errors. I need `PinManager::default` and the `WebVowl` constructor.
- Whether a second `loadOntology()` resets pins and layout state on `WebVowl`. The CSR side documents that "every load clears the pins" (VW-03.5); the `VowlGraph` side is silent, so stale pins keyed by node id could survive a reload and pin unrelated nodes.
- `GraphBuilder` behavior when a property's source/target references a class id absent from the `class` array — does `add_edge` fail, create a phantom node, or drop the edge (VW-02.3)? This decides whether malformed ontologies render broken graphs silently.
- The worker's view lifecycle: whether `positionsPtr()` is re-fetched every frame or the `Float32Array` is cached at init (VW-04.6 shows construction, not cadence) — this decides whether F-04 fires in practice.
- The default value of `max_classes` and what `validate()` actually checks (src/ontology/parser.rs:485) — these determine how likely F-01's silent truncation is on real ontologies.

## vowl-wasm / premortem

# Post-mortem — vowl-wasm production incident

Basis: the vowl-wasm estate documentation (topics VW-01…VW-05), files stamped `verified_commit 28af4365a68d9aacfd7c04f63299cc96ad717ce3`, re-read at `89d1a0a` on 2026-10-29 without re-running tests. No incident record exists in the material; the root causes below are ranked hypotheses built only from defects the material states or directly implies. All citations are the ones the material itself gives; where a claim rests on absence from a diagram rather than an assertion, that is said.

### R-1 — `loadOntology` silently truncates large ontologies at `max_classes` and returns success
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-02.1, VW-02.3
- Chain of events:
  1. A client ontology JSON arrives with a `class` array longer than `ParserConfig.max_classes`.
  2. `parse_classes` truncates the array at the cap silently — no error, no warning (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64).
  3. `StandardParser::validate` then validates the already-truncated `OntologyData` (src/ontology/parser.rs:485), so it passes.
  4. `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` builds a graph missing the dropped classes, while properties still flow into `graph.add_edge(source, target, edge)` (src/graph/builder.rs:38-55) with behavior on missing endpoints unstated in the material.
  5. `loadOntology` resolves Ok (src/bindings/mod.rs:56-70); the client's ontology renders as a subset with zero indication of loss, and decisions are made on incomplete data.
- Evidence: VW-02.2 states verbatim that "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected" (config at src/ontology/parser.rs:17). The VW-02.1 sequence shows no stage between parse and validate that could detect truncation. VW-02.3 shows edges built from the full property list.
- Warning signs visible today: the truncation behavior is documented as a present fact of the parser; no test or CI check anywhere in the material covers over-cap input.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists (stated); inferred that it is this incident's cause.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — NGG1 format ownership left the repo; the byte layout is unverifiable in-repo and the reader landscape is unconsolidated
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.8, VW-01.4, VW-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The NGG1 codec moved to `ontomark-ngg1` 0.1.0 from crates.io behind the `ngg1` feature (Cargo.toml:45-46, :67); `src/ngg1.rs` is a bare `pub use` re-export (src/ngg1.rs:1-5).
  2. The material's own re-read at 89d1a0a could not confirm header size, node stride or field offsets from this repository (VW-03.6, "Open").
  3. ADR-001 explicitly does not claim the TypeScript reader consolidation (ADR-001:89-93; VW-04.8 N2) — at least one other NGG1 reader exists outside this crate's tests.
  4. A tier written by a producer on a different codec/reader revision is decoded here under 0.1.0's layout: offsets misread → wrong graph, or `decodeNgg1` succeeds on bytes the writer meant differently (src/bindings/ngg.rs:119-121). Releases build `--locked` with an inventory-bound closure (release.yml:94-97; docs/release-inventory.json:2-12), so skew arises across releases and producers, not within one build.
  5. `encodeNgg1` re-encodes the misread snapshot (src/bindings/ngg.rs:182-200); the only round-trip evidence is one golden fixture (ci.yml:241-242, tests/ngg1_wasm.test.mjs), so skew on any other tier shape is undetected and the corrupted tier is written back and propagated.
- Evidence: VW-03.6 bullets (ownership moved, the Open byte-layout item, ADR-001:26); VW-04.8 N2; VW-04.5 CHECK4; the doc citation for the 32-byte header / 24-byte node record / `N_DEGREE` at offset 20 already failed re-verification once (VW-03.6).
- Warning signs visible today: the two Open marks; a documentation citation that has already gone stale once; a single-fixture codec test.
- Confidence: medium; inferred (the ownership split and unverifiable layout are stated; the skew-corruption chain is reasoned beyond the material).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Nothing security-gates the publish: the advisory check is weekly on main, not a release step, and the shipped feature paths are the ones the default test legs never run
- Topics: VW-05.1, VW-05.3, VW-05.2, VW-04.5, VW-01 (audit qualification)
- Chain of events:
  1. `cargo-deny` (licenses, advisories, bans, sources) is scheduled weekly "independent of code changes" precisely because "a green build can go red without a code change" (ci.yml:10-11).
  2. The documented release sequence — version assert, inventory verify, wasm-pack build, tests, metadata pin, notices, pack, release upload, publish (release.yml:28-173) — contains no advisory check. (This is asserted by absence from the documented sequence; verify against release.yml directly.)
  3. A newly disclosed advisory against any locked dependency therefore does not block a tag push; the vulnerable bundle is published byte-identical to the GitHub asset (release.yml:161-163).
  4. Compounding: `ngg1_explorer_test` is silently skipped without the `ngg1` feature (Cargo.toml:109-111), and the last documented full test run covered only 136 default-feature native tests — "not optional `ngg1`, `interaction`, browser loading or a deployed consumer" (VW-01 audit note).
  5. Vulnerable or broken explorer code reaches the client's worker; asset immutability (VW-05.6) then prevents replacing the shipped tarball.
- Evidence: ci.yml:10-11 weekly rationale; the release.yml step list as documented; Cargo.toml:109-111; the VW-01 audit qualification paragraph.
- Warning signs visible today: the weekly-only advisory design; the feature-gated silent-skip test; the audit's own statement that browser loading and the deployed consumer were never covered by a test run.
- Confidence: high that the advisory job is weekly and the explorer test is feature-gated (stated); medium that release.yml contains no advisory gate (absence in a sequence diagram); inferred that this is the incident's cause.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Two independent Barnes-Hut implementations; fixes do not propagate
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.3, VW-03.4
- Evidence: author-marked Debt — "ForceSimulation and CsrSimulation each carry their own Barnes-Hut quadtree … a fix to one does not reach the other" (src/layout/quadtree.rs:126, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:160-169); the engines also share no Rust trait (VW-03.7).
- Failure: a numerical guard (coincident-node nudge, softening, theta semantics) corrected in one tree but not the other — the uncorrected engine emits NaN or exploded positions; the guard test `near_coincident_seeds_stay_in_bounds` covers only the CSR paths (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:988).
- Confidence: high (defect marked); failure scenario inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-02 — The production physics engine (`CsrSimulation`) has no debug tracing at all
- Topics: VW-01.8, VW-03.5
- Evidence: `DebugFlags` is bound only to `ForceSimulation` (src/layout/simulation.rs:50, 55; VW-01.8 diagram); the entire VW-03.5 CsrSimulation section exposes no debug hooks; even ForceSimulation's flags compile to no-ops off wasm32 (src/debug.rs:171-183).
- Failure: worker-layout misbehavior in production is undiagnosable — the engine the worker actually drives (VW-04.6) has no positions/forces logging path.
- Confidence: high (absence in material).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — The ids↔positions zip invariant is enforced only by doc comments
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-04.2
- Evidence: "index-stable: ticking, pinning, filtering and folding never reorder or remove nodes" is cited to doc comments (src/bindings/mod.rs:210-256); no test pinning it is named, while the same binding surface exposes `filterByDegree`/`filterHierarchy`/`toggleDatatypes` etc.
- Failure: a filter change removes or reorders nodes; JS that zipped `ids[i] ↔ xy[2i], xy[2i+1]` once per load attributes positions to the wrong ontology classes — wrong data delivered with no error.
- Confidence: medium; inferred (invariant asserted, enforcement unverified).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — `writePositionsInto` silently partial-writes short buffers
- Topics: VW-04.2
- Evidence: "writes min(out.len(), n*2)" and "returns count written" (src/bindings/mod.rs:303-312); no error on shortfall.
- Failure: caller passes a short or stale buffer and ignores the count; the unread tail is last frame's — or another tier's — coordinates.
- Confidence: high (behavior stated); failure inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Edge endpoints are never checked against the node set
- Topics: VW-02.3, VW-02.2
- Evidence: the builder calls `graph.add_edge(source, target, edge)` straight from the property list (src/graph/builder.rs:38-55); the material nowhere states missing-endpoint handling, while the parser guarantees classes can be missing (silent truncation, R-1).
- Failure: an edge whose endpoint class was truncated reaches the graph; depending on `VowlGraph`'s underlying `DiGraph`, this either panics inside `loadOntology` (unhandled wasm exception) or creates a dangling edge rendered as corrupt structure.
- Confidence: low (behavior unstated); inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Zero-copy position views can be invalidated by any boundary allocation
- Topics: VW-04.2, VW-04.6, VW-01.7
- Evidence: `positionsPtr()`/`positionsLen()` expose a raw pointer into `wasm.memory.buffer` (src/bindings/explorer.rs:84, 90); the worker wraps it in a `Float32Array` view (VW-04.6); the only protection is the discipline invariant "NggExplorer never allocates across the WASM boundary per frame" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:10).
- Failure: any allocation that grows linear memory (a `loadCsr` tier switch, a future allocating binding) detaches the previously constructed `ArrayBuffer` — the worker throws mid-frame — or a cached `ptr`/`len` pair is reused against a different-sized tier and reads another graph's bytes as positions.
- Confidence: medium; inferred (detach-on-grow is standard wasm behavior, not stated in the material).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

- Evidence: the published tarball and GitHub asset are "byte-identical" (release.yml:161-163) and an existing browser tarball "is never replaced — a corrected notice ships as a separate companion asset" (release.yml:86-90).
